# Register of proposed cross-paper connections (all, with independent verdicts)

```text
Status: PROPOSED connections with independent skeptical verification; none is a theorem unless its verdict and text say so
Scope: theorem database of 154 full/partial Level-1 readings, the frontier ledger, the repository bridge, 2024-2026 developments
Exact sources or dependencies: per proposal
What was actually run: 8 discovery agents (one per mechanism) and 8 independent verifier agents
Smallest remaining gap: proposals marked needs_correction must be read together with the correction
```

Verdict legend: `sound_and_useful` (checked, not found in prior art), `sound_but_known` (correct, prior art given), `needs_correction` (correction given; read it), `vocabulary_only` / `incompatible_hypotheses` / `false` (negative knowledge).


## CON-0032 — Beurling frontier for RH and quasi-RH: a case split makes the Euler-product + integer-regularity obstruction unconditional for beta > 2/5, and maps the open region (beta < 1/2, Theta >= 2/3) relevant to the 7/8 claim

*Mechanism:* M7-negative-synthesis · *Verdict:* **sound_and_useful** · *Significance:* 6/5 · *Difficulty:* 3/5

**Source statements.**
- Broucke-Debruyne-Revesz, arXiv:2309.01567v2, Theorem 1.1: for any alpha in [0,1) and beta in [1/2,1) there is an [alpha,beta]-system, i.e. psi_P(x) = x + O_eps(x^{alpha+eps}) and N_P(x) = ax + O_eps(x^{beta+eps}) for every eps > 0 and for no eps < 0.
- BDR Theorem 1.3: assume RH. Then there is a [1/2,beta]-system for each 0 <= beta < 1/2, and an [alpha,beta]-system for 1/2 < alpha < 2/3 and 2alpha/(alpha+2) <= beta < 1/2. Sec. 5 construction: P_{alpha,beta} = P union P^{1/beta} minus P_S, so zeta_{alpha,beta}(s) = zeta(s) zeta(s/beta)/zeta_S(s). On Re s >= alpha/2 + eps, log zeta_S(s) = log zeta(s+1-alpha) + O_eps(sqrt(log|t|)), which uses alpha < 2/3. RH enters as 'zeta(s) and 1/zeta(s) << |t|^eps on Re s >= 1/2+eps' and through pi(x) = Li(x) + O(x^{1/2+eps}). N_S(x) = a x^alpha + ..., so zeta_S has a simple pole at s = alpha.
- Hilberdink (2005), via BDR Sec. 1: every [alpha,beta]-system has max{alpha,beta} >= 1/2.
- Broucke, arXiv:2507.13780, Thm 1.6 and Sec. 1: there are systems with N_P(x) = Ax + O(x^{1/2+eps}) whose zeta has infinitely many zeros on a prescribed contour sigma = 1 - f(log|t|) and none to the right (generalising Diamond-Montgomery-Vorhauer). (1.1)-(1.2): N_P = Ax + O(x^theta) gives only the zero-free region sigma > 1 - c(1-theta)/log|t|, which is optimal for theta >= 1/2.
- Observatory quasi-RH dossier Sec. 2.1-2.3: the OpenAI QRH proof uses Mobius multiplicativity, the Mobius-Gauss conversion mu = alpha-bar gamma_2^3 (Hasse; CRT twisted multiplicativity) and the automorphy of Patterson's cubic theta. The generic sextic large sieve cannot work (de Faveri; Liu Remark 10.9). Parity-barrier discussion.
- C2-26 (SP-2): 'no RH-false Beurling system with theta < 1/2 is known unconditionally, and conditionally on RH only down to beta > 2/5 (BDR).'

**Proposed link.** Every Beurling theorem is in particular a theorem about zeta, because the rational system satisfies N(x) = floor(x) = x + O(1), hence P_beta for every beta >= 0. So 'Beurling + P_beta implies X' is refuted either by not-X for zeta (rational witness) or by a construction. A construction that is conditional on X for zeta therefore becomes an unconditional refutation of the implication, by case split.

**Compatibility checks.** Definitions: an [alpha,beta]-system requires exact exponents, while the obstruction needs only the upper-bound property P_beta: N(x) = ax + O(x^{beta+eps}). The rational system and the BDR systems satisfy it. RH-falsity of the BDR Thm 1.3 systems: zeta_S has a simple pole at alpha. zeta(alpha) != 0 for real alpha in (1/2, 2/3). alpha/beta > 1, so zeta(alpha/beta) is finite and nonzero. Hence zeta_{alpha,beta} has a real zero at s = alpha > 1/2 (read from the Sec. 5 text). Range: 2alpha/(alpha+2) <= beta < 1/2 with alpha > 1/2 is solvable iff beta > 2/5, since alpha -> 1/2+ gives 2/5. QRH version (T2): zero-freeness of zeta is needed on Re s >= 1 - alpha/2 + eps, which is > 2/3 because alpha < 2/3, and the primes' error must be O(x^{Theta+eps}) with Theta < alpha. Both follow from QRH(Theta) for Theta < 2/3, using Lindelof to the right of Theta (Titchmarsh 14.2-type argument; dossier Sec. 3). Example: Theta = 0.6 requires beta > 2(0.6)/2.6 = 6/13 = 0.4615. For Theta = 7/8, BDR 1.3 is unavailable (alpha < 2/3), and the double-prime variant (Remark 5.3, alpha < 4/5) needs beta >= 1/2.

**New claim.** T1 (proved here, given BDR 1.3; non-constructive). For every beta > 2/5, the implication 'Beurling system with N_P(x) = ax + O_eps(x^{beta+eps}) implies RH for zeta_P' is FALSE, unconditionally. If RH fails, the rational system is a witness. If RH holds, BDR 1.3 with alpha in (1/2, 2/3) and 2alpha/(alpha+2) <= beta gives a witness with a real zero at alpha. This turns C2-26's RH-conditional boundary 2/5 into an unconditional one. T2 (to verify line by line). BDR Sec. 5 uses RH only through the two inputs above. Hence for Theta in [1/2, 2/3) and beta > 2Theta/(Theta+2), 'Beurling + P_beta implies QRH(Theta)' is false unconditionally, by case split on QRH(Theta) for zeta. T3 (constructive, BDR 1.1). For any Theta < 1 and beta >= 1/2 the QRH(Theta) implication is false. With Broucke 1.6, even zero-free regions beyond de la Vallee Poussin are excluded. T4 (frontier). For beta < 1/2 and Theta >= 2/3, which covers the claimed 7/8 and 11/12, no countermodel is known. Any positive theorem 'Beurling + P_beta implies QRH(Theta)' would prove QRH(Theta) for zeta by a Beurling argument. The Hilberdink-BDR existence conjecture (all [alpha,beta] with max >= 1/2) would make integer regularity useless for QRH. QRH HITTING SET. A proof of QRH(Theta) must use, in its zero-excluding step, an input beyond each of: (a) FE + Dirichlet-series shape (DH has zeros in sigma > 1); (b) sieve axioms (parity: they cannot even give Sum lambda(n) = o(x)); (c) Euler product + integer regularity beta >= 1/2 (BDR 1.1 / Broucke 1.6), and beta > 2Theta/(Theta+2) for Theta < 2/3 (T2); (d) generic large-sieve coefficients (dossier Sec. 2.3/C4). The inputs of the OpenAI 11/12 and 7/8 proofs (Gauss-sum identity, metaplectic theta) lie outside all four, so there is no tension.

**Already proved.** BDR 1.1 and 1.3 (arXiv v2; journal status not checked here). Hilberdink 2005. Broucke 1.6. DMV 2006. T1 is elementary given BDR 1.3 together with my L1 reading of Sec. 5 (pole of zeta_S at alpha, hence a real zero of zeta_{alpha,beta}). T3 is immediate from BDR 1.1.

**Conjectural.** T2 needs a full audit of every invocation of RH in BDR Sec. 5, including the random-prime Theorem 1.2 step and Lemma 5.1. The Hilberdink-BDR conjecture is open. Whether integer regularity beta < 1/2 forces any zero-free region beyond de la Vallee Poussin is open; Broucke-Revesz 2611.03356 (Landau local method) should be read to confirm it gives only the c(1-theta)/log t shape.

**Next step.** (1) Audit BDR Sec. 5 for RH usage and write T2 as a lemma under QRH(Theta). (2) Try to extend the perturbation to alpha >= 2/3 with beta < 1/2, using sharper convolution estimates than Dirichlet's hyperbola method (BDR Remark 5.3(2) says improvements may be possible). The first [alpha,beta]-system with alpha >= 2/3 and beta < 1/2 would extend the Beurling obstruction toward the QRH range. (3) Add the open function A(beta) := sup{alpha : an [alpha,beta]-system exists}, for beta < 1/2, to the frontier ledger as an obstruction-frontier record.

**Potential counterexamples.** A Beurling theorem of the form 'beta < beta_0 implies a zero-free region better than de la Vallee Poussin' would bound A(beta) < 1 and refute the Hilberdink-BDR conjecture. That would be a Beurling route toward QRH-type statements, which must then also hold for zeta. T1 fails if BDR 1.3's construction has an error (refereeing status unverified). T2 fails if Sec. 5 uses RH more deeply than the two inputs identified.

**Tests.** Not run. Planned: for (alpha,beta) = (0.6, 0.47), build the extended-sense system numerically. Use primes up to 10^8 and a deterministic thinning P_S with pi_S ~ Li(x^0.6). Compute N_{alpha,beta}(x) - (zeta(1/beta)/zeta_S(1))x and check its size is about x^{0.47}. Also confirm the sign change of zeta_{alpha,beta}(sigma) at sigma = 0.6 from log zeta_S(sigma) = log zeta(sigma + 0.4) + O(1).

**Prior-art search (proposer).** arXiv API: 'au:Broucke AND au:Debruyne' (found 2309.01567, 2211.08716, 2004.11501, 2109.08509). 'abs:Beurling AND abs:zero-free' (found 2507.13780, 2611.03356, 1101.1199). 'au:Revesz AND abs:Beurling' (found 2012.09045, 2209.01689, 2202.01837, 2207.00665, 2407.12746). 'all:Beurling AND all:quasi AND all:Riemann' (nothing relevant). BDR state 1.3 only conditionally. I found no source with the case-split refutation or a QRH frontier for Beurling systems. Claimed as 'possibly new as an explicit observation'; it is not deep.

**Verifier reasoning.** I read BDR Thms 1.1 and 1.3 and the whole of Sec. 5. The construction is P_{alpha,beta} = P union P^{1/beta} minus P_S, with zeta_{alpha,beta} = zeta(s)zeta(s/beta)/zeta_S(s). Since N_S(x)=a x^alpha+O(x^{alpha/2+eps}) with a = Res zeta_S/alpha > 0, zeta_S has a pole at alpha. Because zeta(alpha) is nonzero on (0,1) and alpha/beta>1, zeta_{alpha,beta} has a real zero at alpha > 1/2. Range: alpha > 1/2 and alpha <= 2beta/(2-beta) are compatible iff beta > 2/5. RH enters Sec. 5 in only two places. (1) pi(x)=Li(x)+O(x^{1/2+eps}), used only to make the prime exponent exactly alpha. (2) zeta and 1/zeta << |t|^eps, which is needed only on Re(s+1-alpha) >= 1-alpha/2+eps > 2/3, to bound zeta_S^{+-1} on Re s >= alpha/2+eps. Lemma 5.1, the random-prime Theorem 1.2, the PNT and nonvanishing on the real axis are unconditional. So T1 holds by the case split, and the logic is valid because the rational system has N(x)=x+O(1). T2 holds under QRH(Theta) for Theta<2/3, using Lindelof and 1/zeta << t^eps to the right of Theta (the Titchmarsh 14.2 argument). The example 6/13 is correct. T3 follows from BDR 1.1. Remark 5.3(1) confirms that the alpha<4/5 variant needs beta>=1/2. BDR do not make the case-split remark. Repo C2 REPORT l.88 lists exactly this question as open ('Is there an unconditional RH-false Beurling system with N(x)=Ax+O(x^theta), theta<1/2?'). T1 answers it, non-constructively, for theta>2/5. Caveats: the witness is non-constructive (it depends on the truth value of RH), BDR is an arXiv preprint whose journal status I did not verify, and the device is an elementary Littlewood-style case split. The 'QRH hitting set' restates known obstructions.

**Verifier correction.** Minor. Define 'RH for zeta_P' as psi_P(x)=x+O(x^{1/2+eps}), since Beurling zeta functions need not continue (these ones do). State T1 as existence of a discrete system, non-constructively. In T2, say that the hypothesis actually needed is zero-freeness of zeta with Lindelof-type bounds on Re s > 1-alpha/2, and that the prime error O(x^{alpha-delta}) matters only for the exactness of alpha.

**Prior art found by verifier.** No statement of the case split found in BDR 2309.01567 or in the Broucke 2507.13780 and 2611.03356 abstracts. The device is classical (Littlewood 1914). Inside the repo, C2-26 states the conditional 2/5 boundary and C2 REPORT l.88 lists the unconditional question as open.


## CON-0038 — Ji's independent Q(i) route cannot reach 11/12 from zero-density inputs (its floor is >= 0.99673 even under GRH for the quartic family), so an OpenAI-independent 11/12 needs a quartic row mean square, with an explicit sufficient window

*Mechanism:* M8-conjecture-generation · *Verdict:* **sound_and_useful** · *Significance:* 5/5 · *Difficulty:* 7/5

**Source statements.**
- Ji arXiv:2611.16665v2, Sect. 2.4: H = X^{65/144}, Y = X^{1/288} (eq. 9); Prop. 2.1: |I(X,H,Y)| << (XHY)^eps X^2 Y^5 H^{-2}; Prop. 2.2: I_np << X^{599/600+eps} Y H^{1/4}; Prop. 2.3: I_pr = K_0 Y H^{1/4} S_W(X) + O(X^{1+eps} Y^{13/25} H^{1/4} + X^{1+eps} Y H^{1/8}).
- Ji Lemma 4.2: reciprocal bound L^{-1} << X^eps right of a + Delta in a zero-free rectangle, valid for 5/8 <= a <= 1; eq. (24): |P_u(z,w)| << |u|^{2(eps + Lambda(sigma,theta))}, Lambda(sigma,theta) = max(0, 1 - 3sigma/2, 1 - sigma - 13 theta).
- Ji eq. (27) and Lemma 4.3 (eq. 28): the number of u_0 with |u_0|^2 ~ Q having a zero with Re s >= sigma is O(Q^{E(sigma)+eps} T^{2+eps}), with E(sigma) from de Faveri arXiv:2610.04045 Cor. 1.5 (n = 4): 1 - q0/(4(1-q0)) on [1/2,3/4], 1 - q0(4q0-1)/(24q0 - 16q0^2 - 6) on [3/4,7/8], 6(1-sigma)/(1+4(1-sigma)) on [7/8,1], q0 = 2sigma - 1; the condition is sigma + (65/144)(max(E - 1/4, 0) + Lambda(sigma,1)) <= 599/600. The top group needs E(Theta) + 6/25 - 1/4 < 0.
- Observatory quasi_rh DOSSIER section 4.1: extraction beta(n,h) = 1/2 + h(n-1)/(2n) from a full-row mean square sum_{N(u)<=D^h} |A_u|^2 << D^{1+h+eps}; (C2): n = 4 over Q(i) would give 7/8; for n = 4 'an explicit quartic metaplectic theta ... that removes mu' is the missing duality partner.
- paper2 Prop. 3.1 (l.679-689): sum_{0<N(u)<=D^{1+theta}} |A_u(D)|^2 << D^{2+theta+eps}, the sextic template.

**Proposed link.** We transcribed Ji's argument into a finite system of exponent inequalities in (Theta, h = log H/log X, y = log Y/log X, a = w-contour). C1: 2 + 4y - 9h/4 <= Theta (Prop. 2.1 divided by the main term Y H^{1/4}). C2: 1 - (1-a)y <= Theta (main-term error / top group). C3: 1 - h/8 <= Theta. C4: top group, E(Theta) + (1-a)/2 - 1/4 < 0, with a generalisation allowing positive exponent. C5: sigma + h(max(E(sigma) - 1/4, 0) + Lambda(sigma,1)) <= Theta on [s_0, Theta). C6: bottom group with trivial E = 1, s_0 + h(3/4 + Lambda(s_0)) <= Theta, s_0 >= 5/8. We then minimised Theta for various density inputs E(sigma). The binding constraints are C1 and C6, not the density: Prop. 2.2 sums absolute values over all ~H nonprincipal rows, which must be beaten by the H^{1/4} principal fourth-power rows. This is the first-moment analogue of the sixth-power extraction, and only a row mean square (cancellation across rows) removes it.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Transcription validated: Ji's published parameters (h, y, a) = (65/144, 1/288, 13/25) are feasible at Theta = 599/600 and infeasible at 0.9983 in our system. Our optimum with de Faveri's E is 0.998327, which matches 599/600 = 0.998333 to 6e-6 (so Ji's choice is essentially optimal). (2) Density normalisation: Q^{E} counts u_0 of norm ~Q. The family has ~Q members of conductor ~Q, so DH reads E_DH(sigma) = 2(1-sigma); de Faveri's E ~ 6(1-sigma) near 1 is three times DH. (3) The GRH scenario sets E = -infinity for sigma > 1/2 and allows s_0 -> 1/2, since under GRH 1/L << X^eps right of 1/2 + Delta. (4) Extraction for n = 4: rows u = p^4 with N(p) ~ Y = H^{1/4} give |A_1|^2 << X^a H^{b-1/4} log + X^2 H^{-1/2} from any bound sum_{N u<=H} |A_u|^2 << X^a H^b. This matches the dossier's beta(4,h) = 1/2 + 3h/8 at a = b = 1.

**New claim.** (A) Computed floor theorem, within our transcription of Ji's inequalities (Props. 2.1-2.3, Lemma 4.2, eq. (24), Lemma 4.3, proof of Prop. 2.2). inf Theta = 0.998327 with de Faveri's E. For EVERY density input, including DH (E = 2(1-sigma)) and GRH for the quartic family, inf Theta >= 0.996732 while Lemma 4.2 requires a >= 5/8. With s_0 -> 1/2 (valid under GRH) the floor is 89/90 = 0.98889 (DH: 0.98957). Even after also deleting the local-factor loss H^{Lambda(sigma,1)}, the floor is 23/24 = 0.95833 > 11/12. So no zero-density improvement can bring Ji's architecture to 11/12, and improving de Faveri's quartic density estimate is not the bottleneck. (B) Target conjecture J4 (an OpenAI-independent route). Let K = Q(i) and A_u(X) = sum_{(n,1+i)=1} mu_K(n) (u/n)_4 W(N n/X). If, for some fixed h with 1 <= h <= 10/9, sum_{0<N(u)<=X^h} |A_u(X)|^2 << X^{1+h+eps}, then zeta_{Q(i)}(s), and hence zeta(s), has no zeros with Re s > 1/2 + 3h/8 (<= 11/12; -> 7/8 as h -> 1+). More generally, any bound X^a H^b with a + (b - 1/4)h <= 11/6 and h >= 1/3 gives 11/12.

**Already proved.** (A) is a finite optimisation over our transcription (scripts ji_floor.py sha256 6d8f863a...7147, ji_floor2.py c19bc57e...a08d, ji_floor3.py e7759e2e...6555; grid over a with 600-800 points, 45-step bisection on Theta, and sigma sampled at 400 points in C5). It is a statement about Ji's inequality system, not about zeta. The extraction implication in (B) is elementary (paper2 Sect. 3 adapted to n = 4; dossier section 4.1).

**Conjectural.** J4 itself: square-root cancellation on average over quartic rows at or just above the diagonal (H = X^h, 1 < h <= 10/9). Its proof would need a quartic duality partner. Candidates: Ji's/DDHL's quartic Kubota series of angular weight 1, and a quartic analogue of the Gauss-Jacobi conversion that removes mu, as eq:convert2 does for order 6. The dossier notes this is missing for n >= 4. Below the diagonal (h < 1) the statement is beyond the large-sieve barrier.

**Next step.** (1) Have an independent reader re-derive the six inequalities from Ji's text (especially C6 and the H^{Lambda} factor) and rerun ji_floor2.py. (2) Ask whether Ji's Prop. 2.2 can be restructured as a mean square over u (Cauchy-Schwarz across rows plus a quartic large sieve). Compute with de Faveri's optimal quartic sieve Theta_4(A,B) << A + B + A^{3/4}B^{1/2} + A^{1/2}B^{3/4} what row mean square follows (dossier C4 says the generic sieve alone gives nothing). (3) Determine whether the angular-weight-1 quartic Kubota coefficients satisfy a mu-removal identity analogous to eq:gj.

**Potential counterexamples.** (a) If our transcription missed a degree of freedom, e.g. a different grouping of characters, a different w-line per group, or Ji's Prop. 2.1 holding with a better exponent in some range, the floor could be lower. The validation at Ji's own parameters makes gross errors unlikely. (b) J4 could fail for structural reasons: for n = 4, Gauss-sum averages without a suitable angular twist can be biased (DR, de Faveri-Dunn-Hoffstein lower bounds). M8-3's numerics show this bias concretely for the cubic case at m = 0.

**Tests.** T1 (done): the floor table above. T2: sensitivity, recomputing the floor with Prop. 2.1 improved to X^2 Y^5 H^{-2-kappa} to find the kappa needed for 11/12 within Ji's first-moment structure. T3 (numerical, small): compute sum_{N u<=H} |A_u(X)|^2 for X ~ 2000, H ~ X^{1.05} over Z[i] and compare with X*H.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** Ji is the only OpenAI-independent route (Observatory dossier section 1.5). arXiv abs:"zero-free half-plane" (12 records) and abs:"quasi-Riemann" (17) show no analysis of the limits of Ji's architecture. The dossier's (C2) states the n = 4 full-row mean square at h = 1 + theta. The new contributions here are the computed floor, which shows that density inputs are irrelevant, and the explicit sufficiency window h <= 10/9 / a + (b - 1/4)h <= 11/6 for 11/12.

**Verifier reasoning.** I re-derived the inequality system from Ji arXiv:2611.16665v2. Inputs:
- (9): H=X^{65/144}, Y=X^{1/288}.
- Prop 2.1: X^2 Y^5 H^{-2}.
- Prop 2.3 errors: X Y^{13/25} H^{1/4} and X Y H^{1/8}.
- Lemma 4.1: bound (24) valid only for theta=Re w >= 1/2, sigma+theta >= 5/4.
- Lemma 4.2: 5/8 <= a.
- (27)-(29) and the grouping in the proof of Prop 2.2.

The resulting constraints C1-C6 match the proposal's. Ji's parameters are feasible at 599/600 (C2 is tight; the C4 margin is -1/15100) and infeasible at 0.9983. Solving analytically:
- C1, C2, C6 with w-contour >= 1/2 and s0 = 5/8 give u = (1/26)/(9+36/13), so Theta = 0.996732.
- With s0 -> 1/2 (GRH-type), Theta = 89/90.
- With the local-factor loss Lambda also deleted, Theta = 23/24.

My grid optimiser (scratchpad m8verify/jifloor.py) gives 0.99674, 0.98899, and 0.99837 with de Faveri's E. The last is on a coarse grid; Ji's own 599/600 is feasible, which supports the stated optimum of 0.998327. C5 binds near sigma ~ 0.83, consistent with the tight cubic in Ji's Lemma 4.3.

Robustness: even with the w-contour constraint dropped, C1+C6 alone give >= 0.98980 unconditionally and >= 25/26 under GRH. So 'no zero-density improvement reaches 11/12 inside this architecture' is robust. Only the 23/24 figure depends on theta >= 1/2.

Part (B): I re-derived the extraction. The H^{1/4} rows u=p^4 carry a correction of size X^2 H^{-1/2}, which gives the window conditions h >= 1/3 and a+(b-1/4)h <= 11/6 for 11/12, and 1/2+3h/8 for J4.

Minor issues:
- Lambda(sigma,theta) is max(0, 1-3sigma/2, 1-sigma-theta/3). The '13theta' is a pdftotext garble; it is harmless at theta=1.
- The text attributes 'a >= 5/8' to Lemma 4.2, conflating the zero-free abscissa s0 with the w-contour. The w-contour constraint actually comes from Lemma 4.1 (theta >= 1/2).
- J4 is the dossier's n=4 statement (C2), made explicit. It is not a new route.

**Verifier correction.** Minor. Correct the formula to Lambda(sigma,theta) = max(0, 1-3sigma/2, 1-sigma-theta/3). State the w-contour constraint a >= 1/2 from Ji's Lemma 4.1 (bound (24) needs theta >= 1/2) separately from s0 >= 5/8 (Lemma 4.2). Note that 0.996732 and 23/24 depend on a >= 1/2, while the conclusion that no density input reaches 11/12 survives without it (floor >= 0.9898 unconditionally, >= 25/26 under GRH). Credit part (B) to quasi_rh DOSSIER Section 4.1 (C2).

**Prior art found by verifier.** No prior analysis of the limits of Ji's architecture found (arXiv 'quasi-Riemann' list as of 2026-11-17). Part (B) is already in the Observatory quasi_rh DOSSIER Section 4.1, (C2) and beta(n,h)=1/2+h(n-1)/(2n).


## CON-0025 — Exponent-2 class groups and Euler's idoneal list: the Lean-checked Siegel constant suffices (QRH is not needed), but not through EKN Theorem 2

*Mechanism:* M6-compatibility · *Verdict:* **sound_and_useful** · *Significance:* 4/5 · *Difficulty:* 2/5

**Source statements.**
- OpenAI, 'The Quasi-Riemann Hypothesis' (11/12), openai/math @4eaee8754, preprints/The-Quasi-Riemann-Hypothesis-October-5-2026/paper2.pdf, Sec.1, paragraph after Corollary 1.2: 'The class-group computations of Elsenhans, Klueners, and Nicolae [8, Theorem 2], building on Weinberger [50], give a complete list of imaginary quadratic fields with class-group exponent dividing two when there are no Landau-Siegel zeros. Together with Grube's characterization of idoneal numbers and his reduction to fundamental discriminants [15] ... this shows that Theorem 1.1 confirms the completeness of Euler's list of 65 idoneal numbers.'
- Elsenhans-Klueners-Nicolae, arXiv:1803.02056v1 (preliminary version of Acta Arith. 193 (2020) 217-233). Theorem 1: 'There are exactly 1555 imaginary quadratic fields with discriminant |D| <= 3.1*10^20 and class group of exponent <= 8' (unconditional computation). Theorem 2: 'Assuming the non-existence of Siegel-zeros, our computation found all fields with exponents 2,4,8.' The operative hypothesis (Lemma 3 and Sec.4, Theorem 4) is 'L(s,chi) != 0 for 1 - 1/(4 log|D|) <= s < 1', i.e. a zero gap (1-beta) log|D| > 1/4, with N_2 = 11 and d_{N_2} <= 2.01*10^11.
- Kimura, arXiv:2610.21511v2, Theorem 1 and Sec.2: (1-beta) log q >= 5811e-7 for all q >= 3 (Lean, two kernels). The certified constant grows with log q: c_N = 0.005506 for log q >= 28.6787 and 0.005965 for log q >= 35.0131 (Table 1). Proposition 9: a zero gap (c,T) gives L(1,chi) >= (c/log q) e^{-P} (1-eps), with P = c(1+log(2VAT)/T). Eq.(4): pi h(-d) >= sqrt(d) L(1,chi_{-d}). Theorem 2: all 16,453,493 fields with h <= 2000 are known, max |d| = 1,062,350,083. Remark 11 covers the half-plane case.
- Guo, arXiv:2610.30476v1, Theorem 1.1: (1-beta) log q > 1/100 for every primitive quadratic chi, q >= 3. Remark 1.2: c = 1/64 is also attainable. Banks, arXiv:2610.15483, Thm 1.1: 3.0e-3 for q >= 10^10.

**Proposed link.** The phrase 'no Siegel zeros' means a zero gap c = 1/4 in EKN, but only c in [5.8e-4, 1/64] in the 2026 exclusions. So EKN Theorem 2 is not triggered by the Siegel-exclusion papers. The OpenAI chain QRH => EKN hypothesis is valid, since (1-beta) >= 1/12 gives (1-beta) log|D| >= 1/4 once |D| >= e^3. A route that uses only the 2026 Siegel constant is the following. (i) Exponent <= 2 forces h(D) = 2^{omega(D)-1}. (ii) Kimura Prop.9 with c >= 0.005965 (log|D| >= 35.01) gives h(D) >= 0.00539 sqrt|D|/(pi log|D|); this constant is deliberately conservative. (iii) Comparing against primorials, ii exceeds 2^{omega(D)-1} for every |D| >= D1 = 3.2981e16. (iv) Below D1 there are two independent closures. Route A: EKN Theorem 1 is unconditional up to 3.1e20, which is more than D1. Route B: omega <= 11 gives h <= 1024, so the field is in Kimura's Theorem 2 list; omega >= 12 with |D| <= D1 is excluded by this session's split-prime scan.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Normalization: q is the conductor |D| of the primitive real character, and w_K = 2 for |D| > 4, so pi h = sqrt|D| L(1,chi). (2) The Prop.9 conversion at T = 38.03 with A = 1e6, V = 1e4 gives P = 0.01026 and eps = 7.4e-4, hence L(1,chi) >= 0.0059/log|D|. The 0.00539 used here is conservative. (3) Primorial blocks: in block k, omega(D) <= k. The left endpoint already satisfies the bound for k >= 15 (6.149e17). Inside block 14 the threshold is 3.298e16. (4) Sensitivity: Guo's 1/100 alone gives a threshold of 1.31e16, and Banks's 3e-3 gives about 6.15e17; both are below 3.1e20. Kimura's all-q 5.811e-4 alone gives 1.92e21, above EKN's range, so the log q-dependent certified constant (or Guo's) is load-bearing. (5) Split-prime criterion (proved here): if Cl has exponent <= 2 and p splits, then P^2 = (alpha) with alpha not rational, so p^2 = N(alpha) >= |D|/4 and p >= sqrt|D|/2. (6) The criterion is sharp for d <= 1e5: exactly 65 survivors, max 5460, all with h = 2^{omega-1} (checked by counting reduced forms). (7) QRH consistency: all 2026 half-planes (7/8, 11/12, 31/32, B-flat) imply EKN's c = 1/4 for |D| >= e^8, and Platt's GRH check covers the small conductors. So the 11/12 attribution is valid but uses more than is needed.

**New claim.** Conditional only on Kimura's zero-gap constants (Theorem 1 and the certified Table 1 values: Lean-checked, unrefereed) and on either EKN Theorem 1 (refereed computation) or Kimura Theorem 2 plus the scan below, the following holds. The imaginary quadratic fields with class group of exponent <= 2 are exactly the 65 known ones, the largest being |D| = 5460. Via Grube's reduction to fundamental discriminants (Kani 2011, Thm 6), Euler's 65 idoneal numbers are then complete. This needs neither QRH nor EKN's c = 1/4 hypothesis. Separately: EKN Theorem 2 as stated is not implied by any 2026 Siegel-exclusion constant.

**Already proved.** Proved in this session: the threshold algebra (D1 = 3.2981e16) and the split-prime criterion. A rigorous exact-integer search (C program exp2scan.c, sha256 2022745278156494fbb9a8f17df73e86d7d2e8e978333c26cb6b73e220bce0ad) covered every fundamental discriminant -d with d <= 3.2981e16 and omega(d) >= 12: 2,217,855 of the form d=n, 49,375,797 of the form 4n and 32,728,456 of the form 8n. Each has a split prime p <= 163 < sqrt(d)/2, so there are 0 survivors. Sanity checks on the 65 known exponent-2 discriminants and 26 non-examples passed. Imported and not replayed: Kimura Thm 1, Prop 9 and Thm 2; EKN Thm 1; Grube/Kani.

**Conjectural.** That Kimura's Lean statements match Prop.9 for general T >= 35.0131. The published EKN Theorem 1 range was not checked; it would matter only for Route A. Route B makes EKN unnecessary. Grube's order-level reduction was used as cited and not re-derived. The scan has not been independently replayed.

**Next step.** (1) Replay the scan independently, e.g. with PARI qfbclassno on random samples plus a second implementation of the enumeration. (2) Check the Lean statement of Kimura Prop.9 and the certified-constant table in his repository. (3) Check EKN Theorem 1 in the published Acta Arith. version. (4) Write the order-level (non-fundamental -4n) step from Kani 2011, Sec.2.3. (5) Record a corrected ledger note: idoneal completeness follows from the Siegel exclusion and does not need QRH.

**Potential counterexamples.** An error in Kimura's ell-dependent certified constants between log q = 35 and infinity would leave a gap above D1 (Guo's 1/100 would still cover it if correct). A bug in the enumeration, such as missed d of the form 8n or a wrong pruning bound, would leave a gap; the pruning bound is a product of consecutive primes and was checked. The asserted EKN range might differ in the published version.

**Tests.** Recompute D1 from a fresh implementation. Count fundamental discriminants with omega >= 12 below D1 by inclusion-exclusion and compare with the 8.43e7 tested. Verify that Kimura's Theorem 10 (h > 2000 for log d >= 35.0131) follows from the same constants. Spot-check h for 1e4 random scanned discriminants with an independent class-number routine.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** arXiv API (all:idoneal, sorted by date): no 2026 paper; the latest relevant hits are 2003.08914 (idoneal genera) and older. abs:'one class per genus' OR 'exponent two' with 'class group' returned math/0401334, which is conditional on a GUE-type zero hypothesis. arXiv 2605.26020 (class groups of small exponent, conditional) also appeared. The OpenAI 11/12 paper attributes the result to QRH => no Siegel zeros => EKN Thm 2, and Kimura's Remark 11 treats only the QRH thresholds. Neither notes the c = 1/4 vs c <= 1/64 mismatch or the Siegel-only route.

**Verifier reasoning.** The source statements are as cited. EKN Thm 1 is an unconditional count for |D| <= 3.1e20, and the zbMATH review of the published Acta Arith. 193 version confirms 'discriminant <= 31*10^19'. EKN Thm 2's 'no Siegel zeros' hypothesis is the zero gap L(s,chi) != 0 on [1-1/(4 log|D|),1) (Lemma 3, Thm 4). Kimura Thm 1 gives 5811e-7 for all q and 1193/200000 for log q >= 35.0131, both Lean-checked. Kimura's Prop. 9 is a general statement, valid for any T at which the zero gap holds. Guo has 1/100, and 1/64 in Remark 1.2 / App. A. Banks has 3.0e-3 for q >= 1e10. The OpenAI 11/12 idoneal paragraph is quoted correctly. The mismatch is real: 2026 constants are <= 1/64 (Kimura reaches 0.01015 only for log q >= 1e4), against EKN's 1/4, so EKN's hypothesis is not implied, while the 11/12 half-plane does imply it once |D| >= e^3. The math checks out. Genus theory gives h = 2^{omega-1}. The split-prime criterion is EKN Lemma 1 with c = 2. Primorial blocks follow from EKN Lemma 7. With the stated Prop. 9 form (A=1e6, V=1e4) I get P = 0.01028 and eps = 7.4e-4 at T ~ 37.8, hence D1 = 2.725e16; the proposal's 3.298e16 is conservative. My independent scan (myscan.c: Euler-criterion Legendre symbols, separate enumeration) at D1 = 32981000000000000 reproduces the counts exactly: 2,217,855 / 49,375,797 / 32,728,456, with 0 survivors and largest first split prime 163. Route B also needs the exponent-2 fields inside Kimura's h <= 2000 list, i.e. d <= 1.06e9. I checked this: over all fundamental discriminants d <= 1.1e9 the split-prime criterion leaves exactly 65 survivors, the largest 5460, matching EKN Table 1 (9 + 56). The headline claim holds by both routes.

**Verifier correction.** (1) Sensitivity item (4) is wrong, in the direction that strengthens the result. The proposal tested the inequality only at primorial left endpoints. Bisecting inside each primorial block shows that Kimura's all-q Lean constant 5.811e-4 alone excludes exponent <= 2 for every |D| >= 6.60e19, which is below EKN's 3.1e20 (block 16: the threshold 6.6e19 lies inside [3.26e19, 1.92e21)). So Route A closes with Kimura Thm 1's first clause plus Prop. 9 alone, and the log q-dependent constant is not load-bearing. Corrected thresholds: Guo 1/100 gives 2.14e15 (not 1.31e16), Guo 1/64 gives 8.5e14, Banks 3e-3 gives 1.15e17 (not 6.15e17). (2) Route B needs the exponent-2 classification inside Kimura's list. The proposal only checked d <= 1e5; this verification closes it to 1.1e9 (65 survivors). (3) Prior art inside the Observatory: audits/claims_2026/SIEGEL/DOSSIER.md Sec. 5 item 6 already derived |D| < 1.5655e16 from Guo's constant and left the omega in {12,13,14} window unsearched. M6-1 closes that window, and EKN Thm 1 makes the search unnecessary. (4) Still imported and not re-derived: Grube/Kani's order-level reduction (non-fundamental -4n), the correctness of Kimura's text and Lean, and EKN's computation. EKN's exponent-4 and exponent-8 conclusions are not recovered by this route.

**Prior art found by verifier.** OpenAI 11/12 paper Sec. 1 (via QRH => EKN Thm 2 => Grube/Kani). Observatory SIEGEL DOSSIER Sec. 5 item 6 (reduction to a finite search, which was not carried out). Weinberger 1973 (at most one extra field unconditionally). EKN Acta Arith. 193 (2020) Thm 1 (zbMATH 1453.11146). The arXiv API (all:idoneal, sorted by date) and a web search found no 2026 paper proving idoneal completeness from the Siegel exclusion. Kimura 2610.21511 does not mention exponent-2 fields or idoneal numbers.


## CON-0021 — Littlewood 1912 / Beurling 1955 in Hardy-space form: the decay exponent of the Möbius Nyman–Beurling error equals −2(α−Θ), with Θ = sup Re ρ

*Mechanism:* M5-historical-rediscovery · *Verdict:* **sound_and_useful** · *Significance:* 3/5 · *Difficulty:* 2/5

**Source statements.**
- Littlewood 1912 (C. R. Acad. Sci. Paris 154, 263–266), recorded as an imported dependency of Franel 1924 [CAND-00231]: 'RH <=> M(n)=O(n^{1/2+eps}) (both directions used)'. Classical Θ-form (Titchmarsh §14.25–14.26 type): Θ := sup Re ρ = limsup log|M(x)|/log x.
- Beurling 1955 (PNAS 41, 312–314; statement as quoted in Delaunay–Fricain–Mosaki–Robert arXiv:1101.1199 and Dimitrov–Oliveira arXiv:1608.07887): for 1<p<∞, ζ(s)≠0 in Re s>1/p iff span{ρ_θ(x)={θ/x}−θ{1/x}: 0<θ≤1} is dense in L^p(0,1).
- Báez-Duarte 2003 [CAND-01182]: natural-dilation (k≥2) strengthening of Nyman–Beurling. Báez-Duarte's Thm 2.2, cited in Manzur–Quintero arXiv:2611.03924 Remark 4.2(i): the Möbius coefficients μ(k) do not give convergence in L²(0,∞).
- Manzur–Quintero arXiv:2611.03924v1 (Nov 2026). Prop. 4.1: s_n(α):=||S_n−E||²_{H²(C_α)}=Σ_{m≥1} w_m(α)R_n(m)², with R_n(m)=1+Σ_{k=2}^n μ(k)(m mod k)/k and w_m(α)=(m^{−2α}−(m+1)^{−2α})/(2α), where E=1/s, G_k=(k^{−s}−k^{−1})ζ(s)/s and S_n=Σ_{k≤n}μ(k)G_k. Thm 3.2: S_n→E in H²(C_α) for α>1. Thm 3.5: a zero ρ with Re ρ>α forces d_n(α)²≥2(Re ρ−α)/|ρ|². Eq. (4.2): s_n(α)≥M_1(n)²Σ_{m≤n}m²w_m(α), with the sum ~n^{2−2α}/(2−2α). §4.2: under RH, Balazard–Saias give only s_n(α)=O(n^{−(α−1/2)/3}). Table 1: the decade slopes of s_n(α) for n≤10^6 are within 0.08 of 1−2α.
- Titchmarsh, The Theory of the Riemann Zeta-Function §14.2: on RH, ζ(s)^{±1}=O(t^ε) uniformly for σ≥σ0>1/2. The proof (Borel–Carathéodory plus Hadamard three circles) uses only the absence of zeros to the right.
- OpenAI 2026 quasi-RH, unrefereed (Theorem 1.1 of the 11/12 paper; 7/8 paper): no zeros of ζ or of any Dirichlet L-function in Re s>7/8. Liu arXiv:2610.12234 claims 0.874957..., with Lean claimed.

**Proposed link.** By MQ's own proof of Prop. 4.1 (Plancherel on Re s=α), E−S_n=(1−ζ(s)[M_n(s)−M_1(n)])/s with M_n(s)=Σ_{k≤n}μ(k)k^{−s} and M_1(n)=M_n(1). So s_n(α)=(1/2π)∫|1−ζ(M_n−M_1(n))|²|s|^{−2}dt on Re s=α. This converts Littlewood's abscissa principle for Σμ(k)k^{−s} directly into the decay rate of s_n(α). Upper bound: Perron's formula for M_n(s), shifted to Re(s+w)=β0+ε, using 1/ζ≪t^ε there. Lower bound: MQ (4.2) combined with M_1(x)=Ω(x^{Θ−1−ε}).

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Normalisation and domain. I recomputed s_n(α) two ways, independently of MQ. Series (4.1), truncated at m≤2·10^6 plus a tail estimate, against direct Mellin quadrature of |1−ζ(M_n−M_1(n))|²/|s|² for |t|≤4000. n=10, α=0.8: 3.133958e−2 vs 3.132303e−2. n=30, α=0.75: 1.258654e−2 vs 1.257078e−2. The gaps match the omitted |t|>4000 tail. (2) The lower bound (4.2) evaluates to 2.49e−2 and 7.25e−3, both below s_n. (3) Range. The argument needs α>1/2 (so that ∫|ζ(α+it)|²(1+|t|)^{2ε−2}dt<∞) and α>β0. For α≥1 the M_1(n)² term dominates and the exponent becomes −2(1−Θ). This agrees with MQ Thm 3.2 and with their α=1 slope of about −0.93 (n^{−1}log n). (4) α=1/2 gives exponent 0, i.e. no decay, consistent with Báez-Duarte non-convergence and d_n(1/2)²≍1/log n. (5) Beurling: H²(C_α) is weighted L²((0,1),x^{2α−1}dx) and detects the same half-plane σ>α as L^{1/α}. (6) MQ Table 1 maxima, decade slopes: α=0.6 gives −0.171 and −0.236 (prediction −0.2); α=0.75 gives −0.474 and −0.584 (−0.5); α=0.9 gives −0.740 and −0.855 (−0.8). Consistent with Θ=1/2, but finite data cannot test Θ.

**New claim.** Proposition (proposed, not reviewed). Let Θ=sup{Re ρ}. (a) For every α∈(Θ,1) and every ε>0: s_n(α)≪_{α,ε}n^{2(Θ−α)+ε} for all n, and s_n(α)≥n^{2(Θ−α)−ε} for infinitely many n. Hence limsup_n log s_n(α)/log n=−2(α−Θ). (b) For α≥1 the limsup equals −2(1−Θ). (c) Corollaries. RH ⇔ s_n(α)≪n^{1−2α+ε} for one (equivalently every) α∈(1/2,1). Under RH this improves the O(n^{−(α−1/2)/3}) quoted by MQ to the n^{1−2α} rate their Table 1 observes. Zero-free Re s>β0 ⇒ S_n→E in H²(C_α) for every α>β0 with rate n^{2(β0−α)+ε}; so the OpenAI 7/8 claim, if correct, extends MQ Thm 3.2 from α>1 to α>7/8. Power decay s_n(α)≪n^{−δ} at a single α ⇒ Θ≤α−δ/2. Proof sketch. Upper bound: sharp-cutoff Perron for M_n(s) with U=n², shifted to Re(s+w)=β0+ε, gives M_n(s)−1/ζ(s)≪n^{β0−α+ε}(|t|+n²)^ε. Also M_1(n)≪n^{β0−1+ε} by partial summation from M(x)≪x^{β0+ε}, and ∫|ζ(α+it)|²(1+|t|)^{2ε−2}dt<∞. Lower bound: if M_1(x)≪x^{Θ−1−δ}, then 1/ζ(1+s)=s∫_1^∞M_1(x)x^{−s−1}dx continues 1/ζ to Re>Θ−δ, a contradiction; so (4.2) gives Ω(n^{2(Θ−α)−ε}).

**Already proved.** Imported: Littlewood's equivalence and its Θ-version; Beurling's L^p theorem; Báez-Duarte's non-convergence; MQ Prop. 4.1, Thm 3.2, Thm 3.5 and (4.2); Titchmarsh §14.2 under RH. My contributions: the full proof sketch above, which uses only standard tools, and the numerical identity checks.

**Conjectural.** The proposition is my proposed statement and is unreviewed. The QRH version of Titchmarsh §14.2 (ζ^{±1}≪t^ε for σ≥β0+ε given a zero-free half-plane σ>β0) is standard but must be written out. The 7/8 corollary is conditional on the unrefereed OpenAI claim. The lower bound holds only along a subsequence, since s_n oscillates (MQ Fig. 1).

**Next step.** Write the 3–4 page proof of (a) and (b): the explicit Perron remainder for |t|≫n², uniformity in α, and the log factor at α=1. Then check Balazard–Saias, 'Notes sur la fonction ζ de Riemann 4' (Adv. Math. 2004), for an existing weighted-norm rate, and submit the statement as a separately labelled object.

**Potential counterexamples.** No counterexample is known. Points to check: (i) the uniform t^ε bound for 1/ζ needs α−β0 fixed and positive, so the constants blow up as α↓Θ; (ii) the sharp-cutoff Perron error must also be controlled for |t|>n²; (iii) the statement is false or vacuous for α≤1/2; (iv) for α<Θ, s_n need not tend to 0 (MQ Thm 3.5), so 'exponent' there is meaningless.

**Tests.** Done: two-way identity check (n=10, 30), the lower-bound sanity check, and the slope comparison with MQ Table 1. Proposed: push the exact series (4.1) to n=10^7 and fit the envelope against n^{1−2α}(1+nM_1(n)²). This is a consistency test only and cannot decide Θ. Code: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-riemann/e62aa4cb-6226-5e37-8f52-ea0fa055746f/scratchpad/m5code/sn_check.py

**Prior-art search (proposer).** Searches: arXiv API (abs:'Baez-Duarte' sorted by date; abs:zero-free AND half-plane AND Beurling; au:Noor); WebSearch on Nyman–Beurling L^p rates and zero-free half-planes. Full text read: MQ 2611.03924 (and its withdrawn v1 2606.16097). Abstracts read: Manzur–Noor 2610.03568; Gallardo-Gutiérrez–Seco 2206.02654 (weighted Dirichlet spaces give qualitative zero-free half-planes); Ghosh–Kremnitzer–Noor–Santos 2022; Dimitrov–Oliveira 1608.07887 (L^p, p∈(1,2]); DFMR 1101.1199 and 1112.0166. Result: qualitative closure⇔half-plane statements exist, and MQ explicitly call their observed n^{1−2α} 'much faster than the bound known under RH'. I found no exact-exponent statement. Not checked: the full texts of Balazard–Saias 2000–2004.

**Verifier reasoning.** Sources: I read the full text of Manzur-Quintero arXiv:2611.03924v1. Prop. 4.1, the weights w_m(alpha), R_n(m), Thm 3.2, Thm 3.5, Cor. 3.6, eq. (4.2), the n^{-(alpha-1/2)/3} bound in Sec. 4.2 attributed to Balazard-Saias Lemmes 4-5, and Table 1 are all as quoted. MQ sum from k=2, but G_1=0, so this changes nothing. The identity E-S_n=(1-zeta(s)[M_n(s)-M_1(n)])/s follows in one line from the definition of G_k. I recomputed series (4.1) independently: s_10(0.8)=0.0313396, and the (4.2) bound is 0.024900, M_1(10)=0.090476. Both match the proposal (script /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-riemann/e62aa4cb-6226-5e37-8f52-ea0fa055746f/scratchpad/verM5/sn.py).

Upper bound. Write 1-zeta(M_n-M_1(n)) = zeta(1/zeta-M_n) + zeta*M_1(n). Given no zeros in sigma>Theta, 1/zeta(s) is O(|t|^eps) on sigma>=Theta+eps. Dimitrov-Oliveira arXiv:1608.07887 Thm 4 is exactly this zero-free half-plane form of Littlewood/Titchmarsh 14.2, with a Borel-Caratheodory plus three-circles proof. The truncated Perron formula (Titchmarsh Lemma 3.12, x=n+1/2, T=n^2, shifted to Re(s+w)=Theta+eps) then gives M_n(s)-1/zeta(s) << n^{Theta-sigma+eps}(1+|t|)^eps uniformly in t. The truncation error, about n^{-1-sigma} log n, does not depend on t. This is the Titchmarsh 14.25(A) argument. Partial summation from M(x)<<x^{Theta+eps} gives M_1(n)<<n^{Theta-1+eps}. For alpha>1/2 the mean square of zeta on Re s=alpha is about T, so the integral of |zeta|^2(1+|t|)^{2eps-2} converges. Hence s_n(alpha)<<n^{2(Theta-alpha)+eps}. The n^{-delta/3} in Balazard-Saias Lemme 2 (quoted as Lemma 2.1 in Baez-Duarte math/0202141) is therefore a loss, not a barrier.

Lower bound. (4.2) gives s_n >= M_1(n)^2 n^{2-2alpha}/(2-2alpha). If M_1(x)<<x^{Theta-1-delta}, then s*int_1^inf M_1(x)x^{-s-1}dx would continue 1/zeta(1+s) to Re(1+s)>Theta-delta. That half-plane contains zeros of zeta, a contradiction. So M_1=Omega(n^{Theta-1-delta}) and the limsup equals -2(alpha-Theta).

(b) holds for alpha>1, where the sum of m^2 w_m converges. (c) holds in both directions. The case alpha<=Theta needs no Thm 3.5: (4.2) plus the Omega-bound gives s_n=Omega(n^{2Theta-2alpha-eps}) for every alpha<1. The 'power decay implies Theta<=alpha-delta/2' corollary and the conditional 7/8 extension are correct.

**Verifier correction.** Minor corrections. (1) At alpha=1, zeta has a pole at s=1 on the line of integration. The two pieces zeta(1/zeta-M_n) and zeta*M_1(n) each have a pole there, but their sum is regular because M_n(1)=M_1(n). So on |t|<=1, bound the combined expression (M_n(s)-M_n(1))zeta(s) directly; it is O(1) uniformly in n. For alpha>1 nothing changes. (2) The factor (|t|+n^2)^eps can be stated as n^{2eps}(1+|t|)^eps, so the 'Perron remainder for |t|>>n^2' in the potential counterexamples is not an issue. (3) Add the corollary it gives at once. Since d_n(alpha)^2<=s_n(alpha), MQ's threshold satisfies alpha_c<=Theta, and with their Cor. 3.6 this gives alpha_c=Theta unconditionally. The qualitative part (s_n(alpha)->0 for alpha>Theta) already follows from Balazard-Saias 1998 Lemme 2 applied for general alpha; only the exact exponent is new. (4) The Omega-statement holds along a subsequence only, and the implied constants blow up as alpha decreases to Theta, as the proposal says.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Balazard-Saias, Notes sur la fonction zeta 1, Adv. Math. 139 (1998), Lemme 2: zero-free Re s>alpha gives sum_{a<=n} mu(a)a^{-s}=1/zeta(s)+O(n^{-delta/3}(1+|tau|)^eps). It is quoted in Baez-Duarte arXiv:math/0202141, Lemma 2.1, and is weaker than needed. Titchmarsh Thm 14.25(A) supplies the Perron method. Dimitrov-Oliveira arXiv:1608.07887 Thm 4 gives L^{+-1}<<|t|^eps in a zero-free half-plane. Ghosh-Kremnizer-Noor-Santos (Adv. Math. 2024) and MQ 2611.03924 give qualitative half-plane statements. None of these states the exact exponent -2(alpha-Theta), and MQ (Nov 2026) explicitly calls n^{1-2alpha} faster than anything known under RH.


## CON-0008 — Persistence lemma for the de Bruijn–Newman converse: an off-line zero forces Lambda >= c delta^2/log^2 gamma (conjecturally c min(delta^2, delta/log gamma))

*Mechanism:* M2-missing-lemmas · *Verdict:* **sound_and_useful** · *Significance:* 2/5 · *Difficulty:* 3/5

**Source statements.**
- Polymath 2019, Proposition 3.1 (DB: CAND-01890): (i) at a simple zero z_j(t_0) of H_{t_0}, dz_j/dt = 2 sum'_{k != j} 1/(z_j - z_k); (ii) at a zero of order m >= 2, the nearby zeros behave like z_j + sqrt2 (t - t_0)^{1/2} lambda_i + O(|t - t_0|), with lambda_i the roots of He_m.
- Polymath 2019, Theorem 1.5(iv) (DB: CAND-01890): for 0 < t <= 1/2 and all X >= 0, N_t(X+1) - N_t(X) <= O(log(2+X)).
- Polymath 2019, Theorem 3.2 (de Bruijn Thm 13): zeros of H_t lie in |Im z| <= max(y_0^2 - 2(t - t_0), 0)^{1/2}. This is the forward (upper) bound on how long non-real zeros survive.
- Rodgers–Tao 2020, Theorem 1 (Lambda >= 0) and Theorem 11 (zero ODE for Lambda < t <= 0) (DB: CAND-01803).
- frontiers/research_programs.md, DBN item 7(4) and Open questions: 'a rigorous converse: if Lambda <= eps, then zeta has no zeros off the line below height exp(c/eps). As far as I can tell this is not established.'
- Campaign C1-detection-horizons claim C1-E1 (HEURISTIC + NUMERICAL-NONRIGOROUS): collapse time t_c ~ 2 delta^2 (isolated pair) and ~ 4 delta/log(T/2 pi) (lattice regime).

**Proposed link.** Let rho = 1/2 + delta + i gamma be a zero, i.e. z_0 = 2 gamma - 2 i delta is a zero of H_0 with |Im z_0| = 2 delta. Lambda >= t holds as soon as H_t still has a non-real zero. Use a windowed energy E(t) = sum_{k: y_k(t) > 0} phi(x_k(t)) y_k(t)^2, with phi a smooth bump of width 1 at x_0 = 2 gamma, and differentiate with Prop. 3.1(i). Symmetrising each pair term gives bounded contributions. Upper-half-plane pairs give -4 phi (y_k - y_j)^2/|z_k - z_j|^2 >= -4 phi. A conjugate pair gives y_k(y_k + y_j)/|z_k - conj z_j|^2 <= 1, since |z_k - conj z_j| >= y_k + y_j; the self-conjugate term is exactly -2 phi. Real zeros give y_k^2/|z_k - x_j|^2 <= 1. The horizontal flux terms phi'(x_k) y_k^2 Re 1/(z_k - z_j) symmetrise to O(1) per pair because |phi'(x_k) y_k^2 - phi'(x_j) y_j^2| <= C|z_k - z_j|. With Thm 1.5(iv) there are O(log x_0) zeros in the window, each with sum_j min(1, d^{-2}) = O(log x_0). So |dE/dt| <= C log^2 x_0, while E(0) >= 4 delta^2. E is continuous through collisions (the zero multiset moves continuously; Prop. 3.1(ii)).

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Normalisation: Polymath H_t(z) = int_0^inf e^{t u^2} Phi(u) cos(zu) du with dH/dt = -d^2H/dz^2. Zeros satisfy dz/dt = H''/H' = 2 sum 1/(z_k - z_j), which matches Prop. 3.1(i). (2) Validity range: Thm 1.5(iv) holds for 0 < t <= 1/2, and the classical Riemann–von Mangoldt bound covers t = 0. The lemma uses t in [0, min(Lambda, 1/2)], and Lambda <= 0.2 is proven. (3) Strip: by de Bruijn all zeros have y <= 1 for t >= 0, so y_k(y_k + y_j) <= 2 and the per-pair bounds hold. (4) Conditionally convergent sums: the ODE sum is symmetric/principal value; pairing z_j with conj z_j gives absolute convergence of the imaginary parts at rate 1/d^2. (5) Direction: the lemma bounds Lambda from below given an off-line zero. It complements de Bruijn's upper bound and does not conflict with Rodgers–Tao (Lambda >= 0 holds anyway).

**New claim.** Target Lemma (Persistence). There are absolute constants c, gamma_0 > 0 such that if zeta(1/2 + delta + i gamma) = 0 with 0 < delta < 1/2 and gamma >= gamma_0, then Lambda >= c delta^2/(log gamma)^2. Equivalently: Lambda <= eps implies every zero satisfies |beta - 1/2| <= C sqrt(eps) log |gamma| (|gamma| >= gamma_0). The window-energy argument above gives the proof outline. The expected sharp form, from the C1-E1 heuristic and the local Hermite model, is Lambda >= c min(delta^2, delta/log gamma), i.e. Lambda <= eps implies delta <= C max(sqrt eps, eps log gamma). The sharp form needs the local field of the real zeros (about y log gamma) to dominate, instead of crude per-pair bounds.

**Already proved.** The inputs: Prop. 3.1, Thm 1.5(iv), de Bruijn Thm 13, Lambda >= 0, Lambda <= 0.2. The energy computation is a proof sketch written here. It is not checked line by line, and in particular the treatment of collision times and window flux needs to be written out.

**Conjectural.** The sharp form c min(delta^2, delta/log gamma), and any explicit value of c. Numerically even the sharp form is uninformative today: with Lambda <= 0.2 and c ~ 2-4 it constrains only gamma below about 10^4, which is already verified. The value is structural: a rigorous version of the Polymath Sec. 10 heuristic that Lambda carries information only up to height exp(O(1/Lambda)).

**Next step.** (1) Write the energy proof rigorously: an almost-everywhere ODE for E(t) between collision times, continuity at collisions using Prop. 3.1(ii), and an explicit constant using Polymath's explicit form of Thm 1.5(iv) if one is available. (2) Attempt the sharp form by splitting real zeros into near (|x_j - x_k| <= y_k) and far parts and using the average spacing 4 pi/log(T/2pi) (Rodgers–Tao Thm 9 asymptotics), which yields the y log gamma drift. (3) Validate against C1's gap-lattice ODE model (exp_dbn_jensen.py) as a non-rigorous cross-check.

**Potential counterexamples.** Clusters of near-coincident complex zeros: individual y_k can then change fast, which is why the lemma is stated for a windowed energy and not for one zero. A configuration in which the off-line zero sits inside a dense cluster of real zeros has a larger descent rate (about y log gamma). This is the regime that separates the proved delta^2/log^2 from the conjectured delta/log gamma.

**Tests.** Model checks with Farmer-type toys: cos z with one pair moved to j +- i y_0. Run the exact backward/forward heat flow numerically (Arb-certified root tracking, as in C1 jensen_root_certify.py) and compare the measured collapse time with c y_0^2/log^2 j and with C1-E1's t_c, for j in {10, 20, 40, 60}.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** Web search for a lower bound on Lambda in terms of a hypothetical off-line zero: none found. Full-text grep of Newman–Wu survey arXiv:1901.06596 (sections on lower bounds and Lehmer pairs: only Csordas–Smith–Varga-type bounds from real Lehmer pairs, no off-line persistence). Polymath 2019 and Rodgers–Tao read via the DB records. The Observatory's own DBN analysis (research_programs.md) records the converse as not established. Csordas–Smith–Varga 1994 (Constr. Approx.) not opened.

**Verifier reasoning.** The inputs match the DB. Polymath Prop. 3.1 gives the ODE dz_j/dt = 2 sum' 1/(z_j - z_k) and the Hermite collision law. Thm 1.5(iv) gives N_t(X+1) - N_t(X) <= O(log(2+X)) for 0 < t <= 1/2. Thm 3.2 is de Bruijn's canopy. Lambda <= 0.2 comes from Platt-Trudgian.

I re-derived the energy computation. d(y_j^2)/dt = -4 sum_k y_j(y_j - y_k)/|z_j - z_k|^2. The self-conjugate term is exactly -2. Upper-upper pairs symmetrise to -4(y_j - y_k)(phi_j y_j - phi_k y_k)/d^2 >= -4phi - ||phi'||, using |y_j - y_k| <= d and |phi_j - phi_k| <= ||phi'|| d. Conjugate pairs give y_j(y_j + y_m)/|z_j - conj z_m|^2 <= 1. Real zeros give y_j^2/|z_j - x_k|^2 <= 1. Far zeros contribute 1/d^2 and sum to O(log x_0) per zero. Horizontal flux symmetrises to O(1) per near pair. Unpaired real or lower zeros contribute at most y_j.

With O(log x_0) zeros in a unit window, |dE/dt| <= C log^2 x_0. Since E(0) >= 4delta^2 and E(t) > 0 forces t < Lambda, we get Lambda >= c delta^2/log^2 gamma. Collisions are harmless: the He_m roots sum to zero, so the O(|t-t_c|^{1/2}) terms cancel in sum phi y^2 and E stays Lipschitz. The C1-E1 regimes (2delta^2 and 4delta/log) are consistent with the conjectured sharp form, and the 'informative only below ~10^4' remark is arithmetically right.

Two gaps must be written out. First, the far-field part of the horizontal velocity (a conditionally convergent sum) needs a bound O(log x) on H_t'/H_t at distance about 1 from the zeros, uniformly for t in (0,1/2]. This comes from the Hadamard product plus Polymath Thm 1.3-type asymptotics, not from Thm 1.5(iv) alone. Second, the a.e.-ODE and absolute-continuity argument.

Usefulness. It answers, in the correct delta-dependent form, the open converse in research_programs.md l.102. That δ-free version ('Lambda <= eps implies no off-line zeros below exp(c/eps)') cannot follow from dynamics alone, because a pair with delta -> 0 collapses in time about 2delta^2. Significance is low, as the proposal itself says.

**Verifier correction.** No mathematical correction is needed. Note that the far-field velocity bound uses an H_t'/H_t estimate at distance about 1 from the zeros for 0 < t <= 1/2, beyond Thm 1.5(iv). Note also that the frontier's delta-free converse (research_programs.md l.102) is not obtainable this way; the delta-dependent form is the correct target.

**Prior art found by verifier.** None found. arXiv API search (Bruijn+Newman+lower+bound) returned 1904.12438, 1310.3477, 2610.27343 and 2609.37164. 2609.37164 ('Collective Contraction...') proves the opposite-direction forward contraction d/dtau y^2 <= -2 - 4y^2 G for the maximal zero, which is an upper-bound tool. Csordas-Smith-Varga-type lower bounds use real Lehmer pairs, not off-line zeros. The frontier records the converse as not established (l.102).


## CON-0015 — Zeros of xi′: the bandwidth-one window class is exhausted at 0.868642 (below Wu's 0.86957); transplant Knausgård's multi-point certificate principle to decide whether simple-on-line can beat Wu

*Mechanism:* M3-transplantation · *Verdict:* **sound_and_useful** · *Significance:* 2/5 · *Difficulty:* 2/5

**Source statements.**
- Alpöge–Furman, arXiv:2608.13637v2, Remark 7.1, p.14: unconditionally, xi′ simple-on-line >= 0.85838 (flat window) and 0.86864 (quartic window v(s) = 1 − (7/100)(2s)^2 − (51/200)(2s)^4); Wu [Wu15 §3] has 0.86957 on the line (not necessarily simple); RH-conditional: 85.84% (Farmer–Gonek–Lee) and 0.8825 simple (Chirre–Gonçalves–de Laat Cor. 7).
- Farmer–Gonek, arXiv:0803.0425, Theorem 1.1 (under RH): F_1(a) = (1+o(1))T^{−2|a|}log T + |a| − 4a^2 + sum_{k>=1}((k−1)!/(2k)!)(2|a|)^{2k+1} + o(1), for |a| < 1; Cor. 1.3: more than 85.84% of the zeros of xi′ are simple.
- Knausgård, arXiv:2610.08965, Theorem 5.1 (via p.7): a pair (x,b) with interaction energy >= x·n − b·length for n simple points gives liminf N0s/N >= (2 − C(f) − b)/(1 − x); Theorem 3.2 is the distinct analogue; §7 gives ceilings via periodic trial states (an average-cost control dual).
- Alpöge–Furman §7.2, pp.12–14: bandwidth-one ceiling LP over periodic configurations (0.6818 for zeta).

**Proposed link.** In the xi′ setting the window optimization is a Fourier extremal problem: minimize [||psi||^2 + ∫G_1(u)(psi*psi)(u)du]/(∫psi)^2 with G_1 the Farmer–Gonek form factor. I solved it numerically. Since windows cannot reach Wu's on-line proportion, the next tool is Knausgård's local-interaction certificate from the zeta programme, transplanted to the xi′ zero process, with the decision made first by the xi′ analogue of Alpöge–Furman's bandwidth-one ceiling.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Normalization, checked. With G_1 as above, my functional reproduces Alpöge–Furman's flat 0.8583842 and quartic 0.8686406 to 7 digits, and the zeta kernel |u| reproduces 0.6725007. (2) Optimizer. A discretized QP with psi >= 0 enforced (minimum psi about 0.727 > 0) gives R* = 1.1313465, 1.1313555 and 1.1313578 at n = 200, 400, 800. Richardson extrapolation gives optimum simple-on-line 0.868641 ± 2e-6 and distinct 0.934321. So the quartic window is within about 1e-6 of the optimal window. (3) Knausgård's local inequalities depend on the window kernel and the point-process geometry (bounded separation, trace normalization), not on Euler-product arithmetic. They need the xi′-specific kernel and the unconditional xi′ zero count N_1(T) = N(T) + O(log T).

**New claim.** (A) Numerical, non-rigorous floating point. The supremum over admissible windows of the Alpöge–Furman-type xi′ certificate is 0.868641 ± 2e-6 (simple-on-line) and 0.934321 (distinct). This is 9.3e-4 below Wu's unconditional on-line 0.86957. No window can make 'simple and on the line' exceed Wu's 'on the line'. (B) Proposal. Exceeding 0.86957 with simplicity requires a beyond-window certificate. Whether any bandwidth-one certificate can do it is decided by the xi′ ceiling LP; if that ceiling exceeds 0.8696, a Knausgård-type 7–8-point inequality is the natural instrument.

**Already proved.** Alpöge–Furman Remark 7.1 constants (unrefereed, Lean-claimed). The Farmer–Gonek form factor under RH; Alpöge–Furman state the unconditional use. My window optimum is floating-point only, not interval-certified.

**Conjectural.** That the xi′ bandwidth-one ceiling exceeds 0.86957. That Knausgård's local machinery (labels, storage-function corrections) transfers to xi′ zeros, whose local statistics differ from GUE (Farmer–Gonek Fig. 1).

**Next step.** Run the periodic-configuration ceiling LP of Alpöge–Furman §7.2 with the Farmer–Gonek form factor (bandwidth one, on-line and off-line partition) and report an interval-enclosed ceiling. Abandon if it is below 0.86957 + 1e-4. Otherwise build a Knausgård certificate (x,b) for xi′ using his Theorem 5.1 formula with C(f) = R*(psi) = 1.13136.

**Potential counterexamples.** If the ceiling is below Wu's value, the direction is closed, which is itself a useful negative result. Wu's 0.86957 counts zeros on the line with multiplicity; the comparison is between different quantities, so a simple-on-line value above it is meaningful only as a strengthening, not a contradiction.

**Tests.** Re-run xiprime.py with n = 1600 and with interval arithmetic for the final evaluation. Cross-check the series G_1 against the closed form for k <= 40 terms (terms decay factorially for |a| < 1).

**Prior-art search (proposer).** arXiv: ti:"pair correlation" AND ti:derivative found Farmer–Gonek 0803.0425 (read). WebSearch found Farmer–Gonek, Gaps between zeros of xi′ (0903.4006) and 1611.10037. Read Alpöge–Furman Remark 7.1 and Knausgård §§1–3 (cached). No source found reporting the optimal xi′ window value or a Knausgård-type certificate for xi′.

**Verifier reasoning.** **Sources match.**
- AF Remark 7.1 gives 0.85838/0.92919 (flat window) and 0.86864/0.93432 (quartic window v(s) = 1 − (7/100)(2s)^2 − (51/200)(2s)^4). It notes that Wu's 0.86957 (on the line, not necessarily simple) is not exceeded.
- The Farmer–Gonek Theorem 1.1 form factor is |alpha| − 4alpha^2 + Σ_{k>=1}((k−1)!/(2k)!)(2|alpha|)^{2k+1} for |alpha| < 1, under RH; AF claim its unconditional use.
- Knausgård Theorem 5.1 matches: liminf >= (2 − C(f) − b)/(1 − x).
- The AF §7.2 ceiling is 0.6818 for zeta.

**Independent recomputation.**
- Method: Legendre basis up to degree 24, with 160-point Gauss–Legendre quadrature split at the |u| kink. The same code reproduces zeta's Montgomery–Taylor value 1.3274992963 exactly.
- For xi′: the flat window gives 2 − R = 0.8583841 and AF's quartic window gives 0.8686405.
- The optimal window gives R* = 1.13135850. Simple-on-line 2 − R* = 0.86864150 and distinct (3 − R*)/2 = 0.93432075, stable from degree 8 to 24.
- The optimizer is positive (min/max about 0.671). The quadratic form is positive definite on even functions (smallest eigenvalue 0.0475 in the basis), so this is the global optimum, signed windows included.

**Consequences.**
- AF's quartic window is within 1e-6 of optimal.
- The window class tops out 9.3e-4 below Wu's 0.86957.
- The proposed next step (decide first with an xi′ analogue of the bandwidth-one ceiling LP, then try a Knausgård-type interaction certificate) is coherent. Knausgård's interaction gain for zeta was about 0.001 (0.6725 to 0.6735), the same size as the 9.3e-4 needed, so the test is meaningful.

**Caveats.**
- The result is floating point, not interval-certified.
- AF already observed that their constants do not beat Wu.
- Transferring Knausgård's local inequalities to xi′ zeros, whose local statistics are not GUE, is conjectural, as the proposal says.
- The comparison with Wu is between different quantities, as the proposal also notes.

**Verifier correction.** None needed. Recommended additions: certify the final evaluation with interval arithmetic; record that the optimum is a global minimum over signed windows (the quadratic form is positive definite on even windows); state AF Remark 7.1's own observation that neither of its constants exceeds Wu.

**Prior art found by verifier.** - Farmer–Gonek–Lee 2014 (85.84%, flat-window level).
- Chirre–Gonçalves–de Laat, arXiv:1810.08843, Thm 6 and Cor 7: SDP under RH gives N1* <= 1.1175·N1, i.e. 0.8825 simple; this uses positivity outside the support, a different class.
- AF Remark 7.1 (quartic 0.86864).
- No source found reporting the support-1 window optimum for xi′ (CCLM 1406.5462 treats only zeta).


## CON-0018 — Piltz divisor exponents from the formally claimed statement ζ(s)≠0 for Re s>7/8: α_k ≤ 7/8 − 49/(24k), better than the records for every k ≥ 14

*Mechanism:* M4-strengthen-weaken · *Verdict:* **sound_and_useful** · *Significance:* 2/5 · *Difficulty:* 1/5

**Source statements.**
- Hardy–Littlewood, via Hardy 1922 (CAND-00219), (4.22)-(4.24), Sec. 4.1-4.2 pp.68-69. The fourth-moment bound yields α_k ≤ (k−2)/k for k>3. The improvement α_k ≤ (k−1)/(k+2) would follow from the then-unproved ζ(1/2+it)=O(t^{1/6+ε}). This is the archetype of 'a partial Lindelöf input improves α_k'.
- ANTEDB divisor chapter, Lemma 16.9 (citing Ivić §13.3): for integer k ≥ 2, if M(σ,k)=1 then α_k ≤ σ. Records: Li [186] Thm 16.20 for k=9..20 (e.g. α_14 ≤ 0.731898, α_20 ≤ 0.791374); Trudgian–Yang Thm 16.19, α_21 ≤ 0.79951; Ivić Thm 16.16, (k−2)/(k+2) for 12≤k≤25 and (k−1)/(k+4) for 26≤k≤50; Heath-Brown Lemma 16.17, 1−0.849k^{−2/3}; Bellotti Thm 16.18, 1−1.421(k−1.18)^{−2/3} for k≥30.
- ANTEDB zeta-moment chapter, Definition 9.1 (∫_T^{2T}|ζ(σ+it)|^A dt ≪ T^{M(σ,A)+o(1)}). Lemma 9.9 (Ivić Thm 8.4): M(σ,A)=1 for A=10/(5−6σ) on 5/8<σ≤35/54 (so A=9 at σ=35/54), for A=12 at σ=5/7, and so on.
- OpenAI Lean Comparator challenge (QuasiRiemannHypothesis.lean, per recent_developments_2024_2026.md §4.1): riemannZeta s ≠ 0 for 7/8 < Re s, standard axioms only (not compiled by the Observatory). Titchmarsh §14.2 method; the repo bridge BRG-MELLIN_WAVELET-P6 records that 'replacing 1/2 by Θ gives the same proof': no zeros in σ>θ implies ζ(s)^{±1} ≪ t^ε for σ>θ.

**Proposed link.** Use U2. ζ ≠ 0 on Re s>θ gives μ(σ)=0 for σ>θ (Borel–Carathéodory plus three circles), hence M(σ',B)=1 for every B and σ'>θ. Gabriel's convexity theorem (the interpolation behind Ivić's Thm 8.4 table, where 1/A is linear in σ) interpolates between an unconditional anchor M(σ1,A1)=1 and (θ+, B→∞). It gives M(σ,A)=1 for A < A1(θ−σ1)/(θ−σ), for σ1 ≤ σ < θ. Lemma 16.9 then gives α_k ≤ θ − (θ−σ1)A1/k for k ≥ A1. Maximising (θ−σ1)A1 over the Ivić anchors (piltz.py): θ=7/8 is optimised at (35/54, 9), giving C=49/24 ≈ 2.0417. θ=11/12 is optimised at (5/7, 12), giving C=17/7 ≈ 2.4286.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Only QRH for ζ is used, which is exactly the Lean-formalised 7/8 statement, not the Dirichlet or Hecke version. (2) Normalisations: M uses dyadic [T,2T] and Lemma 16.9 uses integer k ≥ 2, both matching. Gabriel's convexity needs regularity in the strip; at large |t| one uses (s−1)ζ(s). (3) The B=∞ endpoint is handled by taking finite B→∞ (μ(θ+ε)=0 gives M(θ+ε,B)=1 for each B) and the monotonicity of M in A. (4) Sanity check: Ivić's own table is reproduced by the same interpolation, e.g. (1/2,4)–(5/8,8)–(35/54,9). (5) The anchors are unconditional. Li's and Trudgian–Yang's α_k values were not used as anchors because I did not verify that they come from M(σ,k)=1.

**New claim.** Conditional theorem (proposed). If ζ(s) ≠ 0 for Re s>θ, then M(σ,A)=1 whenever σ1 ≤ σ < θ and A ≤ A1(θ−σ1)/(θ−σ), for any unconditional anchor M(σ1,A1)=1. Hence α_k ≤ θ − C(θ)/k with C(7/8)=49/24 for k ≥ 9 and C(11/12)=17/7 for k ≥ 12, and sup_k α_k ≤ θ (unconditionally α_k → 1 like 1−c k^{−2/3}). Against the ANTEDB records, QRH(7/8) improves α_k for every k ≥ 14: k=14 gives 0.72917 vs 0.73190, k=20 gives 0.77292 vs 0.79137, k=30 gives 0.80694 vs 0.84883, k=100 gives 0.85458 vs 0.93352. At k=13 it gives 0.71795 vs 0.71752, which is no gain. QRH(11/12) improves the listed values for k ≥ 22, but see the risks.

**Already proved.** The ingredients are classical and published: Titchmarsh §14.2 method, Gabriel's convexity, Ivić Thm 8.4 and §13.3. The qualitative 'zero-free half-plane ⇒ α_k ≤ θ' is of Hardy–Littlewood type (Titchmarsh Ch. XII–XIII, LH ⇔ α_k ≤ 1/2 for all k). The explicit θ − C/k bounds and the record comparison were computed here (piltz.py).

**Conjectural.** ζ ≠ 0 on Re s>7/8 (Lean-claimed, not independently replayed) or on Re s>11/12 (unformalised). Whether Gabriel's convexity, in the exact form of Ivić Lemma 8.3, is stated so that it covers the limiting B=∞ endpoint was not checked in the book.

**Next step.** Check Ivić Lemma 8.3 and Lemma 16.9's proof (Ivić §13.3) for exact hypotheses. Then rerun with the anchors behind Li's [186] and Trudgian–Yang's tables: an anchor such as (0.638889, 9) would raise C(7/8) to about 2.125. Then add the hypothesis 'μ(σ)=0 for σ>7/8' to the ANTEDB pipeline to propagate to β_k, μ(σ) on (1/2,7/8), and zero-density A(σ) on [3/4,7/8].

**Potential counterexamples.** There is none for the implication. The risk is an artifact: Li and Trudgian–Yang tabulated only k ≤ 21. The apparent improvements for k ≥ 22 under 11/12 (and partly under 7/8) are against Ivić's older formulas, and the Li/TY methods would likely give about 0.80 at k=22. The 7/8 improvements for k=14..21 are against actually tabulated records.

**Tests.** piltz.py prints both columns; rerun it after correcting the anchors. Cross-check that θ=1/2 (Lindelöf) gives α_k ≤ 1/2 for all k. Cross-check that the Ivić table is itself reproduced by interpolating (1/2,4)–(5/8,8)–(35/54,9).

**Prior-art search (proposer).** WebSearch 'Piltz divisor problem alpha_k zero-free half-plane quasi-Riemann'. It found ANTEDB Lemma 16.9 and Elaissaoui 2406.00331 (an LH–Piltz equivalence) but no QRH statement. arXiv API: abs:"divisor problem" and abs:Piltz (2025-2026 hits, including Omega results 2609.33820 and 2605.21476) found no QRH consumer. The 2026 QRH-consumer list contains no divisor-problem paper; Bellotti–Kerr 2610.29636 (Gauss sphere problem under QRH) is the nearest and was not opened.

**Verifier reasoning.** I checked the live ANTEDB divisor and zeta-moment chapters. Lemma 16.9: for integer k ≥ 2, M(σ,k)=1 implies α_k ≤ σ (Ivić §13.3). Lemma 9.9 (Ivić Thm 8.4): A=10/(5-6σ) on (5/8,35/54], equal to 9 at 35/54, plus extra anchors (2/3, 9.6187), (7/10, 11), (5/7, 12). Records: Li Thm 16.20 (α14 ≤ 0.731898, α20 ≤ 0.791374), Trudgian–Yang Thm 16.19 (α21 ≤ 0.79951), Ivić Thm 16.16, Bellotti Thm 16.18. Each step holds. (1) A zero-free half-plane gives μ(σ)=0 for σ>θ by Borel–Carathéodory plus Hadamard three circles applied to log ζ (the Titchmarsh §14.2 method with 1/2 replaced by θ). (2) Then M(θ+ε,B) ≤ Bμ+1 = 1 for every B (ANTEDB Lemma 9.2(vii)). (3) By Gabriel / Hardy–Ingham–Pólya log-convexity, a·M(σ,1/a) is convex along lines in (σ,a). Statements M=1 therefore interpolate with 1/A linear; this is how Ivić's table itself is built, and it reproduces 4/(3-4σ) and 10/(5-6σ). (4) Letting B → ∞ and using Hölder monotonicity in A gives M(σ,A)=1 for A < A1(θ-σ1)/(θ-σ), hence α_k ≤ θ-(θ-σ1)A1/k for k ≥ A1. (5) The maximiser is right: (7/8-σ)·10/(5-6σ) increases and (7/8-σ)·19/(6-6σ) decreases, so C(7/8)=(49/216)·9=49/24 at the anchor (35/54, 9). C(11/12)=(17/84)·12=17/7 at (5/7, 12). Rerunning piltz.py reproduces the table: the first gain is at k=14 (0.72917 vs 0.73190), there is no gain at k=13, and the 11/12 gains start at k=22. Only QRH for ζ is used, which is the Lean-claimed statement. The improvement is modest but real, conditional on unrefereed QRH.

**Verifier correction.** No mathematical correction is needed. Wording: the qualitative consequence α_k ≤ θ is classical (zero-free half-plane implies Lindelöf in that half-plane). Only the explicit θ - C/k and the comparison with the records are new. For k ≥ 22 the comparison is against Ivić's older formulas, since Li and Trudgian–Yang tabulate only k ≤ 20 and k ≤ 21 respectively. The 7/8 gains for k=14..21 are against actually tabulated records. Everything is conditional on QRH(7/8), which no one has independently checked.

**Prior art found by verifier.** None found. The arXiv QRH listing (19 hits, through 2026-11-12) has no divisor-problem paper. The live ANTEDB has no QRH entries. Bellotti–Kerr 2610.29636 (Gauss sphere problem under QRH; abstract only) is the nearest.


## CON-0019 — Exceptional sets for the PNT in short intervals: ζ≠0 on Re s>7/8 lowers the Gafni–Tao exponent µ(θ) only for 0.2445<θ<5/12, and leaves the 17/30 and 2/15 thresholds unchanged

*Mechanism:* M4-strengthen-weaken · *Verdict:* **sound_and_useful** · *Significance:* 2/5 · *Difficulty:* 1/5

**Source statements.**
- Gafni–Tao, arXiv:2505.24017v1 (Ess. Number Th. 5, 2026), Theorem 1.2 (p.5): µ(θ) ≤ inf_ε sup_{0≤σ<1, A(σ) ≥ 1/(1−θ)−ε} µ_{2,σ}(θ), with µ_{2,σ}(θ)=(1−θ)(1−σ)A(σ)+2σ−1. Theorem 1.3 (p.6) adds µ_{4,σ}(θ)=(1−θ)(1−σ)A*(σ)+4σ−3. Convention (p.2): A(σ)=−∞ if N(σ,T) vanishes for all T. Section 3: µ(17/30) ≤ 7/12 and µ(2/15+Δ) ≤ 1−9Δ/13. Tables 1-2 give A(σ) and A*(σ) from ANTEDB.
- Guth–Maynard (arXiv:2405.20552, Ann. Math. 2026): sup_σ A(σ)=30/13, attained at σ=7/10 (ZDE programme, research_programs.md §ZDE item 3).
- OpenAI 7/8 Lean Comparator statement: riemannZeta s ≠ 0 for 7/8<Re s.

**Proposed link.** Under ζ≠0 on Re s>7/8, N(σ,T)=0 for σ>7/8, so A(σ)=−∞ there by GT's own convention. The sup in Theorems 1.2/1.3 is then restricted to σ ≤ 7/8. GT's proof (explicit formula, then L∞/L2/L4 bounds on S_I(x)) treats each σ-range separately, and Lemma 2.1, the right-edge contribution, becomes vacuous. For θ where the unconditional sup is attained at the boundary σ*(θ)>7/8 with A(σ*)(1−θ)=1 (there µ_{2,σ*}=σ*), the cap replaces it by min(µ_2,µ_4) at σ=7/8. That equals min((3/14)(1−θ)+3/4, (3/5)(1−θ)+1/2), using A(7/8)=12/7 and A*(7/8)=12/(4·7/8−1)=24/5. The condition A(7/8) ≥ 1/(1−θ) holds iff θ ≤ 5/12.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Code check: my implementation of Tables 1-2 with Theorem 1.3 (gt2.py) reproduces both published sample values: µ(17/30−)=0.583333 and µ(2/15+0.01)=0.99308=1−9(0.01)/13. (2) Zeros on Re s=7/8 itself are allowed by QRH. A(7/8) is taken from the table (3/(2σ) and 3/(10σ−7) agree at 7/8, both 12/7). (3) Only QRH for ζ is used. (4) A* values for σ ≤ 7/8 are not improved here. QRH might also improve A and A* below 7/8 via the μ(σ) gains of M4-P3; that was not computed. (5) GT's bounds are the current best-known µ(θ) (their Figure 4 uses the same procedure), so the comparison is against the record.

**New claim.** Conditional on ζ(s)≠0 for Re s>7/8, the GT exponent satisfies µ(θ) ≤ max(U_{<7/8}(θ), min((3/14)(1−θ)+3/4, (3/5)(1−θ)+1/2)). Here U_{<7/8} is the GT supremum restricted to σ<7/8. This strictly improves the unconditional bound exactly for θ in (≈0.2445, 5/12), with a maximal gain of about 0.0109 at θ≈0.27 (0.91718 → 0.90632). Examples: µ(0.30) ≤ 0.900 vs 0.910; µ(0.36) ≤ 0.884 vs 0.8893; µ(0.40) ≤ 0.860 vs 0.8629. By GT (1.6), #{p_n in [N,2N]: p_{n+1}−p_n > 2p_n^θ} ≪ N^{µ(θ)−θ+o(1)} improves correspondingly. There is no change for θ ≤ 0.244 or θ ≥ 5/12. In particular the all-interval threshold 17/30 and the almost-all threshold 2/15 are unchanged, because the binding point σ=7/10 lies inside the half-plane-free region. QRH(11/12) improves only θ in (≈0.245, ≈0.27), by at most about 0.008.

**Already proved.** GT Theorems 1.2/1.3 hold for any admissible A and A*, including the −∞ convention. The capped optimisation is a computation run here (gt.py, gt2.py; grid N=40000-80000 in σ). The closed form on [0.28, 5/12) was verified against the grid.

**Conjectural.** The half-plane claim itself. Reading 'no improvement for θ<0.2445' as a statement about µ, rather than about this method's bound, would be a misreading.

**Next step.** Feed the QRH-improved μ(σ) on [3/4,7/8] (M4-P3) into ANTEDB large-value and zero-density routines to see whether A(σ) or A*(σ) improve below 7/8. That could extend the improved θ-range. Then submit the capped computation as an ANTEDB 'conditional' entry.

**Potential counterexamples.** None for the conditional bound. Grid discretisation could misplace the range endpoints by about 0.0005. The endpoint 5/12 is exact (A(7/8)(1−θ)=1).

**Tests.** Rerun gt2.py with finer grids and exact rational arithmetic at the breakpoints. Check that monotonicity of µ in θ is preserved.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** arXiv API abs:"exceptional set" AND abs:"short intervals" returned no results. abs:quasi AND Riemann AND hypothesis (2026 list) shows no exceptional-set paper. The nearest is Li–Liu 2610.26756 (Goldbach exceptional sets under QRH, a different problem). Fiori–Jaskari 2609.23222 combine 7/8 with zero density for explicit PNT error terms, not exceptional sets.

**Verifier reasoning.** I checked Gafni–Tao arXiv:2505.24017: Theorem 1.2 (p.5), Theorem 1.3 (p.6, sup over σ with A(σ) ≥ 1/(1-θ)-ε of min(µ_{2,σ},µ_{4,σ})), and the convention (p.2) that A(σ)=-∞ if N(σ,T) vanishes for all T. A and A* are the true exponents. Under QRH(7/8) the true A(σ) is -∞ for σ>7/8, so those σ leave the sup. Because µ_2 and µ_4 increase in A and A*, replacing the true values by the tabulated bounds on σ ≤ 7/8 gives a valid upper bound. Zeros on Re s=7/8 are allowed and are counted in A(7/8)=12/7: 3/(2σ) and 3/(10σ-7) agree there, both giving 12/7. A*(7/8)=12/(4σ-1)=24/5, from the Heath-Brown row of Table 2. Closed forms: µ_{2,7/8}=(3/14)(1-θ)+3/4 and µ_{4,7/8}=(3/5)(1-θ)+1/2. A(7/8) ≥ 1/(1-θ) holds iff θ ≤ 5/12. I checked the gt.py and gt2.py transcriptions of Tables 1–2 against the text near σ ∈ [0.7, 0.93]. Rerunning gt2.py reproduces GT's sample values µ(17/30-)=7/12 and µ(2/15+Δ)=1-9Δ/13. A 0.0025-step scan shows the 7/8 improvement exactly on θ ∈ (0.245, 0.4167) ≈ (0.2445, 5/12), with maximal gain 0.0108 at θ≈0.2725. µ(0.30) drops from 0.910 to 0.900 and µ(0.40) from 0.8629 to 0.860. At θ ≤ 0.24 the binding point is σ=0.7, so the 17/30 and 2/15 thresholds do not change. This is a correct, modest conditional improvement of a record exponent in a middle range.

**Verifier correction.** One secondary number is wrong. For QRH(11/12), the gain on (0.245, 0.2725) is at most about 0.0026 (at θ≈0.25: 0.92188 to 0.91927), not 'about 0.008'. Also state the result as a bound produced by the GT method. Do not present the 0.2445 endpoint as a statement about the true µ.

**Prior art found by verifier.** None found. The arXiv QRH listing has no paper on exceptional sets for PNT in short intervals; Li–Liu 2610.26756 treats Goldbach exceptional sets and Visser 2610.20058 treats all-interval primes under 7/8. The live ANTEDB primes and zero-density pages contain no QRH entries.


## CON-0024 — Pólya's approximant ξ* (1926) under the de Bruijn–Newman heat flow: compute Λ(Φ*) as an arithmetic-free control for Λ(ξ)=0

*Mechanism:* M5-historical-rediscovery · *Verdict:* **sound_and_useful** · *Significance:* 2/5 · *Difficulty:* 3/5

**Source statements.**
- Pólya 1926, Acta Math. 48, 305–317 (secondary: Hejhal 1990 zbMATH review Zbl 0723.11039; Newman–Wu arXiv:1901.06596 §2.2): Pólya showed that suitable combinations of Bessel functions K_z(a), with |Im z|=|t|/2, approximate ξ(1/2+it) well and have only real zeros. Here Φ*(u)=2π²(e^{9u}+e^{−9u})exp(−π(e^{4u}+e^{−4u})) in Polymath normalisation, the symmetrised n=1 term of Φ.
- Pólya 1927 (Newman–Wu Thm 2, universal factors): if H_λ has only real zeros then so does H_{λ'} for every λ'>λ.
- Newman 1976 (Newman–Wu Thm 9 and Thm 10, eq. (20)): Λ_DN(ρ)=−∞ iff ρ is a two-point measure or f(t)=K t^{2m}exp(−αt⁴−βt²)Π(1+t²/a_j²)e^{−t²/a_j²}; such f decay no faster than exp(−Ct⁴).
- Hejhal 1990, J. Anal. Math. (Zbl 0723.11039): for F(z)=2Σ_{j≤N}c_jK_z(2A_j), N(T)=(T/π)log(T/eA_1)+O(log T) and N(δ,T)=O_δ(T), i.e. almost all zeros are real.
- Rodgers–Tao 2020 Thm 1 (Λ≥0, using zero statistics of ζ) and Dobner 2021 Thm 2 (Λ_F≥0 on S♯, and infinitely many off-line zeros for every t<0); both are listed in the archive DBN programme.

**Proposed link.** Φ* decays like exp(−πe^{4|u|}), so it is not of Newman's form (20). Hence Λ*:=Λ_DN(Φ*) is finite and, by Pólya, ≤0. ξ* has no Dirichlet series and no Euler product, so Rodgers–Tao and Dobner do not apply, and Λ*<0 is expected. The size of Λ* quantifies how much of the 'barely true' character of RH (Λ=0) comes from the arithmetic n≥2 terms; ξ* is roughly the n=1 truncation of Polymath's A+B approximation.

**Compatibility checks.** Normalisation: H*_λ(x)=∫_0^∞e^{λu²}Φ*(u)cos(xu)du, the same convention as Polymath's H_t(z)=∫e^{tu²}Φ(u)cos(zu)du. At λ=0 the zeros of H*_0 in [0,60] are 17.987, 38.147, 48.957 and 57.957, versus 2γ = 28.27, 42.04, 50.02, 60.85 for ζ: the same count, as expected of a crude low-height approximant. No Fourier critical points at λ=0, consistent with Pólya's theorem. Newman's Theorem 10 needs ∫e^{λu²}Φ* finite for every λ, which holds.

**New claim.** Empirical, non-rigorous. Quadrature error is uncontrolled; the arb balls certify only rounding. (1) No Fourier critical points (positive minima or negative maxima of H*_λ) were found on [0,200] for λ∈{−0.05,−0.2,−0.5,−1,−2} (mpmath, dps 70, 4800-node Gauss–Legendre), nor on [0,600] for λ∈{−4,−128,−512,−2048} (python-flint arb, 3840 nodes, 800 bits). (2) Argument-principle counts equal the real sign-change counts: λ=−4 on the box [0,99.75]×[−20,20], 9.000 versus 9; λ=−128 on [0,185]×[−20,20], 2.000 versus 2. (3) As λ decreases, the zeros drift outward without visible collisions. The first zero moves 18.00, 18.61, 19.25, 61.26 and 195.0 at λ=0, −2, −4, −128 and −512; at λ=−2048 there are no real zeros in [0,600]. Conjecture: Λ*≤−4 in Polymath normalisation, possibly far lower, so the n=1 Gamma-factor part is robustly hyperbolic. The question is to determine Λ* and the location of its first collision.

**Already proved.** Pólya 1926 (real zeros at λ=0); Pólya 1927 monotonicity; Newman 1976 finiteness (Λ*>−∞); Λ*≤0. Nothing about the value of Λ* is proved.

**Conjectural.** Λ*≤−4, and the claim that no nonreal zeros exist at large x for any fixed λ. The latter is a heuristic large-x Hermite–Biehler argument: the heat factor changes the |A(z)|/|A*(z)| ratio only by exp(|λ|πy/8x), against the Gamma-ratio factor (x/4π)^{−y/2}. Where the collisions predicted by Newman occur (the outward-moving zero 'front', or near the origin for very negative λ) is unknown.

**Next step.** (a) Prove a large-x Hermite–Biehler lemma for H*_λ from the saddle-point expansion, u_0=(1/4)log(z/4π)+iπ/8. That reduces Λ* to a finite certified computation. (b) Carry out that computation with rigorous quadrature error bounds (arb integration with error bounds, or the Bessel-type contour representation). (c) Use ξ* as a positive control for the 2026 de Bruijn–Newman barrier and canopy certification codes: a pipeline that cannot certify Λ*≤0 for ξ* is lossy.

**Potential counterexamples.** Nonreal zeros with |Im z|>20, or outside the scanned windows, are not excluded. The critical-point test is necessary but not sufficient, and was complemented by the argument principle only in two boxes. If Dobner-type off-line zeros exist at large x for all λ<0 even without arithmetic, then Λ*=0, which would be surprising and important.

**Tests.** Scripts and outputs are in /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-riemann/e62aa4cb-6226-5e37-8f52-ea0fa055746f/scratchpad/m5code/: polya_dbn.py (run 1), polya_dbn_arb2.py (runs 3–5), polya_argprinc.py (polya_ap.txt).
