An earlier Chebyshev-recurrence run (polya_dbn_arb.py; λ=−16, −32, −64 on [0,300]: 50, 46 and 39 sign changes, no critical points) had blown-up ball radii. Its midpoints matched the direct runs at λ=−128 (61.256, 156.8). Proposed: argument-principle boxes along the moving zero front at λ=−128 and λ=−512, extended to X=1000 with higher precision.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** Sources: zbMATH API (Hejhal 1990 with review; Gasper 2008 Zbl 1163.33007, real zeros of Ξ* by integrals of squares; Shi arXiv:1502.06844, real-rooted Pólya-like approximations); Newman–Wu 1901.06596 in full (Theorems 9, 10, examples); WebSearch, two queries. Archive grep: the DBN programme mentions Pólya only for monotonicity and universal factors. I found no computation of Λ for Pólya's ξ* or for Hejhal's Bessel combinations.

**Verifier reasoning.** Framework. Newman-Wu arXiv:1901.06596 Thm 10, read in full: the form (20) is a condition on the density f(u). Its members satisfy f>=Kt^{2m}exp(-Ct^4-beta t^2). Phi*(u)=4pi^2 cosh(9u) exp(-2pi cosh 4u) is positive and decays like exp(-pi e^{4|u|}), so it is not of form (20), and Lambda*>-infinity. Polya's real-zero theorem for xi* at lambda=0, with monotonicity (Newman-Wu Sec. 2.2 and Sec. 2.4), gives Lambda*<=0. The normalisation checks out: H*_0(x)=pi^2 Re K_{(9+ix)/4}(2pi), |Im nu|=t/2 in the Titchmarsh variable, which agrees with the Hejhal/Polya description and with direct quadrature to 12 digits.

Reproduction (scripts /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-riemann/e62aa4cb-6226-5e37-8f52-ea0fa055746f/scratchpad/verM5/polya0.py and polya4.py). The lambda=0 zeros in [0,62] are 17.9856, 38.1308, 48.9495 and 57.9499. The proposal's 38.147, 48.957 and 57.957 are off by 0.008-0.016, apparently from grid interpolation. The count of 4 and the absence of Fourier critical points are confirmed. At lambda=-4, there are 9 sign changes on [0,100], the first in (19.0,19.25), and no positive minima or negative maxima, matching the proposal's 9.000 versus 9 and 19.25. Rodgers-Tao and Dobner (S-sharp needs a Dirichlet series) indeed do not apply.

Prior art. I found none computing Lambda for Polya's xi* or for Hejhal's Bessel combinations. The searches covered Newman-Wu, Shi 1502.06844, Ki-Kim, the arXiv/web 'Polya approximation de Bruijn-Newman' queries, and the archive. The conjecture Lambda*<=-4 is empirical but plausible.

A structural reason, which the proposal missed, supports a very negative Lambda*. Phi* factors as cosh(9u) times the K-Bessel kernel exp(-2pi cosh 4u). By de Bruijn's strong-universal-factor theorem (Newman-Wu Thm 8 with lambda=9, N=1), H*_lambda can have nonreal zeros only if the Fourier transform of e^{lambda u^2}exp(-2pi cosh 4u) has zeros with |Im z|>9/sqrt2. The sharp two-term form f(z+9i)+f(z-9i) gives |Im z|>9. This cannot be made rigorous directly, because de Bruijn's strip theorem (Newman-Wu Thm 7) covers only forward flow (lambda>0).

**Verifier correction.** Correct the lambda=0 zero list to 17.9856, 38.1308, 48.9495 and 57.9499 (root-refined). Keep all lambda<0 statements labelled empirical, since quadrature error is uncontrolled. For next step (a), consider replacing the saddle-point Hermite-Biehler route with the factorisation Phi*=4pi^2 cosh(9u)*exp(-2pi cosh 4u): by de Bruijn's cosh shrink, Lambda*<=inf{lambda: all zeros of FT(e^{lambda u^2}exp(-2pi cosh 4u)) lie in |Im z|<=9/sqrt2}. That reduces the problem to a strip-width question for the heat-flowed K_{iz/4}(2pi) kernel. Also keep 'Lambda*<0 is expected' as a heuristic: nothing proves Lambda*<0, and Lambda*=0 is not excluded.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Polya 1926 (xi* real zeros); Polya 1927 (universal factors, monotonicity); Newman 1976 (finiteness), as stated in Newman-Wu arXiv:1901.06596 Thms 2, 7-10; de Bruijn 1950 (strong universal factors, Thm 8); Hejhal 1990 (Zbl 0723.11039); Shi arXiv:1502.06844 (real-rooted Polya-like approximations, no Lambda computed); Ki-Kim arXiv:0806.0892 (real zeros of K_{iz}(a)). No computation of Lambda(Phi*) was found.


## CON-0035 — Positive proportion (>= 0.4170) of distinct zeros for every self-dual GL(2) L-function via the Alpoge-Furman/Lamzouri inertia certificate at bandwidth 1/2; exact bandwidth threshold 0.5502 for simple+critical zeros

*Mechanism:* M8-conjecture-generation · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 7/5 · *Difficulty:* 5/5

**Source statements.**
- Alpoge-Furman arXiv:2608.13637v2, Theorem A (p.1-2): N_0^s(T,2T) >= (2 - R(psi) - o(1)) N(T,2T) and N_d(T,2T) >= (1/2)(3 - R(psi) - o(1)) N(T,2T), unconditional; Lemma 5.6: R(psi) = (int psi^2 + iint |u-v| psi(u)psi(v)) / (int psi)^2; Remark 6.1 (p.12): with X = (T/2pi)^lambda, 0<lambda<1, the argument gives 2 - R_lambda(psi) with R_lambda(psi_0) = 1/lambda + lambda/3.
- Lamzouri arXiv:2609.02882v2, Prop. 2.1 (pp.6-12): pure algebra valid for ANY finite conjugation-invariant multiset Z and any even real eta with supp in (-lambda,lambda): n >= 2|Z| - sum_{z,s} K(z-s)^2 (eq. 2.4) and the distinct-zero analogue (eq. 2.5, giving C_1 = (C_0+1)/2); Thm 1.1 uses only Prop. 2.1 plus BGSTB24 Lemma 5.
- Baluyot-Goldston-Suriajaya-Turnage-Butterbaugh arXiv:2306.04799 (Acta Arith. 214), Lemma 5 (theorem DB CAND-02364): unconditional sum over all zeros of r-hat(i(rho-rho')L/2pi) w(rho-rho') = (T/2pi) log T (r(0) + 2 int_0^1 alpha r(alpha) d alpha + O(1/sqrt(log T))) for r supported in [-1,1] -- the degree-1 input whose degree-2 analogue (support [-1/2,1/2] in GUE units) is needed.
- Montgomery 1973 (theorem DB CAND-00343, Sec. 3 eqs. (28)-(31)): Montgomery-Vaughan mean value int_0^T |sum a_n n^{-it}|^2 dt = sum |a_n|^2 (T + O(n)), giving the diagonal range X <= T.
- Iwaniec-Luo-Sarnak 2000 (theorem DB CAND-00722), Prop. 3 (Rudnick-Sarnak explicit formula for f in H_k^*): sum phi(gamma_f/2pi) = archimedean term - 2 sum_p sum_{nu>=1} log p p^{-nu/2}(alpha_f(p)^nu + beta_f(p)^nu) phi-hat(log p^nu).
- Observatory CRIT23 DOSSIER section 7.3 table: Delta = 1/2 gives 2 - C = -0.1667 and (3-C)/2 = 0.4167 (flat window), labelled 'useless' for zeta, whose unconditional bandwidth is 1.
- Booker-Milinovich-Ng arXiv:1806.01959 (abstract): quantitative simple-zero estimates for modular-form L-functions of arbitrary conductor (Conrey-Ghosh method) -- not a positive proportion; prior state for GL(2).

**Proposed link.** The AF/LZ certificate uses exactly three inputs: tr A = N, an upper bound tr A^2 = sum K(z-s)^2 <= (R + o(1)) N, and the inertia (signature) structure of conjugate pairs. For a holomorphic newform f with trivial nebentypus (lambda_f real), the zeros of L(s,f) in (T,2T] form a multiset invariant under rho -> 1 - conj(rho), so LZ Prop. 2.1 applies verbatim. The only analytic input is the GL(2) prime-side second moment, i.e. mean squares of sum_n Lambda_f(n) n^{-1/2-it} w(n/X). Montgomery-Vaughan makes the diagonal exact for X <= T^{1-eps}. Measured in GUE units (local log-conductor log(N_f T^2) ~ 2 log T), this is bandwidth lambda -> 1/2, exactly half of zeta's bandwidth. The diagonal mass sum |Lambda_f(n)|^2/n ~ (1/2)log^2 X comes from the Rankin-Selberg prime number theorem sum_{p<=x}|lambda_f(p)|^2 log p ~ x, and this gives the form factor F(alpha) = delta(alpha) + |alpha| on |alpha| <= 1/2. We computed R*_lambda := min_psi R_lambda(psi), where R_lambda(psi) = A/lambda + lambda B with A = int psi^2/(int psi)^2 and B = iint|u-v|psi psi/(int psi)^2. The minimiser is psi(u) = cos(sqrt(2) lambda u) on [-1/2,1/2] (Euler-Lagrange). At lambda = 1/2, R* = 2.165964 > 2, so the simple+critical certificate 2 - R is negative, but the distinct-zero certificate (3 - R)/2 = 0.417018 is positive.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Normalisation. At lambda = 1 our formula gives R* = 1.327499296 = C_MT, which reproduces AF/LZ's 0.672500704 and 0.836250352 (script bandwidth.py). At lambda = 1/2 the flat window gives 13/6, matching the CRIT23 table row. (2) Bandwidth units. For zeta the density is (1/2pi)log T and X = T corresponds to lambda = 1. For L(s,f) of fixed level N and weight k the density is (1/2pi)log(N T^2/4pi^2), so X = T corresponds to lambda = log T/log(N T^2) -> 1/2. (3) Symmetry. Self-duality (trivial character) gives the conjugation invariance that LZ needs. For non-self-dual f, use the multiset of zeros of L(s,f)L(s,f-bar). (4) No pole term. Unlike zeta there is no polar term, and the cross terms need only sum_{p<=x} lambda_f(p) log p/p = O(1) (prime number theorem for L(s,f)), which is unconditional. (5) Prime squares. Lambda_f(p^2) = (lambda_f(p^2)-1)log p contributes O(1) to a second moment of size ~ log^2 X. (6) Endpoint. If X = T exactly is delicate, take lambda -> 1/2^-; R*_lambda is continuous. (7) Quantifiers: lim inf as T -> infinity for each fixed f; constants are not uniform in f.

**New claim.** Conjecture (expected theorem) GL2-D. Let f be a holomorphic Hecke newform of fixed weight k >= 1 and level N with trivial nebentypus (or a self-dual Hecke-Maass newform). Then, unconditionally, liminf_{T->inf} N_d(f;T,2T)/N(f;T,2T) >= (3 - R*_{1/2})/2 = 0.417018..., where R*_{1/2} = 2.165964 is attained by psi(u) = cos(u/sqrt2) 1_{|u|<=1/2}; the flat window gives 5/12. Companion statements about the certificate (proved here numerically/symbolically): (a) the simple+critical certificate 2 - R*_lambda is positive iff lambda > lambda* = 0.5501939647 (flat window: 3 - sqrt6 = 0.5505103); (b) with the diagonal bandwidth 1/d of a degree-d L-function, the distinct certificate is positive iff d <= 2 (d = 3: R* = 3.1109 > 3), and the simple+critical certificate iff d = 1. Consequently any unconditional upper bound for the GL(2) all-zeros second moment at bandwidth 1/2 + eta with eta > 0.0502 (prime sums of length about T^{1.1}) would give a positive proportion of simple zeros of L(s,f) on the critical line. That would be a first for GL(2).

**Already proved.** AF Theorem A (zeta, lambda = 1, unconditional, unrefereed; audited by the Observatory as CRIT23 with no error found); LZ Prop. 2.1 (elementary algebra for arbitrary finite multisets, re-derived and stress-tested in CRIT23 checks); Montgomery-Vaughan and Rankin-Selberg PNT (classical). Our own computations (bandwidth.py, sha256 be692122...df54): R*_lambda via mpmath quadrature agrees with an independent discretised quadratic programme (n = 400) to 2e-6, and the minimiser is nonnegative; lambda* found by root-finding at 30 digits. Synthetic sharpness check (analytic, via Poisson): a configuration in which every zero is double, on a spacing-2 lattice, has sum K^2/N = 2A = 2 <= 13/6 for the flat window at lambda = 1/2. So bandwidth-1/2 data alone are compatible with 0% simple and 50% distinct zeros: the simple-zero barrier at lambda = 1/2 is genuine, and 0.417 < 0.5 is consistent. The same configuration has ratio 2 > 4/3 at lambda = 1, so it is excluded there, consistent with AF.

**Conjectural.** The single needed input is an unconditional GL(2) analogue of AF Sections 2-5 (or BGSTB24 Lemma 5): for r real, even, supported in [-1/2+eps, 1/2-eps] in GUE units, the sum over ALL zeros of L(s,f) in (T,2T] of r-hat(i(rho-rho') log(N T^2)/2pi) (with AF's Gabor compression or LZ's weight) equals N(f;T,2T)(r(0) + 2 int_0^{1/2} alpha r(alpha) d alpha + o(1)), without GRH. It is not known whether this has been written down. Whether the endpoint lambda = 1/2 itself is attainable is open but immaterial.

**Next step.** Port AF Props 5.2-5.5 and Thm 5.7 to L(s,f): Weil explicit formula with Gamma_C(s+(k-1)/2), Gabor compression at density (1/2pi)log(N T^2), prime side with Lambda_f(n). Verify (i) that the diagonal reproduces |alpha| via Rankin-Selberg; (ii) that the archimedean-prime cross terms are o(N); (iii) the C^2 taper tail bound (AF Prop 4.3) with degree-2 gamma factors. Then apply LZ Prop. 2.1 at lambda = 1/2 - eps. Separately check whether Murty-Perelli (IMRN 1999, GRH-conditional pair correlation in the Selberg class) already implies the conditional 0.417 distinct bound via Montgomery's m^2 >= 3m - 2. This is our inference [O] and was not verified in their text.

**Potential counterexamples.** No counterexample to the conjectured statement is expected (GRH plus GUE predicts 100% simple zeros). The proposal could fail in these ways: (a) the unconditional second moment for complex zeros of L(s,f) might need a zero-free-region input stronger than what is available for GL(2). This is unlikely: the LZ route needs only Korobov-Vinogradov in BGSTB24, AF needs none, and GL(2) has the de la Vallee Poussin region; (b) the Montgomery-Vaughan edge at X ~ T could cost a constant factor, forcing lambda < 1/2 and a slightly smaller constant; (c) dihedral (CM) forms are covered as GL(2) cusp forms, but must not be confused with products of degree-1 functions. Synthetic countermodels (the doubled lattice above) show that the method cannot give simple zeros at lambda = 1/2, so any claim of a positive proportion of simple zeros from bandwidth-1/2 data alone would be an error.

**Tests.** T1 (done): bandwidth.py reproduces C_MT at lambda = 1 and gives R*_{1/2} = 2.165964 and lambda* = 0.550194. T2 (numerical, cheap): take zeros of L(s,Delta) (level 1, weight 12) or of the weight-2 form 11a from LMFDB, up to height ~10^4. Compute the window-weighted sum K^2/N at bandwidth 1/2 and compare with R_{1/2}(psi), which is 13/6 for the flat window and 2.1660 for cos(u/sqrt2). Agreement to a few percent tests the normalisation, not the theorem. T3: count multiplicities in the same data (all simple numerically), as a sanity check. T4 (symbolic): derive the diagonal and off-diagonal of the GL(2) prime-side mean square with Montgomery-Vaughan constants.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** arXiv API, 2026-11-17: abs:"distinct zeros" (59 hits; the only relevant ones are zeta and Dirichlet-family 2026 items: 2609.33043, 2608.16034, 2609.27808, AF, LZ); abs:"simple zeros" AND abs:modular (9; BMN 1806.01959 is quantitative only); abs:"Selberg class" AND abs:"pair correlation" (3; Barrett-McDonald-Miller-Ryan-Turnage-Butterbaugh-Winsor 1410.7765 use Murty-Perelli for small gaps, not multiplicities); abs:rank-trace and Weil/inertia (only AF, LZ, Hua-Yang Dirichlet families). We found no application of the inertia/Bessel certificate to any degree-2 L-function, and no unconditional positive proportion of distinct zeros for GL(2). Not covered: zbMATH (API gave intermittent 502 errors); hexagonmath and Zenodo postings; the post-KN follow-ups (Freund, D'Souza, Chandru, Wang), which were cited but not inspected.

**Verifier reasoning.** Checked and confirmed:
(i) Bandwidth. For a single GL(2) form the diagonal bandwidth is 1/2 in GUE units. Montgomery-Vaughan gives a diagonal only for X<=T, while the zero density is (1/pi)log T. This agrees with Murty-Perelli's GRH range |alpha|<(1-eps)/d, quoted as Prop 5.1/(5.3) in Barrett et al. arXiv:1410.7765.
(ii) Constants. I recomputed them with mpmath quadrature and with an n=400 discretised quadratic programme (scratchpad m8verify/rstar.py, hy.py): R*_1=1.3274993=1/c_MT, R*_{1/2}=2.1659639 (flat window 13/6), lambda*=0.5501939647, R*_{1/3}=3.1109043.
(iii) Source text. LZ Prop 2.1, (2.4)/(2.5); AF Rem 6.1 (R_lambda(psi0)=1/lambda+lambda/3); and the CRIT23 table in Section 7.3 all read as quoted.
(iv) Prior state. Bernard arXiv:1404.6432 Remark 1 confirms that no positive proportion of simple zeros is known for GL(2); he gets only 2.97% of zeros on the line.

Problems:
(a) The distinct-zero bound is weaker than the inputs allow. AF (Z)/Prop 4.1 write the matrix as G~=P+Q with rank P <= N0*, and each off-line block {rho,1-rho-bar} has rank <=2. So rank(P+Q) <= N_d. Cauchy-Schwarz on the eigenvalues of any Hermitian matrix gives rank >= tr^2/||.||_HS^2 (AF Rem 3.3 states the PSD case). Hence N_d >= (1/R - o(1))N unconditionally, from the same three inputs. This beats (3-R)/2 exactly when R>2, i.e. when lambda<lambda*. At lambda=1/2 the bound is 0.461688, not 0.417018.
(b) Companion claim (b) (distinct certificate positive iff d<=2) is an artifact of using (3-R)/2. In fact 1/R*_{1/d}>0 for every d; for d=3 it is 0.3214.
(c) The closed form R*_lambda=1/c*_lambda, with c*_lambda=sqrt2 tan(lambda/sqrt2)/(1+(lambda/sqrt2)tan(lambda/sqrt2)), is already in the abstract of Hua-Yang arXiv:2609.27808. I checked that it equals the cos-window optimum at lambda=1, 0.75, 1/2 and 1/3. So companion claim (a) follows from prior art.
(d) Self-duality is not needed. The symmetry rho <-> 1-rho-bar holds for every primitive L(s,f), from L(s-bar,f-bar)=conj L(s,f) plus the functional equation; compare AF's proof of Thm B for complex chi.

The whole content still rests on the unproved GL(2) analogue of BGSTB24 Lemma 5 at support <1/2. It is plausible (Montgomery-Vaughan plus Rankin-Selberg Mertens) but was not checked here.

**Verifier correction.** Restate GL2-D as follows. Assume the GL(2) all-zeros pair-sum asymptotic for support <1/2. Then liminf N_d/N >= 1/R*_{1/2} = c*_{1/2} = 0.461688, using the rank-trace inequality on the full Hermitian G~ instead of (3-R)/2. Under RH the two-moment integer bound would be (5-R*)/6 = 0.472339; getting it unconditionally would need a new inertia lemma and is open.

Further changes:
- Drop the self-dual/trivial-nebentypus restriction.
- Replace companion (b) with: 2-R*_{1/d} < 0 for every d >= 2, but the distinct bound 1/R*_{1/d} > 0 for every d (d=3: 0.3214).
- Cite Hua-Yang arXiv:2609.27808 for the closed form c*_lambda and treat lambda* as its root (0.5501939647, verified).
- The simple-zero threshold lambda* holds only for this window certificate. KN-type frame refinements may lower it.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Hua-Yang arXiv:2609.27808 (abstract): the same bandwidth-lambda certificate, with the closed form c*_lambda. AF arXiv:2608.13637: Rem 6.1, Lemma 3.2 and Rem 3.3 (rank >= tr^2/HS^2). Murty-Perelli, IMRN 1999 (Zbl 0929.11030): GRH pair correlation for |alpha|<1/d_F. Barrett et al. arXiv:1410.7765: uses Murty-Perelli for small gaps only. Bernard arXiv:1404.6432: 2.97% of zeros on the line, explicitly no simple zeros. Booker-Milinovich-Ng arXiv:1806.01959: quantitative only. My arXiv searches ('distinct zeros'; 'pair correlation' AND unconditional; 'simple zeros' with modular/automorphic/GL(2)) found no GL(2) distinct-zero proportion and no GL(2) port of AF/LZ.


## CON-0037 — Quasi-RH for Hecke Groessencharacters of infinite order over Q(sqrt-3), hence zero-free half-planes for L-functions of CM elliptic curves with j = 0: angular bookkeeping identifies the one biased frequency and how to avoid it

*Mechanism:* M8-conjecture-generation · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 7/5 · *Difficulty:* 7/5

**Source statements.**
- OpenAI 'The Quasi-Riemann Hypothesis' (github.com/openai/math @4eaee875, paper2.tex sha256 d9a8f15aa770cf88...), Theorem 1.1 (l.73-75): 'Every finite-order Hecke L-function over K has no zeros in the half-plane Re s > 11/12.'
- paper2.tex l.800-821: alpha(n) = n/|n|, gamma_j(n) = N(n)^{-1/2} sum_{x mod n} chi_n(x)^j e(x/n), a_xi(n) = conj(alpha(n)) gamma_2(n) xi(n); 'we can absorb functions on a fixed ray class group into a finite sum of twists xi' (the only place where finite order is used, per the Observatory DOSSIER section 2.6).
- paper2.tex Lemma 4.1 (l.823-857): (eq:gj) gamma_2(n)^3 = mu(n) alpha(n); (eq:convert2) mu(n) gamma_{-1}(n) = chi_n(-1) G(n)^{-1} conj(alpha(n)) gamma_2(n).
- paper2.tex App. A.2 (l.3340-3372): J(s) = (3^{5/2}/4)(27/(2pi)^2)^s Gamma(s+1/3)Gamma(s+2/3) T(s,Psi) is entire because 'in each expansion the horizontal derivative removes the constant mode'.
- Observatory quasi_rh DOSSIER E16: the coefficients carry conj(alpha(n)), which makes T(s,Psi) entire; DR Thm 1.4 (GRH) shows that the extremal coefficients for the cubic large sieve are angle-free g~(b), so the angle is load-bearing. Section 2.6: 'Not covered: Groessencharacters of infinite order (e.g. L-functions of CM elliptic curves over Q(sqrt-3))'.
- Ji arXiv:2611.16665v2, Sect. 1 item 1: the quartic Kubota series of angular weight 1 has completed series at the cusps that are entire, and 'the angular factor after reflection makes the zero-frequency term in the second application of Poisson summation vanish' (via David-Dunn-Hamieh-Lin arXiv:2306.11875 Prop. 4.3).
- Liu arXiv:2610.26835v2 (abstract): extension to finite-order Hecke L-functions over every number field. Every 2026 extension found is finite-order.

**Proposed link.** Let psi be a unitary Hecke character of K = Q(sqrt-3) with infinity-type (z/|z|)^l, l in Z. On ideals prime to 3, each ideal has a unique primary generator n, so psi(n) = nu_0(n) alpha(n)^l with nu_0 of finite order. Paper2's sums become A_u(D) = sum mu(n) nu_0(n) alpha(n)^l chi_n(u) W(Nn/D). The angular factor can be absorbed into a smooth non-radial weight Phi(n/sqrt D) = W(|n|^2/D)(n/|n|)^l, so the Littlewood step (l.726-757) and the sixth-power row extraction (l.694-724) are unchanged. Under the Poisson/Gauss-Jacobi conversion (eq:convert2), the dual coefficient becomes chi_n(-1) G^{-1} nu_0 alpha^{l-1} gamma_2: the angular frequency is m = l - 1 instead of paper2's m = -1. App. A.2 realises the angle by a horizontal derivative that kills the constant mode of the cubic theta function. An angle of frequency m != 0 needs |m| derivatives and still kills it. For m = 0 (l = 1, exactly the infinity-type of a CM elliptic curve) there is no angle, and Patterson's constant term survives, which is the biased regime of DR. Since L(s, conj psi) has the conjugate zero set and conj psi has m' = -l-1, every l admits a choice in {psi, conj psi} with m != 0. For l = 1, use conj(psi_E), which has m = -2.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Normalisation: L(E,s) = L(s - 1/2, psi_E) (unitary psi_E of infinity-type 1, Deuring), so 'Re s > 11/12' for psi_E means L(E,s) != 0 for Re s > 17/12 (arithmetic centre 1). (2) Primary generators: units +-1, +-omega, +-omega^2, and 'n == 1 mod 3' selects one associate, so alpha(n)^l defines a Hecke character mod 3 of infinity-type l. (3) Quantifiers: paper2 is qualitative per character, uniform in conductor and height only for the half-plane. The conjecture keeps per-psi qualitative constants, which may depend on l. (4) Recursion: Fourier/Hankel transforms on R^2 preserve angular frequency. Cube completion n -> n b^3 multiplies b-frequencies by 3, and Prop. 5.1 has at most ceil(4/theta) steps, so frequencies stay bounded and Lemma B.2 derivative costs remain finite. (5) Numerical evidence for the bias claim (our run, angular_bias.py, sha256 78148faa...2e9d, using the Observatory's zomega.py): S_m(X) = sum_{N n <= X, n primary squarefree, (n,6)=1} alpha(n)^m gamma_2(n), 908 terms up to X = 3200. For m = 0: |S_0| = 25.9, 57.6, 84.9, 168.4 at X = 400, 800, 1600, 3200, with |S_0|/X^{5/6} = 0.18, 0.22, 0.18, 0.20 (a stable X^{5/6} main term, Patterson's bias). For m in {-3,-2,-1,1,2}: |S_m| <= 23.5, below 0.42 sqrt(X), with no main term.

**New claim.** Conjecture G. For every unitary Hecke character psi of K = Q(sqrt-3), of finite or infinite order and any infinity-type l, L(s,psi) has no zeros with Re s > 11/12. The 7/8 architecture would give Re s > 7/8 if its own angular bookkeeping goes through. Corollaries if true: (i) for every elliptic curve E/Q with j(E) = 0 (CM by Z[omega], e.g. y^2 = x^3 + D), L(E,s) != 0 for Re s > 17/12 (resp. 11/8). This would be the first fixed zero-free half-plane for any GL(2) L-function; the same holds for every CM newform with CM by K. (ii) Hecke angular equidistribution of primary primes with power saving: #{pi : N pi <= x, arg pi in I} = (|I|/2pi) pi_K(x) + O(x^{11/12+eps}) uniformly in the arc I. This follows from Erdos-Turan with M = x^{1/12}, using that zero-freeness for all psi^l is uniform in l. (iii) Lindelof to the right of 17/12 for L(E,s). Mechanistic sub-claim: the only obstruction specific to infinity-type is the frequency-0 case m = l - 1 = 0. It is avoided by passing to conj(psi), and for m != 0 the converted coefficients alpha^m gamma_2 have no Patterson main term.

**Already proved.** Algebra (direct from paper2 eq:convert2): mu psi gamma_{-1} = chi_n(-1) G^{-1} nu_0 alpha^{l-1} gamma_2. The zero set of L(s, conj psi) is the complex conjugate of that of L(s,psi). Numerics (finite, not a proof): the m = 0 X^{5/6} bias and its absence for m != 0 up to N(n) <= 3200. Paper2 itself is unrefereed. The Observatory's Level-2 audit found no error, but imported Patterson/Kubota inputs were not re-proved.

**Conjectural.** The analogue of paper2 Prop. 3.1 (the sextic-twisted Mobius mean square at rows N(u) <= D^{1+theta}) with non-radial weights Phi(n/sqrt D) of fixed angular frequency l. Specifically: (a) Lemma 4.2 Poisson and Prop. 4.5 with non-radial n-weights; (b) the App. A.2 Voronoi/archimedean transform with |m| horizontal derivatives: an entire J_m(s), presumably with Gamma(s+1/3+|m|/2)Gamma(s+2/3+|m|/2)-type factors, and polynomial growth; (c) Lemma B.3 Mellin separation in two real variables (radial and angular).

**Next step.** (1) Grep paper2.tex for every use of radiality of W, and of N(n)-only weights, in Sects. 4-7 and App. A-B, and list them. (2) Derive J_m(s) for m = -2 (the CM case via conj psi_E): repeat the l.3340-3372 computation with partial_{z-bar}^2 and check the cusp-coordinate cocycle terms that a second derivative generates, which are absent for one derivative. (3) Run the Observatory's check_paired.py and check_local.py identities with a non-radial weight. (4) Only then attempt Prop. 5.1's induction with frequency bookkeeping.

**Potential counterexamples.** (a) Second derivatives produce extra terms from d^2 z'/dz-bar^2 at the twisted cusps, which might reintroduce a constant mode. The test is J_m(s) entire for |m| >= 2. (b) If some step of paper2 secretly uses that W depends only on N(n), for instance a radial Mellin transform in Lemma B.3, the non-radial version needs a two-dimensional Mellin-Hecke transform with growth in l. (c) For l = 1 directly (m = 0) the dual sums carry the main term c X^{5/6} seen numerically; feeding psi_E rather than conj(psi_E) into the induction would break Prop. 4.5's bound E << D^eps XF. (d) If paper2 Prop. 3.1 is false, the whole programme fails.

**Tests.** T1 (done): angular_bias.py, which shows the m = 0 bias and no main term for m != 0. T2: extend to X ~ 2*10^4 with CRT-multiplicative gamma_2 (eq:crt-a) to fit the exponent 5/6 for m = 0 and bound the exponent for m != 0. T3 (symbolic): compute J_m(s) for m = -2, -3 and verify the absence of poles. T4 (numerical sanity, not proof): estimate sum_{N u <= H} |A_u(D)|^2 with psi of infinity-type -1 at D ~ 2000-4000, H ~ D^{1.1}, and compare its D*H scaling with the finite-order baseline.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** arXiv API 2026-11-17: abs:"quasi-Riemann" (17 records; all concern finite-order characters, Dedekind zeta, or applications); abs:"zero-free half-plane" (12; none infinite-order); abs:Grossencharacter/Groessencharacter (12; none on zero-free regions); abs:half-plane AND elliptic curve AND zero (2; irrelevant). Liu 2610.26835 and Cureton-Thorner (hexagon, not read) are finite-order only. No zero-free half-plane for infinite-order characters or CM elliptic curves was found. zbMATH not queried for this item.

**Verifier reasoning.** Checked against the local paper2.tex (sha256 d9a8f15a...):
- Thm 1.1 (l.73-75), a_xi and 'absorb ... into a finite sum of twists' (l.808-812), (eq:gj) and (eq:convert2) (l.840-850), and App. A.2 'the horizontal derivative removes the constant mode' (l.3362-3364) all read as quoted.
- Algebra: the identity mu psi gamma_{-1} = chi_n(-1) G^{-1} nu_0 alpha^{l-1} gamma_2 follows directly. Primary generators make alpha^l a Hecke character mod 3 of infinity type l, for every l.
- Normalisation: L(E,s)=L(s-1/2,psi_E) is correct, so 11/12 gives 17/12 and 7/8 gives 11/8.
- Corollaries: the Erdos-Turan corollary (ii) and Lindelof corollary (iii) follow from Conjecture G. Zero-freeness has no constants, so the explicit-formula bounds are uniform in l.

Numerics: I reran S_m(X) with an independent script (zomega.py arithmetic plus CRT multiplicativity, spot-checked against direct Gauss sums; scratchpad m8verify/angbias.py). There are 908 terms up to X=3200. |S_0| = 25.88, 57.64, 84.90, 168.36 at X=400, 800, 1600, 3200, with |S_0|/X^{5/6} = 0.176, 0.220, 0.181, 0.202. For m in {-3,-2,-1,1,2} the maximum is 23.52 (~0.42 sqrt X). This reproduces the proposal exactly; m=3 is also small (25.0). At X <= 3200 this cannot pin down exponents.

Error: the claim 'first fixed zero-free half-plane for any GL(2) L-function' is false relative to the same 2026 claims. Paper2's Thm 1.1 already covers every finite-order Hecke character of Q(sqrt-3). For a character chi that does not factor through the norm, L(s,chi)=L(s,f_chi), where f_chi is a weight-one dihedral cuspidal newform over Q. Liu arXiv:2610.26835 extends this to finite-order Hecke L-functions over every number field.

Not verified: the core input. That is paper2 Prop 3.1 with angular-frequency coefficients, entire J_m(s) for |m|>=2 including the second-derivative cusp cocycle terms, and two-variable Mellin separation (Lemma B.3). Paper2 uses nonnegative radial weights (l.877, l.1025, l.1470). These should stay radial, with the angle carried in the coefficients rather than in a 'non-radial weight'.

**Verifier correction.** Change the significance claim to: 'this would be the first fixed zero-free half-plane for a holomorphic GL(2) newform of weight >= 2 (in particular for an elliptic curve L-function), conditional on the unrefereed 2026 inputs'. Weight-one dihedral forms are already covered by paper2 Thm 1.1 and by Liu.

Keep Conjecture G explicitly conjectural. Its load-bearing unverified step is paper2 Prop 3.1 with angular frequency m=l-1 (or -l-1 for conj psi). When adapting, keep the radial nonnegative majorants and move alpha^l into the coefficients.

**Prior art found by verifier.** None for infinite-order characters or CM elliptic curves. The arXiv query for 'quasi-Riemann' lists 15 items from 2026 (as of 2026-11-17), including Liu arXiv:2610.12234 and arXiv:2610.26835, Xu-Sang arXiv:2610.15291, FSW arXiv:2610.18491 and Ji arXiv:2611.16665; all concern finite-order characters, Dedekind zeta or applications. Related: Dunn-Radziwill (Annals 2024) on Patterson bias, consistent with the m=0 main term, and Ji arXiv:2611.16665 (angular weight 1 kills the zero frequency).


## CON-0031 — One exponent law for Bettin-Gonek and the OpenAI extraction lemma; the diagonal barrier forbids any QRH from proven mollified moments, and any half-plane left of 3/4 from extraction at or above the diagonal

*Mechanism:* M7-negative-synthesis · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 6/5 · *Difficulty:* 4/5

**Source statements.**
- Bettin-Gonek, arXiv:1604.02740, Theorem 1: if for every eps > 0, I_N(0,T) := int_0^T |M_N(1/2+it)|^2 |zeta(1/2+it)|^2 dt << T^{1+eps} for 2 <= N <= T^theta, with M_N(s) = sum_{n<=N} mu(n) n^{-s}(1 - log n/log N), then zeta has no zeros in Re s > 1/2 + 1/(2 theta). Theorem 2 (on [T,2T]): Re s > 1/2 + 2/theta. Remark (printed without proof): for a single zero rho_0, I_N(T,2T) = c_1 N^{2beta_0-1} log T/(T^3 log^2 N)(1 + Re N^{2i gamma_0}|zeta'(rho_0)|^2/zeta'(rho_0)^2 + o(1)) + O(T^{1+eps} + N^{beta_0-1/2+eps}/T), and I_N(0,T) = N^{2beta_0-1}/log^2 N (C(N) + o(1)) + O(...).
- Observatory quasi-RH dossier (audits/quasi_rh/DOSSIER.md Sec. 4.1, citing the extraction lemma at paper2 l.694-724): with H = D^h, h >= 1, Sum_{0<N(u)<=H} |A_u(D)|^2 << D^{1+eps} H, where A_u(D) = Sum mu(n) nu(n) chi_n(u) W(Nn/D). Averaging over about H^{1/n} rows u = p^n gives beta <= 1/2 + h(n-1)/(2n): 11/12 (n=6), 7/8 (n=4), 5/6 (n=3), 3/4 (n=2) at h = 1. Large-sieve barrier: for H < D, positivity plus duality returns DH + D^2.
- Dossier Sec. 4.2: the 7/8 comparison system gives B(l) = 11/12 - l/4 with l < 1 - |b|, so B > 2/3 is a structural floor. Liu, arXiv:2610.12234v2, Thm 34.4: B_new = 0.874957019... is the infimum within the specified comparison system.
- Conrey 1989 via Bui 2011 Thm 3.1 (theorem DB): int_1^T |V psi_1(sigma_0+it)|^2 dt ~ c_1 T for theta_1 < 4/7. With Q=1, R=0, P_1(x)=x this is c_1 = 1 + 1/theta, Levinson's (1.2). Bettin-Chandee-Radziwill (ledger): theta < 17/33 for arbitrary coefficients.
- Dong-Wattanawanichkul-Zaharescu, arXiv:2605.24363, Thm 1.3: sup_T T^{-2 sigma theta} int_1^{T^theta} I_{y,pi}(0,T) dy < inf implies L(s,pi) != 0 for Re s > sigma (GL(m)). Thm 1.8: the family version gives N(sigma,0,T;F)/|F| << T^{-delta+eps}, i.e. QRH for 100% of the family.
- Radziwill, 'Limitations to mollifying zeta' Thm 1 (ledger): I(M_theta) >= c/theta for every mollifier of length T^theta.

**Proposed link.** Both results are mean squares, over a twist family of size H, of a Mobius polynomial of length D, with the per-row square-root-cancellation target 'row average O(1) after normalisation by D^{1/2}'. In both, an off-line zero at beta gives a Landau-Hille/Mellin residue signal D^{2beta-1}. The signal appears on H^r rows, with a deficit H^{-a} on the averaging window. Comparing signal and target, D^{2beta-1} H^{r-a} <= H^{1+eps} (normalised), gives the EXTRACTION EXPONENT LAW beta <= 1/2 + (h/2)(1 - r + a), with h = log H/log D. Checks: Bettin-Gonek Thm 1 (archimedean twists n^{-it}, H = T, D = N, h = 1/theta, r = 0, a = 0) gives 1/2 + 1/(2theta). Bettin-Gonek Thm 2 (a = 3, read off the printed T^{-3} in the single-zero formula) gives 1/2 + 2/theta. OpenAI paper2 (r = 1/n from the trivial rows u = p^n, a = 0) gives 1/2 + h(n-1)/(2n). So Bettin-Gonek is the n = infinity member of the extraction family.

**Compatibility checks.** Normalisations: Bettin-Gonek bound the whole range 2 <= N <= T^theta uniformly, over [0,T], with the |zeta|^2 weight. The extraction lemma uses one length D, smooth W and rows up to H. Exponents are matched by D = N, H = T, h = 1/theta. Objects: Bettin-Gonek conclude for zeta; paper2 concludes for finite-order Hecke L over Q(sqrt(-3)), zeta included as a factor. The law asserts the same exponent relation, not the same theorem. Signs and units: the 'a' term was checked against Thm 2, where the printed single-zero formula gives N^{2beta_0-1} << T^{4+eps}, i.e. beta_0 <= 1/2 + 2/theta, matching Thm 2 exactly. Range: the law is meaningful only for 1/2 <= beta < 1; the archimedean case is vacuous for theta <= 1. Dictionary arithmetic theta = 1/(2 Theta - 1): QRH(7/8) needs theta = 4/3; 11/12 needs 6/5; Liu's B_new needs 1.33349; 3/4 needs 2; 2/3 needs 3. Known lengths: theta = 4/7 gives the vacuous half-plane 11/8; theta = 17/33 gives 1.4706; the plain mean-value theorem gives theta = 1/2 (h = 2), hence 3/2. DWZ Thm 1.3 reproduces Bettin-Gonek's 1/2 + 1/(2theta) under I_y << T^{1+eps}; its Thm 1.1 rectangle version is weaker, 1/2 + (3+m)h.

**New claim.** (A) The extraction exponent law beta <= 1/2 + (h/2)(1 - r + a) holds in all three published or claimed instances. It is not yet an abstract theorem. (B) OBSTRUCTION LADDER. (i) Archimedean mollifier route: any zero-free half-plane needs h < 1 (theta > 1). Every proven mollified second moment (theta < 4/7 structured, < 17/33 generic) gives only a vacuous half-plane. Positivity-only (MVT) methods stop at theta = 1/2. So no argument using only proven mollified second moments plus Bettin-Gonek or DWZ extraction yields any QRH(Theta) with Theta < 1. Reaching the claimed 7/8 this way needs theta = 4/3, a factor 7/3 beyond Conrey's length. (ii) Finite-order route at or above the diagonal (H >= D): Ext(n,1) = 1 - 1/(2n) >= 3/4 for every order n >= 2. So no argument using only full-row Mobius mean squares plus extraction gives a half-plane left of Re s = 3/4. The 7/8 comparison system has floor 2/3. Reaching 1/2 needs h -> 0, i.e. family mean squares at sub-polynomial family size, which is GRH strength for the family (dossier C5). (iii) In both families positivity plus duality cannot cross the diagonal: the archimedean MVT term N and the residue-symbol dual diagonal D^2 dominate. (C) Contrast with M7-OB1: the same mollified moments, fed through Littlewood's lemma, give density currency (at most 100%). Fed through Landau-Hille extraction, they give exponent currency (QRH, and RH as theta -> infinity).

**Already proved.** Bettin-Gonek Thms 1-2 (refereed, Mathematika 2017). DWZ Thms 1.3, 1.8 (unrefereed). The extraction formula and floors of the OpenAI architecture (unrefereed source; symbolic check dossier_checks/limits.py; dossier [O]). Conrey's 4/7 and BCR's 17/33 (refereed). The dictionary arithmetic and the consistency of the law with the three instances (elementary, checked here).

**Conjectural.** The law as an abstract theorem. The claim that r <= 1/n for order-n residue-symbol families, and r = 0 for archimedean twists (no trivial rows beyond |t| = O(1)). The Bettin-Gonek single-zero formulas (printed without proof). The extraction lemma of paper2 (unrefereed, though L2-audited with no error found).

**Next step.** Prove an abstract extraction lemma. Setting: a family {f_u} of size H of completely multiplicative twists, containing H^r members with f_u(n) = 1 for (n, P_u) = 1, and the target Sum_u |Sum_n mu(n) f_u(n) W(n/D)|^2 << D^{1+eps} H. Conclusion: beta <= 1/2 + (h/2)(1 - r). Then derive Bettin-Gonek Thm 1 (r = 0) and paper2 (r = 1/n) as instances. Check whether any natural GL(m) family in DWZ Thm 1.8 has r > 0; for Dirichlet characters mod q only the principal character is trivial, so r = 0.

**Potential counterexamples.** A family where an off-line zero of one member forces signal on many rows without trivial twists, which would make the effective r > 1/n. A different value of the deficit a, if Bettin-Gonek's printed single-zero formula is wrong. The law would then be miscalibrated for dyadic windows, while Thm 2 itself would stand. paper2 is unrefereed.

**Tests.** Symbolic: the three instances, done here by hand. Numerical, not run: use the explicit Beurling toy zeta_Pi of C2-21 (Euler product, meromorphic on C, zeros exactly at rho = 0.75+10i and its conjugate) with its Mobius coefficients. Compute int_0^T |zeta_Pi M_N(1/2+it)|^2 dt for N = T^theta, theta in [0.5, 3], and check that growth starts at theta* = 1/(2beta-1) = 2.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** arXiv API: 'au:Bettin AND au:Gonek' (1604.02740). 'abs:mollifier AND abs:quasi-Riemann' found Dong-Wattanawanichkul-Zaharescu 2605.24363 (May 2026; family theta=infinity implies family QRH), which is archimedean only, with no trivial-row amplification. The dossier's OpenAI extraction lemma. Radziwill (ledger). I found no source stating the unified law or identifying Bettin-Gonek as the n = infinity member of the residue-symbol extraction.

**Verifier reasoning.** Verified verbatim: Bettin-Gonek Thm 1 (Re s > 1/2+1/(2theta)), Thm 2 (Re s > 1/2+2/theta) and both single-zero formulas, including N^{2beta0-1} log T/(T^3 log^2 N). DWZ Thm 1.1 requires int <= c1 T^{2 sigma theta-5-2m}, so I << T^{1+eps} gives 1/2+(3+m)/theta. DWZ Thms 1.3 and 1.8 and Remark 1.4 are as quoted. DWZ Remark 1.7 already records the dyadic '+3'. The dossier Sec. 4.1 extraction formula is |A_1|^2 << D^{1+eps}H^{1-1/n}+D^2H^{-2/n}, giving beta(n,h). All dictionary arithmetic checks: theta = 4/3, 6/5, 1.33349, 2, 3; half-planes 11/8, 1.4706, 3/2; factor 7/3. Problems: (A) is not a law confirmed by independent checks. The parameters are set per instance to match: a=3 is read off the same formula it is then 'checked' against, and r=1/n is the dossier's own row count. What remains is 'signal times the number of rows that carry it <= assumed total mean square'. The dossier already states this ('H^{1/6}D^{2beta} << DH'), and it is the standard zero-detection bookkeeping. Bettin-Gonek's object is |M_N zeta|^2 with target T^{1+eps}, not a pure Mobius polynomial with target DH, so 'n=inf member' is an analogy, not an identity. (B)(i) is correct but explicit in Bettin-Gonek ('for some theta > 1 ... a quasi-Riemann hypothesis'; Thm 2 gives a quasi-RH 'only when theta > 4') and in DWZ Remark 1.6. (B)(ii) is the limit of one lemma, min_n beta(n,1) = 3/4 at n=2. It is not an obstruction to 'any argument using only full-row Mobius mean squares': no countermodel is given (a family satisfying the mean-square bound with a member that has a zero left of 3/4), and the proposer lists r <= 1/n as conjectural. (B)(iii) restates the dossier's large-sieve barrier. (C) is a contrast in vocabulary.

**Verifier correction.** Call (A) heuristic signal-versus-mean-square bookkeeping, not a law, and drop the claim that three instances 'check' it. Credit the dossier Sec. 4.1 and Bettin-Gonek's remark, and DWZ Remark 1.7 for the dyadic deficit. Label (B)(i) a known corollary of Bettin-Gonek Thm 1 plus the known lengths 4/7 and 17/33. Restate (B)(ii) as: 'the paper2 extraction lemma, used with only trivial rows at h>=1, cannot give better than 1-1/(2n)>=3/4'. That is an architecture-specific limit already in the dossier ('11/12 is the floor of this architecture'). To become an obstruction it needs a countermodel family, or a proof that r <= 1/n is forced.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Bettin-Gonek 2017, Introduction (QRH needs theta>1; Thm 2 needs theta>4; single-zero formulas). DWZ 2605.24363, Remarks 1.6-1.7 and Thm 1.8 (family version). Observatory quasi-RH dossier Sec. 4.1, which states the extraction formula, the trivial-row count and the floor. Montgomery 1971 (Topics in Multiplicative Number Theory), for the zero-detecting-polynomial mean-square method.


## CON-0036 — Simple critical zeros on average for GL(2): the Petersson diagonal lifts the bandwidth from 1/2 to 1, giving >= 0.6725 simple+critical and >= 0.8363 distinct zeros on harmonic average over H_k(N)

*Mechanism:* M8-conjecture-generation · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 6/5 · *Difficulty:* 5/5

**Source statements.**
- Iwaniec-Luo-Sarnak 2000 (theorem DB CAND-00722), Remark B after Theorem 1: 'Theorem 1 holds unconditionally for supp phi-hat in (-1,1) (only the diagonal term of Petersson's formula contributes)'; Prop. 2 (Petersson): sum_f omega_f lambda_f(m) lambda_f(n) = delta_{m,n} + 2 pi i^{-k} sum_{c == 0 (N)} S(m,n;c) c^{-1} J_{k-1}(4 pi sqrt(mn)/c); Sect. 3.2: level aspect, N prime, fixed k.
- Alpoge-Furman arXiv:2608.13637v2, Theorem A and Remark 7.2 (pp.14-15): summing the chain over primitive chi mod q <= Q with weights (1-q/Q)^2 and a weighted large sieve (constant 1.263, X <= Q^{2-eps}) gives, unconditionally, family proportions >= 0.811 simple-on-line and >= 0.905 distinct ('details omitted', not in Lean, not audited by the Observatory).
- Hua-Yang arXiv:2608.16034v2 (abstract): for the q-2 nonprincipal characters mod a prime q and (log Q)^{1+eta}/Q <= T <= Q^{A0}, unconditionally N_0^s/N >= C_MT - o(1) = 0.6725007 and N_d/N >= 0.8362504 (Selberg family argument, Gevrey Gabor compression, rank-trace inequality); arXiv:2609.27808: bandwidth lambda_bw = min{1, liminf log(q-1)/l}.
- Lamzouri arXiv:2609.02882v2 Prop. 2.1 (as in M8-1): the certificate is linear in (tr A, tr A^2), so it averages over families with nonnegative weights.
- Chandee-Lee-Liu-Radziwill arXiv:1211.6725, Theorem 1 and Prop. 1 (GRH): family proportion 11/12 of simple zeros of primitive Dirichlet L-functions via the asymptotic large sieve for |alpha| < 2. GRH is used on the prime side (Lemma 4: sum_p Psi(p) Phi(p/X) log p/sqrt p << Q^eps; Lemma 7: S_UE << Q^{1+eps}/C) as well as on the zero side.

**Proposed link.** M8-1 shows that for a single GL(2) form the diagonal bandwidth is 1/2, which is useless for simple zeros. For the level-aspect family H_k^*(N), N prime -> infinity, k fixed, the Petersson formula makes the second moment of prime sums of length X diagonal for X <= N^{1-eps}: the J_{k-1} term is << (sqrt(mn)/N)^{k-1}. With heights T(N) in [(log N)^{1+eta}, (log N)^A], the local log-conductor is log(N T^2) = (1+o(1)) log N. So the family-averaged bandwidth is log X/log(N T^2) -> 1, the same as zeta's. Summing the per-form LZ inequality n_f >= 2 tr A_f - tr A_f^2 with harmonic weights omega_f, and inserting the Petersson-averaged second moment, gives the C_MT constants on average.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Bandwidth. X <= N^{1-eps} and log T = o(log N) give lambda -> 1; this is consistent with ILS Remark B (diagonal range = support (-1,1)). (2) Averaging. AF/LZ are applied per form, and the inequality is linear, so harmonic weights are legitimate. The statement is a harmonic-weighted proportion; removing the weights uses ILS-type techniques, which are standard but not done. (3) Prime powers. The constant part of Lambda_f(p^2) is not orthogonal under Petersson but contributes O(1) relative to log^2 X. (4) Bulk statistics. Zeros at height T -> infinity have GUE local statistics, so the symmetry type (O/SO) affects only the 1-level density near the central point and is irrelevant for zeros in (T,2T]. (5) Localisation at polylog heights needs Hua-Yang's Gevrey taper, transplanted to Gamma_C(s+(k-1)/2). (6) Comparison with AF Rem. 7.2: there, Dirichlet families reach bandwidth > 1 because the family size is the square of the conductor. For GL(2) twist families (f x chi, chi mod q ~ Q) the conductor is ~ q^2, so the large-sieve diagonal again gives only lambda = 1/2. Wide (level or weight) families are needed.

**New claim.** Conjecture (expected theorem) GL2-F. Let k >= 2 be fixed, N prime -> infinity, and T = T(N) with (log N)^{1+eta} <= T <= (log N)^A. Write omega_f for the harmonic weights on H_k^*(N). Then, unconditionally, liminf_{N->inf} [sum_f omega_f N_0^s(f;T,2T)] / [sum_f omega_f N(f;T,2T)] >= 2 - C_MT = 0.672500704, and the same ratio for distinct zeros is >= (3 - C_MT)/2 = 0.836250352. Conditional extension (QRH-type, conjectural): if the Kloosterman-term analysis of ILS Sects. 2-3 can be carried out for X up to N^{1+kappa} with unconditional prime inputs, the constants increase. Here a zero-free half-plane Re s > Theta for Dirichlet L-functions would replace GRH in the primes-in-progressions step; the quantitative gain is not computed.

**Already proved.** The algebraic certificate (LZ Prop. 2.1; AF Lemma 3.2), the Petersson formula and its diagonal range (ILS), and the full degree-1 family analogue (Hua-Yang, unrefereed, mechanism identical). The per-form linearity argument is elementary. Nothing about GL(2) zeros is proved here.

**Conjectural.** The family-averaged all-zeros second-moment asymptotic sum_f omega_f tr A_f^2 = (C(f) + o(1)) sum_f omega_f N_f at bandwidth -> 1, unconditionally, including the localisation and tail control at polylog heights for degree-2 gamma factors.

**Next step.** Rewrite Hua-Yang 2608.16034 Sects. on 'character-averaged first and second traces' with Petersson in place of orthogonality of characters mod q. Bound the J_{k-1} Kloosterman tail uniformly for sqrt(mn) <= N^{1-eps}. Then repeat the rank-trace step verbatim. Optionally carry out the weight aspect (k ~ K, N = 1) as well, where Petersson averaging over k gives bandwidth up to 1 by the same diagonal argument.

**Potential counterexamples.** (a) Forms with small L(1,sym^2 f) dominate the harmonic weights. Harmless for the weighted statement; the 2026 Siegel-zero exclusion also makes L(1, Ad f) >> 1/log(kN) effective. (b) If T grows like a power of N, the bandwidth drops to 1/(1+2tau) and the constant degrades (at T = N^{1/2}, lambda = 1/2 and simple zeros are lost). (c) Oldforms at composite level spoil the clean diagonal. Restrict to N prime.

**Tests.** Numerical: for prime N ~ 10^3-10^4, weight 2, take newform zeros from LMFDB at moderate heights. Compute the harmonic-weighted sum K^2/N at bandwidth ~0.9 and compare with R_{0.9}(psi) (flat: 1/0.9 + 0.3 = 1.4111); check that the per-form ratio fluctuates around the family mean. Symbolic: verify that the Kloosterman tail is O(N^{-1/2+eps}) times the diagonal for X <= N^{1-eps}.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** arXiv API 2026-11-17: abs:simple AND zeros AND Petersson (no relevant hit); abs:rank-trace (only AF, LZ, Hua-Yang Dirichlet families); abs:"pair correlation" AND (modular OR automorphic OR GL(2)) (7 hits, none on proportions of simple zeros). Known GL(2) critical-line proportions come from Levinson/Selberg methods (Hafner; Bernard arXiv:1404.6432, single form) and give no simplicity. Twisted PGL(3) critical zeros >= 1/9 (Conrey-Kwan-Lin-Turnage-Butterbaugh arXiv:2607.00282). No unconditional positive proportion of simple zeros, on average or individually, was found for GL(2) families. zbMATH was not queried successfully for this item.

**Verifier reasoning.** Source checks:
- AF Rem 7.2 matches the text: 0.811 simple-on-line and 0.905 distinct, 'details omitted', not in Lean.
- Hua-Yang arXiv:2608.16034 abstract matches. Note that Q=log q there, so the heights are polylog in q.
- arXiv:2609.27808 defines lambda_bw as quoted.
- LZ Prop 2.1 is linear in (tr A, tr A^2), so averaging with nonnegative harmonic weights is legitimate.
- ILS Remark B matches the Level-1 database entry.

The architecture is coherent and mirrors Hua-Yang: a per-form inertia certificate, Petersson orthogonality, and log(NT^2)~log N at polylog T, which give lambda->1.

The gap is the diagonal-range claim. J_{k-1}(x) << x^{k-1} alone does not give it. With the Weil bound and absolute values, the Kloosterman term is << X^k N^{1/2-k+eps}, against a diagonal of size ~ log^2 X. That makes the off-diagonal negligible only for X <= N^{1-1/(2k)-eps} (bandwidth 3/4 for k=2). Reaching X <= N^{1-eps} needs cancellation in the c-sum. One route: open S(m,n;c), separate sqrt(mn) using the power series of J_{k-1}, and apply the additive large sieve sum_x|sum_m a_m e(mx/c)|^2 <= (c+X)||a||^2. This gives relative size ~ (X/N)^{k-1}N^eps. The stated test target 'Kloosterman tail O(N^{-1/2+eps}) times diagonal for X <= N^{1-eps}' is therefore wrong.

The qualitative conclusion is robust. Even the trivial bandwidth 3/4 (k=2) gives family averages 2-R*_{3/4}=0.4191 simple-on-line and 0.7095 distinct (computed). At bandwidth 1, (3-R)/2 is the right distinct bound (it beats 1/R), so 0.83625 stands if the diagonal lemma holds.

Minor points:
- Counterexample note (b) gives bandwidth 1/(1+2tau) and ignores the hybrid t-average; the natural target is (1+tau)/(1+2tau).
- An effective lower bound on L(1, Ad f) for non-dihedral f is classical (Goldfeld-Hoffstein-Lieman); the 2026 Siegel exclusion matters only in the dihedral case.

No prior art found.

**Verifier correction.** Replace the justification with an explicit diagonal lemma: for fixed k >= 2, N prime and X <= N^{1-eps}, sum_f omega_f|sum_{p<=X} a_p lambda_f(p)|^2 = (1+O((X/N)^{k-1}N^eps)) sum|a_p|^2, proved via the additive large sieve after opening the Kloosterman sums (or a Deshouillers-Iwaniec-type bound), not by Bessel decay alone.
- Unconditional fallback with the Weil bound only: bandwidth 1-1/(2k), giving averages >= 2-1/c*_{1-1/(2k)} and >= (3-1/c*_{1-1/(2k)})/2 (k=2: 0.4191 and 0.7095).
- Correct test T4's target to (X/N)^{k-1}.

**Prior art found by verifier.** None found for GL(2) families: no unconditional proportion of simple or distinct zeros on average. Mechanism precedents: Hua-Yang arXiv:2608.16034 and arXiv:2609.27808 (Dirichlet family, same rank-trace route); AF Rem 7.2 (sketch); ILS 2000 Remark B and Theorem 3 (diagonal and weight-averaged ranges for 1-level density); Conrey-Kwan-Lin-Turnage-Butterbaugh arXiv:2607.00282 (Levinson-type critical zeros, no simplicity).


## CON-0030 — Density-currency ceiling: pair-correlation, Levinson-Conrey and bandwidth-limited rank-trace methods saturate at '100% critical' and are blind to o(T) off-line zeros

*Mechanism:* M7-negative-synthesis · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 5/5 · *Difficulty:* 2/5

**Source statements.**
- Goldston-Lee-Schettler-Suriajaya, arXiv:2503.15449v4, Theorem 1 (Sec. 1): 'Assuming the pair correlation conjecture PCC, then asymptotically 100% of the zeros of zeta(s) are simple and on the critical line.' PCC as stated in Sec. 4: N(lambda) = TL int_0^lambda (1-(sin pi a/pi a)^2) da + o(TL), with L = log T/2pi and N(lambda) = #{rho,rho': 0<gamma,gamma'<=T, 0<(gamma'-gamma)L<=lambda}. Mechanism (Sec. 5, eq. 5.5): N^circledast(T) counts pairs with gamma = gamma'. Off-line pairs beta+i gamma and 1-beta+i gamma act as 'horizontal multiplicity'.
- Bettin-Gonek, arXiv:1604.02740, Introduction p.1: Farmer proved that the theta=infinity conjecture implies kappa = 1. Radziwill's work implies that 'Levinson's method can give kappa=1 only if it is used with mollifiers of length T^theta, where theta is arbitrarily large'.
- Bui (Bui-Conrey-Young) 2011, Sec. 2.1 eq. (2.1) (theorem DB): kappa >= 1 - (1/R) log((1/T) int_1^T |V psi(sigma_0+it)|^2 dt) + o(1), with sigma_0 = 1/2 - R/L. This is Littlewood's lemma plus Jensen/AM-GM. Thm 2.2: the mean value has error O(T L^{-1+eps}).
- Lagarias-Rodgers, arXiv:1905.12123, Theorem 3.1: there is a deterministic sequence c_j with c_{j+1}-c_j in {1/2,1,3/2,...} whose n-level correlations equal the GUE ones for every eta in K_n (Fourier support in {sum xi_i != 0} or {sum|xi_i|<2}).
- Alpoge-Furman, arXiv:2608.13637 (unrefereed), Sec. 7.2, as recorded in frontiers/ledger.jsonl: a bandwidth-one certificate depends on the zero configuration only through its first two trace moments against test functions with Fourier support in [-1,1]. Ceiling about 0.6818 for kappa*.
- Littlewood's lemma (Titchmarsh Sec. 9.9): 2 pi int_{1/2}^{2} N(sigma,T) d sigma = int_0^T log|zeta(1/2+it)| dt + O(log T). The left side is 2 pi D(T), with D(T) := sum_{beta>1/2, 0<gamma<=T} (beta - 1/2).
- Davenport-Heilbronn function (C2-03 / C3-D14 certified zeros; Titchmarsh 10.25): a linear combination of L(s,chi), L(s,chi-bar) mod 5 with a Dirichlet-L functional equation and >> T zeros in sigma > 1.

**Proposed link.** Every method in the list touches the zero multiset Z only through functionals Q_T(Z) normalised by N(T) ~ TL and known with relative error o(1). Each such functional has bounded sensitivity to moving a single zero. Hence it cannot distinguish Z from a multiset Z' in which E(T) = o(T) zeros (PCC, n-level) or o(N(T)) zeros (Levinson D(T)) have been moved off the line. Two on-line zeros at consecutive ordinates gamma_1 < gamma_2 can be replaced by the pair (1/2 +- delta) + i gamma_1; this keeps the local count hypothesis. The ceiling '100%' is attained: GLSS reach it from PCC, and Farmer reaches it from theta=infinity. RH needs exponent currency: functionals in which one off-line zero contributes a growing power, such as Littlewood's log-mean, the Bettin-Gonek length exponent (M7-OB2) or x^beta in the explicit formula.

**Compatibility checks.** Normalisations. GLSS use L = (1/2pi) log T and N(T) = TL + o(TL). PCC has error o(TL), uniform for lambda in compact subsets of (0,inf). The Levinson reduction works at sigma_0 = 1/2 - R/L with V = Q(-L^{-1} d/ds) zeta. The bound concerns zeros of V, and through the standard Levinson-Conrey argument zeros of zeta on the line, so its output is a proportion with o(1) error. D(T) uses multiplicities. Quantifiers. The lemma below needs the local-count hypothesis N(t+h)-N(t) <= hL + C log T for both Z and Z'. Zeta satisfies it because S(t) = O(log T). The modification that preserves ordinates keeps it. Lagarias-Rodgers concerns AH, i.e. vertical spacings, and assumes RH in its target class (Rudnick-Sarnak), whereas GLSS works without RH. Both are ordinate-only statements, so they are compatible. BGST's unconditional F(alpha) (arXiv:2306.04799, eq. 1.2) is NOT ordinate-only: it weights pairs by x^{delta+delta'}, so it is excluded from the lemma. DH witness for value statistics: sup-norm exponents and moment orders on the line hold for DH by the triangle inequality from L(s,chi) mod 5. DH has an FE but no Euler product, so this extends C2 SP-1 and is not independent of it.

**New claim.** LEMMA (elementary; proof sketched here, not reviewed). Let Z and Z' be multisets in {0<Re s<1, Im s>0}, symmetric under rho -> 1 - conj(rho), both satisfying N(t+h)-N(t) <= hL + C log T for 0 < t <= T and h <= 1. Suppose Z' arises from Z by moving E(T) zeros of height <= T. Then: (a) |N_Z(lambda) - N_Z'(lambda)| <= 4 E(T)(lambda + C log T), because each moved zero lies in at most 2(lambda + C log T) ordered pairs at its old position and at most that many at its new one; (b) |D_Z(T) - D_Z'(T)| <= E(T)/2; (c) every n-level correlation sum with bounded eta supported on a window of size lambda/L changes by <= 2n ||eta||_inf E(T)(lambda + C log T)^{n-1}. OBSTRUCTION. Consider arguments whose only zero inputs are some of: (i) Riemann-von Mangoldt with S(t) = O(log T); (ii) PCC, or GUE n-level correlations, with o(TL) error and any test-function support; (iii) Fujii-Tsang moments of Delta_U S(t) with o(T) error; (iv) Levinson-Conrey mean values with relative error o(1), fed through Littlewood's lemma and concavity; (v) bandwidth-limited trace moments (Alpoge-Furman certificates). No such argument can prove RH, exclude one off-line quadruple, or exclude any set of o(T) off-line zeros. Its strongest possible conclusion is '100% critical and simple', and this is attained. CLASSIFICATION. The 2026 critical-line advances (Alpoge-Furman 2/3, Lamzouri 0.6725 and 13/20 isolated, Knausgard 0.6735, Cobeli-Zaharescu 8/9 simple-or-critical, B. Wang's near-limit remark) are all density currency, and no iteration of them reaches RH. VALUE STATISTICS (sub-claim). Critical-line size information (sup exponent, finitely many power moments of standard order) together with an FE and Dirichlet-series shape cannot exclude off-line zeros, with DH as witness. The first log-moment is an exact detector by Littlewood's lemma (for DH, int_0^T log|f(1/2+it)| dt >> T).

**Already proved.** GLSS Thm 1 (PCC implies 100% simple and critical; preprint, Mathematika 2026 per the scan). Farmer 1993: theta=infinity implies kappa=1 (as quoted by Bettin-Gonek). Lagarias-Rodgers Thm 3.1 (Quart. J. Math. 2020). Littlewood's lemma (classical). The DH zeros (C2/C3 certified) and the DH functional equation. The lemma (a)-(c) is elementary counting; its proof is given above and has not been reviewed.

**Conjectural.** Whether BGST's unconditional F(alpha), which carries the exponent-currency weight T^{alpha(delta+delta')}, can be converted into zero-density information near sigma=1/2. Cancellation inside clusters of off-line zeros blocks the naive positivity argument, and BGST themselves assume a density hypothesis (Thm 3), so I make no claim. The classification of each 2026 preprint rests on its abstract or ledger description, not on a line-by-line reading.

**Next step.** (1) Write the lemma with explicit C, from Trudgian's explicit S(t) bound, and attach a 'currency' tag to each ledger record on critical-line proportions. (2) Decide for each 2026 proportion paper which functional actually enters (Weil-form compression, pair correlation, mollified moment), and confirm it satisfies the sensitivity bound. (3) Separately examine BGST's F(alpha) as the only mixed-currency pair-correlation functional.

**Potential counterexamples.** A method that looks like density currency but carries exponent weights, e.g. BGST's x^{rho-rho'} or Selberg-type moments of log|zeta| off the line. A method whose error term beats the sensitivity bound, i.e. relative error o(1/N(T)), which is impossible for asymptotic mean values. The lemma fails if the modification destroys the local-count hypothesis, e.g. by clustering many moved zeros at one ordinate. The obstruction uses a modification that does not do this.

**Tests.** Not run. Planned: take the 10^5 Arb-certified zeros, build Z' by converting k random consecutive on-line pairs into pairs (1/2 +- 0.4) + i gamma_1, and compute N(lambda), nearest-neighbour spacing histograms and KS distance to GUE for k in {10, 10^2, 10^3}. Check |Delta N(lambda)| <= 4k(lambda + C log T) and that GUE-fit statistics stay within sampling noise. Also compute the exact change of D(T), which is k*0.4.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** arXiv API: 'au:Suriajaya AND ti:correlation' (2503.15449, 2501.14545, 2507.06823, 2306.04799, 2205.06503). Lagarias-Rodgers 1905.12123 read in full. Bettin-Gonek intro read. Frontier ledger entries on Radziwill's mollifier limits and the Alpoge-Furman bandwidth ceiling. The slogan 'even 100% is not RH' is folklore (C3-D9; Turnage-Butterbaugh guest post). I found no source stating the sensitivity lemma or the currency classification. At most 'possibly new in this explicit form'.

**Verifier reasoning.** Sources confirmed. GLSS Thm 1 matches. Their PCC (Sec. 4) carries o(TL) error, and Remark 1 lets lambda0->0 and lambda1->inf. Eqs (5.4)-(5.5) define N^circledast, and Sec. 7 gives the horizontal-multiplicity mechanism. GLSS get N^circledast=(1+o(1))TL from PCC plus the unconditional second moment of Delta_U S, and then use the symmetry rho<->1-conj(rho). The Bettin-Gonek introduction (Farmer: theta=inf implies kappa=1; the Radziwill quote) is verbatim. Lagarias-Rodgers Thm 3.1 is confirmed. The Alpoge-Furman ceiling 0.6818287 matches ledger LED-CRIT-10. Lemma (a) is right: a window of height lambda/L holds at most lambda+C log T zeros, so each position lies in at most 2(lambda+C log T) ordered pairs. Lemma (b) is trivial. Item (iii) is also covered, because Delta_U S(t)=Delta_U N(t)-Delta_U theta/pi depends only on ordinates. The headline conclusion is right and folklore: density statements with o(N) slack cannot exclude o(N) off-line zeros, so even 100% is not RH. Defects: (1) Lemma (c) as quantified fails for n>=3. The worst-case window count is about C log T, so the change is at most 2n||eta|| E(T)(C log T)^{n-1}, and this is o(TL) only when E(T)=o(T/(log T)^{n-2}). Also, Rudnick-Sarnak K_n test functions are band-limited, so they cannot have compact support. (2) The modification moves gamma_2 to gamma_1, so it is not ordinate-preserving. That is harmless, but the local-count constant becomes C+1 and the moves must be kept separated. (3) For (iv), mollified mean values are not functionals of the zero multiset, so the sensitivity lemma does not apply to them. The obstruction holds only through the output form (Littlewood plus Jensen gives a lower bound on a proportion of zeros of V psi near sigma_0=1/2-R/L). Lemma (b) is about D(T) for zeta, not for V psi. Bettin-Gonek feed the same mean values into an exponent-type conclusion. So the excluded class must be defined by the method, not by its inputs, as the proposal half-acknowledges. (4) Alpoge-Furman certificates use the on-line/off-line split as well as ordinates. They still output a proportion, so the conclusion is unaffected. (5) Classifying the 2026 proportion papers as 'density currency' is automatic, since their outputs are proportions.

**Verifier correction.** (c): either require E(T)=o(T/(log T)^{n-2}), or choose the moved zeros, both old and new positions, at ordinates where the count in a window of height lambda/L is O(lambda+1). Most zeros qualify, and then 'any o(T)' is true. Replace compact support by Schwartz decay. Drop 'ordinate-preserving' and require the modifications to be separated. Restate (iv) as a statement about the Littlewood+Jensen output inequality, not about the mean values. Present the result as a formalisation of folklore, not as a new obstruction.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Folklore that 100% critical is not RH. GLSS Sec. 7 already identifies horizontal multiplicity as the only route from ordinate statistics to beta. Littlewood's lemma as an exact detector: Littlewood 1924, Titchmarsh 9.9, and Balazard-Saias-Yor 1999 (RH iff int log|zeta(1/2+it)|/|1/2+it|^2 dt = 0). Davenport-Heilbronn 1936 and Titchmarsh 10.25 for the value-statistics witness, plus repo C2 SP-1.


## CON-0006 — Off-line pair defect: the exact obstruction to an RH-free Conrey–Ghosh–Gonek/Bui–Heath-Brown 19/27, and a conditional theorem under a sub-thin-box hypothesis

*Mechanism:* M2-missing-lemmas · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 4/5 · *Difficulty:* 3/5

**Source statements.**
- Bui & Heath-Brown, 'On simple zeros of the Riemann zeta-function' (BLMS 2013; arXiv:1302.5018v1), Sec. 2 eq. (1): N*(T) >= |sum_{0<gamma<=T} B zeta'(rho)|^2 / sum_{0<gamma<=T} |B zeta'(rho)|^2 for any regular B (Cauchy–Schwarz; rho simple iff zeta'(rho) != 0). DB: CAND-01275 'Eq. (1)'.
- Same paper, Sec. 2 Lemma 1, no RH: S_1 := sum_{0<gamma<=T} B zeta'(rho) = TL^2/(2 pi) - M_1 + O(TL) + O(y T^{1/2+eps}) and S_2 := sum_{0<gamma<=T} B zeta'(rho) B zeta'(1-rho) = (TL^3/2pi)(...) - 2Re M_2 + .... Here B(s) = sum_{k<=y} mu(k) P(log(y/k)/log y) k^{-s}, P real, P(0)=0, P(1)=1, y = T^theta, theta < 1/2. Lemma 2, Sec. 2-3, also unconditional: M_{nu,3} << y^{1/3}T^{5/6+eps} + eta^{-1/2} T L^C. DB: CAND-01275 Lemma 1 and Lemma 2.
- Same paper, p.4, after (10): with P(x) = -theta x^2 + (1+theta)x and theta -> 1/2, S_1 ~ (19/24) TL^2/(2pi) and S_2 ~ (57/64) TL^3/(2pi). Verbatim: 'Assuming RH we have S_2 = sum |B zeta'(rho)|^2. Note that this is the only place we need RH.'
- Conrey, Ghosh & Gonek 1998, Theorem 2 and Sec. 2 optimisation (DB: CAND-00742): the same S_1, S_2 under GLH; Cauchy–Schwarz plus RH gives 19/27.
- Baluyot–Goldston–Suriajaya–Turnage-Butterbaugh, arXiv:2501.14545v3, Theorem 1 and Remark 3: under the thin-box hypothesis (all zeros with T<gamma<=2T in B_b, b -> 0) the pair-correlation method gives >= 2/3 simple and on the line. Remark 3 cites Conrey: unconditionally more than 99.9% of zeros lie within 8/log T of the line.
- Alpöge–Furman, arXiv:2608.13637v2, Sec. 1.2 (Z)+(L), Lemma 3.2 (rank–trace) and Prop. 4.1: inertia replaces positivity for the Gram matrix of modulated windows. Each zero's contribution is bounded by the Gabor frame identity (Lemma 2.1). Sec. 7.2(c): 'every multiple on-line point and every off-line pair sits on the index side at the flat charge 4.'

**Proposed link.** Write a(s) := B(s) zeta'(s) (B has real coefficients) and rho* := 1 - conj(rho), the reflection of rho in the critical line. rho* has the same ordinate and the same multiplicity as rho. Since B and zeta' have real coefficients, B zeta'(1-rho) = conj(a(rho*)). Hence, exactly and unconditionally, S_2 = sum_{beta=1/2} |a(rho)|^2 + sum_{off-line pairs {rho,rho*}} 2 Re(a(rho) conj a(rho*)). Multiple zeros contribute 0 to S_1, S_2 and the defect, because zeta'(rho) = 0. Apply Cauchy–Schwarz with on-line simple zeros as weight-1 coordinates and each off-line simple pair (u,v) = (a(rho), a(rho*)) as one weight-2 coordinate with value u+v. With |u+v|^2/2 = 2Re(u conj v) + |u-v|^2/2 this gives the unconditional inequality (*) N*(T) >= |S_1|^2 / (S_2 + D/2), where D := sum_{off-line pairs, 0<gamma<=T} |B zeta'(rho) - B zeta'(1-conj rho)|^2. Toy check: one pair with u=1, v=t gives (1+t)^2/((1+t)^2/2) = 2, which is exact. With BHB's unconditional S_1, S_2, the whole RH dependence of 19/27 (simple zeros, on or off the line) is therefore the single statement D = o(T L^3).

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Index ranges: BHB sum over 0<gamma<=T with multiplicity; the reflection preserves gamma, so pairs never straddle the range. (2) Real coefficients: b(k) = mu(k)P(...) with P real, so conj(B(s)) = B(conj s). This is needed for B zeta'(1-rho) = conj(a(rho*)); a complex-coefficient mollifier would break it. (3) BHB's main terms need theta < 1/2. M_{nu,2} uses Siegel's theorem (ineffective but unconditional), and M_{nu,3} is BHB Lemma 2 (unconditional). So S_1 and S_2 are unconditional, as stated on p.4. (4) What is counted: (*) counts simple zeros anywhere, not simple on-line zeros. Counting on-line ones needs separate control of sum_off |a|^2 and sum_off a. (5) Difference from Alpöge–Furman: their per-zero normalisation (Gabor frame, tr P <= N_0) is what makes off-line pairs harmless (flat charge 4). The discrete moments have no per-zero bound on |a(rho)|, so the inertia/rank–trace device does not transfer. The scale-free form rank P >= (tr P + 2 tr Q)^2/||P+Q||^2 - 4 n_+(Q) points the wrong way once n_+(Q) is bounded by the off-line count. I checked this algebraically: it yields only an upper bound on N_0^s. (6) Thin box: BGST's hypothesis (b -> 0) is NOT enough for (*). Within distance b/(2L), |a(rho) - a(rho*)| <= 2 delta sup|a'|. Unconditional bounds give sum_rho sup|a'|^2 << T L^6, one power of L worse than the size of S_2 (~TL^3) times L^2, because the pointwise local zero count is only O(L). So D << b^2 T L^4, which is o(S_2) only when b = o(L^{-1/2}). (7) Conrey's 99.9%-within-8/L (BGST Remark 3) controls the far off-line zeros but not the near ones; those give D of order TL^4 (8/L)^2 L^2 / L^2 ... i.e. not o(TL^3).

**New claim.** (a) Unconditional inequality (*): N*(T) >= |S_1|^2/(S_2 + D/2), with D the off-line pair defect defined above. (b) Missing lemma ML-CGG (weighted near-line concentration; strictly weaker than RH): sum_{T<gamma<=2T, beta != 1/2} |B zeta'(rho) - B zeta'(1-conj rho)|^2 = o(T (log T)^3) for the BHB mollifier with theta -> 1/2. It implies liminf N*(T)/N(T) >= 19/27 unconditionally, via BHB Lemma 1, Lemma 2 and (10). A sufficient form: sum_{beta != 1/2} (beta - 1/2)^2 W(gamma) = o(T L^3), with W(gamma) = sup_{|sigma-1/2| <= |beta-1/2|} |(B zeta')'(sigma + i gamma)|^2. (c) Conditional theorem, provable now: if for all large T every zero with T<gamma<=2T satisfies |beta - 1/2| <= eta(T)/(log T)^{3/2} with eta(T) -> 0, then liminf N*(T)/N(T) >= 19/27. Proof sketch: subharmonicity of |a'|^2 on discs of radius 1/L; the local count N(t+1) - N(t) << log t; and the unconditional mollified mean values int_T^{2T} |(B zeta')'(sigma+it)|^2 dt << T L^4, uniform for |sigma - 1/2| <= 3/L (Balasubramanian–Conrey–Heath-Brown/Conrey range theta < 1/2). Together these give D << eta^2 T L^3 = o(S_2). The hypothesis is weaker than RH but stronger than BGST's b -> 0. (d) Reaching BGST's b -> 0 would need, in addition, an average local zero count (Fujii-type moments of N(t+h) - N(t), h ~ 1/L) and a mollified fourth-moment bound int |(B zeta')'|^4 << T L^8 near the line. That bound is available through the BBLR twisted fourth moment only when B^2 has length < T^{1/4}, i.e. theta < 1/8, at the cost of a smaller CGG constant.

**Already proved.** BHB Lemma 1, Lemma 2 and the asymptotics (10) (unconditional, S_1 and S_2 as analytic sums). BHB Theorem 19/27 under RH. CGG Theorem 1 under RH+GLH. BGST/GS: 2/3 simple and on the line under the b -> 0 thin box. Alpöge–Furman Theorem A: >= 0.6725 simple and on the line, unconditionally. Inequality (*) and conditional theorem (c) are elementary given these inputs; their proofs are sketched here, not refereed.

**Conjectural.** ML-CGG in (b), i.e. any unconditional bound D <= eta T L^3 with eta below about 0.046 (S_2/N), which would be enough to beat 0.6725 and the bandwidth-one ceiling 0.6819 for simple zeros. It requires near-line concentration of off-line zeros at scale o(L^{-3/2}) on (weighted) average. The best unconditional statements are only O(1/L): Selberg's sum_{beta>1/2}(beta - 1/2) << T, and Conrey's 99.9% within 8/L.

**Next step.** (1) Write (*) and theorem (c) as a referee-ready note. Check that BHB's error terms O(T L^{2+eps}) do not interact with the defect, and fix whether the pointwise local count O(L) or an averaged count is used. (2) Quantify how small eta must be for (*) to beat 0.6819 using only Conrey's 99.9%-within-8/L input plus a mean value over the remaining near-line zeros, i.e. find the largest explicit admissible eta. (3) Compute the optimal CGG constant at mollifier length theta < 1/8 (where the fourth-moment route to b -> 0 is open) to see whether b -> 0 with a smaller constant still beats 2/3.

**Potential counterexamples.** Davenport–Heilbronn-type functions: same functional equation, no Euler product, many off-line zeros. The analytic S_1, S_2 exist but D is of the same order as S_2, so (*) gives nothing. This shows ML-CGG must use arithmetic (Euler product) or zero-density input, not the functional equation alone. Hypothetical zeta configurations with a positive proportion of zeros at distance about c/L off the line are consistent with all known density results and make D comparable to S_2. So ML-CGG cannot follow from Selberg-type density estimates with unspecified constants.

**Tests.** (i) Synthetic: take Gram-like on-line points plus injected off-line pairs at depth lambda/L, compute S_1, S_2 and D from a model a(s), and check (*) against the true count, including the toy u=1, v=t equality case. (ii) For the first 10^5 actual zeros (all on the line by Platt–Trudgian), D = 0 and (*) reduces to CGG. A numerical S_1, S_2 at finite T (theta small) checks the main-term constants, as a non-rigorous floating-point check. (iii) Davenport–Heilbronn function at T ~ 10^3: compute D/S_2 to illustrate the obstruction.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** arXiv API (metadata): abs:'simple zeros' AND abs:zeta (40 most recent; the hits are AF 2608.13637, Lamzouri 2609.02882, Knausgård 2610.08965, GS 2511.20059, GS 2603.28104, BGST 2501.14545; all are pair-correlation or inertia routes, none touches the CGG discrete moments without RH); abs:Conrey AND Ghosh AND Gonek; abs:mollified AND 'simple zeros'. Web search 'unconditional 19/27 simple zeros ... 2026' found no unconditional 19/27 and no discussion of the off-line defect. Full texts read: AF Sec. 1.2-1.3, 3, 7.1-7.3 (the CGG device is cited only for its integrality step); BHB complete; BGST 2025 intro and Remark 3. zbMATH not queried. Inequality (*) is elementary, so a prior statement in a paper not indexed by these searches is possible.

**Verifier reasoning.** Sources check out. BHB (arXiv:1302.5018v1) p.3 says 'Up to this point, all the analysis is unconditional'. Lemma 2 is proved without GLH or RH. p.4 reads verbatim: 'Assuming RH we have S2 = sum|B zeta'(rho)|^2. Note that this is the only place we need RH.' B has real coefficients because P is real and b(k) = mu(k)P(...).

Identity. With rho* = 1 - conj(rho) we have conj(rho*) = 1 - rho, so B zeta'(1-rho) = a(1-rho) = conj(a(rho*)). Hence S_2 = sum on-line |a|^2 + sum over pairs 2Re(u conj v), exactly and without RH. The pair has the same ordinate and the same multiplicity, and multiple zeros give a = 0.

Inequality (*). Cauchy-Schwarz with weight-2 coordinates (u+v)/2 gives |S_1|^2 <= N* (sum on-line |a|^2 + sum |u+v|^2/2). The identity |u+v|^2/2 = 2Re(u conj v) + |u-v|^2/2 is correct, so (*) N* >= |S_1|^2/(S_2 + D/2) holds. Random test: 0 violations in 20,000 configurations, and the equality case u=1, v=t gives 2.

Conditional theorem (c). Subharmonicity of |a'|^2 on discs of radius 1/L, plus the overlap multiplicity O(L) from N(t+1)-N(t) << log t, plus int |(B zeta')'|^2 << TL^4 near the line, give sum_rho sup|a'|^2 << L^2 * L * (1/L) * TL^4 = TL^6. With delta <= eta/L^{3/2} this gives D << eta^2 TL^3 = o(S_2). The mean value follows from Cauchy's formula and the BCHB theta<1/2 bound for int |B zeta|^2 at sigma = 1/2 + O(1/L). (19/24)^2/(57/64) = 19/27 is confirmed.

The BGST statements check out against v3: Theorem 1 (b -> 0 gives 2/3 simple and on the line), Theorem 2 (fixed b = 0.3185 gives 2/3, b = 0.001 gives 0.6725), and Remark 3 (Conrey: 99.9% of zeros within 8/log T). So (*) and (c) are sound.

The 'conjectural' threshold is wrong. It says 'D <= eta T L^3 with eta below about 0.046 would beat 0.6725 and 0.6819'. The value 0.046 is the RELATIVE threshold D/(2S_2) < 0.0464, and it only beats 0.6725.

**Verifier correction.** (1) Thresholds, using S_1 ~ (19/24)TL^2/2pi and S_2 ~ (57/64)TL^3/2pi. In absolute form D <= eta T L^3, one needs eta < 0.01315 to beat 0.6725007, eta < 0.01074 to beat 0.67800919 and eta < 0.0091 to beat 0.6818287. In relative form the conditions are D/(2S_2) < 0.0464, 0.0379 and 0.0321. (2) Inequality (*) counts simple zeros anywhere. The right comparator is therefore Knausgård's simple-anywhere value 0.67800919 (arXiv:2610.08965; computer-assisted, unrefereed) and the kappa* window cap 0.68185, not Alpöge-Furman's 0.6725, which counts zeros that are simple and on the line. (3) ML-CGG as written sums over all off-line zeros, which is 2D. This is harmless for o(TL^3), but D must be defined consistently. (4) The pair-correlation route needs only a fixed thin box (BGST v3 Theorem 2: b = 0.3185 gives 2/3, b = 0.001 gives 0.6725). The gap to (c)'s delta = o(L^{-3/2}) is therefore wider than 'b -> 0'. (5) The Davenport-Heilbronn 'D is of order S_2' claim is unverified. BHB's S_1 and S_2 asymptotics use the Möbius mollifier and do not transfer to DH, so it should be stated as a heuristic only. The rest stands: inequality (*) and theorem (c).

**Prior art found by verifier.** No external prior art found. Searched: arXiv API (simple+zeros+mollifier, simple+zeros+Conrey, the 'simple zeros'/'critical line' combinations; hits include 1806.01959, 2511.20059, 2603.28104, 2501.14545, none of them with an off-line defect for CGG moments) and web. Internal: research_programs.md CRIT item 8 (l.65) already names an 'RH-free version of the CGG/BHB discrete moments' as a parallel lemma. The BHB DB record (papers_compact, research_potential) notes that RH enters only at S_2 and suggests a zero-density version. P1 sharpens both: S_1 and S_2 are already unconditional, and the whole obstruction is D. The pair-correlation analogue is BGST 2501.14545 and Goldston-Suriajaya 2603.28104.


## CON-0011 — Rank–trace/inertia certificate (2026 critical-line machinery) transplanted to an individual GL(2) L-function: exact bandwidth barrier for simple zeros, and a 41.7% distinct-zero target

*Mechanism:* M3-transplantation · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 4/5 · *Difficulty:* 3/5

**Source statements.**
- Alpöge–Furman, arXiv:2608.13637v2 (unrefereed; Lean claimed), Theorem A and chain (1.2), pp.1–2: N0s >= (2 − R(psi) − o(1))N; Nd >= ((3 − R(psi))/2 − o(1))N; R(psi_0)=4/3, R(psi_MT)=c_MT^{-1}.
- Alpöge–Furman, Lemma 3.2 (rank–trace inequality r >= 2 tr P + 4 tr Q − 4b − ||P+Q||_HS^2), p.5; Prop. 4.1 (on-line rank-one / off-line signature (1,1) blocks).
- Alpöge–Furman, Remark 6.1, p.12: with L = lambda·l (X=(T/2pi)^lambda), the argument gives 2 − R_lambda(psi), R_lambda(psi_0)=1/lambda+lambda/3.
- Alpöge–Furman §7.2(e), p.12: prime-side moments are available only in the Rudnick–Sarnak range X^k <= T^{2−eps}; §1.4, p.3: 'the inputs ... hold for Davenport–Heilbronn and Epstein zeta functions'.
- Hua–Yang, arXiv:2608.16034v2, §11 eqs (11.1)–(11.2) and Proposition 11.1, p.31: optimal window v*(s)=cos(sqrt2·lambda·s); simple-on-line >= 2 − [1+(lambda/sqrt2)tan(lambda/sqrt2)]/(sqrt2·tan(lambda/sqrt2)); distinct >= 3/2 − [same]/(2sqrt2·tan(lambda/sqrt2)), for 0<lambda<1 (prime-modulus Dirichlet family).
- Milinovich–Ng, arXiv:1306.0854, Theorem 1.1 (under GRH: N_f^s(T) >> T(log T)^{-eps}) and Introduction p.3 (Rudnick–Sarnak gives, under GRH, a positive proportion of zeros of multiplicity <= 2; Farmer: unconditionally a positive proportion of multiplicity <= 3, level one).
- Andersen–Thorner, arXiv:2004.03581: >= 6.96% of zeros of each GL(2) L-function are on the line (archive ledger LED-CRIT-19).
- Barrett–McDonald–Miller–Ryan–Turnage-Butterbaugh–Winsor, arXiv:1410.7765, Theorem 1.3 and Hypothesis A: the Murty–Perelli pair correlation for degree m, used under GRH, with small-gap bounds weakening as m grows.

**Proposed link.** The Alpöge–Furman certificate uses only tr G and ||G||_HS^2 of a Gabor compression of Weil's form, evaluated on the prime side by Montgomery–Vaughan (MV) for frequencies log n with n <= X <= T/2pi. For L(s,f) of a fixed primitive GL(2) form of level q, the zero density at height T is (1/2pi)log(q(T/2pi)^2). The same MV range X ~ T therefore gives normalized bandwidth lambda_f = log(T/2pi)/log(q(T/2pi)^2), which tends to 1/2 from below. The form factor is GUE, F_f(a) = delta + |a| for |a| < 1/2 (Rudnick–Sarnak, Murty–Perelli), so the slope is kappa = 1.
