Substituting lambda = 1/2 into the Remark 6.1 / Hua–Yang formula gives a window-independent no-go for simple-on-line zeros and a positive bound for distinct zeros.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Normalization, checked numerically. My functional R_lambda(psi) = (||psi||^2/lambda + lambda∫∫|x−y|psi(x)psi(y))/(∫psi)^2 reproduces Alpöge–Furman's 2/3, 5/6, 0.6725007036794 and 0.8362503518397 exactly. Its Euler–Lagrange minimizer cos(sqrt2·lambda·x) gives R*(lambda) = lambda/2 + (1/sqrt2)cot(lambda/sqrt2), identical to Hua–Yang Prop. 11.1. An independent discretized QP (n = 800) agrees to 2e-6 at lambda = 1, 3/4, 1/2 and 0.45. (2) Analytic inputs, case by case. The pairing rho -> 1−conj(rho) holds for every f, so G is real symmetric. N_f(t,t+1) << log(qt) holds. Stirling holds for Gamma(s+(k−1)/2). The Chebyshev bound sum|Lambda_f(n)| << X follows from Deligne |Lambda_f| <= 2Lambda; for Maass forms use Rankin–Selberg plus Kim–Sarnak 7/64 for prime powers. The Mertens analogue sum_{n<=X}|Lambda_f(n)|^2/n = (1/2)log^2 X + O(log X) follows from the Rankin–Selberg PNT. The MV error sum|Lambda_f(n)|^2·n << X log X is o(T log^2 X) for X <= T/2pi, as for zeta. The local terms lambda_f(p^2) − 1 contribute a bounded deterministic part. (3) Range. lambda < 1/2 strictly, with deficit O(1/log T), so the limit constant is the lambda = 1/2 value. (4) Quantifiers. f is fixed, T -> infinity, and the statement is liminf over [T,2T].

**New claim.** (A) Proved here, elementary, given the certificate form. For every window psi >= 0 on [−1/2,1/2] and every lambda <= 1/2, 2 − R_lambda(psi) < 0. Proof: Cauchy–Schwarz gives ||psi||^2 >= (∫psi)^2, so R_{1/2} >= 2 + (1/2)M(psi)/(∫psi)^2 > 2, and R_lambda decreases in lambda on (0,1]. The simple-on-line certificate is positive iff lambda > lambda* = 0.5501939647, the root of 2 − lambda/2 = (1/sqrt2)cot(lambda/sqrt2); the flat window needs 3 − sqrt6 = 0.5505103. The distinct certificate (3 − R)/2 is positive iff lambda > 0.3466589. Consequence: the 2026 machinery yields no simple or critical zeros for any individual L-function of degree >= 2. For degree 3 it yields nothing at all (distinct bound −0.0555). This matches Milinovich–Ng, whose GRH result is only T(log T)^{-eps} simple zeros. (B) Conditional on carrying out the transplant. For a fixed primitive holomorphic or Maass newform f, unconditionally, liminf N_{d,f}(T,2T)/N_f(T,2T) >= 11/8 − (1/(2sqrt2))cot(1/(2sqrt2)) = 0.4170180748. This would far exceed what follows from Farmer's multiplicity-<=3 proportion, and it would remove GRH from the Rudnick–Sarnak multiplicity-<=2 remark.

**Already proved.** Alpöge–Furman Theorems A and B for zeta and Dirichlet L-functions are unrefereed and Lean-claimed (Arias de Reyna repaired one boundary step). The Hua–Yang window formula is unrefereed. (A) is a complete elementary argument relative to the certificate form. All constants were computed to 30 digits and cross-checked by QP (scripts listed in notes).

**Conjectural.** (B) requires writing out the GL(2) analogues of Alpöge–Furman Props. 4.1–4.3, 5.4–5.7 and Theorem 5.7; this has not been done. The validity of the zeta argument itself rests on unrefereed work. The identification kappa = 1 for an individual form at lambda < 1/2 is standard (GUE diagonal via Rankin–Selberg) but was not written out here.

**Next step.** Write the GL(2) prime-side second-moment lemma: for X <= T/2pi, ∫|sum_{n<=X} Lambda_f(n) n^{-1/2-itau}|^2 against the Gabor kernel equals the zeta diagonal with Lambda replaced by Lambda_f, plus O(T log X). Handle separately the lambda_f(p^2) − 1 terms and, for Maass forms, the prime-power bounds. Then rerun chain (1.2) with lambda = log(T/2pi)/log(q(T/2pi)^2) and the window cos(x/sqrt2).

**Potential counterexamples.** Epstein zeta for x^2+5y^2 has degree 2 and certified off-line zeros (archive C2-09/C2-10). It is consistent with (A): at degree-2 bandwidth the simple-on-line bound is negative. So Alpöge–Furman §1.4's phrase 'inputs ... hold for Epstein' must not be read as Theorem A holding for Epstein; Epstein also lacks the Lambda-explicit formula. Non-primitive degree-2 functions such as zeta_K = zeta·L(chi) have slope kappa = 2 (a superposition of two GUE sequences). There R = 2.3305, the simple bound is −0.330 and the distinct bound is 0.3348, worse than the trivial 0.418 obtained from the factors separately. Primitivity (slope 1) is therefore load-bearing. Dihedral or CM forms are primitive and give no counterexample.

**Tests.** (i) Zero-side sanity test, non-rigorous. Using LMFDB zeros of L(s,Delta) (level 1, weight 12) at heights about 10^3–10^4, compute (1/N)sum_{gamma,gamma'}|psi-hat(L(gamma−gamma'))|^2/(∫psi)^2 with L = log(T/2pi) and psi = cos(x/sqrt2). The prediction is about 2.166, versus 1.3275 for zeta at L = log(T/2pi). (ii) Re-derive (A) symbolically (done numerically: thresholds.py). (iii) Check that Remark 6.1's distinct analogue (3 − R_lambda)/2 follows from the same rearrangement at lambda < 1; Hua–Yang Prop. 11.1 states it.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** arXiv API: all:"distinct zeros" (62 hits; relevant: Wu 1206.3737/1206.1679 for zeta and Dirichlet; Hua–Yang 2608.16034 and 2609.27808 for Dirichlet families; B. Wang 2609.07918 for short intervals). abs:"simple zeros" AND modular (Booker–Milinovich–Ng 1806.01959). abs:"pair correlation" AND modular (nothing relevant). abs:"critical line" AND newforms (Milinovich–Ng; Baluyot–Chandee–Li 2310.07606, low-lying zeros). au:Alpoge. abs:"Selberg class" AND "pair correlation" (Barrett et al. 1410.7765). abs:inertia AND zeros AND zeta (only Alpöge–Furman). WebSearch: 'positive proportion of distinct zeros L-function modular form pair correlation unconditional' (Milinovich–Ng, de Faveri 2109.15311, Andersen–Thorner, Conrey–Kwan–Lin–Turnage-Butterbaugh 2607.00282). The archive's 2024–2026 scan and the cached follow-ups (2609.02882, 2610.26841, 2610.08965, 2610.24612, 2611.14486) contain no GL(2) application. No source found states (A) or (B). The archive's CRIT 'Open questions' lists the GL(2) extension as open.

**Verifier reasoning.** **Sources match.**
- AF Theorem A, chain (1.2), Lemma 3.2, Prop 4.1 and Remark 6.1 (R_lambda(psi_0) = 1/lambda + lambda/3) are as cited.
- §7.2(e) (Rudnick–Sarnak range) and §1.4 (inputs hold for Davenport–Heilbronn and Epstein) are as cited.
- Hua–Yang §11 gives c_lambda = sqrt2·tan(lambda/sqrt2)/(1 + (lambda/sqrt2)tan(lambda/sqrt2)), which is 1/R*(lambda).
- Milinovich–Ng p.3 says what is claimed: Rudnick–Sarnak gives, under GRH, a positive proportion of zeros of multiplicity <= 2; Farmer gives multiplicity <= 3 at level one.

**Numbers reproduce to 30 digits:**
- R*(1/2) = 2.165963850.
- Simple bound 2 − R* = −0.165964.
- Distinct bound (3 − R*)/2 = 0.417018075, equal to 11/8 − cot(1/(2sqrt2))/(2sqrt2).
- The simple threshold is lambda* = 0.5501939647, and 3 − sqrt6 = 0.5505103 for the flat window.
- The distinct threshold is 0.3466589.

**Part (A) holds within the window class.** Cauchy–Schwarz gives ||psi||^2 >= (∫psi)^2, M >= 0 for psi >= 0, and dR/dlambda = −A/lambda^2 + B <= 0 because B <= 1 <= A. The unconstrained (signed) minimizer is the positive cosine, so signed windows do not help. The bandwidth limit 1/m for degree m is the Murty–Perelli range: F = delta + |alpha| for |alpha| < 1/m, under GRH plus Hypothesis MP.

**Error 1: the target constant in (B).** For R > 2 the AF chain value (3 − R)/2 is not the best bound the same inputs give.
- The transplanted Props 4.1–4.3 give G~ = (P + Q) + E~ with ||E~||_1 << T^{-1/2}.
- rank(P+Q) <= #on-line points + 2·#off-line pairs = N_d(I′). Each off-line pair consists of two distinct zeros rho and 1 − conj(rho) with the same ordinate.
- AF Remark 3.3 gives rank >= (tr)^2/||·||_HS^2, valid for every Hermitian matrix.
- With tr = N(1 + o(1)) and HS^2 = (R + o(1))N, this gives N_d >= (1/R − o(1))N.
- At lambda = 1/2: 1/R* = 0.4616882, against 0.4170181.
- At zeta's lambda = 1, 1/R = 0.753 < 5/6, which is why AF did not need this inequality.

**Error 2: degree 3.** The claim that degree 3 yields nothing at all (−0.0555) is false. 1/R*(1/3) = 0.3214499 > 0. Given the transplant, every primitive degree-m function gets a positive distinct proportion of at least 1/R*(1/m).

**Other checks.**
- The Epstein and zeta_K remarks are consistent. Primitivity, i.e. slope 1, is indeed load-bearing.
- The GL(2) analytic inputs listed (Rankin–Selberg Mertens, the Montgomery–Vaughan range X <= T, the pairing rho <-> 1 − conj(rho)) are standard.
- The transplant of Props 4.1–5.7 itself was not written out by the agent, and I did not write it out either.

**Verifier correction.** (1) Replace the target 0.4170180748 by max{(3 − R)/2, 1/R} at R = R*(1/2), which is 1/R*(1/2) = 0.4616882225. It comes from rank(G~ − E~) >= tr^2/||·||_HS^2 (AF Remark 3.3) together with rank(G~ − E~) <= N_d(I′). For comparison, the GRH-conditional integer-multiplicity bound would be (5 − R*)/6 = 0.4723.

(2) Delete 'for degree 3 it yields nothing at all'. The certificate gives distinct >= 1/R*(1/3) = 0.3214 for degree 3, and >= 1/R*(1/m) > 0 for every degree m. The no-go concerns only simple-on-line and critical zeros.

(3) Present (A) as the known bandwidth-1/m obstruction for the window class. It is not a statement about all bandwidth-1/2 certificates: for those, an analogue of AF's §7.2 ceiling LP at bandwidth 1/2 would be needed.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Das–Ismoilov–Ramos, arXiv:2502.05106, Cor. 8 (from Murty–Perelli, IMRN 1999, under GRH + Hypothesis MP): for primitive degree-m elements of the Selberg class, the window-optimal pair-correlation constant is 1/(2m) + (1/sqrt2)cot(1/(sqrt2·m)) = R*(1/m). Since R*(1/2) > 2, Montgomery-type window arguments give no simple zeros in degree 2; this is (A) in substance.

de Laat–Rolen–Tripp–Wagner, arXiv:1908.04876, Thm 1.2 and Cor 1.3: GRH Montgomery/SDP bounds for Dedekind zeta, giving 45.85% distinct for quadratic fields, with c_n ~ n + 1/3.

The Rudnick–Sarnak multiplicity-<=2 remark is in Milinovich–Ng's introduction.

I found no unconditional AF-type GL(2) result. The archive follow-ups (2609.02882, 2610.24612, 2610.26841, 2611.14486, 2610.08965) are zeta-only.


## CON-0033 — Heat-open RH consequences cannot imply RH: infinite log-concavity of the Taylor coefficients of xi (Liu-Mao 2026) is numerically insensitive to t < 0 and to off-line quadruples

*Mechanism:* M7-negative-synthesis · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 4/5 · *Difficulty:* 4/5

**Source statements.**
- Rodgers-Tao, Forum Math. Pi 8 (2020) e6, Theorem 1 (theorem DB CAND-01803): Lambda >= 0, where H_t(z) = int_0^inf e^{tu^2} Phi(u) cos(zu) du and all zeros of H_t are real iff t >= Lambda. Hence H_t has a non-real zero for every t < 0, unconditionally.
- Liu-Mao, arXiv:2610.04972v1, abstract and Sec. 1: with F(x) = xi(1/2 + sqrt x) = sum lambda_n x^n and lambda_n = xi^{(2n)}(1/2)/(2n)!, the sequence (lambda_n) is strictly infinitely log-concave (L^j lambda_n > 0 for all j >= 1 and n). The proof (table of contents, Secs. 3-5) combines asymptotics for n >= 12000, m <= ceil(log2 n), interval arithmetic for n <= 12000, m <= 14, and a global closure argument.
- Branden, arXiv:0909.1927 (Crelle 2011), as quoted in Liu-Mao Sec. 1: an entire function in the Laguerre-Polya class with nonnegative coefficients has an infinitely log-concave coefficient sequence. Hence RH implies infinite log-concavity of (lambda_n).
- Polya (as quoted in Liu-Mao Sec. 1): RH holds iff F belongs to the Laguerre-Polya class with positive coefficients.
- C2-29 (SP-6): a class of kernels containing Phi and closed under K -> e^{-eps u^2} K cannot have the property 'real-rooted cosine transform', by Rodgers-Tao.

**Proposed link.** Liu-Mao prove a necessary consequence of RH (via Branden). Rodgers-Tao imply that any property P with P(H_t) for all t in a left neighbourhood (-t0, 0] of 0 cannot imply RH, because those H_t are RH-false. I test whether infinite log-concavity is such a 'heat-open' property.

**Compatibility checks.** Normalisation: lambda_k(t) := 8 * 4^k b_k(t)/(2k)!, with b_k(t) = int_0^inf e^{tu^2} Phi(u) u^{2k} du. This follows from H_0(z) = Xi(z/2)/8 and xi(1/2+z) = 8 sum b_k 4^k z^{2k}/(2k)!. Geometric rescaling c^n commutes with L up to c -> c^2, so positivity of L^j does not depend on the scale. The numerical check lambda_0(0) = 0.4971207781883141099 equals xi(1/2). Synthetic xi-tilde = xi * prod_{rho in Q}(1 - s/rho): in x = z^2 the quadruple factor is (x+A)^2 - 4 delta^2 x with A = delta^2 + gamma^2, whose x-roots are complex. Truncation: L^j is applied to the first 121 coefficients, with the length decreasing by one per iteration, so every reported entry depends only on computed coefficients.

**New claim.** (a) Proved here (elementary, checked with exact rationals). In degree 2, (1, b, c) is infinitely log-concave iff b^2/c >= phi^2 = (3+sqrt5)/2 = 2.618..., via the recursion u_{j+1} = u_j^2 - 1 with u_1 = b^2/c - 1 and fixed point phi. Real-rootedness needs b^2/c >= 4. So 1 + 1.7x + x^2 is infinitely log-concave with complex zeros; b^2 = 2.5921 fails at j = 5 and b^2 = 2.6244 passes. (b) Numerical (non-rigorous, mpmath at 300 digits, tabulated Gauss-Legendre with 5376 nodes on [0, 3.2]). For H_t with t in {0, -0.01, -0.1, -1, -10, -100, -1000, -10000}, L^j lambda_n(t) > 0 for all 1 <= j <= 10 and 0 <= n <= 120-j. For xi-tilde with quadruples at 0.6+5i, 0.6+14i, 0.9+5i and 0.99+2i, the same positivity holds; the last factor alone has b^2/c = 3.145, in [phi^2, 4). Failures occur only for unrealistically low configurations: 0.999+0.5i (L^j lambda_1 <= 0 for every j) and 0.999+0.1i (the coefficient itself is negative). (c) Conjecture and obstruction. There is t0 > 0 such that (lambda_n(t)) is strictly infinitely log-concave for all t in (-t0, 0]. If so, then by Rodgers-Tao 'strict infinite log-concavity of (lambda_n)', and every weaker coefficient inequality in the hierarchy (Turan, fixed-degree higher Turan, r-log-concavity), cannot imply RH. That route is closed unless a step uses a feature that H_t lacks for small t < 0, e.g. exact theta modularity.

**Already proved.** Rodgers-Tao (refereed). Branden (refereed). Liu-Mao (unrefereed; partly computer-assisted). Item (a) is elementary and its proof is given above. Item (b) is non-rigorous numerics: script /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-riemann/e62aa4cb-6226-5e37-8f52-ea0fa055746f/scratchpad/m7/ilc_heat2.py (sha256 ec0cdd05f7795f262460b41b7d2054fdafa43f1bb3fe1df9574ba45c2763e6c1) and output ilc_K120_J10.json in the same directory (sha256 526a3c8170b2f0b5ec1cd37f516c66cf85b941050fa39c0984895ce39f6e8078), runtime 59 s. The scratchpad is not durable.

**Conjectural.** Heat-openness of infinite log-concavity for all n. This needs Liu-Mao's Sec. 3 saddle-point estimates redone for the kernel e^{tu^2} Phi(u). The saddle sits at u_n ~ (1/4) log n, so the perturbation exp(t u_n^2) = exp(t (log n)^2/16) varies slowly; whether the iterated log-ratios g_m stay positive uniformly is unproved. Whether the non-real zeros of H_t (for small |t| at heights about exp(c/|t|)) make L^j lambda_n(t) fail at n ~ exp(2c/|t|) is unknown. If they did, infinite log-concavity would be RH-strength rather than heat-open; that outcome would also be interesting.

**Next step.** Redo Liu-Mao Sec. 3 with kernel e^{tu^2} Phi(u) for |t| <= t0 and track the t-dependence of g_0 and g_m. Then use interval arithmetic for n <= 12000, m <= 14 at t = -t0 (python-flint Arb). A success gives an unconditional theorem: infinite log-concavity holds for an RH-false H_t, so it cannot imply RH.

**Potential counterexamples.** Large-n failure for every t < 0 (see conjectural). Note that low-index tests cannot detect high zeros: by C1 horizons, Jensen degree d ~ T^2, so n <= 120 sees only heights of order 10. The numerics therefore show insensitivity at low index, not heat-openness at all indices.

**Tests.** Run (see new_claim (b)). Extension, not run: n <= 2000 and j <= 14 at t in {-1e-3, -1e-2} with Arb enclosures, recording for each t the first (n, j) with L^j lambda_n(t) <= 0, if any.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** arXiv API: 'all:infinitely AND log AND concave AND Laguerre' (found Liu-Mao 2610.04972, Banerjee-Bringmann-Rolen 2609.14295, Branden 0909.1927, Zhao 2409.08085). 'log-concav AND de Bruijn': none. 'Bruijn AND Newman AND Turan': none. McNamara-Sagan (Adv. Appl. Math. 2010; from memory, not re-read) discuss infinite log-concavity versus real-rootedness. C2-29 states the Rodgers-Tao principle for Turan-moment classes. I found nothing applying it to infinite log-concavity or to the 2026 result.

**Verifier reasoning.** Liu-Mao Thm 1.1 is verified: L^m lambda_n > 0 for all m and n, by analysis for n>=12000, interval arithmetic for n<12000 and m<=14, and a closure step via the ratio bound (3+sqrt5)/2. The Branden, Polya and Rodgers-Tao statements are as quoted. Part (a) is correct: the recursion v -> (v-1)^2 has fixed point phi^2. But it is McNamara-Sagan Lemma 2.1 (r0=(3+sqrt5)/2 suffices; Adv. Appl. Math. 2010) plus an elementary necessity check for three terms, and Liu-Mao use that lemma. The normalisation lambda_k(t)=8*4^k b_k(t)/(2k)! and the quadruple factor check out, and 0.99+2i gives b^2/c=3.145. Errors: (1) The claim that the Turán and fixed-degree higher Turán inequalities are 'weaker' than infinite log-concavity is false. Those inequalities (Csordas-Norfolk-Varga, Dimitrov-Lucas, Jensen hyperbolicity) are stated for gamma_n = n! lambda_n and are stronger than log-concavity of lambda_n (Liu-Mao Sec. 1). Infinite log-concavity of lambda does not imply them. Numerical illustration (mpmath, 400 digits): take lambda_n = 1/(n!)^a with a in {0.8, 0.9, 0.95, 0.99}. Then gamma_n=(n!)^{1-a} is strictly log-convex, so the gamma-Turán inequality fails at every n, and by Rolle so does Jensen hyperbolicity of degree >= 2 at all shifts. Yet L^j lambda_n > 0 for all j<=12 and n<=147; a=0.6 fails at j=3. Only r-log-concavity of lambda is genuinely weaker. (2) The numerics in (b) for t<=-1 and n<=120 carry no information. For k << |t|, b_k(t) ~ Phi(0)Gamma(k+1/2)/(2|t|^{k+1/2}), so lambda_k(t) ~ 4 sqrt(pi) Phi(0)/(k!|t|^{k+1/2}). These are the coefficients of exp(x/|t|), which lies in the Laguerre-Polya class, so positivity is forced by the Gaussian regime and says nothing about heat-openness. (3) The substantive claim (c) is an unproven conjecture, and the principle behind it is already known.

**Verifier correction.** Restrict the obstruction to infinite log-concavity of lambda_n and its truncations (r-log-concavity of lambda). Remove the Turán and higher-Turán inequalities, and Jensen hyperbolicity, from the 'weaker' list, and note that all-degree Jensen hyperbolicity is equivalent to RH. Credit (a) to McNamara-Sagan. Describe (b) as low-index numerics in the Gaussian regime. Keep (c) as a conjecture, with the Liu-Mao Sec. 3 redo at kernel e^{tu^2}Phi as the next step.

**Prior art found by verifier.** McNamara-Sagan 2010 (arXiv:0808.1065, Lemma 2.1). Rodgers-Tao 2020 and Tao's blog: proofs of RH must be sharp under the heat flow. Repo C2-29 (SP-6), which states the same principle for Turán-moment classes. Kim-Lee 2022 (J. Korean Math. Soc. 59): large-shift Jensen hyperbolicity holds for all real entire functions of order < 2 with zeros in a strip. Farmer 2022 (Adv. Math. 411, arXiv:2008.07206): Jensen polynomials are not a plausible route to RH.


## CON-0003 — Robin's inequality: a zero-sum tail argument (Platt-Trudgian H) together with Buthe's RH-quality theta range would extend the certified range from log n <= 3e13 (Morrill-Platt) to log n ~ 5e22 unconditionally, and to log n <= 2.169e25 under QRH, where it is capped by the S^2 term

*Mechanism:* M1-hypothesis-matching · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 3/5 · *Difficulty:* 3/5

**Source statements.**
- Robin 1984, Theoreme 1 (RH iff sigma(n) < e^gamma n loglog n for all n>5040) and Section 3, Proposition 1: for consecutive colossally abundant (CA) numbers N <= n <= N', G(n) <= max(G(N),G(N')) (bridge BRG-ROBIN-P4).
- Ramanujan, annotated by Nicolas-Robin (DB CAND-00778): under RH, limsup (sigma(n)/n - e^gamma loglog n) sqrt(log n) <= -e^gamma(2 sqrt2 - 4 - gamma + log 4pi) = -1.393. In W-units the margin is 2(sqrt2-1) - beta = 0.7822 (beta = 0.04619).
- Morrill-Platt, arXiv:1809.10813v4: Theorem 13 (Robin for 5040 < n <= 10^(10^13.11485), by interval-arithmetic CA enumeration); Lemma 5 (|theta(x)-x| <= sqrt x log^2 x/(8pi) for 599<=x<=B=2.169e25 from H=3e12); Lemmas 6-7 (tail integral C1 = int_B^inf (theta-t)(1+log t)/(t^2 log^2 t) dt bounded only via Dusart's pointwise |psi-x| <= 1.388e-10 x).
- Nicolas 2012, arXiv:1202.0729, Lemma 2.1 lower bound: log f(x) >= K(x) - S^2(x)/(x^2 log x), and (2.18).
- Platt-Trudgian 2021: RH for |Im rho| <= H = 3.0002e12.

**Proposed link.** At a CA number N with largest prime x: sigma(N)/N = prod_{p<=x}(1-1/p)^{-1} prod_p(1-p^{-(a_p+1)}) and log N = theta(x)+theta(x_2)+..., with x_2 ~ sqrt(2x). Hence G(N)/e^gamma <= f(x)^{-1} prod(1-p^{-(a_p+1)}) (log theta(x)/loglog N). This needs a LOWER bound for log f(x). Nicolas' Lemma 2.1 gives it with (i) the zero-sum term -(2+W)/(sqrt x log x), handled as in M1-P2 (zeros above H contribute <= x^{Theta-1/2} S_H, S_H = 2.96e-12), and (ii) -S^2/(x^2 log x), which requires |theta(x)-x| <~ 0.4 x^{3/4}, i.e. RH-quality theta bounds at x. Ingredient (ii) is exactly what Morrill-Platt Lemma 5 supplies for x <= B. Morrill-Platt used only pointwise bounds for the tail beyond B, which integrate to log x/(4pi sqrt x) >> the margin 0.78/(sqrt x log x). That is why they could not go beyond computation. The zero-sum route keeps the 1/gamma^2 decay.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Sign bookkeeping: the first-order (theta(x)-x)/(x log x) terms cancel exactly between log theta(x) and the Mertens product; the second-order term has the unfavourable sign for Robin (unlike Nicolas). (2) Margin: 2(sqrt2-1) comes from the prod_{x_2<p<=x}(1-p^{-2}) and the theta(x_2) term in log N, each about sqrt2/(sqrt x log x). It requires RH-quality theta at x_2 ~ sqrt(2x) <= B (fine). (3) Unconditional: x^{1/2} S_H (1+0.13) < ~0.70 gives X ~ 5.4e22 < B, so the S^2 requirement is met. (4) Under H_Theta the zero-tail threshold rises to 2.1e30 (7/8) or 1.9e27 (11/12), but beyond B neither Buthe nor the explicit QRH bound of Fiori-Jaskari (|psi(x)-x| < 0.04934 x^{3/4}log^2 x + 0.02636 x^{7/8}) gives |theta-x| <= 0.4x^{3/4}: x^{1/8} <= 15 fails. A density-based attempt (Chourasiya-Simonic B1=21.77) fails by a factor ~7 at x~3e31. So the QRH range is min(tail threshold, B) = B = 2.169e25. (5) Robin's interpolation (Prop 1) transfers from CA numbers to all n. Morrill-Platt's computation covers x <= 3e13, overlapping the analytic start, where lower-order terms are <= 0.1 of the margin.

**New claim.** (Unconditional, given the refereed inputs Platt-Trudgian H, Buthe/Morrill-Platt Lemma 5, explicit N(T) and the classical zero-free region) Robin's inequality should hold for all 5040 < n <= exp(c*5e22), roughly n <= 10^(10^22.4), against the published 10^(10^13.11). Conditional on H_{7/8} or H_{11/12}: for all 5040 < n <= exp(2.169e25), roughly 10^(10^24.97). Here the binding constraint is the RH-quality theta range B(H), not Theta, so raising H (B ~ H^2 log H) helps more than lowering Theta.

**Already proved.** Morrill-Platt Thm 13 (computation to 10^(10^13.11485)); their Lemma 5 (B); Nicolas' Lemma 2.1 and the explicit-formula identity; Ramanujan/Robin's RH-conditional margin. The extension itself is NOT proved: the numbers are pre-proof estimates from the dominant terms.

**Conjectural.** That all lower-order terms (J1, J2, r_rho with 1/(1-beta) factors, the F_{1/3} term, prime-power corrections in prod(1-p^{-(a_p+1)}) and in log N) fit inside the ~0.08 slack in W-units uniformly for x in [3e13, 5e22]. The QRH part is additionally conditional on unrefereed QRH. For the repo's ROBIN programme this is a bounded-range contribution only: by Robin's Omega-result (BRG-ROBIN-O1), no argument short of RH closes the tail.

**Next step.** Write the explicit inequality G(N) < e^gamma for a CA number with parameter x as: (2 + W_H(x) + E_off(x)) - 2sqrt2 + R(x) < 0, with every term of R(x) bounded explicitly for x >= 3e13. Certify in interval arithmetic (the repo's directed enclosures) with S_H from explicit N(T). Then state the range theorem with exactly two analytic inputs (H, B) besides Morrill-Platt's finite certificate.

**Potential counterexamples.** A miscount of the second-order terms (e.g. if the S^2 coefficient were larger than 1/(2 log x)) would shrink the range but not invalidate the mechanism. A genuine obstruction would be an off-line zero just above H with beta close to 1. The classical zero-free region bounds the r_rho blow-up, but the estimate x^{1/2}S_H already assumes the worst case beta->1, so this is covered.

**Tests.** (i) Reproduce Morrill-Platt's G(N) values at the largest CA numbers they checked and compare with the analytic formula including W_H. The predicted margin (0.8284 - W_H(x))/(sqrt x log x) should match to O(1/log x). (ii) Compute W_H(x) for x in [1e13,1e14] using the first 1e7 zeros to confirm |W_H| < beta numerically. (iii) Sensitivity: recompute the threshold with margin 0.65..0.75 and S_H +-10%.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** arXiv abs:"Robin's inequality" (25 hits through 2611.13606): none uses a partial-RH zero-sum tail. 2611.13606 and 2510.23889 analyse the least CA counterexample structurally; 2503.03159, Axler 2110.13478 and Morrill-Platt 1809.10813 treat t-free families with pointwise Dusart bounds. zbMATH ti:Robin 2019-2026 (Luca-Sole 2025, Nicolas 2024, Morrill-Platt 2021, Washington 2021, 2025 least-CA note): no analytic range extension found. The repo bridge (BRG-ROBIN-P5) lists 10^(10^13.11485) as the current published range. Risk: an unindexed paper or monograph remark may contain the partial-RH argument; Robin 1984 original not accessed.

**Verifier reasoning.** I checked the mechanism. For CA N with largest prime x, the two -sqrt2/(sqrt x log x) terms (prod over x_2<p<=x of (1-p^{-2}), with x_2 ~ sqrt(2x), and theta(x_2) in log N) give the margin 2sqrt2-2-beta = 0.7822. This equals Ramanujan's 2sqrt2-4-gamma+log4pi, so the sign bookkeeping is right. Nicolas Lemma 2.1's lower bound carries the -S^2/(x^2 log x) term at the single point x. Morrill-Platt (full text) Thm 13 is purely computational: Briggs' algorithm in 100-bit interval arithmetic, last CA largest prime 29,996,208,012,611. Their Lemma 8 uses |theta-t| in absolute value, which can never certify a signed margin, so the signed zero-sum route is genuinely different. I recomputed the unconditional threshold as 4.4e22 (margin 0.70, factor 1.13), consistent with ~5e22 and below B, so the S^2 term is covered by Lemma 5 there. Error: the QRH range is NOT capped at B = 2.169e25. The S^2 term only needs |theta(x)-x| <~ 0.3 x^{3/4}. Morrill-Platt Lemma 6 (Dusart) gives |theta(x)-x| <= 1.405e-10 x for log x >= 55. At x = 2.1e30 that is 3e20 against 0.4x^{3/4} = 2.2e22, and S^2/(x^2 log x) is 3.7e-5 of the margin. The term stays harmless up to x ~ 1.6e37. So under H_Theta the range is the zero-tail threshold, about 1.5e30 (7/8) or 1.5e27 (11/12), i.e. n up to about 10^(10^29.8). The claim that the 'binding constraint is B(H), not Theta' is wrong in the QRH regime. The theta(sqrt t) tail for t > B^2 needs the same unconditional treatment as in P2. The claim that all lower-order terms fit in ~0.08 slack from x = 3e13 is unverified; the (2+beta)/log x term alone is 0.066 at log x = 31.

**Verifier correction.** (1) Remove the B cap. Under H_Theta the predicted Robin range is x <= X(H,Theta) ~ (0.62/S_H)^{1/(Theta-1/2)}: about 1.5e30 for 7/8 and 1.5e27 for 11/12. The S^2 term beyond B is handled by Dusart's |theta-x| <= 1.405e-10 x (Morrill-Platt Lemma 6), which is safe up to ~1e37. (2) Keep the unconditional claim (x up to ~4-5e22, i.e. n <= 10^(10^22.3)) as an unproved estimate, pending an explicit R(x) bound from x0 = 3e13. (3) Add the unconditional tail bound for psi-theta at t > B^2. (4) Say that Morrill-Platt's 10^(10^13.11) is a computation, not an analytic barrier.

**Prior art found by verifier.** None found. arXiv abs:"Robin's inequality" (25 hits through 2611.13606) and abstracts of 2005.09307, 2510.23889, 2611.13606, 2609.26787, 2610.12816 and 1602.03384: none extends the verified range analytically via partial RH. A web search found only Morrill-Platt 10^(10^13.11485) as the current range.


## CON-0007 — Graded criteria: routes whose missing lemma at level omega is exactly a zero-free half-plane, and what the unrefereed quasi-RH claims would give each one (including a threshold obstruction for Báez-Duarte)

*Mechanism:* M2-missing-lemmas · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 3/5 · *Difficulty:* 2/5

**Source statements.**
- Suzuki, 'Aspects of the screw function corresponding to the Riemann zeta-function', arXiv:2206.03682, Sec. 11, eq. (11.1): Psi_omega(t) := e^{-omega t} Psi(t) + 2 omega int_0^t e^{-omega u} Psi(u) du + omega^2 int_0^t (t-u) e^{-omega u} Psi(u) du. Theorem 11.1: xi(s) != 0 for Re s > 1/2 + omega iff Psi_omega(t) >= 0 for all t >= t_0, for some t_0. Text before Thm 11.1: a zero-free half-plane gives Psi_omega >= 0 for all t. Psi_omega >= 0 unconditionally for omega >= 1/2. DB: CAND-01970 'Theorems 4.1, ..., 11.1'.
- Suzuki 2025, 'Screw functions of Dirichlet series in the extended Selberg class', Corollary 1.1 (DB: CAND-02381): eventual nonnegativity of Re(-g_F) implies no zeros in Re s > 1 - delta.
- Báez-Duarte, arXiv:math/0202141v2, Lemma 2.1 (Balazard–Saias): if zeta(s) != 0 for Re s > alpha (1/2 <= alpha < 1), then sum_{a<=n} mu(a) a^{-s} = 1/zeta(s) + O(n^{-delta/3}(1+|tau|)^eps) for alpha + delta <= Re s <= 1. Lemma 2.2: |zeta(1/2 - eps + i tau)/zeta(1/2 + eps + i tau)| <= C(1+|tau|)^eps for eps <= eps_0 < 1/4. Sec. 2.2: the majorant (1+|tau|)^{-1+2 eps} is used and needs 0 < eps < 1/4. Sec. 3: 'One should extend it to the L^p case, that is, to the condition that zeta(s) does not vanish in a half-plane Re(s) > 1/p.' DB: CAND-01182.
- Beurling (1955, PNAS 41, 'A closure problem related to the Riemann zeta-function'), classical, recalled and not opened here: for 1 < p < infinity, zeta(s) != 0 for Re s > 1/p iff span{rho_theta : 0 < theta <= 1} is dense in L^p(0,1).
- Repository bridge BRG-MELLIN_WAVELET-O3 (connections/repository_bridge.md): for theta in (0,1/2), negative mass int_1^Y F_- dx/x << Y^{theta+eps} for a fixed noncancelling detector excludes zeros with Re rho > 1/2 + theta. Conversely, a zero-free half-plane Re s > 1/2 + theta gives |F(X)| << X^{theta+eps} (Titchmarsh 14.2-type). Marked there as an analyst derivation needing review.
- Polymath 2019 Theorem 3.2 (de Bruijn [Thm 13]) (DB: CAND-01890): if H_{t_0} has no zeros with |Im z| > y_0, then Lambda <= t_0 + y_0^2/2. Normalisation: H_0(z) = xi(1/2 + iz/2)/8, so rho = 1/2 + delta + i gamma corresponds to z = 2 gamma - 2 i delta.
- Lagarias 2007, Li coefficients for automorphic L-functions, Theorem 1.1 and Theorem 6.1 (DB: CAND-01219): lambda_n = (N/2) n log n + C_1 n - lambda_n(sqrt n, pi) + O(sqrt n log n). The incomplete Li sum involves only zeros with |gamma| < sqrt n, so Li is height-weighted, not graded by distance from the line.
- Griffin–Ono–Rolen–Thorner–Tripp–Wagner 2022, Theorem 1.2 (DB: CAND-01931): RH_m(T) and d <= floor(T)^2 imply J^{d,n} hyperbolic for n >= m. Jensen is also height-weighted.

**Proposed link.** Sort the RH routes by whether their criterion depends on the horizontal abscissa of the zeros (graded) or on their height (height-weighted). Graded: Suzuki Psi_omega (de Branges/screw route); Beurling's L^p Nyman–Beurling; Mertens/negative-mass detectors (the host repository's MELLIN programme); the de Bruijn strip width; the Robin/Nicolas Omega exponents (Robin 1984 via Lagarias Prop. 3.2: violations of relative size (log n)^{-beta} with beta > 1 - sup Re rho). For each graded route, the level-omega version of the missing lemma is equivalent to, or a direct consequence of, the zero-free half-plane Re s > 1/2 + omega. So no route-internal lemma is weaker than the corresponding quasi-RH statement, and the quasi-RH claims (7/8: omega = 3/8; 11/12: omega = 5/12), if verified, settle the graded versions at these levels all at once. Height-weighted routes (Li, Jensen, Weil windows, the d_N rates) gain nothing from a half-plane beyond the trivial bound delta <= 3/8.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Suzuki: Theorem 11.1 is stated for xi (zeta), with omega real. The 7/8 claim is stated for zeta (the Lean Comparator statement is riemannZeta s != 0 for 7/8 < Re s), so omega = 3/8 matches and gives Psi_{3/8} >= 0 on all of R (not just eventually), by the remark before Thm 11.1. (2) Beurling L^p: 7/8 = 1/p gives p = 8/7; 11/12 gives p = 12/11. The density is in L^p(0,1) for the continuous family 0 < theta <= 1, not the natural-number subfamily. (3) Báez-Duarte natural strengthening: his L^2 argument needs eps < 1/4, because the majorant (1+|tau|)^{-1+2 eps} must be square-integrable. It also needs Lemma 2.1 at Re s = 1/2 + eps >= alpha + delta and Lindelöf at abscissa 1/2 + eps, which follows from a zero-free half-plane only to its right. So a 'graded natural NB in L^2' via this argument needs alpha < 3/4. The 7/8 and 11/12 claims do NOT reach it, and Liu (arXiv:2610.12234, Sec. 34, per frontiers/research_programs.md ZFR) proves 0.874957 is optimal within the 7/8 comparison system. This compatibility check came out negative. (4) de Bruijn: delta <= 3/8 gives a strip |Im z| <= 3/4 and Lambda <= 9/32 = 0.28125, weaker than the proven 0.2. The 7/8 claim helps only inside Polymath-type criteria (Hakimoglu-Brown 0.13963, unrefereed), consistent with the DBN frontier entry. (5) MELLIN O3: the converse direction (half-plane gives |F(X)| << X^{theta+eps}) needs 1/zeta(s) << |t|^eps on Re s >= 1/2 + theta + eps. That follows from a zero-free half-plane by the Borel–Carathéodory/Hadamard argument of Titchmarsh 14.2. It is standard but should be written out for the repository's 1/s^2-smoothed rows.

**New claim.** (a) The graded missing lemmas coincide: for omega in (0, 1/2), the statements 'Psi_omega >= 0 eventually' (Suzuki), 'span dense in L^{1/(1/2+omega)}(0,1)' (Beurling), 'negative mass << Y^{omega+eps} for the repository's fixed detectors' (MELLIN, if O3 holds), and 'zero-free half-plane Re s > 1/2 + omega' are mutually equivalent or implied by the last. So none of these routes has a route-internal missing lemma weaker than quasi-RH at that level. (b) Conditional on the unrefereed OpenAI 7/8 theorem: Psi_{3/8}(t) >= 0 for all t; Beurling density holds in L^{8/7}(0,1); the MELLIN graded premise holds at theta = 3/8 + eps; the bridge statement O3 ('no one has ever established theta < 1/2') would need updating. These would be the first omega < 1/2 instances of each criterion. (c) Threshold obstruction: Báez-Duarte's natural-integer criterion, as proved, cannot be graded beyond alpha < 3/4. The missing lemma he proposed (natural-integer L^p version: zeta != 0 on Re s > 1/p implies chi in the L^p-closure of span{rho_a : a in N}, for 1 < p < 2) is genuinely weaker than RH and not found in the literature searched. With a verified 7/8 it would give an unconditional natural-NB statement at p = 8/7. (d) Li and Jensen criteria are height-weighted. Lagarias' Thm 6.1 shows lambda_n sees only zeros below sqrt n; GORTTW Thm 1.2 shows degree d sees height sqrt d. So a half-plane changes nothing in their finite horizons (C1 campaign Theorem L1 and C1-J1).

**Already proved.** Suzuki Thm 11.1 (2022/23); Beurling 1955 (classical); Báez-Duarte Thm 1.1 and Lemmas 2.1-2.2; de Bruijn Thm 13; Lagarias 2007 Thms 1.1 and 6.1; GORTTW Thm 1.2. The consequences in (b) are immediate from these, given QRH.

**Conjectural.** The QRH inputs themselves (OpenAI 7/8 and 11/12: unrefereed, Lean replay not done here). The natural-integer L^p strengthening in (c). Whether Báez-Duarte's L^2 argument can be pushed to eps in [1/4, 1/2) with a sharper error term than Lemma 2.1's O(n^{-delta/3}(1+|tau|)^eps). The MELLIN O3 equivalence for the repository's exact detector class.

**Next step.** (1) Prove the natural-integer L^p strengthening for 1 < p < 2, conditional on a zero-free half-plane Re s > 1/p. In L^p the Mellin–Plancherel step is lost; try Hausdorff–Young for the forward direction and Báez-Duarte's eps-summability with an L^p multiplier theorem for zeta(1/2 - eps + i tau)/zeta(1/2 + eps + i tau). (2) Write the Titchmarsh 14.2 step for the MELLIN rows and propose a correction to bridge item O3 to the repository's own review (no change to integrated status). (3) Record in the frontier ledger that QRH would make Psi_{3/8} >= 0 and L^{8/7}-Beurling density theorems, gated on QRH verification.

**Potential counterexamples.** None for (a), which consists of equivalences already in the sources. For (c): if the L^p natural strengthening is false for some p < 2, a counterexample would be a zero-free half-plane situation in which the natural span misses chi in L^p. Beurling-type systems with N(x) = Ax + O(x^{1/2+eps}) and zeros near sigma = 1 (Diamond–Montgomery–Vorhauer; BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-O7) are natural test cases for the converse direction.

**Tests.** (i) Numerically evaluate Psi_omega(t) for omega in {3/8, 5/12, 1/4} on t in [0, 40] from formula (1.1) with primes up to e^40, with interval arithmetic. Positivity on a finite range is consistency evidence only, not a proof (Suzuki Thm 11.1 needs all t >= t_0). (ii) Check the (1+|tau|)^{-1+2 eps} majorant numerically for eps in {0.2, 0.3} at tau up to 10^4 to illustrate the 1/4 threshold.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** Suzuki 2206.03682 full text, Sec. 11. Báez-Duarte math/0202141 full text (Sec. 3 states the L^p extension as an open suggestion). arXiv API: abs:Nyman AND abs:Beurling (40 most recent). Relevant prior art: Alouges–Darses–Hillion arXiv:2006.02953 (sigma_0-graded sufficient conditions for zero-free strips 1/2 < sigma <= sigma_0); Delaunay–Fricain–Mosaki–Robert arXiv:1101.1199 (Beurling–Nyman criteria for zero-free discs and Siegel zeros); Báez-Duarte arXiv:math/0205003 (rate (log log n)^{-1/3} under RH). No paper found proving the natural-integer L^p version. Beurling 1955 recalled, not opened. zbMATH not queried.

**Verifier reasoning.** I checked Suzuki 2206.03682 Sec. 11 verbatim. Theorem 11.1: for real omega, xi != 0 on Re s > 1/2+omega iff Psi_omega >= 0 for all t >= t_0. The preceding text says a zero-free half-plane gives Psi_omega >= 0 for all t (via class N and Krein-Langer Satz 5.9). The following text says Psi_omega >= 0 unconditionally for omega >= 1/2. Beurling's L^p theorem is confirmed as Theorem A in Dimitrov-Oliveira (1608.07887), in the form with theta_k >= 1 and the restriction sum b_k/theta_k = 0. The 7/8 Comparator statement is about riemannZeta (recent_developments l.59), so omega = 3/8 and p = 8/7 are the correct translations. The Báez-Duarte Lemma 2.1/2.2 statements match the DB, and the eps < 1/4 square-integrability arithmetic is right. The de Bruijn arithmetic is right: delta <= 3/8 gives |Im z| <= 3/4 and Lambda <= 9/32.

Parts (a), (b) and (d) are sound, but they are immediate corollaries of known equivalences (Beurling 1955; Suzuki Thm 11.1; Lagarias Thm 6.1; GORTTW Thm 1.2), so their content is known.

Part (c) is the only novelty claimed, and it misses prior art. Dimitrov-Oliveira, arXiv:1608.07887, Theorems 1 and 2 treat p in (1,2] and any Dirichlet L-function, zeta included (principal character, q=1). They prove that L(s,chi) != 0 on Re s > 1/p iff d_n(L,p) -> 0, where d_n is the L^2(0,infinity) distance from 1_(0,1) to span{kappa(1/(kx)) : k <= n} with NATURAL k and kappa(x) = beta x^alpha - sum_{k<=x} chi(k)k^{1/2-1/p}. This is a graded natural-integer Báez-Duarte criterion covering every abscissa 1/p in [1/2,1). So the '3/4 threshold obstruction' belongs only to Báez-Duarte's 2003 proof route (the (1+|tau|)^{-1+2eps} majorant), not to graded natural-integer criteria. Báez-Duarte, IJMMS 31 (2002) 387-406, also gives L^p (1<p<infinity) versions with Möbius-built approximants. With a verified 7/8 (p = 8/7), D-O already gives d_n(zeta, 8/7) -> 0. The specific L^p-norm statement with rho_a in L^p might still be open, but it is not the only 'natural-integer graded' form.

Secondary issue: the 'Liu 0.874957 optimal' citation is stale. The frontier critique (research_programs.md l.412) records claims down to 0.8740585 and says the 'optimal within the comparison system' results have been bypassed repeatedly. The same critique records a second formalized quasi-RH: Cureton-Thorner 31/32 (Lean, AxiomProver). That gives omega = 15/32 instances (Psi_{15/32} >= 0; Beurling density in L^{32/31}) without depending on the OpenAI manuscripts.

**Verifier correction.** (1) Present (a), (b) and (d) as immediate corollaries of Beurling 1955 and Suzuki Thm 11.1 conditional on QRH, not as new results. (2) Replace (c). Say instead that Báez-Duarte's 2003 L^2 argument stops at alpha < 3/4, but graded natural-integer criteria for every 1/p in (1/2,1) already exist (Dimitrov-Oliveira 1608.07887 Thms 1-2; see also Báez-Duarte IJMMS 2002). Under QRH 7/8 they give d_n(zeta, 8/7) -> 0 at once. What remains open, if anything, is the exact L^p-norm version with rho_a(x) = {1/(ax)}; it should be stated against D-O. (3) Add the restriction sum b_k/theta_k = 0 (or the theta_k >= 1 normalisation) to the Beurling statement. (4) Update the quasi-RH status: claimed exponents now go below 0.8749. Add Cureton-Thorner 31/32 (formalized; not refereed) as an independent omega = 15/32 input.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Dimitrov & Oliveira, 'An extremal problem related to generalizations of the Nyman-Beurling and Báez-Duarte criteria', arXiv:1608.07887 (2016), Theorems 1-2 (graded natural-integer criterion for Re s > 1/p, p in (1,2], Dirichlet L incl. zeta). Báez-Duarte, 'New versions of the Nyman-Beurling criterion for the Riemann hypothesis', IJMMS 31 (2002) 387-406 (L^p versions). Beurling, PNAS 41 (1955) (as Theorem A in D-O). Suzuki arXiv:2206.03682 Thm 11.1 and its remarks.


## CON-0010 — Route-by-route ledger of missing lemmas: exact statement, RH-equivalent vs genuinely weaker, and the restricted version already proved

*Mechanism:* M2-missing-lemmas · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 3/5 · *Difficulty:* 1/5

**Source statements.**
- Weil/explicit formula: Bombieri 2000 Thm 2 (RH iff T[f*f^*] >= 0 on C_0^inf(0,inf)); Thm 8 (negative index of H(Gamma;t) = number of conjugate off-line pairs); Thm 12 (Yoshida: positivity for support length < log 2) (DB CAND-00816). Connes–Consani 2021 Thm 1 (W_inf positivity, support [2^{-1/2}, 2^{1/2}], hat g(+-i/2) = 0) (CAND-01965). Connes 2021 Conjecture 4.1 (semi-local S = {inf} union {p < q} suffices for support (q^{-1/2}, q^{1/2})) (CAND-02189). Zhu arXiv:2608.24827 Thm 1.2 (certified Q(f) >= 8.9e-18 ||f||^2, supp f in [-0.8, 0.8]), Thm 1.3 (under RH lambda*(L) <= exp(-L e^L)), Thm 1.4 (certificate barrier T_1(L) = 2 pi e^{A_L}, A_L ~ 4e^L), Sec. 9 (resolution height T*(L) = 2 pi e^{2L}).
- Hilbert–Pólya/operator: Connes 2026 (CAND-02653) Thm 6.1 (Connes–van Suijlekom: simple isolated even lowest eigenvalue implies all zeros of hat eta real), Sec. 5 and 6.6 programme (simplicity plus eta_x -> Xi uniformly on compacts, then Hurwitz gives RH), Fact 6.4 (hat k_lambda -> Xi with error <= c lambda^{-1/2-alpha}). Connes 2023 Thm 6.4 (zeta-cycle spectra are critical-line ordinates) (CAND-02085). Yakaboylu 2024 Eqs (12)-(18) (self-adjointness weight assumed, not proved) (CAND-02425).
- de Branges/canonical systems: Suzuki 2023 Thms 1.3, 1.4 (RH iff G_g[a] positive/non-degenerate for every a), Thm 4.2 (positive definite for a < a_0), Thm 4.1 (Psi > 0 on (0, t_0), t_0 > log 2) (CAND-01970). Conrey–Li IMRN 2000 (de Branges positivity conditions fail for zeta and chi_{-4}; bridge BRG-XI_PICK-O5).
- Li/Keiper: Lagarias 2007 Sec. 1 (Re lambda_n >= 0 for all n iff RH), Thm 1.1 (if RH fails, the incomplete term is exponentially large) (CAND-01219). Campaign C1 THEOREM L1 (RH up to T, T >= 100, implies lambda_n > 0 for n <= T^2).
- Jensen/Laguerre–Pólya: GORZ 2019 Thm 1 (each d, n large) and Thm 2 (d <= 8, all n; computational) (CAND-01793). GORTTW 2022 Thm 1.1 (n >= c e^{d/2}) and Thm 1.2 (RH_m(T) and d <= T^2 give all n >= m) (CAND-01931).
- Nyman–Beurling: Báez-Duarte 2003 Thm 1.1 and Cor. 3.1 (CAND-01182); campaign C1 THEOREM N1 (floor ||chi - f||^2 >= F ~ 4 delta/T^2).
- Robin/Lagarias: Nicolas 2012 Cor. 1.1 (c(n) bounds equivalent to RH) (CAND-01199). Bridge BRG-ROBIN-P10 (Robin for t-free n: t <= 20 Morrill–Platt, t = 21 Axler Thm 1.2); BRG-ROBIN-O1 (unbounded tail is RH); BRG-ROBIN-O4 (t-free method stalls on explicit Mertens bounds).
- Levinson-type: frontiers CRIT 5(5),(7): Radziwill Thm 1 (I(M_theta) >= c/theta); Farmer theta = inf implies 100%, and Bettin–Gonek show it implies RH. Feng 2012 Cor. 1 (theta = 1 gives only 0.6107) (CAND-01232).
- Function-field transfer: Connes 2019 (CAND-02083) eqs (12)-(14): the strategy needs a Riemann–Roch formula on the square of the scaling site, explicitly 'not available'.
- Host programmes: bridge BRG-OPERATORS_HEAT-O1/O2 (doubly exponential envelope cost, collapsing margins); BRG-XI_PICK-O7 (Sra Thm C.11: PF_k with N(Y) <= k+1 implies RH to height Y); BRG-MELLIN_WAVELET-O3 (graded equivalence).

**Proposed link.** Each route's closing statement is set against the strongest restricted version in the sources and classified. (A) Weil: closing = T[f*f^*] >= 0 for every support (RH-equivalent). Each finite-window statement is a finite arithmetic statement (prime powers up to e^{2L}), proved for L <= 0.8 (Zhu, certified) and for the archimedean part at sqrt2 (Connes–Consani). Uniformity in L cannot come from finite certificates (Zhu Thm 1.4). Connes' Conj. 4.1 is the genuinely weaker per-q family. (B) Hilbert–Pólya (Connes 2026): (H1) simplicity of the lowest eigenvalue for all large lambda is genuinely weaker; its restricted version is Zhu's certified simplicity at support 1.6. (H2) eta_lambda -> Xi on compacts carries RH: if RH fails, the lowest eigenvalue is eventually negative and the eigenvector concentrates on the off-line pair (Bombieri Thm 10), so (H2) fails. Restricted analogue: Fact 6.4 for the explicit k_lambda. The quantitative bridge between them is a Davis–Kahan estimate: dist(k_lambda, C eta_lambda)^2 <= (Q(k_lambda) - eps_1)/(eps_2 - eps_1), plus Paley–Wiener on compacts. This is RH-strength in the limit; at a single lambda it yields only a finite-region verification. Yakaboylu: the missing self-adjoint weight would itself imply RH. (C) de Branges/screw: closing = non-degeneracy for all a (RH-equivalent); restricted to a < a_0; the graded Psi_omega version is in P2. (D) Li: closing and eventual positivity are both RH-equivalent; restricted versions are finite horizons (L1). (E) Jensen: every fixed-d statement is subsumed by verification (d <= 9.0e24 from Platt–Trudgian via GORTTW Thm 1.2), including GORZ's computational d <= 8. Only uniformity in d remains, and it is RH-equivalent. (F) Nyman–Beurling: RH-equivalent; graded via Beurling L^p (P2); finite d_N carries no information beyond its floor (C1 N1/N2). (G) Robin: closing is RH-equivalent; the genuinely weaker infinite family is t-free n, proved for t <= 21; the next missing lemma is t = 22, which needs better explicit Mertens/theta bounds. (H) Levinson: 100% via arbitrarily long mollifiers is RH-hard (Farmer/Bettin–Gonek); genuinely weaker is theta > 4/7 for Conrey/Feng coefficients, which pays only for theta well above 1 (Feng Cor. 1); RH-free CGG is P1. (I) de Bruijn–Newman: P3 plus the explicit canopy lemma (Polymath Thm 1.5(i) with explicit C), already identified in the DBN frontier. (J) Function field: the missing 2-dimensional Riemann–Roch/Hodge-index on the square of the scaling site would imply RH (at least RH-strength); no restricted 2-dimensional version is in the DB.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Zhu normalisation: f supported in [-L, L], hat f of exponential type L, autocorrelation support 2L. 'Support 1.6' means L = 0.8. Bombieri/Yoshida use the support length |I| < log 2 of F. Do not compare Zhu's L with log M across papers without halving or doubling. (2) Connes 2026 Thm 6.1 needs simplicity, isolation and evenness. Zhu's odd-sector computation is what supplies evenness at support 1.6. (3) GORTTW Thm 1.2 needs RH_m(T) for xi^{(m)}. For m = 0 this is RH(T), which Platt–Trudgian give with T = 3.0e12, so d <= 9.0e24 (matches C1-J1). (4) Robin t-free: Axler's t = 21 uses improved explicit Mertens products; the bridge records the method stalling (O4). (5) Bridge items are marked 'verifier: not verified' and are used here as pointers only.

**New claim.** Classification. RH-equivalent closing lemmas, with no weaker route-internal version: Weil (all windows), de Branges (all a), Li (all n or eventually), Jensen (all d), NB (L^2), Robin (all n), Connes (H2), the Levinson theta = inf mollifier. Genuinely weaker but finite-horizon, i.e. subsumed by RH(3e12) or uninformative: fixed-window Weil positivity at small L, fixed-d Jensen, Li n <= 9e24, finite d_N, Robin up to 10^(10^13.11). Genuinely weaker infinite statements (the useful targets): Connes Conj. 4.1 per q; Connes (H1) for all large lambda; Robin t-free for t = 22, 23, ...; Levinson theta > 4/7; ML-CGG (P1); the graded half-plane lemmas (P2), including the natural-integer L^p NB; the explicit Polymath canopy lemma and the persistence lemma (P3); Alpöge–Furman bandwidth > 1 (an RH-free third trace or pair correlation on 1 < |alpha| <= 1 + delta, per the CRIT frontier).

**Already proved.** All restricted versions listed in the source statements (refereed or certified as marked). Zhu's certificates are unrefereed, computer-assisted and certified by the author. Connes 2026 is a survey; Thm 6.1 is cited from Connes–van Suijlekom.

**Conjectural.** Connes Conj. 4.1; (H1) for all large lambda; the Davis–Kahan route from (H1) plus an upper bound on Q(k_lambda) to (H2); Robin t = 22 with current explicit inputs.

**Next step.** (1) In the Connes programme, compute Q_{W_lambda}(k_lambda)/||k_lambda||^2 on the geometric side with interval arithmetic for log lambda <= 0.8 (Zhu's range) and compare with Zhu's certified eps_1, eps_2. This tests whether the Davis–Kahan bridge is numerically viable at all; expect failure because the margins collapse doubly exponentially. (2) Robin t = 22: identify which explicit Mertens/theta inequality (Axler Lemma 2.4 / Dusart 2018) must improve, and by how much. (3) Add this ledger to frontiers/research_programs.md as a cross-route 'missing-lemma' table, gated for review.

**Potential counterexamples.** Weil and Connes routes: Davenport–Heilbronn and Epstein zeta satisfy finite-window analogues and Alpöge–Furman-type inputs but violate RH (CRIT 5(4)). So any finite-window or bandwidth-one lemma must be checked against them; a lemma true for DH cannot carry RH content. de Branges: Conrey–Li show stronger-than-RH positivity conditions fail.

**Tests.** For each 'genuinely weaker' item, run the DH/Epstein test: does the proposed lemma also hold for Davenport–Heilbronn? If yes, it cannot close the route (it may still give proportions, as Alpöge–Furman do).

**Prior-art search (proposer).** Compiled from the theorem DB (141 papers; 1499 statements), the paper-level limitations/research-potential fields, the frontier ledger and research programmes, the repository bridge and campaign C1. External: Zhu 2608.24827 full text (Secs. 1, 9, 11). No new external search for this ledger beyond P1-P4.

**Verifier reasoning.** I spot-checked the cited statements against the DB, and they match. Bombieri Thms 2, 8 and 12 (Yoshida, |I| < log 2). Connes-Consani Thm 1 (support [2^{-1/2}, 2^{1/2}], hat g(+-i/2) = 0). Connes 2026: Thm 6.1 (simple, isolated, even lowest eigenvalue), Fact 6.4 (error c lambda^{-1/2-alpha}(1-2alpha)^{-1}) and the Sec. 5/6.6 programme. Connes Conj. 4.1. Feng Cor. 1 (theta = 1 gives 0.6107). Suzuki Thms 1.3, 1.4, 4.1 and 4.2. GORTTW Thm 1.2, where d <= (3e12)^2 = 9e24 matches (GORTTW's own 9.36e20 is (3.06e10)^2). Radziwill and Farmer/Bettin-Gonek per CRIT 5(5),(7). Robin t <= 21. The 'Davis-Kahan' bound is the elementary spectral-gap inequality 1 - |<k,eta>|^2 <= (Q(k) - eps_1)/(eps_2 - eps_1) for unit k. It is correct, though Temple-type rather than Davis-Kahan.

The classification is mostly sound but largely repeats what the frontier ledger already says. research_programs.md l.247 already sorts unconditional results into three regimes (archimedean-only windows; corollaries of finite verification such as Jensen d <= T^2 and Li n <= T^2; ...). CRIT l.59 covers the Levinson and AF caps, and l.102 the DBN converse.

Errors to fix. (i) Zhu is labelled 'certified'. The frontier critique (l.236) says the lower-bound matrix is 50-digit non-interval mpmath with a priori bounds and a floating-point Cholesky residual, i.e. 'high precision plus error budget, not a fully directed-rounding certificate'. AGENTS.md forbids calling this certified. (ii) The Li horizon is credited to 'C1 THEOREM L1'. The C1 skeptic review (SKEPTIC_REVIEW.md l.208) attributes it to Oesterlé (2000, via Voros), and the frontier (l.262) notes that no complete published proof has been located. (iii) Bombieri Thm 10 assumes finitely many (at least one) off-line zeros, so it does not show by itself that (H2) fails whenever RH fails. The clean statement is that (H1) and (H2) together imply RH (Thm 6.1 plus Hurwitz). (iv) Items inherited from P1 and P2 carry their corrections: the ML-CGG thresholds, and prior art (Dimitrov-Oliveira) for the natural-integer graded NB 'useful target'. (v) The quasi-RH optimum numbers are stale.

I did not verify the claim that fixed-window Weil positivity at small L is 'subsumed by RH(3e12)'. It depends on Zhu's resolution-height statements, which I did not check.

**Verifier correction.** (1) Relabel Zhu 2608.24827 as 'unrefereed high-precision computation with an error budget (not directed rounding / not certified)', both for L = 0.8 positivity and for the simple even ground state. (2) Attribute 'RH(T) implies lambda_n > 0 for n <= T^2' to Oesterlé (2000, unpublished, via Voros), with C1 as an independent proof, and record that no complete published proof has been located. (3) For Connes (H2), say that (H1) and (H2) imply RH (Thm 6.1 plus Hurwitz). Cite Bombieri Thm 10 only with its finitely-many-off-line-zeros hypothesis. (4) Move 'natural-integer L^p NB' out of 'not in the literature': Dimitrov-Oliveira 1608.07887 already gives a graded natural-integer criterion for every 1/p in (1/2,1). (5) Carry over the P1 threshold fix: D <= eta TL^3 with eta < 0.0131 (to beat 0.6725) or eta < 0.0091 (to beat 0.6818). (6) Mark the 'fixed-window Weil subsumed by RH(3e12)' item as unverified. (7) Cross-reference research_programs.md l.247, l.59 and l.102 rather than present the classification as new.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Internal: research_programs.md l.247 (three regimes that miss RH), l.59 (CRIT negative results 1-8), l.102 and l.262-263 (open converses), l.236 (Zhu certification caveat); C1 REPORT/SKEPTIC_REVIEW (Oesterlé attribution). External: Dimitrov-Oliveira arXiv:1608.07887; Bettin-Gonek, Mathematika 2017 (theta = infinity implies RH); Conrey-Li, IMRN 2000 (failure of de Branges positivity).


## CON-0012 — Petersson orthogonality replacing character orthogonality: Hua–Yang/Alpöge–Furman family template for GL(2) newforms in the level aspect (family-averaged C_MT)

*Mechanism:* M3-transplantation · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 3/5 · *Difficulty:* 2/5

**Source statements.**
- Hua–Yang, arXiv:2608.16034v2, Abstract and Prop. 2.3/11.1: for the prime-modulus Dirichlet family, character orthogonality at X = q^lambda < q gives family totals N0s/N >= C_MT − o(1) and Nd/N >= C_d − o(1), uniformly for (log Q)^{1+eta}/Q <= T <= Q^{A0}.
- Alpöge–Furman, arXiv:2608.13637v2, Remark 7.2, pp.14–15: summing chain (1.2) over a family, so that only the family average of ||G_f||^2 enters linearly (Dirichlet average 0.811/0.905).
- Petersson formula: sum_{f in H_k(N)} omega_f lambda_f(m)lambda_f(n) = delta_{m,n} + 2pi i^{-k} sum_{c≡0 (N)} S(m,n;c)/c J_{k−1}(4pi sqrt(mn)/c) (Iwaniec–Luo–Sarnak, Publ. IHES 91 (2000), printed pp.68–69, the same import the host repository uses in reviews/B/REPORT.md §11; Iwaniec–Kowalski Prop. 14.5).
- Host repository firewall: research/integrated/CURRENT_RESULTS.md §11 and OPEN_CUTS.md §5 ('nonprincipal control can miss an arbitrarily large principal component'); AGENTS.md: 'a family identity is not principal-member extraction'.

**Proposed link.** In the Hua–Yang template the only arithmetic input that removes off-diagonal prime pairs is orthogonality over the family. For H_k(N) with N prime, harmonic weights omega_f, and zeros of L(s,f) at heights in [T,2T] (T fixed >= 1 or T <= (log N)^A), the family average of ||G_f||^2 involves <Lambda_f(m)Lambda_f(n)>_omega. By Petersson this is delta_{m,n}(diagonal identical to Montgomery's) plus Kloosterman terms. Off-diagonal bound: expand J_{k−1} in its power series (each term is separable in m and n), open S(m,n;c) = sum_x e((mx + n·xbar)/c), and apply Cauchy–Schwarz over x mod c, which is valid because X < N <= c. This gives |sum_{m,n<=X} a_m conj(a_n) Delta_off(m,n)| <<_k (X/N)^{k−1}(1 + log N·1_{k=2})·sum|a_n|^2, which is negligible for X <= N^{1−eps} and every k >= 2. The log-conductor is log N + O(1), so the normalized bandwidth tends to 1 and chain (1.2) gives the family-averaged Montgomery–Taylor constant.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Diagonal. <lambda_f(p)^2> = 1 + small and <lambda_f(p)lambda_f(q)> = small for p ≠ q, so the prime-diagonal is sum_p log^2p/p·cos(tau log p)cos(tau' log p), exactly the zeta diagonal and slope kappa = 1. The (tau+tau') oscillation integrates out because T·log X -> infinity. Prime-square terms use lambda_f(p)^2 = lambda_f(p^2) + 1 (p ∤ N) and contribute O(1). (2) Density term. mu_f(tau) = (1/2pi)log(N tau^2/4pi^2) + O(1), constant to leading order when T is fixed. (3) Central zeros forced by root number −1 are excluded by T >= 1. (4) Oldforms are absent (k < 12) or of weight O(k/N) for N prime. (5) Grid size d ~ (T/2pi)·lambda·log N -> infinity. With T fixed, Alpöge–Furman's sqrt T margins must be replaced by margins measured in mean spacings, as Hua–Yang do for small T. (6) Harmonic weights make the statement a weighted proportion; removing the weights needs the ILS L(1,sym^2 f) expansion. (7) Cross-check: using only the Weil bound for |S| and no bilinear structure gives the weaker lambda_k = 1 − 1/(2k). Then 2 − R*(lambda_k) = 0.41908 (k=2), 0.56934 (k=4), 0.64219 (k=12) and 0.66905 (k=100), approaching C_MT; this is consistent.

**New claim.** Proof sketch (not yet written in full). Fix k >= 2. For N prime -> infinity and T >= 1 fixed: sum_{f in H_k(N)} omega_f N^s_{0,f}(T,2T) >= (C_MT − o(1))·sum_f omega_f N_f(T,2T), with C_MT = 3/2 − (1/sqrt2)cot(1/sqrt2) = 0.6725007. Likewise sum_f omega_f N_{d,f} >= (0.8362504 − o(1))·sum_f omega_f N_f. This is the GL(2) analogue of Hua–Yang's prime-modulus theorem. It is a family-average statement only: by M3-T1(A), no member-wise conclusion about simple zeros follows.

**Already proved.** Hua–Yang for the Dirichlet prime-modulus family (unrefereed). Alpöge–Furman Remark 7.2 for the Dirichlet average (stated, details omitted, not in Lean). The Petersson formula and the Weil bound are classical. The bilinear Kloosterman bound used here is the standard proof of the Petersson large sieve. The numbers 2 − R*(lambda) were computed (thresholds.py).

**Conjectural.** The full proof has not been written, in particular the T-fixed tail and trace control (the analogue of Hua–Yang §§8–10) and the uniformity of the off-diagonal bound once the Gabor kernel's tau-dependence is included. Pushing beyond lambda = 1 (as Alpöge–Furman's 0.811) would need the ILS-type main terms of Kloosterman sums for X > N and is not claimed.

**Next step.** State and prove the family second-moment lemma: sum_f omega_f ||G_f||_HS^2 = (R(psi) + o(1))·sum_f omega_f N_f for X = N^{lambda}, lambda < 1, via Petersson plus the opened-Kloosterman bilinear bound above. Then copy Hua–Yang §§10–11 verbatim with Petersson in place of character orthogonality.

**Potential counterexamples.** Families where orthogonality fails at the needed length. In the weight aspect with fixed level and a single k, the Bessel transition J_{k−1}(x) at x ~ k gives only X << k, i.e. lambda = 1/2, which is the M3-T1 barrier: no simple-zero output. Averaging over k in [K,2K] (ILS Cor. 8.2) is required to reach lambda -> 1. Also a family average can be carried by a vanishing proportion of forms, so no per-form statement may be inferred.

**Tests.** (i) Numerically verify the bilinear Kloosterman bound for N about 10^4, k = 2, random a_n, X = N^{0.9}. (ii) LMFDB check: averaged over newforms of weight 2 and prime level near 5000, compute the empirical zero-side ||G_f||^2/N_f at T about 10 with lambda = 0.9. The prediction is R*(0.9) = 1.4064.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** Same arXiv queries as M3-T1 ("critical line" AND newforms; "simple zeros" AND modular; au:Alpoge; inertia AND zeros), plus WebSearch 'proportion of zeros on the critical line on average over newforms level aspect Levinson Petersson simple zeros GL(2) family'. Found: Hua–Yang (Dirichlet only), Baluyot–Chandee–Li 2310.07606 (1-level density), Tang–Miller 2405.11172 (lowest first zero), Andersen–Thorner and Bernard (individual forms, Levinson), and Conrey–Iwaniec–Soundararajan's asymptotic large sieve for twists of a fixed GL(2) form (Levinson; per Milinovich–Ng's introduction). No GL(2)-family application of the rank–trace certificate was found.

**Verifier reasoning.** **Sources match.**
- Hua–Yang's abstract and §11 constants are as cited: C_MT = 0.6725007, C_d = 0.8362504, uniformly for (log Q)^{1+eta}/Q <= T <= Q^{A0} with Q = log q, and the result is family-total only.
- AF Remark 7.2 is as cited: average over Dirichlet characters 0.811/0.905, details omitted, not in Lean.

**Off-diagonal Petersson bound: correct and standard.**
- Expand J_{k−1} in its power series, then open S(m,n;c) = sum_x e((mx + n·xbar)/c).
- Cauchy–Schwarz over x mod c, with additive orthogonality valid because m, n <= X < N <= c, gives Σ_c B(c)/c << Σ_l (X/(Nl))^{k−1}·||a||·||b||.
- For k = 2 this diverges logarithmically and needs a Weil-bound tail. That yields (X/N)log N, as claimed. This is the usual Petersson large-sieve argument, uniform in the coefficients, so the tau-dependence of the Gabor kernel is harmless.
- Weil-only bound: lambda_k = 1 − 1/(2k). The values 2 − R*(lambda_k) = 0.419075 (k=2), 0.569339 (k=4), 0.642191 (k=12) and 0.669052 (k=100) reproduce.
- Slope 1 and lambda -> 1 hold, so C_MT and C_d follow formally.

**What the plan omits.** Hua–Yang do not use character orthogonality alone. Their §3, 'Counting and zero-density input', imports two things:
- Selberg's family-averaged argument estimate.
- The Hiary–Zhao zero-density deletion of O(q/Q^2) exceptional characters.
Their §9 adds a Gevrey exterior-tail estimate.

These inputs are essential at fixed T:
- AF's tail bound ||E~||_1 << X^{1/2}/T (Prop 4.3) is useless when X = N^lambda and T is fixed.
- Zeros off the line by |y| outside the window leak with weight about X^{|y|/2}, which a Gevrey taper beats only after deleting members that have zeros at distance > (log N)^{-c} from the line.

The proposal's next step ('copy §§10–11 verbatim') and its 'conjectural' list (§§8–10) never mention the zero-density deletion or the averaged counting input. GL(2) level-aspect analogues exist (Kowalski–Michel zero-density for prime level, with harmonic weights) but must be imported and their uniformity checked.

**Minor points.**
- The stated prediction R*(0.9) = 1.4064 is wrong; the correct value is 1.406898.
- The result is a harmonically weighted family average. The proposal correctly says no member-wise statement follows.

**Verifier correction.** (1) Add the missing family inputs, as GL(2) analogues of Hua–Yang §3:
- a zero-density estimate for {L(s,f): f in H_k(N)} near Re s = 1/2 (e.g. Kowalski–Michel, Duke 1999), used to delete exceptional forms whose zeros lie farther than (log N)^{-c} from the line;
- an averaged argument/counting estimate for Σ_f omega_f N_f(T,2T) uniform at fixed T;
- a Gevrey Fourier–Laplace exterior-tail bound (the analogue of Hua–Yang §9).
Without these the fixed-T tail cannot be controlled.

(2) Correct the test prediction to R*(0.9) = 1.406898.

(3) State the theorem as a harmonic-weight family average. Removing the weights needs the ILS L(1, sym^2 f) device.

**Prior art found by verifier.** No source found applying the rank–trace certificate to a GL(2) family. The closest are:
- Hua–Yang 2608.16034 (Dirichlet, prime modulus);
- AF Remark 7.2 (Dirichlet average);
- Chandee–Lee–Liu–Radziwiłł style GRH family results for Dirichlet L-functions;
- Baluyot–Chandee–Li 2310.07606 (1-level density).
The Petersson off-diagonal bound is standard: ILS 2000 §2, Iwaniec–Kowalski Prop. 14.5 and the Petersson large sieve.


## CON-0014 — Transplanting the quasi-RH trivializing-row extraction to lower-order residue families: exponent table 1 − 1/(2r), and the quadratic case is exactly circular

*Mechanism:* M3-transplantation · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 3/5 · *Difficulty:* 3/5

**Source statements.**
- OpenAI, 'Quasi-RH 11/12' manuscript (unrefereed; archive audit audits/quasi_rh/ARCHITECTURE.md): Theorem 1.1 (l.73); Proposition 3.1 (l.679: sum_{N(u)<=D^{1+theta}}|A_u(D)|^2 << D^{2+theta+eps}); Section 3 extraction (l.694–724: rows u = p^6, A_{p^6} = A_1 + O(D/Np), |A_1|^2 << D^{1+eps}H/J + D^2/Y^2, giving 11/12 = (1+5/6)/2); Lemma 4.1 Gauss–Jacobi mu(n) = conj(alpha(n))·gamma_2(n)^3 (l.823ff; archive architecture items 1–2, 15, E6).
- Heath-Brown, 'A mean value estimate for real character sums', Acta Arith. 72 (1995) 235–275, Theorem 1: sum*_{m<=M}|sum*_{n<=N} a_n (n/m)|^2 << (MN)^eps (M+N) sum|a_n|^2 over odd squarefree m, n.
- Ji, arXiv:2611.16665v2, Theorem 1.1: zeta_{Q(i)} has no zeros in Re s > 599/600 via quartic Gauss sums and the quartic Kubota series.
- Liu, arXiv:2610.12234 (via archive programmes ZFR §3): 0.874957... is optimal within the exponent comparison system of the 7/8 method.
- Host repository OPEN_CUTS §5 (principal-member and native-source transfer).

**Proposed link.** The 11/12 argument is a principal-member extraction from a family whose principal member coincides, up to O(D/p), with J ≈ H^{1/r}/log H 'trivializing' rows u = p^r. Abstractly: if the full mean square over all H = D^{1+theta} rows (trivializing rows included) is << D^{1+eps}H, then |A_1|^2 << D^{1+eps}H/J + D^2 H^{-2/r}. This is a candidate tool for host §5 and an obvious route to beating 11/12 by lowering r. I test whether it transplants.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Formula check. Exponent a(r,theta) = max{(1 + (1+theta)(1−1/r))/2, 1 − (1+theta)/r}, which tends to 1 − 1/(2r) as theta -> 0. r = 6 gives 11/12, exactly matching the manuscript's arithmetic (11+5theta)/6 at l.711–716; r = 4 gives 7/8, r = 3 gives 5/6 and r = 2 gives 3/4. (2) Field requirement: r-th power residue symbols with reciprocity need mu_r in the base field (Q for r = 2, Q(i) for r = 4, Q(sqrt−3) for r = 3, 6). (3) The quadratic case was checked against Heath-Brown's theorem, whose moduli must be squarefree.

**New claim.** (a) The extraction ceiling of the template with order-r families is 1 − 1/(2r) (elementary; above). (b) Proof sketch. For r = 2 over Q, the needed hypothesis sum_{u<=D^{1+theta}}|sum_n mu(n)(u/n)W(n/D)|^2 << D^{2+theta+eps} carries no information beyond its conclusion. Group rows by squarefree part u1. Rows with u1 >= D^{1−theta} are covered unconditionally by Heath-Brown's large sieve, since sqrt(H/U)(U+D)D <= HD for U >= D^2/H. Rows with u1 < D^{1−theta} are near-principal, each repeated sqrt(H/u1) times. Their contribution is within the target iff the Möbius sums of the real characters of conductor u1 ~ U satisfy, on dyadic average, |A^{(u1)}| << D^{1/2}(H/U)^{1/4}. At U = 1 this is A_1 << D^{3/4+theta/4}, which is precisely the extraction's output (quasi-RH 3/4 strength). Poisson summation in u does not escape this: the dual coefficients are mu(n)·epsilon_n·n^{-1/2} (quadratic Gauss sums carry no Möbius-absorbing structure), and the dual square rows k = j^2 reproduce the principal Möbius sums of zeta and L(s,chi_{−4}). So the quadratic mean square is equivalent, modulo Heath-Brown, to its own conclusion. (c) Design rule, for the archive and host §5. Row extraction transfers family control to the principal member only if (i) the trivializing-row density J/H is a power of H and (ii) the full mean square is proved by a duality whose dual principal rows do not contain the principal source. In the sextic case Lemma 4.1 converts mu times a sextic Gauss sum into cubic Gauss sums (Patterson coefficients), which breaks the loop. That is the structural reason the method needs sextic symbols over Q(sqrt−3) and pays (1+5/6)/2.

**Already proved.** (a) is elementary bookkeeping that reproduces the manuscript's exponent. The Heath-Brown large sieve is classical. The 11/12 and 7/8 manuscripts are unrefereed (expert reception favourable for 11/12, per the archive's secondary sources).

**Conjectural.** (b) is a proof sketch: the bookkeeping of coprimality conditions, the epsilon_n splitting into chi_{−4}-twists, and the dual non-square rows must be written out with smooth weights. The claim that r = 3 or r = 4 can reach the ceiling (5/6 or 7/8) is not made. Whether a non-circular dual exists for r = 3 is open.

**Next step.** For r = 3 over Z[omega], compute the Poisson-in-u dual coefficients of the cubic family. Test numerically whether mu(n) times a normalized cubic Gauss sum is a Patterson-type theta coefficient or correlates with one, using the archive's own zomega.py utilities (audits/quasi_rh/architecture_checks) over primary n with N(n) <= 10^6. A theta-type structure would make the template's 5/6 target meaningful; otherwise r = 6 is the smallest admissible order and 11/12 is the template's ceiling. In parallel, locate where Ji's quartic proof (599/600) loses against the r = 4 ceiling of 7/8.

**Potential counterexamples.** The 7/8 manuscript reaches 7/8 by a different two-stage comparison, not by row extraction, so the table constrains only the extraction template, not quasi-RH methods in general. Dirichlet-character families (rows = characters mod q <= Q) have trivializing density 1/Q, and their full mean square including principal characters is equivalent to Barban–Davenport–Halberstam with the principal term, i.e. to power-saving averaged Chowla for mu. That case is circular in the same way.

**Tests.** (i) Small-scale numerics for D = 2·10^4, theta = 0.1: compute sum_u|A_u|^2 split by squarefree part u1 < D^{0.9} versus >= D^{0.9}. This illustrates the bookkeeping only; it cannot test circularity, since RH holds numerically. (ii) Symbolic check of a(r,theta) against the manuscript for r = 6 (done).

**Prior-art search (proposer).** WebSearch 'quasi-Riemann hypothesis 11/12 sextic characters Gauss-Jacobi cubic theta why not quadratic characters Tao blog Green formulation'. It returned no explanation of the choice of order (hits: sextic theta series 1312.0568, MathWorld on Kubota theta, Dunn–Radziwiłł 2109.07463, a tracker page). Archive sources read: 11/12 architecture audit, Ji 2611.16665 (full text). Tao's 19 Oct 2026 post and the oai-math-revised repository were not accessible. No source found states the order-r table or the quadratic circularity explicitly; prior discussion in non-public write-ups cannot be excluded.

**Verifier reasoning.** **Sources match.**
- The archive's ARCHITECTURE.md item 1 matches the cited lines: rows p^6, |A_1|^2 << D^{1+eps}H/J + D^2/Y^2 = D^{(11+5theta)/6} + ..., at l.711–716.
- Heath-Brown's 1995 theorem (squarefree m, n) is as stated.
- Ji 2611.16665 Theorem 1.1 (599/600, quartic) and Liu 2610.12234 (0.874957..., optimal within his comparison system) are as stated.

**(a) The exponent table is correct and already known.** The formula a(r,theta) = max{(1 + (1+theta)(1−1/r))/2, 1 − (1+theta)/r} reproduces (11+5theta)/12 for r = 6. But the archive's own ARCHITECTURE.md item 1 already says '11/12 = (1+5/6)/2 ... the 5/6 is the loss from using only H^{1/6} sixth-power rows ... Within this extraction 11/12 is optimal'. Replacing 6 by r is trivial.

**(b) The circularity claim is overstated.**
- The order-2 mean square contains the square rows u = v^2. There are about sqrt(H) of them, each approximately A_1. So hypothesis ⟹ conclusion is immediate.
- The same 'contains its own conclusion' property holds for r = 6 (rows p^6). It therefore does not distinguish r = 2 from r = 6.
- The claimed equivalence 'modulo Heath-Brown, to its own conclusion' is false as stated. I re-derived that HB covers squarefree parts u1 >= D^{1−theta}. For each dyadic U in [1, D^{1−theta}) the hypothesis additionally requires averaged bounds |A^{(u1)}| << D^{1/2}(H/U)^{1/4} for Möbius sums twisted by real characters of conductor about U. Heath-Brown alone gives the needed saving only at U >= D^2/H. These U > 1 bounds are quasi-GRH-type input that the U = 1 bound does not imply.

**What survives of (b).** The Poisson-in-u dual (quadratic Gauss sums eps_n·sqrt(n), split by chi_{−4}) returns the principal Möbius sums of zeta and L(chi_{−4}) in its square dual rows. That makes this particular proof strategy circular for r = 2: a heuristic obstruction, not a theorem.

**(c) The design rule restates the archive.** ARCHITECTURE.md item 2 already describes the mechanism: Gauss–Jacobi mu = conj(alpha)gamma_2^3, cube completion into Kubota/Patterson theta, and the quadratic large sieve.

**Other remarks.**
- Ji's 599/600 uses a quartic-character zero-density bound plus a residue and fourth-power lattice average. That is a different template from row extraction, so the r = 4 ceiling comparison is not like-for-like.
- The BDH/Chowla remark about Dirichlet families is unsupported.

**Verifier correction.** (a) Credit the extraction-ceiling formula to the archive's ARCHITECTURE.md item 1, generalized trivially from 6 to r.

(b) Restate the quadratic case:
- The order-2 full mean square is strictly stronger than its conclusion. It is equivalent, modulo Heath-Brown, to the conclusion plus dyadic-average bounds |A^{(u1)}| << D^{1/2}(H/U)^{1/4} for every 1 < U < D^{1−theta}.
- The Poisson-dual route is circular because its dual principal rows return Σ mu(n)n^{-1/2}·{1, chi_{−4}(n)}.
- Drop 'carries no information beyond its conclusion' and 'exactly circular'.
- Present the design rule as a heuristic about provability by duality. Note that the 'contains its conclusion' property holds for every r.

**Prior art found by verifier.** - Archive audits/quasi_rh/ARCHITECTURE.md items 1–2 (the extraction ceiling 11/12 = (1+5/6)/2 and the structural role of the sextic, cubic and quadratic steps).
- Heath-Brown, Acta Arith. 72 (1995), Thm 1.
- No public source found stating the order-r table or the quadratic obstruction explicitly.


## CON-0017 — Pólya–Vinogradov under QRH: (log q)^{6/7} for every θ<1, √q log log q only for θ<2/3 (the claimed 7/8 does not reach Montgomery–Vaughan)

*Mechanism:* M4-strengthen-weaken · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 3/5 · *Difficulty:* 2/5

**Source statements.**
- Montgomery–Vaughan 1977 (CAND-00336), Theorem 2 (Sec. 1 p.70; proof Secs. 8-9, pp.78-81): assume GRH (no Dirichlet L(s,χ) has a zero with Re ρ>1/2). Then Σ_{n≤x}χ(n) ≪ q^{1/2} log log q for every non-principal χ mod q. Paper record: 'Lemma 2 (GRH smoothing of character sums to y-smooth numbers)' is the load-bearing GRH step.
- Granville–Soundararajan, arXiv:math/0503113v1 (JAMS 20, 2007), Theorem 2.1 (p.5, unconditional). Let ξ mod m be the primitive character of conductor ≤ (log q)^{1/3} minimising D(χ,ξ;q). Then M(χ) ≪ (1−χ(−1)ξ(−1))(√(qm)/φ(m)) log q exp(−D(χ,ξ;q)^2/2) + √q (log q)^{6/7}, where D(χ,ξ;y)^2 = Σ_{p≤y}(1−Re χ(p)ξ̄(p))/p.
- Granville–Soundararajan math/0503113, §5: (5.1) under GRH Σ_{p≤x}ψ(p) ≪ √x log(mx). Lemma 5.1 (GRH, x<q^{3/2}): Σ_{p≤x}χ(p)e(pθ) ≪ x^{5/6} log q, proved with R=x^{2/3} and (5.2) Σ_{p≤x}χ(p)e(bp/r) ≪ √(rx) log(qx). Lemma 5.2: the restriction to S(y) costs O(x y^{−1/6} log q). Proposition 2.3 (GRH): Σ_{n≤q}χ(n)e(nα)/n = Σ_{n≤q, n∈S((log q)^{12})} χ(n)e(nα)/n + O(1).

**Proposed link.** (a) Use U3. Under QRH(θ) for the twists η=χξ̄ (conductor ≤ q(log q)^{1/3}, non-principal because χ is primitive mod q and m<q), the explicit formula gives Σ_{p≤x}η(p) ≪_θ x^θ log^2(qx), using |ρ| ≥ 1−θ. Hence Σ_{y<p≤q} η(p)/p = O_θ(1) for y=(log q)^{3/(1−θ)}. Therefore D(χ,ξ;q)^2 ≥ log log q − log log y − O_θ(1) for every ξ in GS Theorem 2.1. The first term there becomes ≪ √q (log q · log y)^{1/2} = √q (log q)^{1/2+o(1)}, so M(χ) ≪_θ √q (log q)^{6/7}. (b) In GS §5 the GRH bound is used for every r ≤ R. With x^θ in place of √x, Lemma 5.1 becomes (1+|β|x)√r x^θ log^2 q ≤ (√R + x/R)x^θ log^2 q, which is optimised at R=x^{2/3} and gives x^{θ+1/3}. Lemma 5.2 then becomes x y^{θ−2/3}/(2/3−θ), and Prop. 2.3 holds with y=(log q)^{3/(2/3−θ)+ε}. So M(χ) ≤ (C/(2/3−θ))√q log log q exactly when θ<2/3. The minor arcs are the obstruction: for r > x^{2(1−θ)}, QRH gives no saving in Σ_{p≤x}χ(p)e(bp/r), and nothing unconditional applies for polylog(q) ≤ x ≤ q^{3/2}.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) GS's normalisation: M(χ) = max_x|Σ_{n≤x}χ(n)|, χ primitive mod q, q large. ξ ranges over primitive characters of conductor < (log q)^{1/3}, including ξ=1 (m=1, where the parity factor kills the term for even χ). Terms with p|q add 1/p to D², which only helps. (2) The QRH used is only for the twists χξ̄. This is weaker than QRH for all L, and the OpenAI claim (all Dirichlet L) covers it. (3) The 6/7 is as printed in arXiv v1 Theorem 2.1. The JAMS version and later minor-arc refinements (Goldmakher; Lamzouri–Mangerel) may change the exponent; not checked. (4) For (b), x<q^{3/2} is needed so that r ≤ x^{2/3} < q keeps χψ non-principal, exactly as in GS. The constant blows up like (2/3−θ)^{-1}. (5) Littlewood-type L(1,χ) ≪ (1−θ)^{-1} log log q under QRH is needed for the L(1,χ) term in GS (2.1). It follows from the same smooth-number truncation.

**New claim.** (a) Assume QRH(θ) for L(s,χξ̄), for all primitive ξ of conductor ≤ (log q)^{1/3} and some fixed θ<1. Then max_x|Σ_{n≤x}χ(n)| ≪_θ √q (log q)^{6/7} for every primitive χ mod q, a power-of-log improvement of Pólya–Vinogradov. A zero-free disc of width about (log q)^{−5/7} around s=1 for these twists suffices, since it gives |L(1+1/log q, χξ̄)| ≤ (log q)^{5/7}. This minimal-hypothesis claim is heuristic and must be checked against GS 2003-type truncation lemmas. (b) Assume QRH(θ) for all Dirichlet L-functions with θ<2/3. Then M(χ) ≤ C(2/3−θ)^{-1}√q log log q, so Montgomery–Vaughan's GRH theorem survives every half-plane strictly left of 2/3 via the GS route. (c) The claimed θ=7/8 and θ=11/12 give (a) but not (b). What is missing is a uniform bound Σ_{p≤x}χ(p)e(pα) ≪ x^{1−δ} for polylog(q) ≤ x ≤ q^{3/2} on minor arcs.

**Already proved.** GS Theorem 2.1 (unconditional) and GS §5 (under GRH) are published. The substitutions in (a) and (b) are short deductions done here and not written out in full. That zero-free information near 1 gives PV improvements is known in spirit: Goldmakher's 'Character sums to smooth moduli are small' (Canad. J.
