Math. 2010) uses Chang/Iwaniec-type input for smooth moduli (recalled, not re-read), and GS JEMS 2018 Cor. 1.2 (as cited by Mangerel arXiv:2207.14377 p.2) gives short-sum cancellation from few zeros in small rectangles near Re s=1.

**Conjectural.** QRH (unrefereed). Whether the original MV 1977 route (Secs. 8-9, not read) has a different threshold than GS's 2/3. Whether better minor-arc technology lowers 6/7 under QRH.

**Next step.** Read MV 1977 Secs. 8-9 and GS (JAMS) §6 to confirm that GRH enters only through Prop. 2.3 / MV Lemma 2 and the L(1,·) bounds. Then write (a) in full. Then test whether the minor-arc estimates of Lamzouri–Mangerel (arXiv:2505.07651 and its predecessors) give an exponent below 6/7 once pretentious obstructions are removed by QRH.

**Potential counterexamples.** For (b), a θ in [2/3,1) is consistent with every known constraint, so no contradiction is possible. For (a), a family of χ pretending to be ξ at all primes up to q^{o(1)} would violate QRH-derived bounds. Paley-type characters (M ≫ √q log log q) are consistent with both (a) and (b).

**Tests.** Numerically compute D(χ,ξ;q)² for prime q ≤ 10^7 and small ξ, and compare with log log q − log log y. This is illustrative only. Check the algebra by setting θ=1/2 in (b) to recover GS's x^{5/6} and y^{−1/6}.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** WebSearch 'Pólya–Vinogradov improvement assuming zero-free region pretentious'. This found GS math/0503113 and Mangerel 2207.14377, which cites GS JEMS 2018 Cor. 1.2 for short sums. arXiv API: abs:Pólya AND abs:Vinogradov (latest 2025-2026 hits: 2505.07651, 2405.00544, 2207.14377) and abs:"character sums" AND Vinogradov, with no QRH-conditional PV statement found. None of the 2026 QRH-consumer abstracts treats character sums. The 2/3 threshold has no precedent found.

**Verifier reasoning.** Part (a) checks out. GS math/0503113 Theorem 2.1 is as quoted (ξ of conductor below (log q)^{1/3}, error term √q(log q)^{6/7}). Under QRH for χξ̄, Σ_{p≤z}η(p) ≪ z^θ log²(qz)/(1-θ). This gives Σ_{y<p≤q}η(p)/p = O(1) for y=(log q)^{3/(1-θ)}, hence exp(-D²/2) ≪ (log y/log q)^{1/2} and M(χ) ≪ √q(log q)^{6/7}. In (b) the algebra on GS's route is right. In Lemma 5.1, (1+|β|x)√r·x^θ with r ≤ R and |β| ≤ 1/(rR) is ≤ √R·x^θ + x^{1+θ}/R; R=x^{2/3} gives x^{θ+1/3}. Lemma 5.2 then gives x·y^{θ-2/3}/(2/3-θ), so GS's Prop. 2.3 route needs θ<2/3. But claim (c), and the title's 'the claimed 7/8 does not reach Montgomery–Vaughan', is false as a statement about QRH. The 2/3 is an artifact of GS's route, which uses GRH on every arc (Lemma 5.1 with r up to x^{2/3}). Montgomery–Vaughan's own proof works differently, per the theorem-database record of MV 1977 §9 (lines 99–105) and GS Lemma 4.2. At each scale u, take a Dirichlet approximation with denominator ≤ u/R and R=(log u)^3. If r>R, the unconditional MV Corollary 1 gives Σ_{n≤u}χ(n)e(nα) ≪ u/log u. If r ≤ R (major arc), GRH enters only through MV Lemma 2, which smooths Σ_{n≤x}χψ(n) to y-smooth n. Lemma 2's only analytic input is a prime-sum bound. Its combinatorial proof (GS Lemma 5.2 with α=0: n=mp, p=P(n)>y) under QRH(θ) gives error ≪ x·y^{θ-1}(log kx)²/(1-θ)² + x/y. The major-arc twist e(nβ) costs (1+|β|u) ≤ R/r, and the Gauss-sum expansion costs √r ≤ R^{1/2}, both (log q)^{O(1)}. Both are absorbed by taking y=(log q)^{C/(1-θ)}. The smooth part is bounded trivially by Ψ(u,y), and partial summation gives Σ_{n≤q}χ(n)e(nα)/n ≪ log log q + log y ≪ (1-θ)^{-1} log log q uniformly in α. Hence QRH(θ) for every fixed θ<1, including 7/8 and 11/12, appears to give the full MV bound √q log log q. That also supersedes (a). Caveat: this is my sketch. MV §§8–9 were not re-read; the database record was used.

**Verifier correction.** Replace (b) and (c) with the following. Assume L(s,χψ) ≠ 0 for Re s>θ for every ψ of modulus ≤ (log q)^{C}, for some fixed θ<1. Then M(χ) ≪ (1-θ)^{-1}·√q log log q for every primitive χ mod q, by Montgomery–Vaughan's route: unconditional MV Corollary 1 on minor arcs, plus a QRH version of MV Lemma 2 with error x·y^{θ-1}(log kx)²/(1-θ)² on major arcs r ≤ (log u)^{O(1)}. The threshold θ<2/3 is specific to the Granville–Soundararajan Prop. 2.3 route, which uses GRH on all arcs; it is not a limit of QRH. Part (a) is correct but superseded. Write out the QRH Lemma 2 and MV §9 bookkeeping in full before promoting this.

**Prior art found by verifier.** No statement of MV under quasi-GRH was found in one WebSearch and the arXiv QRH listing (19 hits). Related: Granville–Soundararajan, arXiv:1501.01804 (JEMS 2018), Theorem 1.3, which controls character sums assuming few zeros of L(s,χ) in a rectangle with Re s ≥ 3/4. The MV 1977 structure, with GRH only on major arcs via Lemma 2, comes from the theorem-database record of Invent. Math. 43 §§8–9.


## CON-0020 — Hooley's Artin theorem needs GRH-quality zeros only up to θ_H≈0.5466: quasi-GRH(θ) gives density ≥ A(a)−2log(θ/(1−θ)), so neither 7/8 nor the 1−10^{-6} cyclotomic strip suffices

*Mechanism:* M4-strengthen-weaken · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 3/5 · *Difficulty:* 2/5

**Source statements.**
- Hooley 1967 (CAND-00328), Theorem (Sec. 7, pp.219-220): under the extended RH for the Dedekind zeta functions of Q(b^{1/k_1}, 1^{1/k}) (k squarefree), N_a(x) ~ A(a) li(x). Equation (33) (Sec. 6, p.219): M_a(x,ξ_1,ξ_2) ≤ Σ_{ξ_1<q≤ξ_2}(li x/(q(q−1)) + O(x^{1/2} log x)) = O(x/log^2 x), using the conditional prime-ideal estimate (28). Paper record: 'determine which finite window of k the GRH input is used on'.
- OpenAI release, 'Primitive roots, all admissible bases' (2026-10-04), per recent_developments_2024_2026.md §4.1: a uniform zero-free strip Re s>1−10^{-6} for all finite-order Hecke L over cyclotomic F ⊇ μ12, giving infinitude (not density) in Artin's conjecture for every admissible base.
- Serre 1981 (CAND-00728), Theorem 4 (Sec. 2.4, (14_R)): effective Chebotarev |π_C(x)−(|C|/|G|)Li(x)| ≤ c_0(|C|/|G|)x^{1/2}(log d_L+n_L log x) under GRH for ζ_L. Frei–Sofos–Widmer arXiv:2610.18491 prove the QRH analogue (abstract).
- Murty–Naik arXiv:2608.21573 (abstract): a non-abelian large sieve inequality (assumed) implies Artin's conjecture; partial unconditional results via duality.

**Proposed link.** Run Hooley's four ranges under QRH(θ) for ζ_{K_q}, K_q=Q(ζ_q, a^{1/q}). Range (i), q ≤ (1/6)log x: Möbius sieve over x^{o(1)} fields with error x^{θ+o(1)}, fine for any θ<1. Range (ii), q ≤ ξ_2=x^{1−θ}/log^3 x: Chebotarev under QRH gives π(x,K_q)=li x/(q(q−1))+O(x^θ log(qax)), and the summed error is ≪ ξ_2 x^θ log x = o(x/log x). Range (iii), ξ_2<q ≤ √x log x: only Brun–Titchmarsh π(x;q,1) ≤ 2x/(φ(q)log(x/q)) is available, which discards the q-th-power condition. Its total is (2x/log x)∫_{1−θ}^{1/2} du/(u(1−u)) = (2 log(θ/(1−θ))+o(1))π(x) for θ>1/2. Range (iv), q>√x log x: Hooley's elementary bound, o(π(x)). Hence N_a(x) ≥ (A(a) − 2 log(θ/(1−θ)) − o(1))π(x).

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Field compatibility: K_q is abelian (Kummer) over Q(ζ_q), so ζ_{K_q} is a product of Hecke L over Q(ζ_q). To meet OpenAI's 'F ⊇ μ12', pass to K_q(ζ_12), which is abelian over Q(ζ_{lcm(q,12)}). K_q(ζ_12)/K_q is abelian Galois, so ζ_{K_q} divides ζ_{K_q(ζ_12)} with an entire quotient. The cyclotomic strip claim therefore gives QRH(1−10^{-6}) for ζ_{K_q}. (2) Chebotarev normalisation (Serre/LO form) with |C|=1: the error per field is x^θ(log d/n + log x) ≪ x^θ log(qax), since log d_{K_q} ≪ q^2 log(qa) and n=q(q−1). (3) Brun–Titchmarsh (Montgomery–Vaughan) holds for all q<x with constant 2. (4) A(a) is Artin's constant 0.3739558 for generic a, and Heilbronn-corrected otherwise. (5) θ=1/2 recovers Hooley exactly, since the range (iii) loss is 2 log 1 = 0.

**New claim.** Proposed. Let a be admissible and suppose ζ_{Q(ζ_k,a^{1/k})} has no zeros in Re s>θ for all squarefree k, with 1/2 ≤ θ<1. Then liminf N_a(x)/π(x) ≥ A(a) − 2 log(θ/(1−θ)). This is positive iff θ < θ_H(a) = e^{A(a)/2}/(1+e^{A(a)/2}). For generic a, θ_H = 0.54661... . The claimed θ=7/8 (loss 2 log 7 ≈ 3.89) and θ=1−10^{-6} cannot give positive density by this route. The single missing statement is a bound #{p ≤ x: p splits completely in K_q} ≪ x/(q^{1+δ} log x) on average over q in [x^{1−θ}, x^{1/2}]. That is the q-th-power-residue saving which Brun–Titchmarsh throws away, i.e. an averaged Chebotarev / non-abelian large sieve of the kind Murty–Naik assume.

**Already proved.** Hooley's ranges and Brun–Titchmarsh are published. QRH-conditional effective Chebotarev is in Frei–Sofos–Widmer 2610.18491. The upper bound N_a(x) ≤ (A(a)+o(1))π(x) is unconditional (Chebotarev for finitely many fields, letting the sieve level grow slowly). The threshold computation is done here.

**Conjectural.** QRH for Kummer–Dedekind zeta functions. The OpenAI strip is unrefereed; the 'Cureton–Thorner' Dedekind QRH is cited by FSW but was not located or read here. Improving the constant 2 in range (iii) (Iwaniec-type Brun–Titchmarsh for q≈x^{u}) would raise θ_H slightly but cannot approach 7/8.

**Next step.** Read Hooley Secs. 5-6 ((28) and the exact ranges) to confirm the bookkeeping. Then check whether Murty–Naik's partial unconditional large sieve covers any subrange of [x^{1−θ}, x^{1/2}], which would raise θ_H. Then determine the analogous threshold for the 'infinitude only' statements (Gupta–Murty/Heath-Brown style) that the OpenAI strip is said to reach.

**Potential counterexamples.** The deduction has no counterexample. The bound could be weak if the q-th-power condition is correlated with p ≡ 1 mod q in range (iii). This does not affect the lower bound, which uses only Brun–Titchmarsh.

**Tests.** Compute A(a) − 2 log(θ/(1−θ)) for several a, including a ≡ 1 mod 4 cases with Heilbronn correction, and tabulate θ_H(a). For x up to 10^9, numerically compare the actual range-(iii) counts with the Brun–Titchmarsh majorant to see how lossy it is. This is illustrative only.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** WebSearch 'Hooley Artin weaker hypothesis zero-free region Kummer Brun-Titchmarsh middle range'. It found Fan–Pollack 2505.05601 (a GRH uniform version) and a snippet of a Murty paper assuming zero-free regions with fixed ε for Kummer fields over prime-power n; that paper was not accessible (the queensu link redirects) and is unread. arXiv API abs:Artin AND "primitive root" (2025-2026) gave Murty–Naik 2608.21573 and Sgobba 2508.08996 (unconditional Artin-type problems under convergence conditions). No explicit θ_H threshold was found.

**Verifier reasoning.** The arithmetic and the Brun–Titchmarsh bound are right. The range-(iii) loss is (2x/log x)∫_{1-θ}^{1/2} du/(u(1-u)) = 2 log(θ/(1-θ))·π(x). The threshold θ_H=e^{A/2}/(1+e^{A/2}) is 0.54661 for A=0.3739558. The QRH Chebotarev error for the identity class is x^θ(log d/n + log x) ≪ x^θ log(qax), and summing over 2^{π(ξ1)}=x^{o(1)} fields is fine. But two claims are wrong. (1) 'Only Brun–Titchmarsh is available' in range (iii) is false. Unconditional Bombieri–Vinogradov, restricted to prime q ≤ √x/(log x)^B, gives Σ π(x;q,1) = li(x)Σ 1/(q-1) + O(x/log^A x). The q ∈ (√x/log^B x, √x log x) tail is o(π(x)) by Brun–Titchmarsh. So the loss is log(1/(2(1-θ))), and the threshold is already θ < 1-e^{-A}/2 ≈ 0.656. (2) 'No explicit θ_H threshold was found' misses published work. zbMATH 1011.11005 reviews A. Nongkynrih, 'A conditional proof of Artin's conjecture for primitive roots', C. R. Math. Acad. Sci. Soc. R. Can. 23 (2001) 46–52. It proves that 2 is a primitive root for a positive proportion of primes if ζ_K, K=Q(ζ_k, 2^{1/k}), has no zeros in Re s > 1-(1/2)e^{-12A/5}-δ, i.e. θ < 0.7962. It uses Murty–Murty's Bombieri–Vinogradov variant for Galois extensions. The same review records Hooley's remark: infinitude for a=2 if there are no zeros in Re s > 1-(1/2)e^{-1}-δ, i.e. θ < 0.816. Moree's survey (math/0412262, item 35) cites Nongkynrih but drops the factor 1/2. So the known threshold for positive density is ≈0.796, not 0.5466. The qualitative conclusion still holds: θ=7/8 (0.875) and θ=1-10^{-6} exceed both 0.796 and 0.816, so Hooley-type routes do not give positive density or infinitude from the claimed strips.

**Verifier correction.** Replace θ_H(a)=0.5466 with the known results. (i) Elementary BV in range (iii) gives liminf N_a(x)/π(x) ≥ A(a) - log(1/(2(1-θ))), positive for θ < 1-e^{-A(a)}/2 (≈0.656 for generic a). (ii) Nongkynrih (2001), for a=2, gives positive proportion for θ < 1-(1/2)e^{-12A/5} ≈ 0.796. (iii) Hooley's remark gives infinitude for a=2 when θ < 1-1/(2e) ≈ 0.816. Keep the conclusion that 7/8 and 1-10^{-6} fall outside all of these. Revise the 'single missing statement': an averaged Chebotarev / BV-for-Galois input at the level Nongkynrih uses already exists. What is missing for θ=7/8 is a further gain beyond that level, such as a non-abelian large sieve over q ∈ [x^{1/8}, x^{1/2}].

**Prior art found by verifier.** Nongkynrih, C. R. Math. Acad. Sci. Soc. R. Can. 23 (2001) 46–52, Zbl 1011.11005; review read, threshold 1-(1/2)e^{-12A/5}. Hooley's 1967/1976 remark on the 1-(1/2)e^{-1} threshold, as quoted in that review. Moree, 'Artin's primitive root conjecture – a survey', arXiv:math/0412262, item 35 'Hooley–Heath-Brown hybrids', which also cites Cojocaru [103,104] (quasi-3/4-GRH for elliptic cyclicity) and Ram Murty [373]. M.R. Murty–V.K. Murty, 'A variant of the Bombieri–Vinogradov theorem' (1987).


## CON-0026 — Locating Cureton-Thorner and the field-scope/exponent dependency map of the 2026 quasi-GRH cluster and its consumers

*Mechanism:* M6-compatibility · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 3/5 · *Difficulty:* 2/5

**Source statements.**
- Cureton-Thorner (AxiomMath), 'A quasi-Riemann hypothesis for Dedekind zeta functions', hexagon:2610.00200v2 (12 Oct 2026). Abstract: 'if K is any number field, then the Dedekind zeta function zeta_K(s) does not vanish in the half-plane Re s > 31/32 ... the L-function of any finite-order Hecke character defined over K also does not vanish in the half-plane Re s > 31/32, wherever it is holomorphic. These results were proved and formalized in Lean using AxiomProver ... the hardest part was the formalization of the work of Kazhdan and Patterson on metaplectic forms in order to accommodate all number fields.'
- Liu, arXiv:2610.26835v2. Theorem 17.1: L_K(s,chi) != 0 for Re s > B-flat = 0.874886647... for every number field K. Sec.18, Thm 18.2: the Part II derivation applies 'verbatim' to any F containing mu_12. Remark 17.2 and the reference [CT26] = hexagon:2610.00200v2, with [CTLean] revision 09afbb4ce6570c1615d58509dc197fc9f0956b7d. The abstract states that Part II covers cyclotomic F containing mu_12 and pairs with metabelian normal closure.
- OpenAI, 'Primitive roots for every admissible integer base' (release 4eaee8754), Theorem 1.2: no zeros in Re s > 1-10^-6 for every finite-order Hecke L over every cyclotomic F containing mu_12. Lean: no.
- Shunia, arXiv:2611.02280v1, Sec.6.1: 'it suffices to have a common zero-free boundary for the fields Q(mu_12q) and Q(mu_12q, n^{1/q})'. Corollary 6.1 gives bit complexity L^{max{6,1/(1-theta)}+o(1)}, and Table 1 has the Cureton-Thorner row 31/32 -> 32.
- OpenAI, 'Deterministic Polynomial Factorization over Prime Fields', Sec.10. Proposition: l <= c0 B^{20000000} from delta = 10^-6, for K = Q(mu_12q) and M = K(p^{1/q}). Total cost O(B^{400000100}). Closing remark: 'the reduction yields deterministic polynomial-time factorization under GRH independently of the companion's proof'.
- Bhargava-Vemulapalli arXiv:2611.09031, Frei-Sofos-Widmer arXiv:2610.18491 and Cao-Gao-Huang-Nie arXiv:2610.37179 (abstracts): each assumes or uses the Cureton-Thorner Dedekind quasi-RH 'as announced'.
- Observatory history/recent_developments_2024_2026.md Sec.5: 'Unlocated dependency ... No arXiv record was found in this scan'.

**Proposed link.** The 'unlocated' dependency is hexagon:2610.00200v2, with a Lean formalization. Because a Dedekind zeta function is a product of Hecke L-functions over a base field whenever the extension is abelian, consumers split into two scopes. (a) Consumers that only need Kummer fields Q(mu_12q, a^{1/q}), which are abelian over a cyclotomic field containing mu_12: Shunia, the OpenAI factorization paper, and the OpenAI primitive-roots paper. Three independent claims supply their input: OpenAI Thm 1.2 (delta = 1e-6, no Lean), Liu Part II (B-flat), and Cureton-Thorner (31/32, Lean). (b) Consumers that need arbitrary non-abelian fields: Bhargava-Vemulapalli (odd-degree fields), Frei-Sofos-Widmer (l-torsion for all fields), Cao et al. (splitting fields of sparse polynomials), and Artin-L uses. Only Cureton-Thorner or Liu Part III supply these. Both rest on extending Kazhdan-Patterson metaplectic theory to all F containing mu_12: Cureton-Thorner formalize it, while Liu argues it in a short line-by-line transfer (pp.126-130). Exponents scale as 1/(1-theta) throughout.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Logical order: Liu B-flat (all K) => Cureton-Thorner 31/32 (all K) => OpenAI Thm 1.2 (cyclotomic, 1-1e-6) => Dirichlet QRH. OpenAI 7/8 and 11/12 cover only Q(sqrt-3) Hecke and Dirichlet L-functions, so they supply neither scope (a) nor scope (b). Ji's 599/600 covers only zeta_{Q(i)}. (2) Uniformity: every half-plane claim is qualitative per L-function ('thresholds may depend on the fields', Liu Thm 17.1). Consumers use only the smoothed explicit formula, whose error x^{1-delta}(log D + d) depends only on zero counting (factorization Lemma analytic:explicit; Shunia Lemma 4.1). The qualitative statement is therefore enough, with no hidden uniformity gap. (3) Exponent bookkeeping: Shunia's 32 = (1-31/32)^-1, and GRH gives 2 = (1-1/2)^-1. The factorization search cutoff is x = B^{20/delta}: delta = 1e-6 gives B^{2e7}, while delta = 1/32 would give B^{640} and a total of roughly O(B^{12900}) under the paper's 20E+100 accounting, instead of B^{4e8}. (4) Artin L: Brauer induction plus zero-freeness of all Hecke L-functions over all fields gives Artin L-functions holomorphic and nonvanishing in Re s > 31/32 (or B-flat). Scope (b) is needed for this; scope (a) is not enough. (5) Classical barrier: deterministic polynomial-time factorization over F_p was open even under GRH (Evdokimov 1994: quasi-polynomial). So the factorization paper's unconditional algebraic reduction (its Thm 1.2) is itself a new GRH-level claim and the real audit target. This is not a conflict with any known theorem.

**New claim.** Provenance correction: the Cureton-Thorner dependency cited by four 2026 papers is hexagon:2610.00200v2 (Re s > 31/32 for every number field, Lean via AxiomProver). Scope map: consumers in scope (a) are supported by three independent unrefereed claims, and their exponents improve if a smaller theta is accepted. Shunia's L^{32} becomes L^{7.993} with B-flat, or L^{7.94} with Liu's hexagon:2610.00655v3 B_tw = 0.87406. Consumers in scope (b) have a single load-bearing step: Kazhdan-Patterson over arbitrary F containing mu_12.

**Already proved.** Location and content of the Cureton-Thorner abstract (Hexagon metadata fetched). Liu v2 text read: Thm 17.1, Sec.18, Remark 17.2 and references. Shunia's Sec.6.1, Cor.6.1 and Table 1 read. The factorization paper's Sec.10 bookkeeping read. The implications in the logical order above are standard (abelian zeta factorization, Brauer induction).

**Conjectural.** The correctness of any of the half-plane claims, and whether the Cureton-Thorner Lean statement matches its paper. Whether Bhargava-Vemulapalli or Frei-Sofos-Widmer results depend on the numerical theta or only on theta < 1 (abstracts suggest qualitative use; not checked). The B^{12900} rescaling is approximate.

**Next step.** Download the Cureton-Thorner PDF and Lean repository; record the exact formal statement and #print axioms. Read the Bhargava-Vemulapalli and Frei-Sofos-Widmer hypotheses to tag each consumer as scope (a) or (b) and as theta-dependent or not. Update the Observatory history Sec.5 and the ledger entries ZFR-1 to ZFR-3 and LFUN-3 with the scope column.

**Potential counterexamples.** A consumer in scope (b) that silently uses only OpenAI Thm 1.2, which is cyclotomic only, would be unsupported for non-abelian fields. Cao et al. might rely on Frei-Sofos-Widmer's prime-ideal bound in a form that needs uniformity in degree beyond what the qualitative half-plane supplies.

**Tests.** For each consumer, list the number fields to which the zero-free theorem is applied, then check whether they are abelian over Q(mu_12m). Recompute each consumer exponent as a function of theta and compare with the stated value.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** Liu 2610.26835v2 already cites Cureton-Thorner with its Hexagon locator, and Shunia cites it as Theorem 1.1, so the location is known in the literature but missing from the Observatory. No paper was found that separates consumer scope (a) from scope (b).

**Verifier reasoning.** The location is confirmed: hexagonmath.org/pdf/2610.00200v2 carries 'hexagon:2610.00200v2'. Its Thm 1.1 is zeta_K != 0 for Re s > 31/32, s != 1; Cor. 1.2 covers Hecke L 'wherever it is holomorphic'; Sec. 15 is the AxiomProver Lean certificate (github.com/AxiomMath/DedekindZetaZeroFree). The descent uses F = K(zeta_12) and Kazhdan-Patterson over general F containing mu_3, matching the proposal. Liu Thm 17.1 (B-flat, every K) and Remark 17.2 cite CT v2. Liu's 'implied constants and thresholds may depend on the fixed fields' refers to the internal comparison argument; the boundary itself is independent of K and chi, so the uniformity point (2) is right. OpenAI primitive roots Thm 1.2 (cyclotomic F containing mu_12, 1-1e-6) is confirmed. The factorization paper's Prop. 11.3 uses exactly K = Q(mu_12q), M = K(p^{1/q}) cyclic over K and abelian zeta factorization, so it is scope (a). The bookkeeping is right: x = T B^{20/delta} gives error x^{-delta}B^3 = B^{-17}, and the cost is B^{20E+100}. With delta = 1/32 this gives B^{640} and about B^{12900}. Shunia Cor. 6.1 is L^{max(6,1/(1-theta))+o(1)} and Table 1 has 31/32 -> 32; with B-flat this becomes 7.993, and with B_tw 7.940. BV parametrize by N (N = 32 for CT); Cao et al. use pi_K bounds uniform in D_K and d_K for general fields; FSW give effective Chebotarev for general L. All three are scope (b). The Brauer-induction and abelian-factorization implications are standard. The logical order holds (B-flat < 31/32 < 1-1e-6). The proposal misses a version-dependent constant, which is exactly the M6 target.

**Verifier correction.** (1) Cureton-Thorner has two Hexagon versions with different constants. v1 (hexagon:2610.00200v1, fetched) claims Re s > 1-1/6000. v2 (PDF dated 14 Oct 2026) claims Re s > 31/32. FSW arXiv:2610.18491v2 cite v1 and write QRH(1/6000). BV (2611.09031), Cao et al. ('Theorem 1.1, version 2'), Shunia, Kimura and Liu cite v2 (31/32). The provenance note must record both versions and say which consumer uses which; FSW's results are parametric in delta, so this is not an error on their side. (2) The locator is not new to the Observatory. audits/quasi_rh/DOSSIER.md (lines 194, 436) already records hexagon:2610.00200v2, and its line 279 already gives the abelian-over-K versus general-field split for Chebotarev. Only history/recent_developments_2024_2026.md Sec. 5 ('Unlocated dependency') is stale. (3) Locator fix: in the factorization paper the auxiliary-prime bound l <= c0 B^{20000000} is Proposition 11.3 (Sec. 11, input Thm 11.1), and the cost is Prop. 10.1. The 'GRH independently of the companion' remark ends Sec. 11. (4) Keep 'the factorization reduction is a new GRH-level claim' as the audit target. It was checked against Evdokimov's quasi-polynomial GRH bound and does not contradict it.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Liu 2610.26835v2 Remark 17.2 and Shunia 2611.02280 cite CT v2. FSW 2610.18491v2 cites CT v1 ([hex26] hexagon:2610.00200v1, QRH(1/6000)). The Observatory's audits/quasi_rh/DOSSIER.md has the v2 locator and the abelian-descent scope note. Brauer induction plus Hecke zero-freeness giving zero-free Artin L-functions is classical.


## CON-0002 — Partial RH to height H (Platt-Trudgian) plus a zero-free half-plane gives explicit finite ranges for RH-equivalent inequalities whose RH proof uses only a |rho|^{-2}-weighted zero sum: Nicolas' primorial inequality and Johnston's integral criteria

*Mechanism:* M1-hypothesis-matching · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 2/5 · *Difficulty:* 2/5

**Source statements.**
- Platt-Trudgian 2021 (DB CAND-01775): every zero with 0<Im rho<=H=3,000,175,332,800 has Re rho=1/2.
- Nicolas 2012, arXiv:1202.0729: Lemma 2.1 (=Prop.1 of Nicolas 1983), for x>=121: K(x) - S^2(x)/(x^2 log x) <= log f(x) <= K(x) + 1/(2(x-1)), with f(x)=e^gamma log theta(x) prod_{p<=x}(1-1/p) and S=theta-x. Proof of Lemma 2.5: 'In [6,(17)-(19)], for x>1, it is proved that J(x) = -sum_rho F_rho(x)/rho - J1(x)' (an explicit-formula identity, used without RH), F_z(x)=x^{z-1}/((1-z)log x)+r_z(x) (Lemma 2.2). Prop 2.1 (2.19), under RH: log f(x) <= -(W(x)+2)/(sqrt x log x) + (2+beta)/(sqrt x log^2 x) + 4beta/(sqrt x log^3 x) + 1/(2(x-1)), W(x)=sum_rho x^{i Im rho}/(rho(1-rho)), |W|<=beta=0.04619... Nicolas' inequality N_k/phi(N_k) > e^gamma loglog N_k is equivalent to f(p_k)<1 and, for all k, to RH (Nicolas 1983).
- Johnston 2023, arXiv:2201.06184, Theorem 1.2: RH iff int_2^x (theta(t)-t)dt < 0 for all x>2 (Thm 1.1 likewise for pi-li). Lemma 2.6: under RH, int_2^x(psi(t)-t)dt <= 0.08 x^{3/2} for x>=3000 (via sum_rho 1/|rho(rho+1)| = 0.046209). Lemma 2.3: theta(x) < psi(x)-0.98 sqrt x for x>=121. Lemma 2.1 (Buthe): theta(x)<x for 2<=x<=1e19.
- Morrill-Platt, arXiv:1809.10813v4, Lemma 5: |theta(x)-x| <= sqrt x log^2 x/(8 pi) for 599<=x<=B=2.169e25, which is RH-quality from H=3e12 via Buthe.
- QRH claims H_Theta with Theta=7/8 (OpenAI 30 Sep 2026), 11/12 (5 Oct 2026), 0.874886647 (Liu/Xu-Sang refinements, conditional on OpenAI estimates); all unrefereed.

**Proposed link.** In both criteria, RH enters the 'global' part only through an absolutely convergent sum over zeros with weights 1/(rho(1-rho)) or 1/(rho(rho+1)), plus RH-quality bounds for theta near x (and near sqrt x). Without RH the unconditional identity reads J(x) = -sum_rho x^{rho-1}/(rho(1-rho)log x) - sum_rho r_rho(x)/rho - J1(x) (and similarly psi_1). Split the zeros at |gamma|=H. The zeros below H are on the line and give W_H(x) with |W_H| <= sum_{|gamma|<=H} 1/(1/4+gamma^2) < beta. Each zero above H has beta_rho <= Theta, hence contributes at most x^{Theta-1/2}/gamma^2 in W-units. So |E_off(x)| <= x^{Theta-1/2} S_H, with S_H := sum_{|gamma|>H} |rho(1-rho)|^{-1} <= 2(log(H/2pi)+1)/(2pi H) = 2.96e-12 (computed with explicit N(T)). The inequality survives while x^{Theta-1/2} S_H (1+delta) < M, where M is the RH margin: M ~ 2-beta-(2+beta)/log x ~ 1.90 for Nicolas, and 0.653-0.08 ~ 0.57 for Johnston.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) The S^2 term in Lemma 2.1 enters only the LOWER bound for log f, while Nicolas' inequality needs only the UPPER bound, so no global bound on theta(x)-x is needed. (2) RH-quality inputs: Lemma 2.3/2.4 need theta at x^{1/k}, k>=2, i.e. x^{1/2} <= B (fine up to x ~ 4.7e50). Johnston needs only psi-theta >= 0.98 sqrt x (same). (3) r_rho(x): for off-line zeros, integrate by parts twice to get |r_rho| << |rho| x^{beta-1}/(|1-rho|^2 log^2 x) (1 + 2/((1-beta)log x)). With 1-beta >= 1/(5.56 log gamma) (MTY) unconditionally, or 1-beta >= 1-Theta under H_Theta, the correction factor is (1+O(log H/log x))/log x: about 0.13 at log x ~ 54 unconditionally, about 0.02 under H_{7/8}. (4) |rho(1-rho)| >= gamma^2 for 0<beta<1, and paired zeros have Re(1/(rho(1-rho)))>0, so |W_H| < beta unconditionally. (5) Numbers (mpmath, H=3.000175e12, N(T) error constants 0.1038/0.2573/9.3675 as recalled from Hasanalizade-Shen-Wong; verify): S_H = 2.959e-12. Nicolas threshold X: 4.0e23 (Theta=1, before the 0.13 r_rho correction, ~3e23 after); 2.1e28 (Theta=11/12); 3.0e31 (Theta=7/8 or 0.874887). Johnston criterion: 3.6e22 / 1.2e27 / 1.2e30. Johnston's trivial range is x <= 1e19 (theta<x pointwise).

**New claim.** (A) Without QRH, only H and refereed explicit inputs: Nicolas' inequality N_k/phi(N_k) > e^gamma loglog N_k holds for all primorials with p_k <= ~3e23 (Nicolas computed c(N_k) up to p_k ~ 1e9). Johnston's int_2^x(theta-t)dt<0 and int_2^x(pi-li)dt<0 hold for 2<x<=~3e22, beyond the 1e19 Buthe range. (B) Under H_Theta: the ranges become X(H,Theta) = (M/((1+delta)S_H))^{1/(Theta-1/2)}, i.e. ~3e31 (Nicolas) and ~1e30 (Johnston) for Theta=7/8. (C) A quantitative answer to Nicolas' 2012 sec.4 challenge: under H_Theta, |c(N_k) - e^gamma(2+W_H(p_k))| <= e^gamma S_H p_k^{Theta-1/2}(1+o(1)) + O(1/log p_k). So if RH fails but QRH holds, c(N_k) cannot leave [e^gamma(2-beta)-eps, e^gamma(2+beta)+eps] before p_k ~ 1e31.

**Already proved.** Nicolas' explicit-formula identity for J(x) (unconditional) and his RH-conditional Prop 2.1; Johnston's RH-conditional Lemma 2.6; Platt-Trudgian H; Buthe/Morrill-Platt B. The thresholds above are my pre-proof estimates; no explicit theorem has been written or certified.

**Conjectural.** Part (A) is unconditional but not yet a proof: the lower-order constants (J1, J2, r_rho, F_{1/3} terms, the r_rho near beta~1) must be made explicit, and the values are estimates (+-30%). Part (B) depends on the unrefereed QRH. Whether (A) already appears in the literature is not settled (see prior art).

**Next step.** Rewrite Nicolas 2012 Lemmas 2.2-2.5 and Prop 2.1 with 'RH' replaced by 'RH up to H and beta<=Theta above H', carrying every constant. Evaluate S_H with interval arithmetic from an explicit N(T) bound and W_H with the first ~10^6 zeros plus a tail bound. Then certify the threshold X(H,Theta) for Theta in {1, 11/12, 7/8}. Repeat for Johnston's Lemma 2.6 (psi_1 explicit formula).

**Potential counterexamples.** The template fails for any criterion whose RH proof uses 1/|rho| (not 1/|rho|^2) weights, e.g. pointwise theta(x)<x or psi(x)-x bounds, where the tail sum over zeros diverges. It also fails if a lower bound requiring the S^2 term is needed (Robin; see M1-P3). An undetected error in the explicit N(T) constants changes S_H only by a factor 1+O(1/log H).

**Tests.** Compute c(N_k) directly for p_k up to 1e11 (extending Nicolas' 1e9). This checks the RH-zone constants but is not evidence for the analytic range. Cross-check S_H by direct summation of known zeros between 1e12 and 3e12 against the N(T) integral. Verify numerically that the identity J(x) = -sum F_rho/rho - J1 reproduces J(x) at x=1e6 with the first 1e5 zeros plus a tail bound.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** arXiv: abs:'Nicolas inequality' (5 hits, incl. 2509.11182 'On the verification of a Nicolas inequality', which concerns the sign of the Mertens error term, not partial-RH ranges); abs:"Robin's inequality" (25). zbMATH: ti:Nicolas & ti:inequality (3: 2008 withdrawn note, 2009 geometry, 2025 verification note); Nicolas 2024 NZJM 'A Robin inequality for n/phi(n)' (an RH-equivalent with log A = 1590171.6, not a range result). Full text: Johnston 2201.06184 handles x>1e19 only under RH and uses Buthe's 1e19 for small x; no partial-RH extension. Nicolas 1202.0729 sec. 4 poses the RH-false bound as an open challenge. Not checked: Nicolas 1983 and Robin 1984 originals (they may contain partial-verification remarks); Broughan's 'Equivalents of the RH' monograph.

**Verifier reasoning.** I checked Nicolas 1202.0729 directly. Lemma 2.1 is Prop 1 of [6] and unconditional. Lemma 2.2/(2.6) give r_z for general z. Lemma 2.5's identity J = -sum F_rho/rho - J1 is unconditional ([6,(17)-(19)]). The upper bound (2.19) is assembled without any S(x) bound, which confirms P2's point that Nicolas' inequality needs only the upper bound. In Johnston 2201.06184, Lemma 2.3 (theta < psi - 0.98 sqrt x, x>=121) is unconditional via RS62. Lemma 2.6 uses Ingham's psi_1 formula with sum 1/|rho(rho+1)| = 0.046209, and the final margin is -0.57 x^{3/2}. An off-line zero above H contributes <= x^{beta-1/2}/|rho(1-rho)| in W-units (x^{3/2}-units for Johnston). I recomputed S_H = 2.959e-12; the N(T) remainder changes it by a relative 2e-12. The thresholds reproduce: Nicolas 4.1e23 / 2.2e28 / 3.1e31 (Theta = 1, 11/12, 7/8) and Johnston 3.7e22 / 1.2e27 / 1.2e30. The r_rho factor (1+2/((1-beta)log x)) follows from (2.6); with the MTY zero-free region it grows like log gamma, which is still summable. So (A) and (B) are sound as pre-proof estimates; nothing is a theorem yet. Defects: (i) Corollary 2.1 (J-K >= F_{1/2}) uses RH-quality theta(sqrt t) for ALL t >= x, not just x^{1/2} <= B. The tail t > B^2 must be handled with an unconditional bound (e.g. Morrill-Platt Lemma 6, |theta-x| <= 1.405e-10 x for log x >= 55). Its contribution is about 3e-10/(B log B), negligible, but it is a missing step. (ii) Part (C) conflates two thresholds. E_off <= S_H p^{3/8} is already about 1.25 at p = 1e31, so c(N_k) could move by about e^gamma*1.25 = 2.2 there. Staying within eps = 0.1 of e^gamma[2-beta, 2+beta] is guaranteed only up to p ~ 2.5e27 under H_{7/8}; positivity (Nicolas' inequality) holds to ~3e31. (iii) The pi-li version of Johnston was asserted without the R_1-integral bookkeeping. It is plausible (same S_H structure against a margin of about 0.587 x^{3/2}/log x) but unchecked.

**Verifier correction.** In (A)/(B), add the unconditional tail treatment of psi(t)-theta(t) for t > B^2 in Nicolas' Lemma 2.4 / Corollary 2.1. Restate (C): under H_Theta, c(N_k) = e^gamma(2+W_H(p_k)+E) + O(1/log p_k) with |E| <= S_H p_k^{Theta-1/2}(1+O(log H/log p_k)). The band [e^gamma(2-beta)-eps, e^gamma(2+beta)+eps] is guaranteed only while S_H p_k^{Theta-1/2} < eps e^{-gamma} (about 2.5e27 for eps = 0.1, Theta = 7/8). Positivity c(N_k) > 0 holds to about 3e31. Mark the pi-li extension as unverified. All thresholds remain pre-proof estimates (+-30%).

**Prior art found by verifier.** None found. arXiv: 'Nicolas' AND primorial gives 1506.01041 and 1012.3613, both reformulations. Johnston 2201.06184 uses only Buthe's 1e19 range plus RH. No partial-RH range extension of Nicolas' or Johnston's criteria was located.


## CON-0004 — Luca-Sole's non-Robin counting bound #NR(x)=O(x^eps) improves to exp((log x)^{(1+Theta)/2+o(1)}) under a zero-free half-plane (15/16 for Theta=7/8). The method's phi-relaxation has an intrinsic floor of exp(c(log x)^{1/2})

*Mechanism:* M1-hypothesis-matching · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 2/5 · *Difficulty:* 2/5

**Source statements.**
- Luca & Sole, 'On Robin's inequality', INTEGERS 25 (2025) A8 (DB CAND-02415), Theorem 3: for every eps>0 and x>x(eps), #NR(x)=O(x^eps), NR(x)={7!<n<=x: sigma(n) >= e^gamma n loglog n}. Key step (DB 'Proof of Thm 3'): sigma(n)/n <= n/phi(n) forces -log P_2(n,x) = O(exp(-a(loglog x)^{3/5}(logloglog x)^{-1/5})), using Vinogradov's Mertens remainder at y = log x. Then n misses at most m << log x/exp((loglog x)^{1/2}) primes below log x.
- Nicolas 2012 arXiv:1202.0729 Lemma 2.1 (two-sided bounds for log f(x) with K(x) and S^2 terms) and Thm 1.1 (1.7): under RH, c(N_k) >= c(2) = 2.2086 for all k.
- QRH (unrefereed): zeta(s) != 0 for Re s > 7/8 (or 11/12).

**Proposed link.** The only analytic input of Luca-Sole Thm 3 is the size of the Mertens-product error at y ~ log x. Under H_Theta, delta_k := -log f(p_k) << p_k^{Theta-1}/log p_k (from Lemma 2.1: the K-term is ~ x^{Theta-1}beta/log x, and the S^2 term is << x^{2Theta-2}log^3 x). Write a violator's prime support S as T_k with missing set M and added set A, |A|=|M|=j. Then the violation forces dev(S) < delta_k and log(log n/theta(p_k)) < delta_k. So sum_A log a - sum_M log m + log(n/rad n) <= theta(p_k)(e^{delta_k}-1) =: Delta ~ p_k^Theta/log p_k. Since both A and M are packed near p_k, sum log(a/m) >~ j^2 log p_k/p_k, so j <~ p_k^{(1+Theta)/2}. The number of (M,A,n/rad n) is exp(O(j log p_k + Delta)), with p_k <= y ~ log x.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Normalisations: y is defined by theta(y)=log x, so p_k <= y ~ log x. Theta-1 < 0 is needed for delta_k -> 0. (2) Missing small primes cost 1/m >= delta_k unless m >= delta_k^{-1} = p_k^{1-Theta}, consistent with the counting. (3) Exponent check: Theta=1/2 gives 3/4. With VK input, delta = exp(-c(log p_k)^{3/5}...), which reproduces Luca-Sole's x^{o(1)} (j ~ p_k sqrt(delta)). That matches their m ~ log x exp(-(loglog x)^{1/2}) shape. (4) Floor: the set Phi(x) = {n<=x: n/phi(n) >= e^gamma loglog n} contains NR(x) and all primorials. Under RH, n = N_k t with t p_k-smooth and log t <= 1.23 sqrt(theta(p_k)) stays in Phi (by c(N_k) >= 2.2), so #Phi(x) >= exp(c(log x)^{1/2}). Thus no bound via n/phi(n) alone can beat exp((log x)^{1/2}); finiteness needs Robin's extra factor prod(1-p^{-(a+1)}).

**New claim.** Under H_Theta (Theta<1): #NR(x) <= #Phi(x) <= exp((log x)^{(1+Theta)/2}(loglog x)^{O(1)}). For the claimed QRH this gives exp((log x)^{15/16+o(1)}) (7/8) and exp((log x)^{23/24+o(1)}) (11/12), against x^{o(1)} unconditionally. Under RH, exp((log x)^{1/2+o(1)}) <= #Phi(x) <= exp((log x)^{3/4+o(1)}). Combined with M1-P3, NR(x) is empty up to exp(~5e22) unconditionally (if M1-P3 holds), so the counting bound only matters beyond that.

**Already proved.** Luca-Sole Thm 3 (refereed, INTEGERS 2025). Nicolas' lemmas. The derivation sketch above is mine and is not written out in full; the Luca-Sole full text was not available here (DB statements only).

**Conjectural.** The exponent (1+Theta)/2 rests on my counting of swap configurations (j <~ p_k^{(1+Theta)/2}). It must be checked against Luca-Sole's actual argument (their P_1,P_2,P_3 decomposition). Everything QRH-instantiated is conditional on unrefereed QRH.

**Next step.** Obtain the Luca-Sole paper (INTEGERS z8) and rewrite its eq. (7) with the H_Theta Mertens remainder. Then formalise the swap-counting lemma: #{(M,A): |M|=|A|=j, sum log(a/m) <= Delta} <= exp(O(j log p_k)) with j <= C sqrt(Delta p_k/log p_k). Also prove the RH lower bound #Phi(x) >= exp(c sqrt(log x)) via Dickman counting of p_k-smooth t.

**Potential counterexamples.** If Luca-Sole's argument loses more than my sketch (e.g. counting over all subsets of primes <= log x), the H_Theta improvement could be smaller, for instance exp((log x)^{1-(1-Theta)/2...}). If the Nicolas lower bound for log f had an extra S^2 contribution larger than p_k^{Theta-1}, delta_k would be dominated by it, but 2Theta-2 < Theta-1, so this is safe.

**Tests.** Enumerate Phi(x) exactly for x <= 1e12 (n/phi(n) depends only on rad n; search near primorial supports). Fit log #Phi(x) against (log x)^{1/2} and (log x)^{3/4} (illustrative only). Verify NR(x) is empty up to 1e12 as a sanity check of the code.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** zbMATH: Luca-Sole 2025 review read (Thms 1-3, x/loglog x, x/sqrt(log x), x^eps). arXiv abs:"Robin's inequality" (25) and abs:'Nicolas inequality' (5): no counting results under zero-free half-planes. The DB research-potential note for CAND-02415 already flags that 'any better zero-free region sharpens m'. The quantitative H_Theta exponent and the phi-relaxation floor were not found.

**Verifier reasoning.** I read the Luca-Sole full text (INTEGERS 25 (2025) A8). Their Thm 3 proof compares n with the fixed cutoff log x. Step (5) asserts sum over log x<p<4log x of 1/p = O(loglog(4x)-loglog x) = O(1/log x). The correct value is loglog(4 log x)-logloglog x = log(1+log4/loglog x), of order 1/loglog x. They wrote loglog(4x) for loglog(4log x). With the correct size, (6)-(7) give only m << log x/loglog x, and the x^{o(1)} count does not follow as printed. P4's comparison at p_k with k = omega(n) repairs this: pair added primes > p_k with missing primes <= p_k, using rad(n) >= N_k and n <= x. With the Vinogradov-Korobov delta_k it gives x^{o(1)}, so Thm 3 is true but needs this argument. I re-derived the H_Theta upper bound: delta_k << p_k^{Theta-1}log p_k, Delta ~ p_k^Theta, j << p_k^{(1+Theta)/2}, count exp(O(j log p_k + Delta)). The exponent (1+Theta)/2 is plausible but still a sketch. Error: the phi-relaxation 'floor' is understated. Under RH, Nicolas (1.7) gives mu_k = -log f(p_k) >~ 1.24/(sqrt p log p) for every k. Swapping j primes just below p_k for j just above costs about 8j^2 log p/p^2, so any j <= c p^{3/4}/log p keeps n in Phi. The C(2j,j)^2 choices give #Phi(x) >= exp(c(log x)^{3/4}/loglog x), not just exp(c sqrt(log x)). In the numerical illustration at p_k = 999983 and 9999991 (mu matches 2/(sqrt p log p)), 50/50 random swaps with j = 0.6 p^{3/4}/log p stay in Phi, giving log-counts of about 3800 and 18000 against sqrt p = 1000 and 3162. All swaps fail at j = p^{3/4}/log p. If RH fails, Nicolas' Omega+- for log f gives #Phi >= exp(p^{(1+vartheta)/2-eps}) infinitely often. So (1+Theta)/2 is the intrinsic limit of the n/phi(n) relaxation. The NR-empty claim inherits P3, which is unproved.

**Verifier correction.** Replace the floor statement. Under RH, log #Phi(x) = (log x)^{3/4+o(1)} (both bounds). If RH fails with sup Re rho = vartheta, then log #Phi(x) >= (log x)^{(1+vartheta)/2-o(1)} along a sequence. So no argument through n/phi(n) alone can beat exp((log x)^{(1+Theta)/2}), and an improvement must use Robin's extra factor prod(1-p^{-(a+1)}). Keep #NR(x) <= exp((log x)^{(1+Theta)/2+o(1)}) under H_Theta as a sketch to be written out. Record that Luca-Sole's printed step (5) is wrong (it should be of order 1/loglog x) and that the swap comparison at p_{omega(n)} is needed to make Thm 3's proof complete. Drop the dependence on M1-P3 or mark it unproved.

**Prior art found by verifier.** No counting of NR(x) or Phi(x) under zero-free half-planes was found (arXiv abs:"Robin's inequality", abs:'Nicolas inequality'; the Luca-Sole references). The swap and primorial comparison is classical in spirit (Nicolas 1983/2012; Choie-Lichiardopol-Moree-Sole 2007 'On Robin's criterion'). The gap in Luca-Sole's step (5) appears not to have been noted.


## CON-0005 — QRH instantiates Gorodetsky's theta-formulated smooth-number theorem: Psi(x,y) ~ x rho(u)K(-loglog x/log y) for y >= (log x)^{8+eps}, and any RH-failure oscillation window is confined to [(log x)^{8/7}, (log x)^8]. This addresses the repo's SHARP_NATIVE obstruction O4

*Mechanism:* M1-hypothesis-matching · *Verdict:* **needs_correction** · *Significance:* 2/5 · *Difficulty:* 1/5

**Source statements.**
- Gorodetsky, arXiv:2211.08973v6, Theorem 1.1 (p.2): with vartheta := sup Re rho. (1) If vartheta != 1 and x >= y >= (log x)^{1/(1-vartheta)+eps}, then Psi(x,y) ~ x rho(u) K(-loglog x/log y). (2) For A in (1/vartheta, 1/(1-vartheta)) there is x=x(y) with y=(log x)^{A+o(1)} and Psi(x,y) = x rho(u) exp(Omega_sgn(y^{vartheta+A^{-1}-1-eps})). (3) If vartheta != 1 and 2 log x <= y <= (log x)^{1/vartheta-eps}, then Psi(x,y) = x rho(u) exp(Theta_eps(log^2 x/(y log y))). K(t) = 1 - t int_1^inf {x}x^{-2-t}dx is decreasing.
- Hildebrand 1984 (Mathematika 31), as restated by Gorodetsky (1.3) and by de la Breteche-Tenenbaum's note: RH => Psi(x,y) = x rho(u) exp(O_eps(log(u+1)/log y)) for y >= (log x)^{2+eps}, and conversely. Unconditional range: log y >= (loglog x)^{5/3+eps}.
- Repo bridge BRG-SHARP_NATIVE-O4: 'Uniform growing-P control with relative margins once P extends to primes up to about (log X)^2 and beyond lives where the smooth-number analogue is RH-equivalent.'
- QRH (unrefereed): vartheta <= 7/8 (or 11/12; 0.874887 refined).

**Proposed link.** Gorodetsky's theorem is stated in terms of vartheta, and QRH supplies vartheta <= 7/8. Part (1) then holds for y >= (log x)^{8+eps}, against the unconditional exp((loglog x)^{5/3+eps}) and RH's (log x)^{2+eps}. Part (3) holds for 2log x <= y <= (log x)^{8/7-eps}. Part (2) shows that if RH fails with vartheta in (1/2, 7/8], the ratio Psi/(x rho(u)) is unbounded along y=(log x)^A for every A in (1/vartheta, 1/(1-vartheta)), a subset of (8/7, 8). The mechanism, as in the de la Breteche-Tenenbaum note: the correction is int_1^y dQ(t)/t^alpha with alpha = 1-1/c+o(1), which converges iff alpha > vartheta.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) vartheta is defined as sup over all zeros. QRH gives vartheta <= 7/8 for zeta, which is all that Theorem 1.1 uses (no Dirichlet input). (2) Thresholds: 1/(1-7/8)=8, 1/(7/8)=8/7; for 11/12: 12 and 12/11; for 0.874887: about 7.99 and 1.143. (3) Part (1) is a '~' statement with K != 1 for fixed c, i.e. Psi ≍ x rho(u). K(-t)>1 for t>0, so Pomerance's Psi(x,y) >= x rho(u) follows for y=(log x)^c with FIXED c>8 (for large x). On the range (log x)^{8+eps} <= y <= exp((loglog x)^{5/3}), where t = loglog x/log y -> 0, the o(1) of part (1) must be compared with K(-t)-1 ~ t. That is not given by the statement. (4) For the repo: this concerns Psi(x,y) (counts), not signed Mobius sums over smooth numbers. Any transfer to the native SHARP objects needs a separate argument; the repo marks this as RH-scale.

**New claim.** Conditional on the unrefereed QRH(7/8): Psi(x,y) ~ x rho(u) K(-loglog x/log y) uniformly for x >= y >= (log x)^{8+eps}. Psi(x,y) = x rho(u) exp(Theta_eps(log^2 x/(y log y))) for 2 log x <= y <= (log x)^{8/7-eps}. Pomerance's inequality Psi(x,y) >= x rho(u) holds for y=(log x)^c, c>8 fixed, and for y <= (log x)^{8/7-eps}, x large. Any failure of Psi ≍ x rho(u) at y=(log x)^c must have c in [2, 8]. This turns the repo's O4 statement 'growing-P control beyond (log X)^2 is RH-equivalent' into: polylogarithmic smooth-number control at (log X)^{8+eps} is available under QRH; below that exponent it is not available without more than QRH.

**Already proved.** Gorodetsky Thm 1.1 (preprint, v6 2023; publication status not checked) in vartheta-form; Hildebrand 1984 (RH case). The instantiation is a direct substitution, valid if QRH is.

**Conjectural.** QRH itself (unrefereed). The Pomerance-inequality consequence on the moving range c(x)->infinity below exp((loglog x)^{5/3}) needs an explicit rate in Gorodetsky's part (1). Whether the repo's native rough/smooth Mobius objects inherit any of this is open.

**Next step.** Extract from Gorodetsky's proof (Lemma 3.5, Remark 4, eq. (2.13)) the explicit error in part (1) under vartheta <= 7/8. Decide whether Pomerance's inequality then holds on the whole range y >= (log x)^{8+eps}. Record in the SHARP_NATIVE bridge that QRH moves the O4 threshold from 'RH-equivalent at (log X)^2' to 'QRH-available at (log X)^{8+eps}', without any signed-sum claim.

**Potential counterexamples.** None to the substitution itself. Misreading risk: Psi ≍ x rho(u) is not '~ x rho(u)' for fixed c, because the K factor matters. The Omega-window (2) is vacuous if RH holds.

**Tests.** Numerically evaluate the Hildebrand-Tenenbaum saddle-point approximation of Psi(x,y) against x rho(u)K(...) for log x in [50, 200] and y=(log x)^c, c in {3,5,8,10}. This is illustrative only, since at such heights the zeros below H dominate and behave as under RH.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** WebSearch: Hildebrand 1984 statement and Gorodetsky's resolution; the de la Breteche-Tenenbaum note (pdf read: Theta-dependent Omega argument via int dQ/t^alpha). Gorodetsky 2211.08973 full text: Theorem 1.1 is already in vartheta-form, so the only new content is the QRH instantiation and its translation for the host repo. arXiv QRH-consumer list (17 hits) contains no smooth-number application.

**Verifier reasoning.** Gorodetsky (J. Anal. Math. 151 (2023) 139-169; arXiv 2211.08973v6) Thm 1.1 is exactly as quoted, in vartheta-form, and parts (1)-(3) use only zeta's zeros. The thresholds 1/(1-7/8) = 8 and 1/(7/8) = 8/7 (12 and 12/11 for 11/12) are correct. The QRH instantiation is therefore a direct substitution into a theorem built to be parametric; Gorodetsky himself instantiates vartheta = 3/4 after Cor 3.1. Its content is essentially known. Errors: (a) 'Any failure of Psi ≍ x rho(u) at y=(log x)^c must have c in [2,8]' is false. Under QRH, part (3) gives Psi/(x rho(u)) = exp(Theta(log^2 x/(y log y))) -> infinity for 2log x <= y <= (log x)^{8/7-eps}. Thm 1.2(1) gives the same unconditionally for y <= exp((loglog x)^{3/5-eps})log x. Under RH, ≍ fails for every c < 2 (Thm 1.2(2)). If RH fails, part (2) gives Omega+- on (1/vartheta, 1/(1-vartheta)). So ≍ fails for all c < 2 in every case, holds for c > 8 under QRH, and only c in [2,8] is undetermined. (b) The proposal's open 'next step' is already settled in the paper. Remark 4, eq. (3.8), gives Psi = x rho(u)K(s-1)(1+O(1/log x + (log x)(loglog x)/y^{1-vartheta})) for y >= (log x)^{1/(1-vartheta)+eps}. Eq. (4.1) gives K(beta-1) = 1+Theta(log(u+1)/log y). Exactly as in his section 4.2 RH argument, these yield Pomerance's Psi >= x rho(u) on the whole moving range (log x)^{8+eps} <= y <= x^c, with Thm 1.2(1) beyond, not only for fixed c > 8. (c) The relevance to O4 is limited to the count Psi: nothing transfers to signed Mobius or native SHARP sums, as the proposal itself says.

**Verifier correction.** Restate: under QRH(7/8), Psi(x,y) ~ x rho(u)K(-loglog x/log y) and Psi(x,y) >= x rho(u) for all y >= (log x)^{8+eps}, x >= C_eps (Gorodetsky Thm 1.1(1), Remark 4 (3.8), (4.1), Thm 1.2(1)). Also Psi = x rho(u)exp(Theta(log^2x/(y log y))) >= x rho(u) for 2log x <= y <= (log x)^{8/7-eps}. Psi ≍ x rho(u) fails for every c < 2 regardless; the window QRH leaves open is c in [2,8]. Label the result a substitution into Gorodetsky's vartheta-form theorem, conditional on unrefereed QRH, with no consequence for signed sums.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Gorodetsky, 'Smooth numbers and the Dickman rho function', J. Anal. Math. 151 (2023) 139-169 (zbMATH 1545.11083; arXiv:2211.08973): Thm 1.1 in vartheta-form, Remark 4 eq. (3.8), section 4.2. Hildebrand, Mathematika 31 (1984), is the RH case.


## CON-0039 — Class-group exponents of imaginary quadratic fields: the claimed QRH gives E(-d) >= (delta - o(1)) log d/log log d effectively (Weinberger's GRH theorem with GRH replaced by QRH), while Siegel-zero exclusion alone covers exponents dividing 2^a * 3

*Mechanism:* M8-conjecture-generation · *Verdict:* **sound_but_known** · *Significance:* 6/5 · *Difficulty:* 2/5

**Source statements.**
- Frei-Sofos-Widmer arXiv:2610.18491v2, Corollary 1.2: if zeta_L satisfies QRH(delta) (no zeros with Re s > 1 - delta), then for x >= c(delta)(log Delta_L)^{1/delta}(log log Delta_L)^{2/delta} every conjugacy class C has between (1/4)(#C/#G)x/log x and (3/4)(#C/#G)x/log x unramified primes p with Frobenius C and x/2 < N p <= x; c(delta) is absolute and effective (Thm 1.1).
- OpenAI 7/8 manuscript Thm 1.1 (tex78 l.106-111) and paper2 Thm 1.1 (l.73-75): every Dirichlet L-function, zeta included, is zero-free for Re s > 7/8 (resp. 11/12), i.e. QRH(1/8) (resp. QRH(1/12)). Lean comparator DirichletSevenEighths claimed, not replayed by the Observatory.
- zbMATH review of Heath-Brown, 'Imaginary quadratic fields with class group exponent 5' (2008, Zbl 1268.11150): 'The Generalized Riemann Hypothesis implies that E(-d) >> (log d)/(log log d), but without assuming such a condition, it is unknown even whether E(-d) -> infinity'; E(-d) = 2 is known to occur for finitely many d (Euler's idoneal numbers), and E(-d) = 3 for finitely many d by Boyd and Kisilevsky.
- Elsenhans-Kluners-Nicolae arXiv:1803.02056 (abstract): 'Under the assumption that no Siegel zeros exist we compute all such D with E(D) a divisor of 8. We compute all D with |D| <= 3.1*10^20 such that E(D) <= 8.'
- Uniform Siegel-zero exclusion (unrefereed, several independent proofs): Guo arXiv:2610.30476, (1-beta) log q > 1/100 for all q >= 3; Banks arXiv:2610.15483, 3.0e-3; Kimura arXiv:2610.21511, 5.8e-4 (Lean kernel-checked) and class number <= 2000 completed.
- paper2 l.116-125 (Q5): Euler's 65 idoneal numbers are complete via Elsenhans-Kluners-Nicolae plus Grube (E(D) | 2).

**Proposed link.** Weinberger's argument: if p splits in Q(sqrt D) and the class of a prime ideal P above p has order e, then P^e = (alpha) with alpha not rational, since P^e != conj(P)^e. Hence p^e = N(alpha) = (x^2 + |D| y^2)/4 with y != 0, so p^e >= |D|/4 and E(D) >= log(|D|/4)/log p_s(D), where p_s(D) is the least split prime. FSW Cor. 1.2 with L = Q(sqrt D), whose zeta_L = zeta(s) L(s, chi_D) is covered by the Dirichlet QRH claim, gives p_s(D) << (log|D|)^{1/delta}(log log|D|)^{2/delta}. Siegel exclusion alone gives only polynomial split-prime bounds and cannot do this. It does give h(D) >= c'' sqrt|D|/log|D| effectively, which beats the trivially bounded 2-part plus any effective 3-torsion bound h_3(D) << |D|^{1/2 - eta}.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) QRH conventions: FSW's QRH(delta) means no zeros with Re s > 1 - delta, so OpenAI 7/8 is delta = 1/8 and 11/12 is delta = 1/12. Only Dirichlet L-functions are needed; the Cureton-Thorner Dedekind claim is not used. (2) D a fundamental discriminant with |D| > 4 (units +-1). The argument needs chi_D(p) = 1. If P is principal, p >= |D|/4, which is even stronger. (3) Siegel tier: E(D) | 2^a * 3 gives |Cl| <= 2^{a(omega(D)-1)} h_3(D), since the 2-rank is omega(D) - 1 (genus theory) and the 2-part has exponent dividing 2^a. Here 2^{a omega(D)} = |D|^{o(1)}, and h_3 << |D|^{1/3+eps} (Ellenberg-Venkatesh 2007) or |D|^{27/56+eps} (Pierce 2006), both believed effective (memory-level, not re-verified). Against h >= c'' sqrt|D|/log|D| this bounds |D| effectively. (4) Practical size: with delta = 1/8 and c(delta) = 1 (illustrative), E(D) = 3 forces log|D| <= log 4 + 24 log log|D| + 48 log log log|D|, i.e. |D| <~ 10^91. That is far beyond EKN's computed range 3.1*10^20. GRH (Bach, p <= 6 log^2|D|) would suffice there.

**New claim.** Proposition (conditional on the unrefereed QRH(delta) for real Dirichlet characters). For every fundamental D < -4: E(D) >= log(|D|/4) / [(1/delta) log log|D| + (2/delta) log log log|D| + log c(delta)] = (delta - o(1)) log|D|/log log|D|. In particular E(D) -> infinity effectively, an open problem unconditionally per the 2008 review. For every n, {D : E(D) | n} is finite with |D| <= exp((1+o(1))(n/delta) log(n/delta)), effectively computable. Proposition (conditional on uniform Siegel-zero exclusion only, no QRH). For every a >= 0, {D : E(D) | 2^a * 3} is finite and effectively computable. EKN's 'no Siegel zero' list for E(D) | 8 becomes unconditional modulo the 2026 exclusion, provided EKN's quantitative hypothesis matches the proved constant (to be checked).

**Already proved.** Weinberger's lemma (elementary); FSW Cor. 1.2 (conditional on QRH, refereeing status unknown); genus theory; effective h(D) lower bounds from Siegel exclusion (Kimura, Banks, Guo; unrefereed). The deduction is a few lines and is our derivation [O], not checked by a second reader.

**Conjectural.** Nothing beyond the inputs: the statements are conditional implications. The inputs (QRH 7/8 or 11/12; Siegel exclusion) are unrefereed 2026 claims, and the EV/Pierce effectivity is memory-level. Making the QRH bound practically complete for small exponents (e.g. E(D) = 3, 5) would need an explicit split-prime bound far stronger than (log|D|)^8, which is open.

**Next step.** (1) Make c(delta) in FSW Thm 1.1 / Cor. 1.2 explicit for L = Q(sqrt D), using an explicit zero count for L(s,chi_D) (e.g. Trudgian's N(T,chi) bounds) and a smoothed explicit formula with zeros restricted to Re rho <= 7/8, and compute D_3(1/8). (2) Read EKN to match their 'no Siegel zero' hypothesis with Guo/Banks/Kimura constants. (3) Check whether Heath-Brown's 2008 exponent-5 finiteness becomes effective with Siegel exclusion.

**Potential counterexamples.** The implication cannot fail if its inputs hold. The inputs could fail: an error in the 7/8 or 11/12 manuscripts would remove tier one, though tier two survives with Siegel exclusion. A numerical sanity check is that E(D) exceeds log(|D|/4)/log p_s(D) for all computed D. Fields with unusually large p_s(D), such as D with many small inert primes, are the extremal cases to tabulate.

**Tests.** T1: for all fundamental D with |D| <= 10^7, compute p_s(D) and record max p_s(D)/(log|D|)^2 (Bach scale) versus the QRH(1/8) scale (log|D|)^8, to show how far the QRH bound is from the truth. T2: verify E(D) >= log(|D|/4)/log p_s(D) on EKN-type data. T3: compute the explicit D_n(1/8) once c(delta) is explicit.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** arXiv API 2026-11-17: abs:"class group" AND exponent AND "imaginary quadratic" (1 irrelevant hit, 1809.05750); ti:"small exponent" (EKN 1803.02056; Aiken-Ross-Shvydkoy-Xue 2605.26020, a conditional classification from May 2026, before QRH). A full-text grep of FSW 2610.18491 finds no class-group exponent or Weinberger statement. None of the 17 arXiv records mentioning 'quasi-Riemann' (as of 2026-11-17) states it. zbMATH query ti:exponent & ti:class group & ti:quadratic gave Heath-Brown 2008 (Zbl 1268.11150), Chakraborty-Hoque 2020 and function-field papers. Weinberger's 1973 GRH theorem (Acta Arith. 22) is cited from memory and through the zbMATH review.

**Verifier reasoning.** Checked:
- FSW Cor 1.2 verbatim (arXiv:2610.18491v2): for x >= c(delta)(log Delta_L)^{1/delta}(log log Delta_L)^{2/delta}, between (1/4) and (3/4)(#C/#G)x/log x unramified primes with Frobenius C lie in (x/2,x]. FSW Thm 1.1 has an effective c1.
- The Weinberger lemma is EKN Lemma 1 (if p^c < |D|/4 then the order of the prime ideal above p exceeds c).
- The deduction E(D) >= log(|D|/4)/log p_s(D) >= (delta-o(1))log|D|/loglog|D| is correct, as is the bound |D| <= exp((1+o(1))(n/delta)log(n/delta)). The ~10^91 size estimate reproduces (log|D| ~ 210).

Prior art:
(1) zbMATH 0217.04202 (Weinberger 1973): if some neighbourhood of 1 contains no zero of any zeta function of an imaginary quadratic field, then E(-d) >> (log d)^{1/2-eps}. QRH(delta) implies that hypothesis. So 'E(D)->infinity under QRH' is already a corollary of Weinberger. The delta log/loglog sharpening is the routine GRH argument with FSW's split-prime bound plugged in.
(2) EKN cite Heath-Brown, Forum Math. 20 (2008), Thm 2: finitely many D with E(D)=2^r or 3*2^r, unconditionally but ineffectively. So the Siegel tier adds only effectivity, and that also needs an effective 3-torsion bound (not verified).
(3) The hedged claim about EKN fails as stated. EKN's 'no Siegel zero' hypothesis is quantitative: Lemma 3 needs L(s,chi_D) != 0 on [1-1/(4 log|D|), 1). The 2026 constants are Guo 1/100, Banks 3.0e-3 and Kimura 5.8e-4, so they do not imply it. EKN's E|8 list is therefore not unconditional modulo Siegel exclusion without recomputing their Tatuzawa-type bounds. QRH does supply the hypothesis, and paper2 l.116-125 already states the E|2 (idoneal) consequence that way.

**Verifier correction.** Credit the qualitative 'E(D)->infinity' to Weinberger 1973 (Acta Arith. 22, 117-124; Zbl 0217.04202), which needs only a zero-free neighbourhood of 1 and is implied by QRH. Present the bound E(D) >= (delta-o(1))log|D|/loglog|D| as the routine QRH analogue of Weinberger's GRH theorem. Credit finiteness for E | 2^a*3 to Heath-Brown 2008 Thm 2, with Siegel exclusion adding effectivity only if the 3-torsion bound used is effective. State that EKN's 'no Siegel zero' list (E | 8) requires zero-freeness on [1-1/(4log|D|),1). The 2026 Siegel-exclusion constants (<= 1/100) do not give this, so recomputation is needed; QRH does give it.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Weinberger, Acta Arith. 22 (1973) 117-124, Zbl 0217.04202: zero-free neighbourhood of 1 implies E(-d) >> (log d)^{1/2-eps}; GRH implies log/loglog. Heath-Brown, Forum Math. 20 (2008) 275-283, Thm 2: E in {2^r, 3*2^r} finite. Elsenhans-Kluners-Nicolae arXiv:1803.02056: Lemma 1 (Weinberger lemma), Lemma 3 (quantitative no-Siegel-zero, constant 1/4), Theorem 2. OpenAI paper2 l.116-125: QRH plus EKN gives completeness of the 65 idoneal numbers, and h(D) >> sqrt|D|/loglog|D|. FSW arXiv:2610.18491: Cor 1.2 and QRH-unconditional Ellenberg-Venkatesh torsion bounds; no statement on exponents.


## CON-0001 — A uniform zero-free half-plane for all Dirichlet L-functions supplies the hypothesis of the Chen-Gupta-Li 'good zero-free region' corollary. This gives a Linnik exponent L(Theta) <= 12/5+eps from refereed inputs and <= 7/3+eps with CGL; QRH-conditional

*Mechanism:* M1-hypothesis-matching · *Verdict:* **sound_but_known** · *Significance:* 4/5 · *Difficulty:* 2/5

**Source statements.**
- OpenAI, 'The Quasi-Riemann Hypothesis' (5 Oct 2026, paper2.pdf sha256 f919b578...), Theorem 1.1 (p.2): 'Every finite-order Hecke L-function over K [=Q(sqrt-3)] has no zeros in the half-plane Re s > 11/12. Thus every Dirichlet L-function, including zeta(s), has no zeros for Re s > 11/12.' Corollary 1.2 states only sup_{q<=x}|pi(x;q,a)-li(x)/phi(q)| << x^{11/12} log x (effective). The 30 Sep 2026 manuscript claims Re s > 7/8 (ledger LED-ZFR-1/2). Both are UNREFEREED; the Lean comparator for 7/8 was not replayed here.
- Chen-Gupta-Li, arXiv:2507.08296v2, Theorem 1.2 ('In all cases' bound): sum_{chi mod q} N(sigma,T,chi) <= (qT)^{o(1)}(q^{7/3(1-sigma)}T^{2(1-sigma)} + (qT)^{30/13(1-sigma)}), 1/2<sigma<1 (unrefereed). Same page: 'Huxley [8] obtained A = 12/5, Jutila 2.460, Forti-Viola 2.463, Montgomery [Thm 12.1] 2.5' for sum_{chi mod q} N(sigma,T,chi) <= (qT)^{A(1-sigma)+o(1)} uniformly in q,T.
- Chen-Gupta-Li, arXiv:2507.08296v2, Corollary 1.4: if prod_{chi mod q} L(s,chi) != 0 for sigma >= 1-eta(q,T), |Im s|<=T, with eta(q,x^{eps/3}T_0) log x -> infinity, then psi(x+h;q,a)-psi(x;q,a) = (1+o(1)) h/phi(q) provided (for all h and q) h/phi(q) >= x^{1/2+eps} q^{1/6} + x^{17/30+eps}. The paper says only 'this can be deduced via a smoothed explicit formula'.
- Montgomery, Topics in Multiplicative Number Theory, Thm 12.1, as recorded in Pintz-Althofer arXiv:2610.15559 eq. (4.1): N_q(sigma,U) << (qU)^{3(1-sigma)/(2-sigma)} log^9(qU), 1/2<=sigma<=3/4, uniformly in q>=1, U>=2.
- Heath-Brown 1992 (DB CAND-00740), Theorem 6: P(a,q) << q^{5.5}. Xylouris 2018 'Linnik's constant is less than 5' (zbMATH 2018). Bruna arXiv:2603.25612: GLH => P(a,q) << q^{2+eps}.

**Proposed link.** The conclusion of QRH (no zero of any L(s,chi) in Re s > Theta, uniformly in q and t) is exactly the hypothesis of CGL Cor. 1.4, with eta = 1-Theta constant, so eta*log x -> infinity trivially. More generally, consider the smoothed explicit formula sum_n Lambda(n) 1_{n=a (q)} w(n/x) = x w^(1)/phi(q) - (1/phi(q)) sum_chi chibar(a) sum_rho w^(rho) x^rho. Under H_Theta the zeros only contribute at sigma <= Theta. With a family density bound N_q(sigma,T) <= (qT)^{A(sigma)(1-sigma)+o(1)} and T=q^{eps}, the zero sum is << max_{1/2<=sigma<=Theta} (q^{A(sigma)+eps}/x)^{1-sigma}. Since 1-sigma >= 1-Theta > 0, this is a power saving once x >= q^{L(Theta)+eps} with L(Theta) := sup_{1/2<=sigma<=Theta} A(sigma). No log-free density or Deuring-Heilbronn input is needed, and an exceptional zero is excluded by H_Theta itself.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Domain and quantifiers. The OpenAI claim covers every Dirichlet character, imprimitive ones included, uniformly in q and t. That is the 'for all q' uniformity CGL Cor 1.4 and Linnik need. (2) Density normalisation. CGL, Huxley and Montgomery all sum over chi mod q (fixed modulus), with parameter qT. This is the right family; Huxley's hybrid sum over q<=Q (Q^2T) would be too weak. (3) Binding point. Refereed inputs give Ingham-Montgomery 3/(2-sigma) on [1/2,3/4] (=12/5 at 3/4) and Huxley's q-analogue 3/(3sigma-1) <= 12/5 on [3/4,1). So L(Theta)=3/(2-Theta) for Theta<=3/4 and 12/5 for 3/4<=Theta<1. With Montgomery alone (2/sigma for sigma>=4/5) the sup is 5/2 at sigma=4/5. With CGL's uniform 7/3 bound, L = 7/3 for Theta >= 5/7. (4) Re-derived CGL's condition h/phi(q) >= x^{1/2+eps}q^{1/6}: it is (q^{7/3}T_0^2/x)^{1-sigma} = o(1) with T_0=x/h. With h=x this needs x >= q^{7/3+eps}. The second term needs q <= x^{13/30}, i.e. 30/13 < 7/3, so it does not bind. (5) Truncation at T=q^{eps} is legitimate with smooth compactly supported w (w^(rho) << |rho|^{-k}). Prime powers contribute O(sqrt x log x) << x/phi(q) once x >> q^{2+eps}. (6) Effectivity: OpenAI states an effective constant, and the density bounds are effective, so P(a,q) <= C_eps q^{L+eps} effectively.

**New claim.** Conditional on H_Theta (all Dirichlet L-functions zero-free in Re s > Theta, Theta<1 fixed), P(a,q) <<_{eps,Theta} q^{L(Theta)+eps} effectively for all q and (a,q)=1. Here L(Theta) = 3/(2-Theta) for Theta <= 3/4 and L(Theta) = 12/5 for 3/4 <= Theta < 1 (refereed density inputs), improved to 7/3 using CGL (unrefereed). In addition, psi(x;q,a) = (x/phi(q))(1+O(x^{-delta})) uniformly for q <= x^{5/12-eps} (x^{3/7-eps} with CGL). This is an individual-modulus range of Bombieri-Vinogradov strength 5/12 against BV's averaged 1/2 and Siegel-Walfisz's (log x)^A. With h << x it also gives the CGL short-interval statement h/phi(q) >= x^{1/2+eps}q^{1/6}+x^{17/30+eps}. Instantiated with the unrefereed OpenAI 7/8 or 11/12 claims: Linnik exponent 12/5 (refereed density) or 7/3 (CGL). For comparison, unconditionally the exponent is 5 (Xylouris), under GLH 2+eps (Bruna), and under GRH 2+eps.

**Already proved.** The density estimates (Montgomery Thm 12.1; Huxley 1975 A=12/5 per CGL's account) are refereed. CGL Thm 1.2 and Cor 1.4 are unrefereed preprints. The deduction 'zero-free half-plane + family density => Linnik exponent = sup of the density exponent below Theta' is a standard explicit-formula argument, and I checked its parameter bookkeeping above. QRH itself is NOT proved (unrefereed AI manuscripts).

**Conjectural.** Everything downstream depends on the unrefereed QRH, and the 7/3 variant also on unrefereed CGL Thm 1.2. Huxley 1975's exact fixed-modulus statement (log powers, primitive vs all characters) was taken from CGL's summary and not re-read.

**Next step.** Write a one-page lemma: for w smooth on [1,2], sum_{n=a(q)} Lambda(n)w(n/x) = x w^(1)/phi(q)(1+O(x^{-delta})) under H_Theta + (D_A) for x >= q^{A+eps}, with explicit delta = (1-Theta)eps/(A+eps)-type dependence. Then read Huxley, Acta Arith 26 (1975) 435-444 to confirm the fixed-modulus 12/5 form, and record L(Theta) as a function in the ledger under ZFR/ZDE consumers.

**Potential counterexamples.** (a) If the QRH manuscripts fail (e.g. Prop 3.1 of the 11/12 paper), the statement is void; only the conditional implication H_Theta => L(Theta) survives. (b) If Huxley's q-analogue holds only for primitive characters or with q^{eps} losses, the conclusion is unaffected (eps absorbed). (c) The explicit Siegel-zero exclusion claims (Banks: (1-beta)log q >= 3e-3 for q >= 1e10; Guo c=1/100) do NOT give this. They leave real zeros in [1-0.348/log q, 1-0.003/log q] possible, so Heath-Brown/Xylouris's non-exceptional branch (lambda_1 >= 0.348), which is the bottleneck, is untouched. The exceptional-zero branch is the favourable one (Heath-Brown 1990: q^{3+eps}).

**Tests.** Symbolic: recompute sup_{sigma<=Theta} A(sigma) for the piecewise refereed table and for CGL, at Theta in {2/3, 3/4, 0.874887, 7/8, 11/12}. Check that 12/5 is attained at sigma=3/4 and 7/3 for Theta >= 5/7. Re-derive CGL Cor 1.4's three conditions from their Thm 1.2 (done by hand above for the 'all cases' form). Numerical sanity only (non-evidential): least primes for q <= 1e6 are far below q^{12/5}.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** arXiv API: abs:'quasi-Riemann' AND abs:Linnik (0 hits); abs:'quasi-Riemann hypothesis' (15 hits; consumers are Goldbach sets 2610.15559/2610.26756, Chebotarev/class groups 2610.18491, 3-manifolds 2611.06705, Visser 2610.20058, Markov triples 2610.36069, small polynomials 2611.09031; none states a Linnik exponent); abs:'Linnik's constant' (4); 'least prime' AND 'arithmetic progression' (31, incl. 2511.05631 P(q)=O(q^5); Bruna 2603.25612 GLH => 2+eps; Kandhil-Languasco-Moree 2607.14515 GRH+pair correlation). zbMATH: ti:Linnik 2015-2026 and ti:'least prime'&progression 2015-2026 (no QRH-Linnik item). Full texts checked: OpenAI 11/12 Cor 1.2 and the consequences paragraph (only x^{11/12} error, least nonresidue, class number, idoneal numbers; no density combination); Pintz-Althofer 2610.15559 sec. 4-5 (uses Montgomery Thm 12.1 with H_theta, but for Goldbach only); CGL sec. 1. No prior statement of 'QRH => P(a,q) << q^{12/5+eps}' was found. Folklore-level deduction; novelty is in the explicit matching.

**Verifier reasoning.** Source statements check out. The CGL v2 abstract and Thm 1.2 ('In all cases' bound q^{7/3(1-sigma)}T^{2(1-sigma)}+(qT)^{30/13(1-sigma)}) are as quoted. So are CGL eqs (1.4) (fixed-modulus Ingham, 3(1-sigma)/(2-sigma)) and (1.5) (Halasz-Montgomery-Huxley, 3(1-sigma)/(3sigma-1)), the line 'Huxley A=12/5, Jutila 2.460, Forti-Viola 2.463, Montgomery 2.5', Cor 1.4 with its 'smoothed explicit formula' remark, and Pintz-Althofer (4.1). I recomputed L(Theta)=sup over sigma<=Theta of the density exponent. Refereed Ingham+Huxley gives 12/5 for Theta>=3/4. Montgomery Thms 12.1+12.2 alone give 5/2. Ingham alone gives 8/3 at 7/8. With CGL it is 7/3 for Theta>=5/7, including CGL's own q1=q form at T=q^eps. The explicit-formula bookkeeping is right. Under H_Theta the (qT)^{o(1)} losses are absorbed because 1-sigma>=1-Theta. Since A(sigma)(1-sigma)<=1, a sharp-cutoff truncation also works. H_Theta excludes beta_1, and all constants are effective. Prior art: Thorner-Zaman Thm 2.3 is stated for an arbitrary zero-free region function delta(T). It uses the refereed log-free density N_q(sigma,T) << (qT)^{(12/5+eps)(1-sigma)} (their Thm 2.1, from Huxley+Jutila) and gives the asymptotic once lambda h/phi(q) >= x^{7/12+eps}. With delta = 1-Theta, h = x and lambda = 1, this is exactly P1's statement: phi(q) <= x^{5/12-eps} with power saving x^{-eps^2(1-Theta)}, hence P(a,q) << q^{12/5+eps}, by their own section 1.2 Linnik template. Their Remark 2.4 (exponent c7 gives theta = 1-1/c7) plus CGL's 7/3, whose non-log-free losses are harmless under H_Theta, gives q <= x^{3/7} and Linnik 7/3. The repo's own DOSSIER (audits/quasi_rh/DOSSIER.md l.276) already states the mechanism, but with Ingham's 3/(2-sigma) only (8/3, 36/13). Its l.273 claims the H_Theta PNT-in-AP range is q <= x^{1-Theta-eps} and 'does not improve BV'. P1 is a correct, folklore-level substitution, and useful mainly as a correction to those two DOSSIER lines. The caveat about Huxley's fixed-modulus form is resolved by TZ (2.1). Caveat (c) is also confirmed: Siegel-zero exclusion alone gives TZ Remark 1.3 (theta=7/12, lambda=1), but the error term exp(-c log x/log q) has no power saving, so it does not give Linnik 12/5.

**Verifier correction.** Cite Thorner-Zaman Thm 2.3 + Remark 2.4 as the source of the deduction, with delta(T) = 1-Theta. Record: H_Theta => P(a,q) << q^{12/5+eps} effectively (refereed inputs, given QRH), or 7/3+eps with CGL. psi(x;q,a) = (x/phi(q))(1+O(x^{-delta})) for phi(q) <= x^{5/12-eps} (x^{3/7-eps} with CGL). Correct DOSSIER l.273 (range is x^{5/12}, not x^{1-Theta}) and l.276 (12/5, not 8/3). Everything remains conditional on the unrefereed QRH.

Z. 306 (2024) Paper 54 (zbMATH 1546.11120; arXiv:2108.10878): Thm 2.1 (log-free 12/5), Thm 2.3 (PNT in APs for any zero-free region), Remark 2.4 (improved density exponent).
