## CON-0016 — Linnik's constant under the quasi-Riemann hypothesis: P(a,q) ≪ q^{7/3+ε} (q^{12/5+ε} from refereed density inputs), not q^{8} or q^{12}

*Mechanism:* M4-strengthen-weaken · *Verdict:* **sound_but_known** · *Significance:* 4/5 · *Difficulty:* 2/5

**Source statements.**
- OpenAI 11/12 manuscript (openai/math preprints/The-Quasi-Riemann-Hypothesis-October-5-2026/paper2.pdf), Theorem 1.1: every Dirichlet L-function (ζ included) has no zero in Re s>11/12. Corollary 1.2 (l.82-95): sup_{q≤x}|π(x;q,a)−li(x)/φ(q)| ≪ x^{11/12} log x (effective). The Observatory's audit_S3.md F9 calls it 'not load-bearing, standard sketch consistent'. The 7/8 manuscript (Sept 30 2026), Thm 1.1, makes the same claim with θ=7/8.
- Heath-Brown 1992 (CAND-00740), Theorem 6 (Sec. 1 p.5; proof Sec. 15): P(a,q) ≪ q^{5.5}, effective. Lemma 15.1 (Sec. 15) bounds K−2Σ_{χ≠χ0}Σ_ρ|F((1−ρ)L)| through the near-1 zero counts N_0(Λ_r). Theorem 5: N(λ) ≤ (1+ε)(67/(6λ))(e^{73λ/30}−e^{16λ/15}) for λ ≤ (1/3) log log log q.
- Chen–Gupta–Li, arXiv:2507.08296v2, Theorem 1.2 (p.3; ledger LED-ZDE-11): Σ_{χ mod q} N(σ,T,χ) ≤ (qT)^{o(1)}(q^{7(1−σ)/3}T^{2(1−σ)} + (qT)^{30(1−σ)/13}). CGL §1 caveat: exceptional zeros and weak q-aspect zero-free regions limit the arithmetic applications.
- Montgomery 1971 (Topics in Multiplicative Number Theory, LNM 227, Ch. 12): Σ_{χ mod q} N(σ,T,χ) ≪ (qT)^{3(1−σ)/(2−σ)} log^c(qT). Huxley 1975 (Acta Arith. 26), q-aspect, cited by CGL as the previous record 12/5: exponent 3(1−σ)/(3σ−1) for σ ≥ 3/4.
- Serre 1981 (CAND-00728), Theorem 5 (Sec. 2.5, (21_R),(22_R)): under GRH, π_C(x) ≥ 1 for x ≥ c_5 (log d_L)^2 (Oesterlé c_5=70). For abelian L/Q of conductor q this is P(a,q) ≪ (φ(q) log q)^2.

**Proposed link.** Under QRH(θ) every zero of every L(s,χ) mod q has 1−θ ≤ β ≤ θ, so there is no exceptional or near-1 zero. In Linnik's three-principle method (zero-free region, log-free density, repulsion), Heath-Brown's Sec. 15 machinery exists only to handle zeros with λ=(1−β)log q = O(1). Under QRH every λ ≥ (1−θ)log q. Take a smooth weight w ≥ 0 supported in [1,2] and x=q^L. The explicit formula gives Σ_{n≡a(q)} Λ(n)w(n/x) = W(1)x/φ(q) − (1/φ(q))Σ_χ χ̄(a)Σ_ρ W(ρ)x^ρ + O(x^{1/2}log q/φ(q)), where W is the Mellin transform of w and decays faster than any power of |γ|. Zeros with |γ| > T=q^η contribute O(x q^{−10}). Zeros with β ≤ 1/2 contribute ≪ x^{1/2}·qT log(qT) = o(x) if L > 2+4η. Zeros with β in [1/2,θ] contribute ≪ x log x · max_σ (q^{A_q(σ)+o(1)}/x)^{1−σ}. Because 1−σ ≥ 1−θ, the q^{o(1)} loss is absorbed: q^{o(1)} ≤ q^{o(1)(1−σ)/(1−θ)}. So the error is o(x) once L > A_q(σ)+ε for every σ ≤ θ. The binding exponent is max(2, sup_{1/2≤σ≤θ} min(3/(2−σ), 3/(3σ−1), 7/3)). That equals 12/5, attained at σ=3/4, without CGL, and 7/3 with CGL, binding on σ in [5/7,16/21]. Both values hold for every θ in [16/21,1), including 7/8 and 11/12. The naive route from Corollary 1.2 needs x^{1−θ} > q log^2 x, giving L = 1/(1−θ) = 8 or 12. That is worse than the unconditional L=5 (Xylouris thesis; Zhao arXiv:2511.05631).

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Normalisation: QRH is stated in the classical s-plane (β ≤ θ). Its reflection gives β ≥ 1−θ, so |ρ| ≥ 1−θ and Σ 1/|ρ| ≪ log^2 q, with no zeros near 0. (2) Family: CGL, Montgomery and Huxley all count Σ over all χ mod q, imprimitive included. Imprimitive zeros are the primitive ones plus zeros on Re s=0 from the Euler factors at p|q, which contribute x^0 and are negligible. (3) Height: only T=q^η is needed, because W decays fast. CGL at T=q^η gives q^{(7/3+O(η))(1−σ)+o(1)}, and 30/13 < 7/3, so the first term dominates. (4) Uniformity: the (qT)^{o(1)} factor must be uniform in σ in [1/2,θ]. Usual log-power losses are uniform. (5) Main term: ~ x/φ(q) ≥ x/q. Prime powers contribute ≪ √x log x = o(x/q) for L>2. (6) Effectivity: QRH excludes Siegel zeros, so the constants are effective if QRH's θ and the density constants are effective. (7) If θ < 3/4 (or < 5/7 with CGL) the supremum is smaller. This is irrelevant for the claimed θ = 7/8 and 11/12.

**New claim.** Conditional theorem (proposed). Assume QRH(θ) for all Dirichlet L-functions, for some fixed θ<1. Then for every ε>0, every q ≥ q_0(θ,ε) and every (a,q)=1: Σ_{x<p≤2x, p≡a(q)} log p ~ x/φ(q) for all x ≥ q^{L*+ε}. Here L* = max(2, sup_{1/2≤σ≤θ} A_q(σ)) and A_q is any admissible q-aspect density exponent. Consequently P(a,q) ≪_{θ,ε} q^{12/5+ε} using refereed inputs (Montgomery 1971 with Huxley 1975), P(a,q) ≪ q^{7/3+ε} with CGL (unrefereed), and q^{30/13+ε} for q that are q^η-smooth. Under QRH plus the q-aspect density hypothesis on [1/2,θ] the bound is q^{2+ε}, the GRH exponent up to ε. Equivalently, the GRH hypothesis in Serre 1981 Theorem 5, restricted to abelian L/Q, can be weakened to QRH + DH at the cost of ε, or to QRH alone at exponent 7/3.

**Already proved.** The ingredients are published: the explicit formula (Davenport Ch. 19-20), Montgomery's q-aspect density, Huxley's 12/5 q-aspect estimate, and Heath-Brown's framework. CGL 7/3 is unrefereed. GRH ⇒ P(a,q) ≪ (φ(q)log q)^2 is classical, and GLH ⇒ P ≪ q^{2+ε} is Bruna, arXiv:2603.25612, Thm 1.1. The deduction above is a standard argument that I have sketched but not written line by line.

**Conjectural.** QRH itself, which is unrefereed and AI-generated; experts are reported to 'largely agree' on 11/12. Whether any θ-dependence enters the implied constant beyond (1−θ)^{-1} factors is unchecked. The improvement from 12/5 to 7/3 rests on unrefereed CGL.

**Next step.** Write the full proof for a general admissible exponent function A_q(σ), with the smoothing and the explicit-formula truncation done explicitly. Then check that the uniformity in σ of the o(1) in CGL Theorem 1.2 holds as stated (read CGL §§5–7). Then extend to the least prime ideal in a ray class over Q(√−3), using OpenAI's Hecke QRH and a large-sieve density estimate for Hecke characters, and compare with Frei–Sofos–Widmer arXiv:2610.18491, which treats the least prime splitting under Dedekind QRH.

**Potential counterexamples.** There is no counterexample to the conditional implication. The only way it fails is if the density estimates fail uniformly at small height. A hypothetical clustering of about q^{12/5(1−σ)} zeros at height ≤ q^η near σ=3/4 is allowed by Huxley's bound, and this is exactly what makes 12/5 the barrier without CGL. If QRH is false the theorem is vacuous. If a correct QRH holds only for primitive characters of conductor up to some bound, the argument needs every conductor dividing q.

**Tests.** (1) Re-derive the binding σ: run sup_σ min(3/(2−σ), 3/(3σ−1)) and with 7/3 added. Hand computation gives 12/5 at σ=3/4, and 7/3 on [5/7,16/21]. (2) Sanity check: set θ=1/2 (GRH) to recover L=2+ε. Remove QRH to recover the failure, since the λ=O(1) zeros make Σ x^{β−1} of size 1. (3) Numerically compare the true P(a,q) for q up to 10^6 with q^{7/3}. This is only an illustration: a finite range carries no evidential weight.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** WebSearch for 'Linnik constant quasi-Riemann hypothesis zero-free strip density' and for '"quasi-Riemann hypothesis" 2026 least prime Linnik'. arXiv API queries: abs:"least prime" AND progression; abs:Linnik (2025-11 to 2026-11, 15 hits); abs:quasi AND Riemann AND hypothesis (all 2026 QRH-consumer papers). No statement was found that QRH gives L < 5. Nearest work: Bruna 2603.25612 (GLH ⇒ 2+ε, via a GLH-derived zero-free region plus DH, which he calls folklore); Kandhil–Languasco–Moree 2607.14515 (pair correlation beyond the GRH bounds); Zhao 2511.05631 (L=5 unconditional); CGL (who note that exceptional zeros block applications). The 2026 QRH consumers cover Goldbach (Li–Liu 2610.26756; Pintz–Althöfer), Chebotarev and least splitting primes (Frei–Sofos–Widmer 2610.18491), PNT (Fiori–Jaskari 2609.23222), and short intervals (Visser 2610.20058). None of them treats Linnik's constant. zbMATH was not searched.

**Verifier reasoning.** The conditional deduction is correct. Checks: (i) the binding exponent. min(3/(2-σ), 3/(3σ-1)) is maximised where the increasing and decreasing branches cross, at σ=3/4, value 12/5. With CGL's 7/3 cap, 3/(2-σ)=7/3 at σ=5/7 and 3/(3σ-1)=7/3 at σ=16/21, so the sup is 7/3 on [5/7,16/21]. (ii) Absorbing the (qT)^{o(1)} loss needs only 1-σ ≥ 1-θ > 0. Zeros with β ≤ 1/2 cost qT log(qT)·x^{-1/2}=o(1) once L>2. (iii) Imprimitive and principal characters only add zeros on Re s=0. I read CGL arXiv:2507.08296 (current version) §1 and Theorem 1.2 matches the quoted form, including the smooth-q bound (qT)^{30/13(1-σ)}. However, the claimed new theorem is already stated in CGL. Their caveat about exceptional zeros is followed directly by Corollary 1.4 (p.4, 'Primes in arithmetic progressions under a good zero-free region'). Assume Π_χ L(s,χ) ≠ 0 for σ ≥ 1-η(q,T), |t| ≤ T, with η log x → ∞; QRH(θ) gives this with η=1-θ. Then ψ(x+h;q,a)-ψ(x;q,a) ~ h/φ(q) provided h/φ(q) ≥ x^{1/2+ε}q^{1/6} + x^{17/30+ε}. If q is x^{13/30-ε/2}q^{-1}-smooth, h/φ(q) ≥ x^{17/30+ε} suffices. Taking h=x gives primes ≡ a (mod q) in (x,2x] for x ≥ q^{7/3+ε}, and for x ≥ q^{30/13+ε} when q is smooth. These are exactly the 7/3 and 30/13 claims, under a hypothesis much weaker than a fixed half-plane. CGL say this follows from Theorem 1.2 'via a smoothed version of the explicit formula', the same argument as the proposal. Two parts are folklore: the 12/5 version, which is the same deduction with Huxley's density, and QRH+DH ⇒ q^{2+ε} (Bruna arXiv:2603.25612 calls GLH ⇒ DH folklore and proves GLH ⇒ 2+ε). So the proposal re-derives known conditional statements. Their QRH specialisation is a one-line corollary.

**Verifier correction.** Cite CGL arXiv:2507.08296 Corollary 1.4 with h=x. It gives P(a,q) ≪ q^{7/3+ε}, and q^{30/13+ε} for suitably smooth q, under any zero-free region of width η with η log x → ∞; QRH is one such hypothesis. Restate the proposal as 'QRH supplies the hypothesis of CGL Cor. 1.4', not as a new conditional theorem. CGL is unrefereed. The 12/5 route via Huxley (refereed inputs) is the standard deduction. Effectivity under QRH holds because Siegel zeros are excluded.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Chen–Gupta–Li, arXiv:2507.08296, Corollary 1.4 (p.4), checked. Bruna, arXiv:2603.25612, §1 (GLH ⇒ DH called folklore; GLH ⇒ P ≪ q^{2+ε}), checked. Heath-Brown 1992 framework. Arguably the classical folklore that 'no near-1 zeros plus a log-free density exponent A gives Linnik exponent A'.


## CON-0034 — If the 2026 Landau-Siegel exclusion holds, the exceptional branch is empty: conditional results on zeta zeros (Conrey-Iwaniec lacunary corollaries, Heath-Brown twin primes) become vacuous, and the class-number motive of the small-gap programme is spent

*Mechanism:* M7-negative-synthesis · *Verdict:* **sound_but_known** · *Significance:* 4/5 · *Difficulty:* 2/5

**Source statements.**
- Conrey-Iwaniec, arXiv:1607.03288 ('Critical zeros of lacunary L-functions'; theorem DB 'Conrey 2020'), Def. 1.1/2.2: chi is eps-exceptional if L(1,chi) log|D| <= eps; a sequence of discriminants is exceptional if eps(D) -> 0. Thm 2.1: N_00(T) = {1 + O(delta)} N(T) for any delta >= eps^{1/5} and |D|^{1/delta} <= T <= |D|^{delta^4/eps} (absolute, non-explicit O-constant). Cor 2.3/2.4: along an exceptional sequence, 100% of the zeros of each L(s,psi), and of zeta, in those windows are simple and critical.
- Guo, arXiv:2610.30476 (unrefereed), Thm 1.1, as recorded in audits/claims_2026/SIEGEL/ARCHITECTURE.md l.40: every real zero of a primitive quadratic L(s,chi) of conductor q >= 3 satisfies (1-beta) log q > 1/100 (c = 1/64 in App. A). Banks 2610.15483 Thm 1.1: delta_0 = 3.0e-3 for q >= 10^10. OpenAI Thm 1 and Kerr: an absolute c.
- SIEGEL audit ARCHITECTURE.md l.272-282 (Observatory derivation [O], not in Guo): from Guo's Thm 1.1, L(1,chi) >= 0.00767/log q for q >= 10. Computed minima of L(1,chi) log q: 0.664 (D=-3); 1.2534 over |D| >= 10 (D=-163) in the computed range; 0.6927 for real D <= 2000.
- Lagarias-Rodgers, arXiv:1905.12123, Sec. 2.2: showing that a positive proportion of normalised gaps are <= delta for some delta < 1/2 'would be of extraordinary interest since it would have as an implication that there are no Landau-Siegel zeros (see [Conrey-Iwaniec, Acta Arith. 103 (2002)])'. The Alternative Hypothesis is introduced as the model in which this fails.
- Farmer-Ki, arXiv:1002.1616, abstract: many zeros of zeta' close to the critical line imply many closely spaced zeros of zeta, which gives a lower bound for class numbers of imaginary quadratic fields.
- Dong-Wattanawanichkul-Zaharescu, arXiv:2605.24363, Remark 1.4: the mollified-moment supremum condition forbids Landau-Siegel zeros.

**Proposed link.** These works either assume exceptional characters (Conrey-Iwaniec Cor 2.3/2.4, Heath-Brown 1983, Chowla-under-Siegel) or aim to exclude them through zero spacing (Conrey-Iwaniec 2002, Farmer-Ki, the small-gap programme). A uniform bound (1-beta) log q >= c gives eps(D) = L(1,chi) log|D| >= c' > 0 for all D. That removes the exceptional branch of the Deuring-Heilbronn / lacunarity dichotomy.

**Compatibility checks.** Conrey-Iwaniec's chi is the quadratic character of Q(sqrt D) with conductor |D|. Guo covers every primitive quadratic character of conductor >= 3, so the domains match. eps(D) is L(1,chi) log|D|; Guo's statement concerns (1-beta) log q. The bridge L(1,chi) >= 0.00767/log q is the Observatory's own derivation and needs review. Even without it, a uniform zero gap plus the standard argument gives L(1,chi) >> 1/log q, which is enough for eps(D) bounded below. The quantitative residue of Thm 2.1: with eps >= 0.00767 the condition delta >= eps^{1/5} forces delta >= 0.378; with the computed minima eps of about 0.66-0.69 it forces delta >= 0.92. The window [|D|^{1/delta}, |D|^{delta^4/eps}] is nonempty iff delta^5 >= eps. Siegel exclusion concerns real zeros near s = 1 only. It does not touch AH, which concerns zeta's vertical spacings at all heights, and it does not imply not-AH.

**New claim.** (a) Suppose some uniform Landau-Siegel exclusion is correct (Guo, Banks, Kerr or OpenAI). Then no exceptional sequence exists, and every theorem with the hypothesis 'eps(D) -> 0 along a sequence' (or L(1,chi) << (log|D|)^{-A}) is vacuous: Conrey-Iwaniec Cor 2.3 and 2.4; Heath-Brown 1983 (Siegel zeros imply infinitely many twin primes); Chowla under a Siegel zero (Benli-Goel-Twiss-Zaman); Friedlander-Iwaniec exceptional-character results. Obstruction form: no argument whose only route to a statement about zeta zeros passes through the exceptional branch of a Deuring-Heilbronn or lacunarity dichotomy can establish that statement. The dichotomy proofs stay valid but collapse to their non-exceptional branch. (b) Quantitative residue. The unconditional Conrey-Iwaniec Thm 2.1 with eps(D) >= 0.00767 gives N_00 >= (1 - C delta) N only with delta >= 0.378, or delta >= 0.92 for the computed small-D minima. It is non-trivial only if the non-explicit absolute constant satisfies C < 1/delta, so as stated no unconditional positive proportion can be extracted. (c) The small-gap programme (Conrey-Iwaniec 2002; Farmer-Ki 2010), whose stated motive was 'gaps < 1/2 the mean spacing would exclude Landau-Siegel zeros', reaches that target by other means. Small-gap results below 1/2 (current unconditional positive-proportion constant 0.7892, Lamzouri 2026) keep their value for GUE versus AH, but no longer for class numbers. AH loses its main arithmetic motive yet stays unrefuted, because the Lagarias-Rodgers correlation compatibility stands.

**Already proved.** The Conrey-Iwaniec statements (arXiv text, read at L1 in the theorem DB). Guo, Banks, Kerr and OpenAI (unrefereed; Observatory L2 audit found no error; no Lean replay). The vacuity in (a) is pure logic once the premise holds. The arithmetic of the delta-window in (b) is elementary.

**Conjectural.** Correctness of the 2026 exclusion theorems. The audit's derivation L(1,chi) >= 0.00767/log q. Whether Conrey-Iwaniec 2002 or Farmer-Ki contain unconditional by-products worth keeping, e.g. explicit spacing statements, once the class-number application is gone.

**Next step.** (1) Grep the theorem DB conditional_on fields for 'Siegel', 'exceptional' and 'Landau-Siegel', and tag each record 'vacuous if SIEGEL holds', with the minimal c it needs. (2) Make the O-constant of Conrey-Iwaniec Thm 2.1 explicit to decide whether any unconditional statement survives at eps of about 0.0077-0.7. (3) Compute eps(D) = L(1,chi) log|D| for all fundamental |D| <= 10^7 to record the empirical minimum.

**Potential counterexamples.** If the exclusion proofs fail, (a) collapses. Results that assume only a fixed small eps (not eps -> 0) stay non-vacuous when eps exceeds the proven lower bound. Conditional results about non-quadratic exceptional zeros (higher-rank Siegel zeros, Thorner 2608.12257) are not covered.

**Tests.** Theorem-DB grep (planned). Exact computation of eps(D) for small D with PARI or flint (planned). A consistency check against the audit's computed minima: 0.664 (D=-3), 1.2534 (|D| >= 10, imaginary), 0.6927 (real D <= 2000).

**Prior-art search (proposer).** arXiv API: 'all:alternative hypothesis AND all:Siegel' (none); 'spacing AND zeros AND Siegel AND class' (found Farmer-Ki 1002.1616). Reception sources in history/recent_developments_2024_2026.md Sec. 6 (Tao: 'The Siegel zero problem has been solved in a number of ways'; Granville: 'It's all moot now'). I found no catalogue of vacated conditional zero-statistics results. Possibly new as a catalogue; it adds no new mathematics.

**Verifier reasoning.** Verified Conrey-Iwaniec 1607.03288 Thm 2.1: (2.2) and (2.3) with absolute constants, delta>=eps^{1/5} and |D|^{1/delta}<=T<=|D|^{delta^4/eps}. Def. 2.2 and Cors 2.3 and 2.4 also match. The Lagarias-Rodgers Sec. 2.2 quote ('extraordinary interest ... no Landau-Siegel zeros') is verbatim. SIEGEL ARCHITECTURE confirms Guo Thm 1.1 (1/100, with c=1/64 in App. A) and Banks delta0=3.0e-3. It also confirms the Observatory's own derivation L(1,chi)>=0.00767/log q for q>=10, and the computed minima 0.664, 1.2534 and 0.6927. Arithmetic: 0.00767^{1/5}=0.378, 0.66^{1/5}=0.920, and the window is nonempty iff delta^5>=eps. The vacuity claim is pure logic once the premise is granted, and a uniform real-zero gap gives L(1,chi) >> 1/log q by the standard argument. It is known in substance. Conrey-Iwaniec themselves note after Thm 2.1 that (2.3) 'is meaningful only if eps(D) is sufficiently small. This does not hold in reality since the Riemann Hypothesis implies L(1,chi) >> 1/log log|D|'. The 2026 results, if correct, only make this unconditional. The reception sources say the same (Tao 2026-10-19; Granville: 'It's all moot now'). Minor errors: (i) a hypothesis 'L(1,chi) << (log|D|)^{-A}' is vacuated only for A>1, or in the o(1/log|D|) form. With A=1 and a fixed constant above 0.00767 it is not vacuous. (ii) 'Small-gap results below 1/2 (current unconditional constant 0.7892)' is misleading. 0.7892 > 1/2 (Lamzouri 2611.14486), and no unconditional positive-proportion small-gap result below 1/2 is known. (iii) The premise is unrefereed, with no Lean replay, and the explicit 0.00767 bridge is the Observatory's own derivation. The catalogue adds no mathematics.

**Verifier correction.** State the A>1 (or little-o) condition for the vacuity of power-of-log hypotheses. Say that no unconditional positive-proportion result for gaps below 1/2 exists. Present (a) as already anticipated by Conrey-Iwaniec's remark under GRH, and now unconditional if the 2026 exclusion theorems are correct. Present (b) as: with the absolute constant left non-explicit, Thm 2.1 yields no quantitative unconditional statement.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Conrey-Iwaniec 1607.03288, remark after Thm 2.1 (vacuous under RH). Reception statements recorded in history/recent_developments_2024_2026.md Sec. 6 (Tao; Granville, 'It's all moot now'). Heath-Brown 1983 and Conrey-Iwaniec 2002 as the conditional and motivational sources.


## CON-0027 — One phrase, many constants: the Siegel-zero thresholds behind classical theorems versus the 2026 exclusion constants

*Mechanism:* M6-compatibility · *Verdict:* **sound_but_known** · *Significance:* 3/5 · *Difficulty:* 1/5

**Source statements.**
- McCurley 1984, as quoted in Kadiri Thm 2.2 and LED-LFUN-1: the product over chi mod q has at most one zero in sigma >= 1-1/(9.645908801 log max(q, q|t|, 10)), and such a zero is real and simple, for a real chi.
- Lu-Zaman-Zhao, arXiv:2602.03626v2, Thm 1.1 and Cor.1.2: for q <= 10^10, L(sigma,chi) != 0 for sigma >= 1-1/(5 log q), so there are no exceptions in McCurley's region.
- Heath-Brown 1992, Thm 1: at most one zero in sigma >= 1-0.348/log q, |t| <= 1, for q large. Thm 4 gives Deuring-Heilbronn repulsion for lambda <= 0.348. Xylouris (arXiv:0906.2749, Thm 1.2): 0.440.
- EKN arXiv:1803.02056, Lemma 3 and Theorem 4: 'no Siegel zero' means L(s,chi) != 0 on [1-1/(4 log|D|), 1). EKN Lemma 2, quoting Tatuzawa [Lemma 9, Thm 2]: '0 < eps < 1/2. There is at most one |D| >= max(e^{1/eps}, e^{11.2}) such that L(1,chi) <= 0.655 eps/|D|^eps.'
- Bondarenko-Heap arXiv:2608.07399 (LED-SPACING-9): 'Siegel zeros of large quality' means beta_j = 1-1/(E_j log q_j) with E sufficiently large.
- Guo arXiv:2610.30476v1, Thm 1.1 (1/100 for all q >= 3) and Remark 1.2 (1/64). Banks arXiv:2610.15483, Thm 1.1 (3.0e-3). Kimura arXiv:2610.21511, Thm 1 (5.811e-4 for all q; 0.005965 for log q >= 35.0131; 0.01015 for log q >= 10^4).
- LED-LFUN-4 caveat: Tatuzawa's constant is quoted as eps/10 (Harcos-Thorner) versus 0.655 eps (Wikipedia), and the discrepancy is unresolved.

**Proposed link.** Write every exceptional-zero clause as '(1-beta) log q >= c_target'. The 2026 claims supply c_2026 <= 1/64 at best (Guo Rem.1.2). A clause is removed only if c_2026 >= c_target. The targets are: EKN 1/4; Lu-Zaman-Zhao 1/5 (computed only for q <= 1e10); McCurley 1/9.6459 = 0.10367; Heath-Brown 0.348; Xylouris 0.440; Bondarenko-Heap 1/E0 with E0 large; and Tatuzawa-type eps/4 at eps = 1/log|D|, which gives 1/4. Conditional theorems whose hypothesis is (1-beta) log q -> 0, or <= 1/E with E > 64, become vacuous.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) All 2026 constants are lower bounds proved by different routes (1/100, 1/64, 3e-3, 5.8e-4 up to 0.0102), so they do not conflict. (2) Normalization: an imprimitive chi mod q shares its zeros in Re s > 0 with the primitive chi* mod q* <= q, and log q* <= log max(q, q|t|, 10). The exclusion therefore transfers to McCurley's modulus normalization. (3) Exception-free region: all non-exceptional zeros already satisfy beta < 1-1/(R log M). The exceptional real zero satisfies 1-beta > c/log M. Hence the region with R* = max(R, 1/c) is exception-free. (4) Tatuzawa: 0.655 eps >= eps/10, so the two quoted constants are compatible. Harcos-Thorner's eps/10 is a weaker valid form if 0.655 eps holds. EKN is a third secondary source, and it gives the eps-range 0 < eps < 1/2 and the lemma numbers. (5) QRH comparison: a half-plane theta gives c(q) = (1-theta) log q. This meets c_target for log q >= c_target/(1-theta): EKN's 1/4 needs q >= e^8 under 31/32.

**New claim.** (i) An exception-free explicit standard region for all q >= 3, modulo the 2026 exclusions. Every Dirichlet L(s,chi) mod q has no zero in sigma >= 1-1/(R* log max(q, q|t|, 10)), with R* = 9.645908801 for q <= 1e10 (Lu-Zaman-Zhao) and R* = 64 for q > 1e10 (Guo Rem.1.2; 100 with Guo Thm 1.1; at most 167.7 from Kimura's Lean constant once log q >= 35.0131). (ii) Not removed: McCurley's exceptional clause for q > 1e10 (needs c >= 0.1037, 6.6 times Guo's best); Heath-Brown and Xylouris's asymptotic one-zero clauses (need 0.348 and 0.440); EKN Thm 2's hypothesis (1/4). The Deuring-Heilbronn regime lambda in (1/64, 0.348] remains in Linnik-type proofs, which is why Linnik's constant L = 5 is unchanged. (iii) Vacuous: every theorem that assumes infinitely many real zeros with E > 64. This includes Bondarenko-Heap's mu < 0.4733 and Heath-Brown-type twin-prime consequences of Siegel zeros.

**Already proved.** The composition argument in (i) and the threshold comparisons are elementary and proved here. Guo's statement, its Remark 1.2 and the EKN lemmas were read in full text.

**Conjectural.** Guo's 1/64 (Remark 1.2, Appendix A) is unrefereed and was not checked. Kimura's constants in the range 23 <= log q < 28.68 are not tabulated, so only the all-q value 5.811e-4 (R* <= 1721) is certified there.

**Next step.** Add a 'c_target' column to ledger rows LFUN-1, LFUN-2, LFUN-6, LFUN-8, LFUN-10, ZFR-14 and SPACING-9. Check whether an explicit PNT for arithmetic progressions with no exceptional-character term (e.g. extending Bennett-Martin-O'Bryant-Rechnitzer beyond 1e5) follows from R* = 64 with acceptable constants.

**Potential counterexamples.** A source whose 'Siegel zero' definition uses a different modulus normalization (for example log d_K for non-quadratic fields, as in Banks's base-field version with c(k)) would need its own translation. In Heath-Brown's Linnik proof the case 1/64 < lambda1 < 0.348 is real and must still be handled.

**Tests.** Numerical consistency: no known real zero of any real primitive L(s,chi) violates the strongest stated c (Lu-Zaman-Zhao's c = 1/5 for q <= 1e10 contains all the others).

**Prior-art search (proposer).** The Observatory ledger LFUN-06 notes that the exclusion 'does not prove the standard region with McCurley's c = 1/10 for all q'. Guo Sec.1.1 compares constants but states no region. An arXiv search (abs:'zero-free region' AND 'Siegel' AND explicit, 2026) found no paper stating the exception-free R* = 64 region. EKN's c = 1/4 has not been compared with the 2026 constants anywhere I found.

**Verifier reasoning.** The constants are as cited. Heath-Brown 1992 Thm 1 gives 0.348 (|t| <= 1, q large), and Thm 4 is the Deuring-Heilbronn statement for lambda <= 0.348. Xylouris 0906.2749 Thm 1.2 gives 0.440 with effective q0 (Liu-Wang 0.364). EKN Lemma 2 quotes Tatuzawa as 0.655 eps/|D|^eps with a real zero in (1-eps/4,1), so the target is 1/4. LZZ Thm 1.1 gives 1/(5 log q) for quadratic chi mod q <= 1e10. Guo has 1/100 and 1/64 in Remark 1.2. Banks has 3.0e-3 for q >= 1e10, extended to all q via LZZ. Kimura's three values are as cited. Bondarenko-Heap assume a family with E = inf E_j 'sufficiently large'. The composition in (i) is valid. Non-exceptional zeros lie outside McCurley's R = 9.6459 region and hence outside any thinner one. The exceptional real zero of a real chi (primitive conductor q* <= q) satisfies 1-beta >= c/log q* >= c/log max(q,10). So R* = max(R, 1/c) is exception-free: 64 under Guo Rem. 1.2, 100 under Guo Thm 1.1, 333 under Banks, 167.6 or 1721 under Kimura. The q <= 1e10 case with R = 9.6459 follows from LZZ Thm 1.1. The threshold comparisons in (ii) are correct arithmetic (0.10367/0.015625 = 6.6). The mechanism is classical and already recorded elsewhere.

**Verifier correction.** (1) Prior art: LZZ 2602.03626 Corollary 1.2 already states the q <= 1e10 exception-free region (with c = 1/10). The Observatory's audits/claims_2026/SIEGEL/DOSSIER.md Sec. 5 item 2 already states the all-q region with c' = min(c1, c). What is new is only the explicit R* = 64 McCurley-normalized form. (2) Linnik: the conclusion 'L = 5 unchanged' is right but the stated reason is not. In Heath-Brown/Xylouris the binding cases are those without an exceptional zero (large lambda1). Exceptional zeros help through Deuring-Heilbronn, so no Siegel-exclusion constant could improve L. The surviving regime lambda1 in (1/64, 0.348] is not why. (3) Bondarenko-Heap is vacuous only if their implicit E0 exceeds 1/c (64 under Guo Rem. 1.2, about 1721 under Kimura's all-q Lean constant). Their E0 is unspecified, so say 'vacuous provided E0 > 1/c'. Heath-Brown's twin-prime hypothesis (lambda -> 0) is vacuous under any c > 0. (4) Kimura states (lean_grid.txt) that his grid constants exceed Banks' 3e-3 for log q > 9.88, so for 23 <= log q < 28.68 the certified value is better than the all-q 5.811e-4. Only two values are stated as theorems.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Lu-Zaman-Zhao 2602.03626 Cor. 1.2. Observatory SIEGEL DOSSIER Sec. 5 item 2. Classical Page/Landau consequence (Davenport Ch. 14). McCurley 1984 (R = 9.645908801). Heath-Brown 1992 Thms 1 and 4. Xylouris 2009 Thm 1.2.


## CON-0009 — The large-sieve quasi-RH route: the missing lemma for the critical line is pointwise square-root cancellation (RH-equivalent); every mean-square extraction is capped by the density of trivialising rows

*Mechanism:* M2-missing-lemmas · *Verdict:* **sound_but_known** · *Significance:* 2/5 · *Difficulty:* 2/5

**Source statements.**
- OpenAI quasi-RH 11/12 manuscript (2026-10-05; sha256 f919b578...), Sec. 3, l.711-716, as reconstructed in audits/quasi_rh/ARCHITECTURE.md item 1(3): with Prop. 3.1, sum_{0<N u<=H} |A_u(D)|^2 << D^{1+eps} H for H = D^{1+theta}, and J ~ Y/log Y rows u = p^6 with Y = H^{1/6}: |A_1|^2 << D^{1+eps}H/J + D^2/Y^2 = D^{(11+5 theta)/6+eps} + D^{(5-theta)/3}, so A_1 << D^{11/12 + 5 theta/12 + eps}. Item 1(4): H cannot drop to D (the duality gap vanishes at H = D). Within this extraction 11/12 is optimal.
- audits/quasi_rh/audit_S3.md l.218: 'So Prop. 3.1 gives exactly 11/12 through these rows and nothing better. This is consistent with the paper's remark that the 7/8 paper changes the extraction.'
- Same manuscript, Littlewood principle (ARCHITECTURE item 1(1), l.726-757): A_1(D) = sum_{(n,S)=1} mu(n) nu(n) W(Nn/D) << D^{beta+eps} for all smooth W implies L_K^S(s,nu) != 0 on Re s > beta.
- frontiers/research_programs.md ZFR items 3(3) and 5(f): Liu arXiv:2610.12234 Sec. 34 proves 0.874957... optimal within the 'exponent comparison system' of the 7/8 method. Ji arXiv:2611.16665 (quartic, Q(i)) reaches only 599/600.

**Proposed link.** Generalise the extraction. Let U be a set of rows u with N u <= H on which A_u(D) = A_1(D) + O(D^{beta_0}) (for u = p^6, chi_n(p^6) = 1 when p does not divide n). Let |U| = H^{nu}. A mean-square bound sum_{N u <= H} |A_u|^2 << D^{1+eps} H then gives |A_1|^2 << D^{1+eps} H^{1-nu}, i.e. beta = 1/2 + (1+theta)(1-nu)/2 (theta -> 0: beta -> 1/2 + (1-nu)/2). With nu = 1/6 this is 11/12, matching l.711-716. Reaching beta = 1/2 + eta needs nu >= 1 - 2 eta (theta -> 0). The critical line needs nu = 1: almost all rows must reproduce A_1, which is false for a non-degenerate sextic family. The only RH-capable statement is the pointwise bound A_1(D) << D^{1/2+eps}, which by the Littlewood principle is GRH for L_K^S(s,nu) itself.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) The identity uses only the displayed inequality chain from the audit (variance D per row, J = H^{1/6}/log H rows). I re-derived the 11/12 = (1 + 5/6)/2 bookkeeping and it matches the audit text. (2) The 7/8 manuscript changes the extraction (amplified rows u_0 a^6). I did NOT check whether its effective row density corresponds to nu = 1/4 (which the formula would need for 7/8); that is flagged as unchecked. (3) Liu's optimality is a statement about the 7/8 comparison system, consistent with but not implied by the nu-formula. (4) Scope: the identity concerns large-sieve mean-square extractions; it says nothing about methods that do not average over a twisted family.

**New claim.** (a) For trivialising-row extractions with a square-root mean-square bound: beta(theta, nu) = 1/2 + (1+theta)(1-nu)/2. So beta < 3/4 requires nu > 1/2 (theta -> 0), and beta -> 1/2 requires nu -> 1. (b) Missing lemma for the route to reach any fixed sigma_0 < 0.8749 (below Liu's optimum): either a set of 'near-trivialising' rows of density H^{nu}, nu > 1/4 (if the 7/8 extraction corresponds to nu = 1/4), where A_u = A_1 + O(D^{beta}), or an inverse/fourth-moment input outside Liu's system. (c) The route-closing missing lemma, A_1(D) << D^{1/2+eps} for all smooth W, is RH-equivalent for the Hecke L-function: no gain. Large-sieve quasi-RH methods are therefore structurally unable to reach the critical line, consistent with the expert view quoted in history/recent_developments_2024_2026.md Sec. 6.

**Already proved.** Nothing new about zeros. The exponent identity is bookkeeping on the audited inequality chain. The 11/12 and 7/8 theorems themselves are unrefereed. The 11/12 Prop. 3.1 is under audit (ARCHITECTURE item 3, unresolved point U1).

**Conjectural.** The effective nu of the 7/8 extraction. Whether any arithmetic mechanism produces near-trivialising rows of density greater than H^{1/4}. Both questions are open, and the nu-framework is only a heuristic guide outside the 11/12 extraction.

**Next step.** (1) From the 7/8 manuscript's amplified extraction, compute its effective nu and check whether beta = 1/2 + (1-nu)/2 reproduces 7/8 and Liu's 0.87496 (as a consistency test of the framework). (2) If it does, add the identity to the quasi-RH dossier as the structural ceiling statement (beta >= 1/2 + (1 - nu_max)/2).

**Potential counterexamples.** Extractions that do not average |A_u|^2 uniformly, e.g. weighted amplifiers with sign-sensitive coefficients, could beat the nu-formula. The formula is an upper-bound heuristic for one design pattern, not a theorem about all large-sieve methods.

**Tests.** Symbolic: plug the 7/8 paper's row set and amplifier lengths into the formula. Numerical toy: with the Observatory's zomega.py (audits/quasi_rh/architecture_checks), count u with N u <= H for which chi_n(u) = 1 for all n coprime to a fixed S, confirming the H^{1/6} density of trivialising rows.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** Read audits/quasi_rh/ARCHITECTURE.md (items 1-4) and audit_S3.md (l.218, l.240); the frontier ZFR entry for Liu and Ji. No external search beyond those; the OpenAI manuscripts were not reopened in this session.

**Verifier reasoning.** The audit text checks out. ARCHITECTURE.md item 1(3) gives |A_1|^2 << D^{1+eps}H/J + D^2/Y^2 with J ~ H^{1/6}, hence 11/12. It also states '11/12 = (1+5/6)/2: the 1 is the variance D per row ... the 5/6 is the loss from using only H^{1/6} sixth-power rows'. audit_S3.md Sec. 4 ('Tightness of the extraction') derives beta <= 11/12 + 5theta/12 from H^{1/6} D^{2beta} << D^{1+eps}H and notes that Liu Thm 10.8 with all a^6 gains only log Y.

The proposal's formula beta = 1/2 + (1+theta)(1-nu)/2 is correct algebra: |A_1|^2 << D^{1+eps}H^{1-nu} with H = D^{1+theta}, and nu = 1/6 reproduces 11/12. But it is a one-parameter restatement of that audit text and of standard amplification bookkeeping: an amplifier of relative length H^nu saves H^{nu/2}, so reaching the square-root level needs a full-length amplifier. The audit's 'Consistency with GRH' also already states that pointwise A_1 << D^{1/2+eps} is GRH for L_K^S(s,nu) (Littlewood principle), which is part (c).

Part (b) is stale and part (c) overreaches. The nu = 1/4 reading of the 7/8 extraction is unchecked, as the proposal admits. Claimed exponents are already below 0.8749 (Liu B_tw 0.8740585, Chang-Wang 0.8744621, Xu-Sang 0.8748867; research_programs.md l.79, l.412). The frontier critique says the 'optimal within the comparison system' results were bypassed four or more times and that the H^{1/6} row limit belongs only to the 11/12 argument. 'Structurally unable to reach the critical line' is a heuristic about one design pattern, not a theorem.

**Verifier correction.** Present (a) as a restatement of audit_S3 Sec. 4 and ARCHITECTURE item 1(3), not as new. Remove the 'sigma_0 < 0.8749' missing-lemma target, which other papers already claim (unrefereed). Downgrade (c) from 'structurally unable' to 'within uniform-trivialising-row mean-square extractions'.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Observatory audits: /home/user/riemann/research/exploratory/riemann-observatory/audits/quasi_rh/ARCHITECTURE.md item 1(3)-(4); /home/user/riemann/research/exploratory/riemann-observatory/audits/quasi_rh/audit_S3.md Sec. 4 (tightness of the extraction, consistency with GRH, Liu arXiv:2610.26835 Thm 10.8 and Remark 10.9). For the general principle: the amplification method (Duke-Friedlander-Iwaniec; Iwaniec-Sarnak), where an amplifier saves the square root of its effective length.


## CON-0013 — Hybrid Euler–Hadamard product (Gonek–Hughes–Keating; Gonek 2012; Conrad) applied to the host repository's corrected-Euler-product cut: an exact identity, RH/quasi-RH equivalence, and non-locality

*Mechanism:* M3-transplantation · *Verdict:* **sound_but_known** · *Significance:* 2/5 · *Difficulty:* 2/5

**Source statements.**
- Host repository research/integrated/CURRENT_RESULTS.md §9: C_X(s) = e^gamma (s−1)L exp[−Ein((s−1)L)], L = log X; the line-one theorem ||F̌_{X,1} − F_1||_2 << exp(−c sqrt(log X)); reviews/B/REPORT.md §10 (DC5: 'On Re(s)=1 it cancels the full continuous prime-tail factor'; DC6: 'Uniform boundedness of the combined corrected products on Re(s)>1/2 is still open').
- Host repository OPEN_CUTS.md §6: 'local boundedness of the corrected products together on compact subsets of Re s > 1/2 ... Concrete output: a signed discrepancy estimate with explicit dependence on distance from the critical line'.
- Gonek–Hughes–Keating (2007), Theorem 1 (Sec. 1, eqs (5)–(7)): zeta(s) = P_X(s)Z_X(s)(1+error), Z_X(s) = exp(−sum_rho U((s−rho)log X)), U built from E_1 (theorem DB CAND-01207).
- Gonek 2012, arXiv:0704.3448, Theorem 3.1 (unconditional, with smoothed Lambda_X and F_2 = 2E_2(2z) − E_2(z), including the pole term) and Theorem 3.5 with converse (Sect. 3, eq. (28)): zeta(s) = P_X(s)(1+o(1)) in sigma >= 1/2 + C loglog t/log X under RH; conversely this forces at most finitely many zeros there.
- Conrad 2005, Theorem 3.3 (Sec. 3): for Re s0 > 1/2, a nonzero limit of the partial Euler product at s0 is equivalent to convergence of the log-series, and then L continues holomorphically and without zeros to Re s > Re s0; Lemma 3.1: the prime-power middle sum is o(1) for Re s > 1/2.

**Proposed link.** Exact identity, checked: Ein(z) = E_1(z) + log z + gamma (to 1e-30 at three complex points), so C_X(s) = exp(−E_1((s−1)log X)). The integrated explicit formula, with signs checked numerically (difference 1e-8 at s = 2 and 1e-6 at s = 1.5+10i, using 200 zero pairs and X = 10006), is: sum_{n<=X} Lambda(n)/(n^s log n) = log zeta(s) − E_1((s−1)L) + sum_rho E_1((s−rho)L) + sum_k E_1((s+2k)L). Hence prod_{p<=X}(1−p^{-s})^{-1} = zeta(s)·C_X(s)·exp(Z_X(s))·E_X(s), with Z_X(s) := sum_rho E_1((s−rho)log X) + sum_k E_1((s+2k)log X) and E_X -> 1 for sigma > 1/2 (Conrad Lemma 3.1). So the host's combined corrected product differs from zeta^{±1} by exactly exp(±Z_X), and the 'signed discrepancy' of OPEN_CUTS §6 is Z_X itself.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Orientation. Whether the host corrects the zeta-side or the Möbius-side product only flips the sign of Z_X; the boundedness question is unchanged. (2) The correction is entire and causal (kernel supported after log X), consistent with E_1((s−1)L) being the continuous version of sum_{p>X} p^{-s}. (3) Sharp cutoff. Item (iv) has not been checked against the host proof.

## CON-0023 — Speiser 1934 → Levinson–Montgomery 1974 → 2026: at most 16.375% of zeros of ζ' (in units of N(T)) lie left of the critical line

*Mechanism:* M5-historical-rediscovery · *Verdict:* **sound_but_known** · *Significance:* 2/5 · *Difficulty:* 1/5

**Source statements.**
- Speiser 1934, Math. Ann. 110, 514–521 [CAND-00069]: RH is equivalent to ζ' having no zeros in 0<σ<1/2. In the archive's reading this is qualitative/geometric ('Lamellen'), made rigorous by Levinson–Montgomery.
- Levinson–Montgomery 1974, Acta Math. 133, 49–65, as stated in CFKLT arXiv:2508.11108 §1.1: '(essentially) one-to-one correspondence between the non-real zeros of ζ(s) and those of ζ'(s) in the region σ<1/2'. Levinson used it in the direction ζ'-left ⇒ ζ-left: 'The same result holds for ζ(s) by [LM74]'. The standard form is N_1^−(T)=N^−(T)+O(log T), with both sides counting zeros in σ<1/2, 0<t<T, with multiplicity.
- Alpöge–Furman arXiv:2608.13637 Thm A and Lamzouri arXiv:2609.02882 Thm 1.1 (unrefereed, two independent proofs): liminf N_0^s/N ≥ 0.6725007. Knausgård arXiv:2610.08965 (computer-assisted, unrefereed): 0.67353820. PRZZ 2020 (refereed): κ ≥ 0.417293962.

**Proposed link.** The functional equation pairs each off-line zero ρ with 1−ρ̄ at the same height, so N^−(T)=(N(T)−N_0(T))/2, with N_0 counted with multiplicity and N_0≥N_0^s. Levinson–Montgomery then transfers any lower bound on the critical proportion into an upper bound on ζ'-zeros left of the line, and conversely.

**Compatibility checks.** Both counts include multiplicity; zeros of ζ' on σ=1/2 occur only at multiple zeros of ζ and are excluded from both sides. The direction I need, N_1^−≤N^−+O(log T), comes from the argument principle for ζ'/ζ on [σ0,1/2]×[0,T]. On σ=1/2, Re ζ'/ζ = −(1/2)log(t/2π)+O(1/t)<0, with indentations at critical zeros; for σ0→−∞, ζ'/ζ is close to a negative real number; the horizontal sides contribute O(log T). This gives a two-sided O(log T). I verified that sketch myself; the exact LM74 wording should still be checked. Total ζ'-zero count (Berndt 1970): N_1(T)=(T/2π)log(T/4π)−T/2π+O(log T)~N(T).

**New claim.** Corollary (conditional on the 2026 theorem). N_1^−(T) ≤ ((1−0.6725007)/2+o(1))N(T) = (0.163750+o(1))(T/2π)log T. With Knausgård's value the constant is 0.163231. The previous refereed constant via PRZZ+LM was 0.291353. Equivalently, at least 83.6% of the zeros of ζ' with 0<t<T lie in σ≥1/2.

**Already proved.** Speiser's equivalence (made rigorous by LM74), the LM correspondence, and the 2026 bound are imported. The corollary is a one-line composition.

**Conjectural.** Nothing beyond the unrefereed status of the 2026 theorem. The composition itself is immediate.

**Next step.** Confirm the exact two-sided form of LM74 Theorem 1 from the paper (DOI 10.1007/BF02392141; zbMATH 0287.10025 has no review text). Record the corollary in the CRIT/derivative-descent ledger as a 'free transfer'. Also check whether Levinson–Montgomery's second theorem, Σ(β'−1/2)~(T/2π)loglog(T/2π), combined with κ*, gives a new lower bound for the mean horizontal distance of the ζ'-zeros that lie right of the line.

**Potential counterexamples.** None is mathematical. The risk is bibliographic: the LM statement might be one-sided as printed, in which case my argument-principle sketch must be written out.

**Tests.** Numerical consistency only: no zeros of ζ' with σ<1/2 are known at any computed height, consistent with RH. The corollary cannot be tested numerically in a meaningful way.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** Grepped the full texts of AF 2608.13637 and Lamzouri 2609.02882 for Speiser, Levinson–Montgomery and ζ': no hits. arXiv API search for recent ζ'-zero papers: 2611.12438 (abstract read) does not state it. CFKLT 2508.11108 §1.1 describes LM. The corollary may be folklore among experts but I did not find it in print.

**Verifier reasoning.** Levinson-Montgomery (Acta Math. 133, 1974), Thm 1, is two-sided: N_1^-(T)=N^-(T)+O(log T), with both sides counting zeros in 0<sigma<1/2, 0<t<T. This is confirmed by the secondary statements (Garunkstis arXiv:1904.03123; Conrey's lectures math/0401126) and by CFKLT arXiv:2508.11108 Sec. 1.1, which I read. CFKLT say Levinson bounded the zeta' zeros left of the line by (1/3)(T/2pi)log T, 'the same result holds for zeta(s) by [LM74]', and then the functional equation gives the critical proportion. The functional equation gives N^-=(N-N_0)/2, with N_0 counted with multiplicity, and N_0>=N_0^s. Hence N_1^-(T)=(N(T)-N_0(T))/2+O(log T)<=((1-kappa)/2+o(1))N(T). With kappa=0.6725007 (AF Thm A, Lamzouri Thm 1.1, both read; unrefereed) the constant is 0.163750. With Knausgard's value it is 0.163231; with PRZZ, 0.291353. Berndt's N_1(T)~N(T) gives the '>=83.6%' phrasing. The arithmetic and the logic are correct. The proposer's argument-principle sketch (Re zeta'/zeta=-(1/2)log(t/2pi)+O(1/t) on the line) is the standard LM mechanism.

**Verifier correction.** None mathematically. Label it a direct substitution into the standard Levinson-Montgomery equivalence (proportion of zeta'-zeros left of the line = (1-kappa)/2+o(1), the identity behind Levinson's method), conditional on the unrefereed 2026 kappa. It is not a new result. Note that the relation is an equality up to the gap between N_0 and N_0^s.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Levinson-Montgomery 1974, Thm 1 (N_1^-=N^-+O(log T)). Levinson 1974 and Conrey 1989: Levinson's method is literally a bound on N_1^- transferred via LM74, as CFKLT arXiv:2508.11108 Sec. 1.1 recounts. The general transfer kappa -> (1-kappa)/2 is implicit in every Levinson-method paper; only the numerical constant 0.16375 is new, and it is conditional on the unrefereed AF/Lamzouri result.


## CON-0028 — The 7/10 pivot: no 2026 half-plane can improve the Guth-Maynard short-interval exponents, and what they do change

*Mechanism:* M6-compatibility · *Verdict:* **sound_but_known** · *Significance:* 2/5 · *Difficulty:* 1/5

**Source statements.**
- LED-ZDE-4 (piecewise A(sigma) recomputed with exact rationals): 3/(2-sigma) on [1/2, 7/10] (Ingham); 15/(3+5 sigma) on [7/10, 19/25) (Guth-Maynard); ... maximum 30/13 at sigma = 7/10.
- Gafni-Tao (as quoted in LED-ZDE-8 and LED-ZDE-9), Thm 1.1: if A(sigma) <= A0 then the PNT holds in [x, x+x^theta] for theta > 1-1/A0 (all x) and theta > 1-2/A0 (almost all x). Thm 1.2: mu(theta) <= sup{(1-theta)(1-s)A(s)+2s-1 : A(s) >= 1/(1-theta)}.
- Visser arXiv:2610.20058: assuming 7/8 QRH, there are primes in (x, x+x^{7/8}) for all x > 1.
- Pintz-Althofer arXiv:2610.15559, abstract: E(X) << X^{7/12+eps} under any fixed half-plane theta0 < 1; g(theta) = theta(5-4theta)/(2(2-theta)) for theta in [1/2, 2/3]. Li-Liu arXiv:2610.26756, Theorems 1-2: under Q_Theta with 1/2 <= Theta < 11/12, E_+-(N) << N^{1/2}(log N)^{100}; minor-arc fourth moment << X^{5/2}(log X)^{100}.
- Chen-Gupta-Li (LED-ZDE-11), Thm 1.2: sum over chi mod q of N(sigma,T,chi) <= (qT)^{o(1)} (q^{7(1-sigma)/3} T^{2(1-sigma)} + (qT)^{30(1-sigma)/13}).

**Proposed link.** Zero-free half-plane theta0 together with density gives sup over [1/2, theta0] of A(sigma). Because the Guth-Maynard extremal sigma = 7/10 lies below every 2026 theta0 (7/8, 11/12, 31/32, B-flat, 599/600, 1-1e-6), the all-interval and almost-all thresholds are unchanged. Only the Gafni-Tao exceptional-set exponents, whose maximizers can sit above theta0, move. For Goldbach and for Linnik's constant, QRH matters because those problems depend on zeros near sigma = 1 or on L^2 averages.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Recomputed sup A on [1/2, 7/8] = 2.30767, which matches 30/13. (2) Endpoint check at theta0 = 1/2: the formulas (1+theta0)/3 and (2theta0-1)/3 give 1/2 and 0, which agree with RH. (3) Goldbach: Pintz-Althofer g(1/2) = 1/2, g(2/3) = 7/12, and Li-Liu's N^{1/2} supersede them for all Theta < 11/12. Li-Liu explicitly cite Pintz-Althofer and Bhowmik-Grimmelt (2Theta-1/2), so there is no conflict. At Theta = 1/2, Li-Liu are consistent with Goldston's GRH bound N^{1/2} log^3 N. (4) Linnik: at T = q^{o(1)} the q^{7(1-sigma)/3} term dominates, so QRH (any theta < 1) gives L <= 7/3 + eps, matching research_programs LFUN (ii). Every theta gives the same value. (5) Gauss sphere (Bellotti-Kerr 2610.29636): the QRH exponent 2274811/1743872 = 1.30446 is below the unconditional 55/42 = 1.30952, which is consistent.

**New claim.** (a) For every theta0 in [7/10, 1), adding N(sigma,T) = 0 for sigma > theta0 to the current A(sigma) table leaves the thresholds 17/30 (all intervals) and 2/15 (almost all) unchanged. So none of the 2026 half-planes improves short-interval PNT exponents; Visser's x^{7/8} matters only because it is explicit for all x. A half-plane theta0 < 7/10 would give (1+theta0)/3 and (2theta0-1)/3. (b) Under QRH 7/8, the Gafni-Tao Thm 1.2 exponent (basic A, not A*) improves only for theta in about (0.23, 0.42), by at most 0.010. Examples: mu(0.25) 0.9219 -> 0.9192; mu(0.30) 0.9100 -> 0.9000; mu(0.35) 0.8924 -> 0.8893; mu(0.40) 0.8800 -> 0.8786. Cureton-Thorner's 31/32 changes nothing, since all unconditional maximizers have sigma* <= 0.922.

**Already proved.** The sup computation and the threshold algebra (python script gt_mu.py, sha256 d663795d...). The Pintz-Althofer and Li-Liu statements and their mutual citation were read in full text.

**Conjectural.** The mu values use Gafni-Tao Thm 1.2 with the basic A(sigma). Their Thm 1.3 with additive energy A* gives better unconditional values (e.g. mu(17/30) <= 7/12), and the QRH effect there was not recomputed. Li-Liu's fourth-moment theorem is unrefereed and unaudited.

**Next step.** Recompute mu(theta) with Gafni-Tao Thm 1.3 (A* data) under theta0 in {7/8, B-flat, 31/32}. Add a 'QRH-sensitive?' flag to the ZDE ledger rows. Audit Li-Liu's threshold 11/12, which uses Theta0 = max(Theta, 7/8) <= 11/12 at l.3434.

**Potential counterexamples.** A QRH consumer claiming a short-interval exponent below 17/30 from theta0 >= 7/10 without new density input would be wrong. None was found.

**Tests.** Implement the A(sigma) table in exact rationals and verify the maximizer and the mu values on a finer grid.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** Gafni-Tao (2505.24017) give the general principle. Li-Liu's introduction surveys the Goldbach consumers. An arXiv search (abs:'quasi-Riemann' AND 'short intervals') returned only 2610.15989 (zero density in short strips) and an unrelated paper. No note was found observing that the 7/10 pivot makes QRH irrelevant for the 17/30 and 2/15 thresholds.

**Verifier reasoning.** Gafni-Tao 2505.24017 is quoted correctly. Thm 1.1 gives PNT in all intervals for theta > 1-1/A0 and in almost all intervals for theta > 1-2/A0. Thm 1.2 is mu(theta) <= inf_eps sup over sigma with A(sigma) >= 1/(1-theta)-eps of (1-theta)(1-sigma)A(sigma)+2sigma-1. The paper already notes that A <= 30/13 gives 17/30 and 2/15. The table matches LED-ZDE-4, including the apparent jumps at 127/167, 279/314 and other breakpoints. Since sup A = 30/13 is attained at sigma = 7/10 (both Ingham and Guth-Maynard), any zero-free half-plane with theta0 >= 7/10 leaves the sup, and hence 17/30 and 2/15, unchanged. The theta0 < 7/10 formulas (1+theta0)/3 and (2theta0-1)/3 are correct. My independent script (mu.py, grid on [0,1)) reproduces (b) exactly: mu(0.25) 0.9219 -> 0.9192; mu(0.30) 0.9100 -> 0.9000; mu(0.35) 0.8924 -> 0.8893; mu(0.40) 0.8800 -> 0.8786. B-flat gives essentially the same, and 31/32 changes none of these (unconditional maximizers <= 0.922). No change at theta = 0.20, 0.23, 0.42 or 0.45. Pintz-Althofer (7/12 under any fixed theta0; g(theta) = theta(5-4theta)/(2(2-theta))) and Li-Liu (N^{1/2}(log N)^{100} for Theta < 11/12) are quoted correctly. Li-Liu cite Pintz-Althofer, the bounds are mutually consistent, and at Theta = 1/2 Li-Liu are weaker than Goldston's log^3, as expected. The Linnik L <= 7/3+eps under QRH follows from Chen-Gupta-Li Thm 1.2 (which is unrefereed); the half-plane absorbs the (qT)^{o(1)} loss.

**Verifier correction.** (a) is an immediate corollary of Gafni-Tao Thm 1.1 together with the Guth-Maynard extremal point, so it is known in substance; no explicit statement for QRH was found. (b) improves only the Thm 1.2 (basic A) bound. Gafni-Tao Thm 1.3 (with A*) gives better unconditional mu, so (b) improves the record only if it survives under Thm 1.3; that has not been recomputed. Li-Liu's claim that any Theta < 11/12 already gives the GRH power N^{1/2} is extraordinary and unaudited, and it should be flagged as an audit target rather than used as a consistency anchor.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Gafni-Tao arXiv:2505.24017 (Thms 1.1-1.3; the 30/13 -> 17/30, 2/15 remark after Table 1). Guth-Maynard Cor. 1.3/1.4. The OpenAI 7/8 paper (l.172-176) says zero-density estimates address a different question but draws no short-interval conclusion. Visser 2610.20058 (explicit x^{7/8} for all x > 1).


## CON-0029 — Omega-ceiling compatibility of half-plane consequences: the 1/(1-theta) scaling law and an explicit Littlewood transfer

*Mechanism:* M6-compatibility · *Verdict:* **sound_but_known** · *Significance:* 2/5 · *Difficulty:* 3/5

**Source statements.**
- OpenAI 11/12 paper, Sec.1: 'Littlewood's short Euler-product argument [29], applied to the fixed zero-free half-plane ... yields the effective class-number bound h(D) >> sqrt|D|/log log|D|'. The same paragraph states that the least quadratic nonresidue is bounded by a fixed power of log p.
- Kimura arXiv:2610.21511, Remark 11: 'the argument of Littlewood gives h(-d) >> sqrt d/log log d with an effective implied constant ... Lamzouri, Li and Soundararajan made this bound explicit under GRH ... We have not made it explicit under the half-plane.'
- Shunia arXiv:2611.02280, Lemma 4.1 and Cor.6.1: under a common boundary theta, the auxiliary-prime cutoff is B = L^{1/(1-theta)+o(1)}, and GRH gives L^{2+o(1)}.
- Montgomery 1977 (CAND-00336, Theorem 2): GRH implies the sum over n <= x of chi(n) is << q^{1/2} log log q.
- Classical Omega-results (Titchmarsh Sec.8.8-8.9; Chowla 1949; Granville-Soundararajan 2003; Paley 1932): |zeta(1+it)| >= (e^gamma+o(1)) log log t and 1/|zeta(1+it)| >= (6e^gamma/pi^2+o(1)) log log t infinitely often. L(1,chi_d) >= (e^gamma+o(1)) log log d and <= (pi^2/(6e^gamma)+o(1))/log log d infinitely often. Character sums are Omega(sqrt q log log q).

**Proposed link.** With a smooth cutoff, the zero sum in log L(1,chi) is << y^{theta-1} log q. The short Euler product therefore has length y = (log q)^{1/(1-theta)+eps}. Every Littlewood-type constant then scales with log y = (1/(1-theta)) log log q. The upper bounds become e^gamma/(1-theta) times log log, and the lower bounds become (1-theta) pi^2/(6e^gamma) divided by log log. The Omega-results above fix the ceilings. The ratio of the half-plane bound to the attained Omega value is exactly 1/(1-theta): 2 under RH (Littlewood's classical factor 2), 7.99 for B-flat, 8 for 7/8, 12 for 11/12, 32 for Cureton-Thorner.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) The ratio 1/(1-theta) is >= 1 for every theta in [1/2, 1). So no half-plane claim can contradict the Chowla, Granville-Soundararajan, Paley or Titchmarsh Omega-results, and each claimed corollary (class number, least nonresidue polylog, character sums << sqrt q log log q) sits inside its ceiling. (2) At theta = 1/2 the constants reduce to Littlewood's GRH constants (pi^2/(12e^gamma) and 2e^gamma): checked. (3) Shunia's exponent 1/(1-theta) for the least prime outside a Chebotarev class is the same scaling; GRH gives 2 (Ankeny/Bach). (4) The Euler-product identity used: the product over p <= y of (1+1/p)^{-1} = (pi^2/6) e^{-gamma}/log y.

**New claim.** Proposition to be written with explicit constants. Suppose every primitive L(s,chi) mod q (respectively zeta) is zero-free in Re s > theta. Then, for q >= q0(theta, eps) effectively: ((1-theta) pi^2/(6e^gamma) - eps)/log log q <= L(1,chi) <= (e^gamma/(1-theta) + eps) log log q. Likewise |zeta(1+it)| <= (e^gamma/(1-theta)+eps) log log t and 1/|zeta(1+it)| <= (6e^gamma/(pi^2(1-theta))+eps) log log t. Also n_chi << (log q)^{1/(1-theta)+eps} and the sum of chi(n) over n <= x is <<_theta sqrt q log log q. Consequences: under 7/8 the class-number bound is h(-d) >= (pi/(48e^gamma) - eps) sqrt d/log log d; under 11/12 the constant is pi/(72 e^gamma). Each bound is a factor 1/(1-theta) from the Omega-ceilings.

**Already proved.** The scaling derivation (a sketch using the smoothed explicit formula with the unconditional zero count << log q in |gamma| <= 1) and its consistency with all quoted Omega-results. The q0 values are not explicit.

**Conjectural.** Explicit q0(theta, eps). The character-sum statement needs the Montgomery-Vaughan/Granville-Soundararajan machinery adapted to the half-plane, which was not checked line by line.

**Next step.** Write the explicit version following Lamzouri-Li-Soundararajan (Math. Comp. 2015), replacing Re rho = 1/2 by Re rho <= theta. Use Cureton-Thorner's Lean-formalized 31/32 as the weakest accepted input. Tabulate constants for theta in {31/32, 11/12, 7/8, B-flat}. Compare with Kimura Remark 11's zero-gap route, which gives h > 2000 for log d >= 27.2.

**Potential counterexamples.** A consumer that claims a constant beyond the 1/(1-theta) ceiling would be refuted by Chowla/Granville-Soundararajan. None was found in the 2026 papers checked (11/12 corollaries, Kimura, Shunia).

**Tests.** Numerical check: compute L(1,chi_d) for d up to 1e7 and confirm that min(L log log d) stays above the theta-scaled lower constant (a weak but cheap check). Recompute Shunia's Table 1 from the scaling.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** Littlewood 1928 and Lamzouri-Li-Soundararajan 2015 (GRH, explicit); Granville-Soundararajan 2003, Prop. 2.2 (zero-free rectangle gives a short Euler product, with a non-optimal 2/(1-theta) length). The 11/12 paper states the result only qualitatively, and Kimura's Remark 11 explicitly leaves the explicit half-plane version open. arXiv searches (abs:'quasi-Riemann' AND 'class number'; AND 'nonresidue') found nothing doing it.

**Verifier reasoning.** The cited statements check out: the OpenAI 11/12 paragraph (Littlewood's short Euler product applied to the fixed half-plane gives h(D) >> sqrt|D|/log log|D| effectively; the least nonresidue is a fixed power of log p via Rodosskii / MV Thm 13.12); Kimura Remark 11 ('We have not made it explicit under the half-plane'; Lamzouri-Li-Soundararajan is the GRH explicit version); Montgomery 1977 Thm 2 (GRH gives sum chi(n) << q^{1/2} log log q). The scaling derivation is the standard one. In a zero-free rectangle sigma > theta the short-Euler-product lemma has error about log q (log y)^2 y^{theta-1}, so y = (log q)^{1/(1-theta)+o(1)} and log y = (1/(1-theta)) log log q. With prod (1-1/p)^{-1} ~ e^gamma log y and prod (1+1/p)^{-1} ~ (pi^2/6) e^{-gamma}/log y this gives e^gamma/(1-theta) and (1-theta)pi^2/(6e^gamma). At theta = 1/2 these reduce to Littlewood's 2e^gamma and pi^2/(12e^gamma), and likewise 12e^gamma/pi^2 for 1/zeta. The class-number constants pi/(48e^gamma) at 7/8 and pi/(72e^gamma) at 11/12 are correct arithmetic. The 'Omega-ceiling compatibility' is trivially true: an upper bound weaker than an attained Omega value cannot contradict it, so it is not a real test.

**Verifier correction.** Present this as a routine qualitative adaptation, not a new scaling law. The 1/(1-theta) exponent for the least nonresidue is classical (Rodosskii; MV Thm 13.12; Pathak arXiv:2405.13316; Bhargava-Ivanyos-Mittal-Saxena Thm 6.7 as cited by OpenAI). The L(1,chi) and zeta(1+it) constants follow at once from the Granville-Soundararajan short-Euler-product lemma. The proposal's description of GS 2003 Prop. 2.2 as giving a 'non-optimal 2/(1-theta) length' was not verified, and with the usual sigma1 = min(sigma0 + 1/log y, ...) choice the length is optimal. The only part of value that remains open is the explicit q0(theta, eps) version, which is the item Kimura Rem. 11 leaves open; this proposal does not supply it. The character-sum claim (sqrt q log log q under a half-plane) is unverified: Montgomery-Vaughan's proof uses GRH both for the smooth-number reduction and for Littlewood's bound, and the adaptation has not been written.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Littlewood 1928. Lamzouri-Li-Soundararajan 2015 (explicit, under GRH). Granville-Soundararajan short-Euler-product lemma (2001/2003). Rodosskii 1954 / Montgomery-Vaughan Thm 13.12. Pathak arXiv:2405.13316 (conditional bounds on the least non-residue from zero-free regions). Bhargava-Ivanyos-Mittal-Saxena Thm 6.7. OpenAI 11/12 Sec. 1. Kimura 2610.21511 Remark 11.


## CON-0022 — Siegel 1932 (Riemann Nachlass) in reverse: the 2026 two-thirds theorem constrains Siegel's auxiliary function R(s) through the 'Siegel excess' of its phase

*Mechanism:* M5-historical-rediscovery · *Verdict:* **vocabulary_only** · *Significance:* 3/5 · *Difficulty:* 4/5

**Source statements.**
- Siegel 1932, 'Über Riemanns Nachlaß zur analytischen Zahlentheorie' (Quellen Studien Gesch. Math. B2, 45–80), as quoted in Conrey–Farmer–Kwan–Lin–Turnage-Butterbaugh arXiv:2508.11108 §1.3, eqs (6)–(8) and (10): h(s)ζ(s)=2Re h(s)𝔣(s) on Re s=1/2; N_0(T)>2·#{𝔣(s)=0: σ<1/2, 0<t<T}; N_0(T)>(3/8π)e^{−3/2}T.
- Levinson 1974 [CAND-00409] p. 384, quoted in CFKLT: 'It appears to me that the function f_1(s) [Siegel's] is not amenable to improvement with a mollifier.' CFKLT Prop. 1: as θ→0+, their optimal Q_θ converges pointwise to the step function Q_∞ that represents 𝔣.
- Arias de Reyna arXiv:2406.12344. Prop. 8: Z(t)=2Re(e^{i(ϑ(t)−ω(t))}g(t)), with R(1/2+it)=e^{−iω(t)}g(t) and g real-analytic. Prop. 9, eq. (7): ω(T)=2πN_r(T)+O(log T), where N_r counts R-zeros with 0<γ≤T and β≥1/2 (zeros on the line counted with weight 1/2). Prop. 12, eq. (9): ω=ϑ+u+d−Bt+arctan 2t+ω(0), B≈0.05592. Eq. (10): u(t)=π(N_r−N_l)+O(t^{1/2}log t).
- Arias de Reyna arXiv:2406.08890: N_R(T)=(T/4π)log(T/2π)−T/4π−(1/2)√(T/2π)+O(T^{2/5}log²T). arXiv:2406.03041: of 162215 zeros with 0<γ<215946.3, 63.9% have β<1/2. arXiv:2406.01474 §11, Problem 4: does lim N_R(β<1/2,T)/N_R(T) exist, and is it 1? Same paper, item 14: 'if N_r≈ϑ/(6π) ... at least 2/3 of the zeros of ζ(s) will be on the critical line'.
- Alpöge–Furman arXiv:2608.13637 Thm A and Lamzouri arXiv:2609.02882 Thm 1.1 (unrefereed, independent proofs): liminf N_0^s(T)/N(T) ≥ 3/2−(1/√2)cot(1/√2)=0.6725007.

**Proposed link.** Let φ=ϑ−ω. The distinct zeros of Z in (0,T] are exactly the solutions of φ∈π/2+πℤ together with the zeros of g. For a continuous φ, each level between φ(0) and φ(T) is crossed an odd number of times and every other level an even number of times. Hence #solutions = (number of levels between) + 2E(T) exactly, where E(T)≥0 counts 'excess crossing pairs'. Arias de Reyna's Prop. 9 together with ϑ(T)/π=N(T)−1−S(T) gives: number of levels between = N(T)−2N_r(T)+O(log T). Siegel's inequality is the statement E≥0. Combining this exact identity with the 2026 lower bound turns a theorem about ζ into a statement about the zeros and phase of R.

**Compatibility checks.** (1) Normalisation. I built my own evaluator R(1/2+it)=Σ_{n≤N}n^{−s}−∫ along the slope-1 line through N+1/2 (saddle), with N=⌊√(t/2π)⌋. 2Re(e^{iϑ}R) agrees with mpmath.siegelz to 10–12 digits at t=20, 50.3, 100.7, 120.3, 333.3, 777.7, 1000.1, 2999.9 and 10500.3. (2) Siegel's 𝔣 is Arias de Reyna's R, and N_R≈N/2−(1/2)√(T/2π), so R-proportions are relative to N/2. (3) Multiplicity: the 2026 bound counts simple critical zeros, which is at most the number of distinct zeros of Z. Zeros of R on the line enter separately. (4) A correction from my own testing. An earlier 'total variation' upper bound, N_0≤TV(φ)/π+1, is false because φ can oscillate across a level with very little variation. Numerically on [100,400]: 173 zeros against TV/π=147.4. The correct bookkeeping counts level crossings (E), not backtracked phase mass.

**New claim.** (i) [Elementary, given Arias de Reyna's Prop. 9] N_0^{dist}(T)=N(T)−2N_r(T)+2E(T)+N_R^{line*}(T)+O(log T), where E(T)≥0 is the excess crossing count of φ=ϑ−ω and N_R^{line*} counts R-zeros on the line that are not already solutions. (ii) [Given the 2026 theorem] E(T) ≥ N_r(T)−(0.16375+o(1))N(T)−N_R^{line}(T)/2. Equivalently, at most about 0.16375·N(T)≈0.3275·N_R(T) right-of-line R-zeros can fail to be compensated by an excess crossing pair. (iii) [RH with simple zeros up to T, known to 3·10^12] E(T)=N_r(T)+O(N_R^{line}+log T): Arias de Reyna's x-ray mechanism, now as an exact count. (iv) [Conjectural attribution lemma] Each excess crossing pair can be injectively attributed to a right-of-line R-zero with β−1/2<c/log γ. Heuristically the needed threshold is c≈6, because a Poisson spike 1/δ must beat a background of about π×(density of left zeros)≈0.16 log t. If (iv) holds, (ii) gives #{R-zeros with β≥1/2+c/log γ, γ≤T} ≤ (0.3275+o(1))N_R(T). In words, at least 67.25% of R-zeros lie left of 1/2+c/log γ: a partial answer to Siegel's 1932 question, conditional only on the 2026 theorem. This is obtained without mollifying R, which Levinson judged impossible.

**Already proved.** Imported: Siegel's identity and inequality; Arias de Reyna Props 8, 9, 12, 14 and the N_R asymptotic (arXiv preprints, read at statement level); the 2026 κ*≥0.6725 (unrefereed, two independent proofs). Item (i) is elementary bookkeeping that I checked. Item (ii) follows from (i) plus the 2026 theorem. Item (iii) is immediate from (i) on the verified range.

**Conjectural.** The attribution lemma (iv) is open. It needs pointwise control of d'(t) (the lower-half-plane zeros of R, which is not real-symmetric) and of clusters of right zeros. The asymptotic right-zero proportion p_r=lim N_r/N_R is unknown; Problem 4 is open.

**Next step.** (a) Write (i)–(ii) rigorously, including Prop. 9's O(log T) and the transversality of crossings. (b) Attack (iv) via eq. (9): ω'−ϑ'=u'+d'−B+O(t^{−2}); show d'(t)≤B−c'log t outside a sparse set. (c) Numerically locate R-zeros by the argument principle on [1/2,2]×[T,T+H] at T=10^4–10^6, and test whether each excess crossing pair has a right zero within 6/log γ.

**Potential counterexamples.** The attribution can fail if some excess pairs come from left zeros, or from d'-fluctuations caused by the fourth-quadrant zeros ρ_{−n}~4π²n/log²n−4πin/log n (Arias de Reyna arXiv:2406.11279). It also fails if clusters of moderately far right zeros jointly cause a backtrack. If p_r stays below 0.3275 asymptotically, (ii) is vacuous; the numerics suggest otherwise.

**Tests.** Run with my own code, double precision, validated against siegelz: /tmp/claude-0/-home-user-riemann/e62aa4cb-6226-5e37-8f52-ea0fa055746f/scratchpad/m5code/siegel_fold_np.py. On [100,1100] (step 0.005): 701 sign changes of Z against Δϑ/π=701.07; net Δφ/π=493.38; E=103.8; Δω/2π=103.85; local right proportion 2ΔN_r/ΔN=0.296. On [10^4,1.1·10^4] (step 0.002): 1182 sign changes against 1181.07; net 787.45; E≈197.3; Δω/2π=196.81; 2ΔN_r/ΔN=0.3333. That matches Arias de Reyna's ϑ/(6π) heuristic and sits above the 2026 threshold 0.3275. Arias de Reyna's cumulative value to 2.16·10^5 is 0.361. So the 2026 theorem is not implied by Siegel's inequality: excess crossings carry at least the margin above 0.3275 asymptotically, if p_r>0.3275 persists. Caveat: on the verified range E=Δω/2π is tautological given RH. Independent validation of Prop. 9 needs actual R-zero counts.

**Prior-art search (proposer).** Searches: arXiv API (abs:'auxiliary function' AND Riemann-Siegel; au:Arias de Reyna 2024–2026, about 25 papers listed; abs:mollifier AND auxiliary function; Levinson AND Siegel). Read: Arias de Reyna 2406.01474 in full (open problems), 2406.12344 (Props 8–14), abstracts of 2406.07968, 2406.14987, 2406.08890, 2406.03041 and 2406.13278. Read CFKLT 2508.11108 §1.3 and §7. Grepped Arias de Reyna 2610.24612 (his exposition of the 2/3 theorem), AF 2608.13637 and Lamzouri 2609.02882: none mentions R(s), Siegel or Levinson–Montgomery. I found no reverse transfer from critical-zero proportions to R-zeros. Direct mollification of R is ruled out by Levinson's remark and CFKLT Prop. 1. The forward route (Siegel ⇒ κ) is dominated: 0.639 < 0.6725.

**Verifier reasoning.** Source check. Arias de Reyna arXiv:2406.12344 Prop. 8 (Z=2Re(e^{i(theta-omega)}g)), Prop. 9 (omega(T)=2piN_r(T)+O(log T), with N_r counting beta>=1/2 and line zeros weighted 1/2), Prop. 12 (B about 0.05592) and Prop. 14 (u=pi(N_r-N_l)+O(t^{1/2}log t)) are as quoted. So are eq. (6) for N_R, the 63.9% figure (103674/162215, arXiv:2406.03041 Sec. 9), Problem 4, and item 14 (arXiv:2406.01474). The AF Theorem A and Lamzouri Thm 1.1 constants (0.67250) are also correct.

The implication fails as a statement about R. In (i), E is by definition (#solutions of cos phi=0 minus #levels)/2, and #levels=N-2N_r+O(log T) by Prop. 9. Substituting (i) into (ii), N_r cancels and (ii) becomes N_0^dist - N_R^{line*} >= (0.6725-o(1))N. That is the 2026 theorem restated, with no information about the zeros or phase of R. (iii) is likewise a restatement of RH on the verified range. The only substantive content is the attribution lemma (iv), which is unproved.

(iv) also carries a consistency burden the proposal does not state. Under RH, which holds numerically to 3e12, (iii) gives E=N_r+O(...). Injectivity into right zeros with beta-1/2<c/log gamma would then force all but o(N) right R-zeros into that window. This is a sharp horizontal-concentration claim, unknown even under RH. Arias de Reyna's density theorem only gives concentration at scale o(1), not 1/log gamma.

The tests are vacuous at the computed heights. With c=6 and gamma<=2.2e5, c/log gamma>=0.49, so the window covers the whole right half of the strip (beta<1 is known for gamma<215946). As the proposal concedes, E=Delta omega/2pi is tautological there. Finally, (ii) is empty if Problem 4 has the answer 1 (N_r=o(N)), a possibility Arias de Reyna explicitly allows.

**Verifier correction.** Restate it as follows. (i)-(iii) are an equivalent reformulation of N_0 counts in Siegel/Arias de Reyna phase variables, and (ii) is equivalent to the 2026 bound. No constraint on R(s) follows without a new lemma. The '67.25% of R-zeros lie left of 1/2+c/log gamma' claim is conditional on both the 2026 theorem and the open attribution lemma (iv), not 'only on the 2026 theorem'. State the necessary consequence of (iv)+RH: #{right R-zeros with beta-1/2>=c/log gamma, gamma<=T}=o(N(T)). Any test of (iv) needs heights where c/log gamma<1/2, i.e. gamma>e^{2c} (about 1.6e5 for c=6), and preferably much higher. Also handle tangential level contacts: E can then be a half-integer.

**Prior art found by verifier.** Siegel 1932 (N_0>2#{left R-zeros}) and Arias de Reyna's 'x-ray' picture (arXiv:2406.01474 item 14 and Sec. 16; arXiv:2406.03041 Sec. 9) already contain this bookkeeping. Arias de Reyna also already states the heuristic that each near-line right zero 'produces its two zeros for zeta(s)' (2406.01474, Sec. 16), which is the content of (iv). Arias de Reyna arXiv:2406.12344, Prop. 9 and the abstract: N_0>=(theta-omega)/pi. I found no published reverse transfer, but the reverse direction as stated has no content.
